## No Sinecure / 绝非闲差

### I / 一

===Original===
I am still uncertain which surprised me more, the telegram calling my attention to the advertisement, or the advertisement itself. The telegram is before me as I write. It would appear to have been handed in at Vere Street at eight o’clock in the morning of May 11, 1897, and received before half-past at Holloway B.O. And in that drab region it duly found me, unwashen but at work before the day grew hot and my attic insupportable.
“See Mr. Maturin’s advertisement *Daily Mail* might suit you earnestly beg try will speak if necessary —﻿—﻿—”

===Chinese===
至今我仍说不准，更叫我吃惊的究竟是那封提醒我留意广告的电报，还是那则广告本身。动笔之际，电报就摊在我眼前。它应该是 1897 年 5 月 11 日早晨八点钟在维尔街（Vere Street）交寄的，八点半以前便送到了霍洛韦（Holloway）分局。就在那片灰扑扑的地界，它如期寻到了我——其时我尚未盥洗，却已抢在天热起来、阁楼待不住人之前，伏案干活了。

“见马图林（Maturin）先生广告每日邮报（Daily Mail）或合足下务恳一试必要时当代为进言———”

===Original===
I transcribe the thing as I see it before me, all in one breath that took away mine; but I leave out the initials at the end, which completed the surprise. They stood very obviously for the knighted specialist whose consulting-room is within a cab-whistle of Vere Street, and who once called me kinsman for his sins. More recently he had called me other names. I was a disgrace, qualified by an adjective which seemed to me another. I had made my bed, and I could go and lie and die in it. If I ever again had the insolence to show my nose in that house, I should go out quicker than I came in. All this, and more, my least distant relative could tell a poor devil to his face; could ring for his man, and give him his brutal instructions on the spot; and then relent to the tune of this telegram! I have no phrase for my amazement. I literally could not believe my eyes. Yet their evidence was more and more conclusive: a very epistle could not have been more characteristic of its sender. Meanly elliptical, ludicrously precise, saving halfpence at the expense of sense, yet paying like a man for “Mr.” Maturin, that was my distinguished relative from his bald patch to his corns. Nor was all the rest unlike him, upon second thoughts. He had a reputation for charity; he was going to live up to it after all. Either that, or it was the sudden impulse of which the most calculating are capable at times; the morning papers with the early cup of tea, this advertisement seen by chance, and the rest upon the spur of a guilty conscience.
Well, I must see it for myself, and the sooner the better, though work pressed. I was writing a series of articles upon prison life, and had my nib into the whole System; a literary and philanthropical daily was parading my “charges,” the graver ones with the more gusto; and the terms, if unhandsome for creative work, were temporary wealth to me. It so happened that my first check had just arrived by the eight o’clock post; and my position should be appreciated when I say that I had to cash it to obtain a *Daily Mail*.

===Chinese===
我照眼前所见，将原文一字不漏地抄录于此：那一口气呵成的电文，夺走了我这一口气；只是略去了末尾的署名缩写，而那恰是惊喜的收梢。那几个缩写再明白不过，指向那位受封爵士的专科名医——他的诊室离维尔街不过一声马车哨的远近，况且他从前还曾造孽似的认下我这门亲戚。晚些时候他叫过我别的名目。我是个耻辱——前头还缀着一个形容词，那形容词在我看来又是另一个。床是我自己铺的，我可以滚去躺在上面等死。我若再敢放肆，把鼻子伸进那座宅子，管保我出去得比进来还快。这些话，外加更多，我这位血缘最近的亲戚都能够当面摔给一个倒霉蛋；能够摇铃唤来仆人，当场交付他那套狠心的逐客令；然后却心回意转，回了这么一封电报！我的惊愕无辞可状。我简直无法相信自己的眼睛。然而眼见的证据越来越确凿：哪怕一封正经长函，也未必比它更能见出寄信人的本色。吝啬地省略，可笑地精确，为省几个便士不惜牺牲文理，却又肯像个爷们儿似的，为“马图林先生”里的“先生”二字付足电报字费——这就是我那位显赫的亲戚，从他的秃顶到他的鸡眼，无一不然。再一想，其余种种也不像不是他的手笔。他素有慈善之名；看来他到底打算名副其实一回。要么如此，要么这就是最善算计的人偶尔也会犯的心血来潮：早茶伴着晨报，偶然瞥见这则广告，余下的全凭一颗负疚的良心从旁催迫。

不成，我总得亲眼瞧瞧，而且越快越好，尽管手头的活计正吃紧。我当时正写一组关于监狱生活的文章，笔尖直捣整个监狱制度；一家文艺兼慈善的日报正在游行展示我的“控诉”，题目越重的越起劲；那稿酬，就创造性劳动而言固然寒酸，于我却是暂时的富贵。偏巧我的第一张支票刚随八点钟的邮班送到；我说自己得先把它兑成现钱，才买得起一份《每日邮报》——我的处境如何，由此可以想见。

===Original===
Of the advertisement itself, what is to be said? It should speak for itself if I could find it, but I cannot, and only remember that it was a “male nurse and constant attendant” that was “wanted for an elderly gentleman in feeble health.” A male nurse! An absurd tag was appended, offering “liberal salary to University or public-school man”; and of a sudden I saw that I should get this thing if I applied for it. What other “University or public-school man” would dream of doing so? Was any other in such straits as I? And then my relenting relative; he not only promised to speak for me, but was the very man to do so. Could any recommendation compete with his in the matter of a male nurse? And need the duties of such be necessarily loathsome and repellent? Certainly the surroundings would be better than those of my common lodging-house and own particular garret; and the food; and every other condition of life that I could think of on my way back to that unsavory asylum. So I dived into a pawnbroker’s shop, where I was a stranger only upon my present errand, and within the hour was airing a decent if antiquated suit, but little corrupted by the pawnbroker’s moth, and a new straw hat, on the top of a tram.
The address given in the advertisement was that of a flat at Earl’s Court, which cost me a cross-country journey, finishing with the District Railway and a seven minutes’ walk. It was now past midday, and the tarry wood-pavement was good to smell as I strode up the Earl’s Court Road. It was great to walk the civilized world again. Here were men with coats on their backs, and ladies in gloves. My only fear was lest I might run up against one or other whom I had known of old. But it was my lucky day. I felt it in my bones. I was going to get this berth; and sometimes I should be able to smell the wood-pavement on the old boy’s errands; perhaps he would insist on skimming over it in his bath-chair, with me behind.

===Chinese===
至于那则广告本身，还有什么可说的？若能找出来，它自会说话，可惜找不出来了，只记得广告要聘的是“男护士兼常任随侍”，应聘去伺候“一位体弱的老先生”。男护士！末尾还缀着一条荒唐的尾巴，许以“优厚薪金，唯大学或公学出身之士”；刹那之间我明白了：这差事只要我去应征，便归我。还有哪一位“大学或公学出身之士”，会做梦想到来应征？又有谁沦到了我这般田地？何况我那位回心转意的亲戚：他不但答应替我进言，而且正是进此言的第一人选。论男护士，哪份荐书敌得过他的？再说，这一行的职守，就必定污浊可憎么？至少，环境总好过我这平民公寓和我那顶专属的阁楼；伙食也是；在走回那座气味可疑的避难所的路上，我能想到的生活的其余条件，无一不是。于是我一头扎进一家当铺——只是此行此业，我在那儿还算生客——不出一个钟头，我已经坐上电车顶层，晾着一身虽属陈年却尚体面、几乎没遭当铺蠹虫腐蚀的衣裳，外加一顶崭新草帽。

广告上的地址是厄尔斯考特（Earl’s Court）的一处公寓，害我横穿了半座城，末程先坐区域铁路（District Railway），再步行七分钟。此时已过晌午，我大步走上厄尔斯考特路，柏油木块铺就的路面闻着煞是受用。重又行走于文明世界，真好。这里的男人背上有外套，淑女们手上戴手套。我唯一的顾虑，是怕迎面撞上哪位旧相识。可这一天是我走运的日子，我骨头缝里都感觉得到。这个差事非我莫属；往后替老头子跑腿，我还能时时闻见这木块路面；没准他还非要坐上他的轮椅车，在这路面上溜上一溜不可，我在后头推着。

===Original===
I felt quite nervous when I reached the flats. They were a small pile in a side street, and I pitied the doctor whose plate I saw upon the palings before the ground-floor windows; he must be in a very small way, I thought. I rather pitied myself as well. I had indulged in visions of better flats than these. There were no balconies. The porter was out of livery. There was no lift, and my invalid on the third floor! I trudged up, wishing I had never lived in Mount Street, and brushed against a dejected individual coming down. A full-blooded young fellow in a frock-coat flung the right door open at my summons.
“Does Mr. Maturin live here?” I inquired.
“That’s right,” said the full-blooded young man, grinning all over a convivial countenance.
“I﻿—I’ve come about his advertisement in the *Daily Mail*.”

===Chinese===
走到那幢公寓楼前，我竟相当紧张。那是小街上的一座小楼，一楼窗前的栅栏上挂着一块医生的铭牌，我看着便替他难过：想必生意做得极小。我也颇替自己难过。我先前纵容自己幻想过比这更好的公寓。没有阳台。门房不穿号衣。没有电梯，而我的病人竟住在三楼！我一步步蹭上去，一面懊悔自己不该有过住芒特街（Mount Street）的日子，半路同一个垂头丧气下楼的家伙擦肩而过。我在要找的那扇门上一叩，一个满面红光的年轻家伙便穿着长礼服，把门呼地拉开了。

“马图林先生住在这儿吗？”我问道。

“没错，”满面红光的年轻人说，一张好客的脸笑开了花。

“我——我是为他登在《每日邮报》上的广告来的。”

===Original===
“You’re the thirty-ninth,” cried the blood; “that was the thirty-eighth you met upon the stairs, and the day’s still young. Excuse my staring at you. Yes, you pass your prelim., and can come inside; you’re one of the few. We had most just after breakfast, but now the porter’s heading off the worst cases, and that last chap was the first for twenty minutes. Come in here.”
And I was ushered into an empty room with a good bay-window, which enabled my full-blooded friend to inspect me yet more critically in a good light; this he did without the least false delicacy; then his questions began.
“ ’Varsity man?”
“No.”

===Chinese===
“你是第三十九位，”那红光满面的家伙嚷道；“你在楼梯上碰见的是第三十八位，这一天还早着呢。恕我这么盯着你。行，你的初试过了，可以进来；你是寥寥可数的那几个之一。最多的是早饭刚过那阵，如今门房把最不像话的都拦下了，刚才那位还是二十分钟里的头一个。进来吧。”

于是我被引进一间空屋，屋里带一扇上好的凸窗，让我这位红光满面的朋友得以在充足的光线里把我端详得愈加苛刻；他端详得毫无假客套；随后，他的盘问开始了。

“念过大学的？”

“没有。”

===Original===
“Public school?”
“Yes.”
“Which one?”
I told him, and he sighed relief.

===Chinese===
“公学出身？”

“是的。”

“哪一所？”

我告诉了他，他如释重负地叹了口气。

===Original===
“At last! You’re the very first I’ve not had to argue with as to what is and what is not a public school. Expelled?”
“No,” I said, after a moment’s hesitation; “no, I was not expelled. And I hope you won’t expel me if I ask a question in my turn?”
“Certainly not.”
“Are you Mr. Maturin’s son?”

===Chinese===
“可算有了！你是头一个不用跟我争辩什么算公学、什么不算公学的。被开除过？”

“没有，”我迟疑了片刻才说；“没有，我没被开除。不过若是我反过来问一句，你总不至于把我也开除了吧？”

“当然不至于。”

“你是马图林先生的儿子？”

===Original===
“No, my name’s Theobald. You may have seen it down below.”
“The doctor?” I said.
“His doctor,” said Theobald, with a satisfied eye. “Mr. Maturin’s doctor. He is having a male nurse and attendant by my advice, and he wants a gentleman if he can get one. I rather think he’ll see you, though he’s only seen two or three all day. There are certain questions which he prefers to ask himself, and it’s no good going over the same ground twice. So perhaps I had better tell him about you before we get any further.”
And he withdrew to a room still nearer the entrance, as I could hear, for it was a very small flat indeed. But now two doors were shut between us, and I had to rest content with murmurs through the wall until the doctor returned to summon me.

===Chinese===
“不是，我叫西奥博尔德（Theobald）。楼底下那块牌子，你兴许看见了。”

“那位医生？”我说。

“他的医生，”西奥博尔德说，眼里一副得色。“马图林先生的医生。请男护士兼随侍，是我的主意；他呢，请得到绅士的话，是想要一位绅士的。我看他多半愿意见你，虽然一整天他也只见了两三位。有些问题他宁可亲自问，同一片地走两遍没有意义。所以趁咱们还没往下谈，我先去对他讲讲你的事。”

说罢他退进一间离大门更近的屋子——我听得出来，因为这公寓实在小得可以。可如今两道门都关上了，我只得安于隔墙听那嗡嗡的低语，直到医生回来传我。

===Original===
“I have persuaded my patient to see you,” he whispered, “but I confess I am not sanguine of the result. He is very difficult to please. You must prepare yourself for a querulous invalid, and for no sinecure if you get the billet.”
“May I ask what’s the matter with him?”
“By all means﻿—when you’ve got the billet.”
Dr. Theobald then led the way, his professional dignity so thoroughly intact that I could not but smile as I followed his swinging coattails to the sickroom. I carried no smile across the threshold of a darkened chamber which reeked of drugs and twinkled with medicine bottles, and in the middle of which a gaunt figure lay abed in the half-light.

===Chinese===
“我说动病人见你了，”他悄声道，“不过恕我直言，我对结果不抱乐观。他这人极难取悦。你得有个准备：迎接一位动辄生怨的病人；而且这差事即便到手，也绝非闲差。”

“请问他到底怎么不好？”

“尽管问——等你差事到手。”

西奥博尔德医生于是领头带路，那身职业尊严完好无损，以致我跟在他甩动的燕尾后摆后面走向病房时，禁不住直想笑。可一跨进那间昏暗病室的门槛，我脸上的笑意便荡然无存——屋里药气逼人，药瓶莹莹闪光，半明半暗里，正当中一张床上躺着瘦骨嶙峋的一副身影。

===Original===
“Take him to the window, take him to the window,” a thin voice snapped, “and let’s have a look at him. Open the blind a bit. Not as much as that, damn you, not as much as that!”
The doctor took the oath as though it had been a fee. I no longer pitied him. It was now very clear to me that he had one patient who was a little practice in himself. I determined there and then that he should prove a little profession to me, if we could but keep him alive between us. Mr. Maturin, however, had the whitest face that I have ever seen, and his teeth gleamed out through the dusk as though the withered lips no longer met about them; nor did they except in speech; and anything ghastlier than the perpetual grin of his repose I defy you to imagine. It was with this grin that he lay regarding me while the doctor held the blind.

===Chinese===
“带他到窗口去，带他到窗口去，”一个尖细的嗓门喝道，“让我瞧瞧他。把帘子掀开一点。用不着那么多，该死的，用不着那么多！”

医生把这句咒骂照单全收，仿佛收的是诊金。我不再可怜他。此刻我看得分明：他有这么一位病人，单他一个就算一桩小小的营生。我当场拿定主意：只要我们二人合力保得他活命，他就得成为我的一份小小行当。不过，马图林先生那张脸，实在是我平生所见最白的一张；他的牙在昏暗中泛出光来，仿佛干瘪的嘴唇再也合不拢、裹不住它们；事实也果真裹不住，除非开口说话；而他安卧时那副永恒的龇牙之笑有多可怖，我谅你想破头也想象不出。医生掀着帘子，他便带着这副笑躺在那里，端详着我。

===Original===
“So you think you could look after me, do you?”
“I’m certain I could, sir.”
“Single-handed, mind! I don’t keep another soul. You would have to cook your own grub and my slops. Do you think you could do all that?”
“Yes, sir, I think so.”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你以为你照管得了我，嗯？”

“我准照管得了，先生。”

“是一个人包揽，记着！我一个别的活人也不雇。你的粗茶淡饭得自己做，我的汤水稀粥也得你来弄。这些你全当真做得到？”

“做得到，先生，我想做得到。”

===Original===
“Why do you? Have you any experience of the kind?”
“No, sir, none.”
“Then why do you pretend you have?”
“I only meant that I would do my best.”

===Chinese===
“凭什么做得到？这一路的事你干过？”

“没干过，先生，一件也没干过。”

“那你装什么干过？”

“我只是说，我会尽力。”

===Original===
“Only meant, only meant! Have you done your best at everything else, then?”
I hung my head. This was a facer. And there was something in my invalid which thrust the unspoken lie down my throat.
“No, sir, I have not,” I told him plainly.
“*He, he, he!*” the old wretch tittered; “and you do well to own it; you do well, sir, very well indeed. If you hadn’t owned up, out you would have gone, out neck-and-crop! You’ve saved your bacon. You may do more. So you are a public-school boy, and a very good school yours is, but you weren’t at either University. Is that correct?”

===Chinese===
“只是说，只是说！那么别的事，你倒都尽力来着？”

我垂下了头。这一问正中面门。何况我这位病人身上有股什么，能把没出口的谎话生生堵回我喉咙里。

“没有，先生，我没有，”我坦白对他说。

“嘻，嘻，嘻！”老恶棍嗤嗤笑道；“认了便好；你认得好，先生，好得很哪。方才你若不认，早叫你连人带铺盖一齐滚出去了！这条小命算是留下了。往后兴许还有更大的用处。这么说，你是公学出身，你那所学校很是不错，可两所大学你一所也没上过。对也不对？”

===Original===
“Absolutely.”
“What did you do when you left school?”
“I came in for money.”
“And then?”

===Chinese===
“完全正确。”

“离开学校以后，你做何营生？”

“我继承了一笔钱。”

“然后呢？”

===Original===
“I spent my money.”
“And since then?”
I stood like a mule.
“And since then, I say!”

===Chinese===
“我把钱花了个精光。”

“打那以后呢？”

我像骡子一样杵在原地。

“打那以后呢，我问你！”

===Original===
“A relative of mine will tell you if you ask him. He is an eminent man, and he has promised to speak for me. I would rather say no more myself.”
“But you shall, sir, but you shall! Do you suppose that I suppose a public-school boy would apply for a berth like this if something or other hadn’t happened? What I want is a gentleman of sorts, and I don’t much care what sort; but you’ve got to tell me what did happen, if you don’t tell anybody else. Dr. Theobald, sir, you can go to the devil if you won’t take a hint. This man may do or he may not. You have no more to say to it till I send him down to tell you one thing or the other. Clear out, sir, clear out; and if you think you’ve anything to complain of, you stick it down in the bill!”

===Chinese===
“我有位亲戚，你去问他便知。他是位显赫人物，又已答应替我说项。我自己宁肯少说。”

“宁肯少说也得说，先生，非说不可！你以为我会以为，一个公学出身的少年，若不是出了什么岔子，肯来谋这样一份差事？我要的是个将就的绅士，哪一种的我不大理会；可是到底出了什么事，你非得告诉我不可，哪怕你对谁都绝口不提。西奥博尔德医生，先生，你再听不出个眉目，就请自便、见鬼去吧。这个人行不行，还两说。等我打发他下去给你句准话之前，这事你插不上嘴了。出去，先生，出去；你要觉得受了什么亏待，就把它一笔一笔记到账单上去！”

===Original===
In the mild excitement of our interview the thin voice had gathered strength, and the last shrill insult was screamed after the devoted medico, as he retired in such order that I felt certain he was going to take this trying patient at his word. The bedroom door closed, then the outer one, and the doctor’s heels went drumming down the common stair. I was alone in the flat with this highly singular and rather terrible old man.
“And a damned good riddance!” croaked the invalid, raising himself on one elbow without delay. “I may not have much body left to boast about, but at least I’ve got a lost old soul to call my own. That’s why I want a gentleman of sorts about me. I’ve been too dependent on that chap. He won’t even let me smoke, and he’s been in the flat all day to see I didn’t. You’ll find the cigarettes behind the *Madonna of the Chair*.”

===Chinese===
我们这场晤谈不上多么激动，那尖嗓门却蓄足了力气；最后那声尖利的侮辱，是冲着那位恪尽职守的医士撤退的背影尖叫出去的；他退得阵脚井然，我由此断定，这句气话他是要记账照办的。卧室门关了，跟着是大门，医生的鞋跟咚咚敲下公共楼梯。公寓里只剩我一人，对着这个万分古怪、相当可怕的老头子。

“滚得真干净，谢天谢地！”病人沙声道，随即用一只臂肘撑起半身。“我这躯壳是没什么可夸口了，可好歹还剩一颗失落的老灵魂，全归我自个儿。所以我要身边有个将就的绅士。我对那家伙依赖得也太久了。他连烟都不许我碰，今天整日守在这公寓里，就为看我碰不碰。香烟在《椅中圣母》（Madonna of the Chair）后头。”

===Original===
It was a steel engraving of the great Raphael, and the frame was tilted from the wall; at a touch a packet of cigarettes tumbled down from behind.
“Thanks; and now a light.”
I struck the match and held it, while the invalid inhaled with normal lips; and suddenly I sighed. I was irresistibly reminded of my poor dear old Raffles. A smoke-ring worthy of the great A. J. was floating upward from the sick man’s lips.
“And now take one yourself. I have smoked more poisonous cigarettes. But even these are not Sullivans!”

===Chinese===
那是拉斐尔（Raphael）名画的一幅钢版刻，画框从墙面上微微翘起；轻轻一碰，一包香烟便从背后跌落下来。

“多谢；再借个火。”

我划了火柴给他举着，病人用寻常的双唇吸了一口；蓦地，我叹了口气。我不由自主想起我那可怜的、亲爱的老拉夫尔斯（Raffles）。一个烟圈，一个无愧于伟大的 A. J. 之名的烟圈，正从病人唇边冉冉升起。

“现在你自己也来一支。比这更毒的烟我也抽过。可再毒，这也不是萨利文（Sullivans）啊！”

===Original===
I cannot repeat what I said. I have no idea what I did. I only know﻿—I only knew﻿—that it was A. J. Raffles in the flesh!

===Chinese===
我说了什么，无从复述。我做了什么，全无印象。我只知道——我当时便知道——那不是别人，正是活生生的 A. J. 拉夫尔斯！

### II / 二

===Original===
“Yes, Bunny, it was the very devil of a swim; but I defy you to sink in the Mediterranean. That sunset saved me. The sea was on fire. I hardly swam under water at all, but went all I knew for the sun itself; when it set I must have been a mile away; until it did I was the invisible man. I figured on that, and only hope it wasn’t set down as a case of suicide. I shall get outed quite soon enough, Bunny, but I’d rather be dropped by the hangman than throw my own wicket away.”
“Oh, my dear old chap, to think of having you by the hand again! I feel as though we were both aboard that German liner, and all that’s happened since a nightmare. I thought that time was the last!”

===Chinese===
“是啊，邦尼（Bunny），那一趟泳可真够呛；不过在地中海，你休想沉得下去。是那轮落日救了我。海面是一片火海。我几乎全没在水底下游，只管把浑身解数使出来，直朝太阳本身扑去；太阳落尽时，我少说已在一英里开外；在它落下去之前，我就是个隐身人。这一切都在我算计之中，只盼没被记作一桩自杀案。我这条命迟早要交代，邦尼，可我宁肯让绞刑吏来收场，也不肯自毁三柱门。”

“哦，我亲爱的老伙计，想不到还能再握住你的手！我恍惚觉得，咱们俩还都坐在那条德国邮轮上，后来的种种不过一场噩梦。我原以为那一回就是永诀了！”

===Original===
“It looked rather like it, Bunny. It was taking all the risks, and hitting at everything. But the game came off, and some day I’ll tell you how.”
“Oh, I’m in no hurry to hear. It’s enough for me to see you lying there. I don’t want to know how you came there, or why, though I fear you must be pretty bad. I must have a good look at you before I let you speak another word!”

===Chinese===
“当时确实很像永诀，邦尼。那一局是豁出全部风险，见什么打什么。可牌打赢了，哪天我讲给你听。”

“哦，我一点不急着听。能看见你躺在这儿，于我足矣。你怎么到的这里、又为了什么，我一概不想知道，尽管只怕你必定病得不轻。在我放你再说一个字之前，我得先好好看看你！”

===Original===
I raised one of the blinds, I sat upon the bed, and I had that look. It left me all unable to conjecture his true state of health, but quite certain in my own mind that my dear Raffles was not and never would be the man that he had been. He had aged twenty years; he looked fifty at the very least. His hair was white; there was no trick about that; and his face was another white. The lines about the corners of the eyes and mouth were both many and deep. On the other hand, the eyes themselves were alight and alert as ever; they were still keen and gray and gleaming, like finely tempered steel. Even the mouth, with a cigarette to close it, was the mouth of Raffles and no other: strong and unscrupulous as the man himself. It was only the physical strength which appeared to have departed; but that was quite sufficient to make my heart bleed for the dear rascal who had cost me every tie I valued but the tie between us two.
“Think I look much older?” he asked at length.

===Chinese===
我掀起一幅百叶帘，在床沿坐下，就这样看了个够。这一看，我说不透他真实的病况究竟如何，心里却斩钉截铁：我亲爱的拉夫尔斯已经不是、也永远不会是从前那个人了。他老了二十岁；看上去至少五十开外。他的头发白了；这里头没有任何花招；他的脸又是另一种白。眼角嘴边的纹路又多又深。可另一方面，那双眼睛依旧灼灼有神、警醒如昔；依旧敏锐、灰亮、闪光，如淬火得宜的精钢。就连那张衔着香烟的嘴，也还是拉夫尔斯的嘴，不是任何别人的：同他本人一样有力，一样不讲情面。仿佛离他而去的只是气力；可单这一样，已足以叫我为那亲爱的恶棍心头发酸——为了他，我舍弃了我看重的每一重牵绊，只余下我们两个之间的这一重。

“看我老得厉害么？”他终于开口。

===Original===
“A bit,” I admitted. “But it is chiefly your hair.”
“Whereby hangs a tale for when we’ve talked ourselves out, though I have often thought it was that long swim that started it. Still, the Island of Elba is a rummy show, I can assure you. And Naples is a rummier!”
“You went there after all?”
“Rather! It’s the European paradise for such as our noble selves. But there’s no place that’s a patch on little London as a nonconductor of heat; it never need get too hot for a fellow here; if it does it’s his own fault. It’s the kind of wicket you don’t get out on, unless you get yourself out. So here I am again, and have been for the last six weeks. And I mean to have another knock.”

===Chinese===
“老了些，”我承认，“不过主要是头发。”

“这里头吊着一段故事，等咱们把话谈尽了再讲；虽说我常想，根子还是那场长泳。不过话说回来，厄尔巴岛（Island of Elba）这地方实在古怪，我向你担保。那不勒斯（Naples）更是古怪到底！”

“你到底还是去了？”

“可不！那是咱们这等高贵人物在欧洲的天堂。不过，说到不传热，天下没有一处赶得上小伦敦；在这儿，一个人永远不必“热”得受不了；当真“热”了，那是他自己招的。这儿的三柱门，你不自取出局，谁也请你不动。于是我又回来了，回来已六个星期。而且我打算再击一记。”

===Original===
“But surely, old fellow, you’re not awfully fit, are you?”
“Fit? My dear Bunny, I’m dead﻿—I’m at the bottom of the sea﻿—and don’t you forget it for a minute.”
“But are you all right, or are you not?”
“No, I’m half-poisoned by Theobald’s prescriptions and putrid cigarettes, and as weak as a cat from lying in bed.”

===Chinese===
“不过说真的，老伙计，你这身子骨可不济了吧？”

“不济？亲爱的邦尼，我已经死了——我已在海底——这话你一分钟也别忘。”

“可你到底好不好？”

“不好。我让西奥博尔德的药方和腐臭的香烟毒了个半死，卧床卧得像只猫一样虚弱。”

===Original===
“Then why on earth lie in bed, Raffles?”
“Because it’s better than lying in gaol, as I am afraid *you* know, my poor dear fellow. I tell you I am dead; and my one terror is of coming to life again by accident. Can’t you see? I simply dare not show my nose out of doors﻿—by day. You have no idea of the number of perfectly innocent things a dead man daren’t do. I can’t even smoke Sullivans, because no one man was ever so partial to them as I was in my lifetime, and you never know when you may start a clue.”

===Chinese===
“那你干吗还躺在床上，拉夫尔斯？”

“因为那总比躺进监狱强——这一节，只怕你是深有体会的，我可怜的伙计。我告诉你，我已经死了；我唯一的恐惧，便是无意间又还了阳。你还不明白？白天，我压根不敢把鼻子伸出大门。一个死人有多少再清白不过的事不敢做，你根本想不到。我连萨利文都不能抽：生前迷恋这烟入骨的，天下没有第二个，而你永远说不准什么时候就牵出一条线索。”

===Original===
“What brought you to these mansions?”
“I fancied a flat, and a man recommended these on the boat; such a good chap, Bunny; he was my reference when it came to signing the lease. You see I landed on a stretcher﻿—most pathetic case﻿—old Australian without a friend in old country﻿—ordered Engadine as last chance﻿—no go﻿—not an earthly﻿—sentimental wish to die in London﻿—that’s the history of Mr. Maturin. If it doesn’t hit you hard, Bunny, you’re the first. But it hit friend Theobald hardest of all. I’m an income to him. I believe he’s going to marry on me.”

===Chinese===
“你怎么住到这幢楼里来的？”

“我一时起意想要套公寓，船上有人荐了这一处；是个大好人，邦尼；到签租约时，他还做了我的担保人。你知道，我是让人用担架抬上岸的——病例惨绝人寰——老澳大利亚人，在故国举目无亲——奉医嘱往恩加丁（Engadine）作最后一搏——不成——半点指望也无——怀着伤感的心愿来伦敦等死——这就是马图林先生的全部来历。这故事若戳不痛你，邦尼，你还是头一个。可戳得最痛的，还得数咱们的朋友西奥博尔德。我于他是一注进项。我相信他打算靠着我娶媳妇了。”

===Original===
“Does he guess there’s nothing wrong?”
“Knows, bless you! But he doesn’t know I know he knows, and there isn’t a disease in the dictionary that he hasn’t treated me for since he’s had me in hand. To do him justice, I believe he thinks me a hypochondriac of the first water; but that young man will go far if he keeps on the wicket. He has spent half his nights up here, at guineas apiece.”

===Chinese===
“他猜到你压根没病么？”

“猜？他门儿清，老天保佑！只是他不知道我知道他知道。自我落入他手，医典里查得着的病，他没有一样不给我治过。凭良心讲，我相信他拿我当头号疑病症瞧；不过这年轻人只要守得住三柱门，前程远大得很。他把半夜半夜的工夫耗在这楼上，一夜一个几尼（guinea）。”

===Original===
“Guineas must be plentiful, old chap!”
“They have been, Bunny. I can’t say more. But I don’t see why they shouldn’t be again.”
I was not going to inquire where the guineas came from. As if I cared! But I did ask old Raffles how in the world he had got upon my tracks; and thereby drew the sort of smile with which old gentlemen rub their hands, and old ladies nod their noses. Raffles merely produced a perfect oval of blue smoke before replying.

===Chinese===
“几尼想必挣得不少了，老伙计！”

“是挣得不少，邦尼。多的我不说了。不过我看不出，凭什么不能再挣一回。”

几尼打哪儿来，我不打算打听。好像我在乎似的！不过我确实问了老拉夫尔斯：他究竟是怎么寻上我的行踪的？这一问，问出了老头子搓手、老太太点头的那一种微笑。拉夫尔斯只是先吐出一个浑圆的蓝烟圈，方才作答。

===Original===
“I was waiting for you to ask that, Bunny; it’s a long time since I did anything upon which I plume myself more. Of course, in the first place, I spotted you at once by these prison articles; they were not signed, but the fist was the fist of my sitting rabbit!”
“But who gave you my address?”
“I wheedled it out of your excellent editor; called on him at dead of night, when I occasionally go afield like other ghosts, and wept it out of him in five minutes. I was your only relative; your name was not your own name; if he insisted I would give him mine. He didn’t insist, Bunny, and I danced down his stairs with your address in my pocket.”

===Chinese===
“我就等你问这一句，邦尼；好久没干过一桩更叫我自得的事了。当然，头一条，一看这些监狱文章我便认出了你；文章虽未署名，可那笔迹，正是我那只坐等挨枪的兔子的笔迹！”

“可谁把我的地址给了你？”

“我从你那位好得无以复加的编辑嘴里哄出来的。我夜半登门——别的幽灵偶尔出游，我也一样——不到五分钟便磨了出来。我自称是你唯一的亲戚；你如今的名字原不是你自己的名字；他若非要问到底，我打算把我的名字报给他。他没有非问，邦尼，于是我揣着你的地址，一路踏着舞步下了他的楼梯。”

===Original===
“Last night?”
“No, last week.”
“And so the advertisement was yours, as well as the telegram!”
I had, of course, forgotten both in the high excitement of the hour, or I should scarcely have announced my belated discovery with such an air. As it was I made Raffles look at me as I had known him look before, and the droop of his eyelids began to sting.

===Chinese===
“昨儿夜里？”

“不，上礼拜。”

“这么说，那则广告是你登的，那封电报也是你发的！”

方才那阵高度的兴奋里，这两桩我自然忘了个干净，否则断不至于摆出那副神气，来宣布这项姗姗来迟的发现。这一来，拉夫尔斯看我的那种眼色，是我从前就领教过的；他那垂下的眼皮，也开始扎得我生疼。

===Original===
“Why all this subtlety?” I petulantly exclaimed. “Why couldn’t you come straight away to me in a cab?”
He did not inform me that I was hopeless as ever. He did not address me as his good rabbit.
He was silent for a time, and then spoke in a tone which made me ashamed of mine.
“You see, there are two or three of me now, Bunny: one’s at the bottom of the Mediterranean, and one’s an old Australian desirous of dying in the old country, but in no immediate danger of dying anywhere. The old Australian doesn’t know a soul in town; he’s got to be consistent, or he’s done. This sitter Theobald is his only friend, and has seen rather too much of him; ordinary dust won’t do for his eyes. Begin to see? To pick you out of a crowd, that was the game; to let old Theobald help to pick you, better still! To start with, he was dead against my having anybody at all; wanted me all to himself, naturally; but anything rather than kill the goose! So he is to have a fiver a week while he keeps me alive, and he’s going to be married next month. That’s a pity in some ways, but a good thing in others; he will want more money than he foresees, and he may always be of use to us at a pinch. Meanwhile he eats out of my hand.”

===Chinese===
“何必费这许多周章？”我负气地嚷道，“你不能索性雇辆马车，径直来寻我么？”

他没有指点我说，我还是像从前一样没救。也没有唤我一声他的好兔子。

他沉默了片刻，然后开口，那声气叫我为自己的声气羞愧。

“你瞧，如今的我共有两三个之多，邦尼：一个在地中海海底；一个是巴望着死在故国的老澳大利亚人——眼下却哪里也死不成。这位老澳大利亚人在城里一个熟人也没有；他必须处处对得上账，不然就全盘穿帮。这位坐地户西奥博尔德是他唯一的朋友，跟他打照面又打得忒多；寻常的烟尘迷不了此公的眼。开窍了没有？把你从人堆里择出来，这便是那局牌；让老西奥博尔德帮着一道择，更是妙上加妙！起先，他是拼死反对我雇任何人的；自然是想独占我；可万万宰不得这只下金蛋的鹅！于是，只消他保我活命，他每星期稳拿五镑，而且下月就要成亲。这在某些方面是件憾事，在另一些方面倒是好事；他要的钱会比他预想的多，而情急之时，他也未始不能为咱们所用。眼下，他乖乖吃我手掌心里的食。”

===Original===
I complimented Raffles on the mere composition of his telegram, with half the characteristics of my distinguished kinsman squeezed into a dozen odd words; and let him know how the old ruffian had really treated me. Raffles was not surprised; we had dined together at my relative’s in the old days, and filed for reference a professional valuation of his household gods. I now learnt that the telegram had been posted, with the hour marked for its despatch, at the pillar nearest Vere Street, on the night before the advertisement was due to appear in the *Daily Mail*. This also had been carefully prearranged; and Raffles’s only fear had been lest it might be held over despite his explicit instructions, and so drive me to the doctor for an explanation of his telegram. But the adverse chances had been weeded out and weeded out to the irreducible minimum of risk.
His greatest risk, according to Raffles, lay nearest home: bedridden invalid that he was supposed to be, his nightly terror was of running into Theobald’s arms in the immediate neighborhood of the flat. But Raffles had characteristic methods of minimizing even that danger, of which something anon; meanwhile he recounted more than one of his nocturnal adventures, all, however, of a singularly innocent type; and one thing I noticed while he talked. His room was the first as you entered the flat. The long inner wall divided the room not merely from the passage but from the outer landing as well. Thus every step upon the bare stone stairs could be heard by Raffles where he lay; and he would never speak while one was ascending, until it had passed his door. The afternoon brought more than one applicant for the post which it was my duty to tell them that I had already obtained. Between three and four, however, Raffles, suddenly looking at his watch, packed me off in a hurry to the other end of London for my things.

===Chinese===
我恭维了拉夫尔斯那封电报的谋篇：十来个单词里，挤进了我那位显赫亲戚的半身形貌；又让他知道，那位老恶棍实际是怎么打发我的。拉夫尔斯并不惊讶；当年我们曾在我那位亲戚府上同桌进餐，还给他的家宅珍藏估过一回行价，立档备查。我这才知道，电报是在广告预定见报于《每日邮报》的前夜，投进离维尔街最近的邮筒的，连发报的钟点都预先标定。这一步也是预先排布停当的；拉夫尔斯唯一的顾虑，是怕它虽经他明确叮嘱，仍被延搁不发，倒逼我寻到那位医生头上去，讨他那封电报的解说。但一切逆势都已一茬又一茬地锄尽，锄到风险再无可减的地步。

照拉夫尔斯的说法，他最大的风险近在肘腋：人人视他为卧床的废人，而他每夜最怕的，便是在公寓左近一头撞进西奥博尔德怀里。不过，就连这一重险，拉夫尔斯也有他标志性的消解之法，此处暂且按下，来日再表；其时他讲了好几桩夜半的奇遇，好在全属清白无邪的一类；他讲的时候，有一件事我留了心。他的房间在进公寓后的第一间。那道纵贯的内墙，不仅把这间屋子与走廊隔开，也同外面的楼梯平台隔开。如此，光石梯上的每一步，躺着的拉夫尔斯都听得分明；每逢有人上楼，他绝不出声，直到脚步过他的门而去。下午又来了不止一位应征人，我的职分便是告诉他们：差事已归了我。三点到四点之间，拉夫尔斯忽然看了看表，急匆匆打发我上伦敦另一头去取行李。

===Original===
“I’m afraid you must be famishing, Bunny. It’s a fact that I eat very little, and that at odd hours, but I ought not to have forgotten you. Get yourself a snack outside, but not a square meal if you can resist one. We’ve got to celebrate this day this night!”
“Tonight?” I cried.
“Tonight at eleven, and Kellner’s the place. You may well open your eyes, but we didn’t go there much, if you remember, and the staff seems changed. Anyway we’ll risk it for once. I was in last night, talking like a stage American, and supper’s ordered for eleven sharp.”

===Chinese===
“只怕你早饿坏了，邦尼。不瞒你说，我吃得极少，还尽在邪门钟点，可我也不该把你忘到这地步。在外头对付口零嘴，忍得住就别吃正餐。今天这个日子，咱们得在今天这个夜里庆贺！”

“今夜？”我喊道。

“今夜十一点，地方是凯尔纳（Kellner’s）。你尽管瞪眼睛：咱们从前不大去那儿，你总记得，再说堂倌似乎也换过了。无论如何，咱们就冒这一回险。我昨晚去过一趟，满口舞台美国腔，夜宵已定在十一点整。”

===Original===
“You made as sure of me as all that!”
“There was no harm in ordering supper. We shall have it in a private room, but you may as well dress if you’ve got the duds.”
“They’re at my only forgiving relative’s.”
“How much will get them out, and square you up, and bring you back bag and baggage in good time?”

===Chinese===
“你对我竟有这么十拿九稳！”

“订一桌夜宵又不折本。咱们在包间里用；不过你要是有行头，最好也换上礼服。”

“在我那唯一肯饶恕我的亲戚手里。”

“要多少钱，才能把它们赎出来，把你欠的账结清，再叫你连人带行李按时赶回来？”

===Original===
I had to calculate.
“A tenner, easily.”
“I had one ready for you. Here it is, and I wouldn’t lose any time if I were you. On the way you might look up Theobald, tell him you’ve got it and how long you’ll be gone, and that I can’t be left alone all the time. And, by Jove, yes! You get me a stall for the Lyceum at the nearest agent’s; there are two or three in High Street; and say it was given you when you come in. That young man shall be out of the way tonight.”

===Chinese===
我不得不盘算一番。

“十镑，富富有余。”

“我给你备好了一张。拿去——换了是我，一分钟也不耽搁。路上不妨去看看西奥博尔德，告诉他你到手了、要离开多久，还有，我不能总没人陪着。啊，对了，老天为证！你顺路在最近的售票代理那里，给我买张兰心剧院（Lyceum）的正厅票；大街上就有两三家；回来只说票是人家送的。今晚非叫那位年轻人靠边站不可。”

===Original===
I found our doctor in a minute consulting-room and his shirtsleeves, a tall tumbler at his elbow; at least I caught sight of the tumbler on entering; thereafter he stood in front of it, with a futility which had my sympathy.
“So you’ve got the billet,” said Dr. Theobald. “Well, as I told you before, and as you have since probably discovered for yourself, you won’t find it exactly a sinecure. My own part of the business is by no means that; indeed, there are those who would throw up the case, after the kind of treatment that you have seen for yourself. But professional considerations are not the only ones, and one cannot make too many allowances in such a case.”

===Chinese===
我寻见我们的医生时，他正待在一间小得转不开身的诊室里，只穿一件衬衫，臂肘边搁着一只大酒杯；至少进门那一眼，我瞧见了那只杯子；此后他便一直站到杯子前头挡着，那份徒劳劲儿颇得我同情。

“这么说，差事到手了，”西奥博尔德医生说。“嗯，正如我从前进过，也如你此后多半自己发觉的，这差事干起来绝算不得闲差。我分内的这一摊也绝非闲差；说实在的，亲眼见过那等款待之后，换作旁人，早把这病例撒手了。但职业上的考量并非唯一的考量，对这样的病例，再多的体谅也不为过。”

===Original===
“But what is the case?” I asked him. “You said you would tell me if I was successful.”
Dr. Theobald’s shrug was worthy of the profession he seemed destined to adorn; it was not incompatible with any construction which one chose to put upon it. Next moment he had stiffened. I suppose I still spoke more or less like a gentleman. Yet, after all, I was only the male nurse. He seemed to remember this suddenly, and he took occasion to remind me of the fact.

===Chinese===
“那究竟是怎么一例病？”我问他。“你说过，我若成了功，便告诉我。”

西奥博尔德医生那一耸肩，配得上他似乎注定要为之生色的这一行；无论你往那耸肩里读进何种解释，它都不与之相悖。下一刻，他便端了起来。我猜，我说话多少还像个绅士。然而说到底，我不过是名男护士。他似乎猛然记起了这一层，便趁势提醒我认清本分。

===Original===
“Ah,” said he, “that was before I knew you were altogether without experience; and I must say that I was surprised even at Mr. Maturin’s engaging you after that; but it will depend upon yourself how long I allow him to persist in so curious an experiment. As for what is the matter with him, my good fellow, it is no use my giving you an answer which would be double Dutch to you; moreover, I have still to test your discretionary powers. I may say, however, that that poor gentleman presents at once the most complex and most troublesome case, which is responsibility enough without certain features which make it all but insupportable. Beyond this I must refuse to discuss my patient for the present; but I shall certainly go up if I can find time.”
He went up within five minutes. I found him there on my return at dusk. But he did not refuse my stall for the Lyceum, which Raffles would not allow me to use myself, and presented to him offhand without my leave.

===Chinese===
“啊，”他说，“那是在我知道你全无经验之前；我必须说，连马图林先生在那之后还肯用你，都叫我吃惊；不过，他这桩新奇的实验还能做多久，全看你自己，也全看我准到几时。至于他患的什么病，我的好伙计，给你一个你如听天书的答案，毫无用处；再者，你的口风牢不牢，我尚待考验。不过我可以这样说：这位可怜的先生，一举集最复杂与最棘手于一身，这份责任已然够重，何况另有几桩情由，几乎叫人消受不起。过此以往，目前我拒绝再谈我的病人；但只要挤得出工夫，我一定上去。”

不出五分钟，他就上去了。我黄昏回转时，发现他正在楼上。不过，兰心剧院那张票他没有推辞——那票拉夫尔斯本不许我自己享用，也未征求我的同意，随手便转赠给了他。

===Original===
“And don’t you bother any more about me till tomorrow,” snapped the high thin voice as he was off. “I can send for you now when I want you, and I’m hoping to have a decent night for once.”

===Chinese===
“明天以前，别再来烦我，”他离去时，那又高又细的嗓门甩下一句。“如今我要人，自会打发人去叫；我还指望头一回睡个囫囵觉呢。”

### III / 三

===Original===
It was half-past ten when we left the flat, in an interval of silence on the noisy stairs. The silence was unbroken by our wary feet. Yet for me a surprise was in store upon the very landing. Instead of going downstairs, Raffles led me up two flights, and so out upon a perfectly flat roof.
“There are two entrances to these mansions,” he explained between stars and chimney-stacks: “one to our staircase, and another round the corner. But there’s only one porter, and he lives on the basement underneath us, and affects the door nearest home. We miss him by using the wrong stairs, and we run less risk of old Theobald. I got the tip from the postmen, who come up one way and down the other. Now, follow me, and look out!”

===Chinese===
我们离开公寓时是十点半，正赶上那嘈杂楼梯上一段空窗。我们谨慎的脚步没有惊破这段空窗。然而在平台上，一桩意外正等着我。拉夫尔斯没有领我下楼，反而带我上了两层，一路走到一片平坦的屋顶上。

“这幢楼有两个入口，”他在星子与烟囱之间解说，“一个通咱们的楼梯，另一个在拐角上。门房却只有一个，住在咱们底下的地下室，惯守离家最近的那扇门。咱们走那“错的”楼梯便避开了他，撞上老西奥博尔德的风险也小些。这诀窍是跟邮差学的：他们从一边上，从另一边下。来，跟着我，脚下留神！”

===Original===
There was indeed some necessity for caution, for each half of the building had its L-shaped well dropping sheer to the base, the parapets so low that one might easily have tripped over them into eternity. However, we were soon upon the second staircase, which opened on the roof like the first. And twenty minutes of the next twenty-five we spent in an admirable hansom, skimming east.
“Not much change in the old hole, Bunny. More of these magic-lantern advertisements﻿ ﻿… and absolutely the worst bit of taste in town, though it’s saying something, in that equestrian statue with the gilt stirrups and fixings; why don’t they black the buffer’s boots and his horse’s hoofs while they are about it?﻿ ﻿… More bicyclists, of course. That was just beginning, if you remember. It might have been useful to us.﻿ ﻿… And there’s the old club, getting put into a crate for the Jubilee; by Jove, Bunny, we ought to be there. I wouldn’t lean forward in Piccadilly, old chap. If you’re seen I’m thought of, and we shall have to be jolly careful at Kellner’s.﻿ ﻿… Ah, there it is! Did I tell you I was a low-down stage Yankee at Kellner’s? You’d better be another, while the waiter’s in the room.”

===Chinese===
小心确有必要：大楼的每一半各有一口 L 形的天井，直坠到底，女儿墙又那样矮，一个趔趄便能直接跨进永恒。好在没多大工夫，我们便到了第二道楼梯，它同第一道一样开在屋顶上。随后的二十五分钟里，有二十分钟，我们坐在一辆呱呱叫的双轮马车上，向东掠行。

“老地界没什么大变，邦尼。幻灯广告多了些……还有全城品味最烂的一绝——这话可不轻——就是那尊骑着马、配镀金马镫和全套镀金什物的骑马铜像；何不索性连那家伙的靴子带马蹄，一齐刷成黑的？……骑车的自然也多了。你若记得，那阵子才刚时兴。本可以给咱们派上用场。……瞧，老俱乐部在那儿，正往木笼里装，为钻禧（Jubilee）大事铺张；天哪，邦尼，咱们本该在那儿的。到了皮卡迪利（Piccadilly），老伙计，你可别探身向前。叫人瞧见你，人家就想起我；到了凯尔纳，咱们就得十二分小心。……啊，到了！我跟你说过没有，我在凯尔纳是个没起子的舞台美国佬？堂倌在屋里的时候，你最好也来上一个。”

===Original===
We had the little room upstairs; and on the very threshold I, even I, who knew my Raffles of old, was taken horribly aback. The table was laid for three. I called his attention to it in a whisper.
“Why, yep!” came through his nose. “Say, boy, the lady, she’s not comin’, but you leave that tackle where ’tis. If I’m liable to pay, I guess I’ll have all there is to it.”
I have never been in America, and the American public is the last on earth that I desire to insult; but idiom and intonation alike would have imposed upon my inexperience. I had to look at Raffles to make sure that it was he who spoke, and I had my own reasons for looking hard.

===Chinese===
我们要了楼上的小间；就在门槛上，连我——自以为熟识拉夫尔斯的我——也骇了一大跳。桌上摆了三副刀叉。我悄声请他注意。

“咦，是咧！”这话音是从鼻腔里挤出来的。“听我说，伙计，那位太太，她不来了，可那套家伙什你给搁在原处。横竖账是我来付，我能要多少是多少。”

我从未到过美国，美国公众也是这世上我最无心冒犯的；可无论惯用语还是腔调，都足以瞒过我这没见过世面的耳朵。我不得不看看拉夫尔斯，以确认说话的正是他本人；而我之所以死死地看，自有我自己的缘故。

===Original===
“Who on earth was the lady?” I inquired aghast at the first opportunity.
“She isn’t on earth. They don’t like wasting this room on two, that’s all. Bunny﻿—my Bunny﻿—here’s to us both!”
And we clinked glasses swimming with the liquid gold of Steinberg, 1868; but of the rare delights of that supper I can scarcely trust myself to write. It was no mere meal, it was no coarse orgy, but a little feast for the fastidious gods, not unworthy of Lucullus at his worst. And I who had bolted my skilly at Wormwood Scrubbs, and tightened my belt in a Holloway attic, it was I who sat down to this ineffable repast! Where the courses were few, but each a triumph of its kind, it would be invidious to single out any one dish; but the Jambon de Westphalie au Champagne tempts me sorely. And then the champagne that we drank, not the quantity but the quality! Well, it was Pol Roger, ’84, and quite good enough for me; but even so it was not more dry, nor did it sparkle more, than the merry rascal who had dragged me thus far to the devil, but should lead me dancing the rest of the way. I was beginning to tell him so. I had done my honest best since my reappearance in the world; but the world had done its worst by me. A further antithesis and my final intention were both upon my tongue when the waiter with the Château Margaux cut me short; for he was the bearer of more than that great wine; bringing also a card upon a silver tray.

===Chinese===
“那位太太究竟是哪路人物？”一有机会，我便惶然发问。

“她不在世上。他们舍不得把这么间屋子浪费在两个人头上，如此而已。邦尼——我的邦尼——为你我二人干这一杯！”

我们碰了杯，杯里晃荡着斯坦因贝格（Steinberg）1868 年的液态黄金；至于那顿夜宵的稀世至乐，我几乎不敢信笔落纸。那不单是一餐饭，也不是粗鄙的暴食，而是一场供挑剔的诸神受用的小小盛宴，卢库鲁斯（Lucullus）哪怕最俭省的时候，也未必辱没了它。而我，那个在沃姆伍德·斯克拉布斯（Wormwood Scrubbs）扒完稀粥、在霍洛韦阁楼里勒紧裤带的人，坐到这桌妙不可言的盛宴前的，竟是我！菜品寥寥，道道却是各自门类里的绝唱，硬要单单点出一道，未免有失公道；可那道“香槟煨威斯特伐利亚火腿”（Jambon de Westphalie au Champagne）实在馋得我心痒。还有我们喝的香槟——不论量，论质！那是宝禄爵（Pol Roger）’84，于我已是绰绰有余；可即便如此，论干冽它不及，论欢腾的气泡它也不及——不及那个一路把我拖向魔鬼、还要领着我手舞足蹈走完后半程的快乐恶棍。我正要这样告诉他。我重现人间以来，已尽了老老实实的本分；人间待我，却拿出了最狠的手段。再添一个对仗，连同我最终的打算，都已在舌尖上了——偏偏端玛歌（Château Margaux）来的堂倌打断了我；因为他捧来的不止那伟大的名酒，还有银盘上托着的一张名片。

===Original===
“Show him up,” said Raffles, laconically.
“And who is this?” I cried when the man was gone. Raffles reached across the table and gripped my arm in a vice. His eyes were steel points fixed on mine.
“Bunny, stand by me,” said he in the old irresistible voice, a voice both stern and winning. “Stand by me, Bunny﻿—if there’s a row!”
And there was time for nothing more, the door flying open, and a dapper person entering with a bow; a frock-coat on his back, gold pince-nez on his nose; a shiny hat in one hand, and a black bag in the other.

===Chinese===
“请他上来，”拉夫尔斯言简意赅。

“这是哪一位？”那人退下后，我喊道。拉夫尔斯隔着桌子探身过来，铁钳一般扣住我的手臂。他的眼睛是两枚钢尖，钉牢了我的眼睛。

“邦尼，站在我这边，”他用旧日那不容抗拒的嗓音说，那嗓音又严峻又动人。“站在我这边，邦尼——万一有一场好斗！”

话至此再无余地：门砰然洞开，一位精干利落的人物鞠着躬走进来；背上长礼服，鼻梁金丝夹鼻镜；一手亮闪闪的礼帽，一手黑色提包。

===Original===
“Good evening, gentlemen,” said he, at home and smiling.
“Sit down,” drawled Raffles in casual response. “Say, let me introduce you to Mr. Ezra B. Martin, of Shicawgo. Mr. Martin is my future brother-in-law. This is Mr. Robinson, Ezra, manager to Sparks & Company, the cellerbrated joolers on Regent Street.”
I pricked up my ears, but contented myself with a nod. I altogether distrusted my ability to live up to my new name and address.
“I figured on Miss Martin bein’ right here, too,” continued Raffles, “but I regret to say she’s not feelin’ so good. We light out for Parrus on the 9 a.m. train to-morrer mornin’, and she guessed she’d be too dead. Sorry to disappoint you, Mr. Robinson; but you’ll see I’m advertisin’ your wares.”

===Chinese===
“晚上好，二位先生，”他说，一副如归自家门厅的笑脸。

“坐，”拉夫尔斯懒洋洋地随口应道。“我说，容我把您引见给埃兹拉·B. 马丁（Ezra B. Martin）先生，希卡戈（Shicawgo）人氏。马丁先生是我未来的内弟。埃兹拉，这位是鲁滨逊（Robinson）先生，斯帕克斯公司（Sparks & Company）的经理，摄政街（Regent Street）上大名鼎鼎的珠宝商。”

我竖起了耳朵，却也只以颔首为度。要我撑得起这新名字新地址，我是全然信不过自己的。

“我原还盘算着马丁小姐也莅临来着，”拉夫尔斯接下去说，“可惜得很，她今儿个不爽快。明儿一早九点的车，我们就奔帕里斯去，她估摸着到时候准得累得半死。对不起，叫鲁滨逊先生空欢喜；可你也看得见，我这分明是在替你们的货色做广告。”

===Original===
Raffles held his right hand under the electric light, and a diamond ring flashed upon his little finger. I could have sworn it was not there five minutes before.
The tradesman had a disappointed face, but for a moment it brightened as he expatiated on the value of that ring and on the price his people had accepted for it. I was invited to guess the figure, but I shook a discreet head. I have seldom been more taciturn in my life.
“Forty-five pounds,” cried the jeweller; “and it would be cheap at fifty guineas.”
“That’s right,” assented Raffles. “That’d be dead cheap, I allow. But then, my boy, you gotten ready cash, and don’t you forget it.”

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯把右手伸到电灯底下，小指上一枚钻戒灼灼闪光。我可以起誓，五分钟前它还不在那儿。

店老板一脸失望，可讲起那枚戒指的身价、讲起他店里点头收下的价码，脸上又亮了一亮。他请我猜猜数目，我审慎地摇了摇头。我这辈子难得像那一刻一样寡言。

“四十五镑，”珠宝商喊道，“五十几尼买去都算捡了便宜。”

“说的是，”拉夫尔斯附和，“那可真是便宜到家了，我承认。不过呢，老弟，你们收的可是现钱，这话你记牢。”

===Original===
I do not dwell upon my own mystification in all this. I merely pause to state that I was keenly enjoying that very element. Nothing could have been more typical of Raffles and the past. It was only my own attitude that was changed.
It appeared that the mythical lady, my sister, had just become engaged to Raffles, who seemed all anxiety to pin her down with gifts of price. I could not quite gather whose gift to whom was the diamond ring; but it had evidently been paid for; and I voyaged to the moon, wondering when and how. I was recalled to this planet by a deluge of gems from the jeweller’s bag. They lay alight in their cases like the electric lamps above. We all three put our heads together over them, myself without the slightest clue as to what was coming, but not unprepared for violent crime. One does not do eighteen months for nothing.

===Chinese===
这一路我如何满腹疑云，不必多叙。只消说一句：恰是这一味疑云，我受用至极。再没有比这更像拉夫尔斯、更像往昔的了。变的只是我自己的态度。

原来那位子虚乌有的女士——我的妹妹——方才与拉夫尔斯订了婚，而他一副迫不及待要拿重礼把她钉牢的架势。那枚钻戒究竟是谁与谁的赠答，我不甚了然；但分明是付过了账的；我的思绪遂漫游到月亮上，琢磨着何时付的、怎么付的。珠宝商袋中一泻而下的宝石雨，把我唤回这颗星球。它们在盒中放光，一如头顶的电灯。我们三个把脑袋凑到宝石上方，我对将要发生的事毫无线索，却对暴力罪行并不全无准备。人不至于平白无故蹲上十八个月。

===Original===
“Right away,” Raffles was saying. “We’ll choose for her, and you’ll change anything she don’t like. Is that the idea?”
“That was my suggestion, sir.”
“Then come on, Ezra. I guess you know Sadie’s taste. You help me choose.”
And we chose﻿—lord! What did we not choose? There was her ring, a diamond half-hoop. It cost £95, and there was no attempt to get it for £90. Then there was a diamond necklet﻿—two hundred guineas, but pounds accepted. That was to be the gift of the bridegroom. The wedding was evidently imminent. It behooved me to play a brotherly part. I therefore rose to the occasion; calculated she would like a diamond star (£116), but reckoned it was more than I could afford; and sustained a vicious kick under the table for either verb. I was afraid to open my mouth on finally obtaining the star for the round hundred. And then the fat fell in the fire; for pay we could not; though a remittance (said Raffles) was “overdo from Noo York.”

===Chinese===
“就这么办，”拉夫尔斯正说着。“我们替她挑，她不中意的，你们随时来换。是这么个章程吧？”

“那正是我的建议，先生。”

“那就来吧，埃兹拉。萨迪（Sadie）的口味你总知道。你帮我挑。”

于是我们挑了起来——天哪！还有什么没挑？有她的戒指，一枚半环钻戒。价码 £95，而且谁也没打算磨到 £90。还有一条钻石项链——开价两百几尼，收两百镑也行。那是新郎官的聘礼。婚期显然已迫在眉睫。我理当尽一份做兄长的本分，于是当仁不让：算准了她必定中意一枚钻石星芒（£116），又估摸着这超出了我的财力；就为这两个动词里的随便哪一个，我在桌子底下挨了狠狠一脚。末了那枚星芒以一百镑整敲定，我连嘴都不敢张。跟着事情便砸了锅：钱，我们付不出；虽然（据拉夫尔斯说）一笔汇款正“从纽约拖期未至”。

===Original===
“But I don’t know you, gentlemen,” the jeweller exclaimed. “I haven’t even the name of your hotel!”
“I told you we was stoppin’ with friends,” said Raffles, who was not angry, though thwarted and crushed. “But that’s right, sir! Oh, that’s dead right, and I’m the last man to ask you to take quixotic risks. I’m tryin’ to figure a way out. Yes, *sir*, that’s what I’m tryin’ to do.”
“I wish you could, sir,” the jeweller said, with feeling. “It isn’t as if we hadn’t seen the color of your money. But certain rules I’m sworn to observe; it isn’t as if I was in business for myself; and﻿—you say you start for Paris in the morning!”
“On the 9 a.m. train,” mused Raffles; “and I’ve heard no-end yarns about the joolers’ stores in Parrus. But that ain’t fair; don’t you take no notice o’ that. I’m tryin’ to figure a way out. Yes, *sir*!”

===Chinese===
“可我不认识二位，先生们，”珠宝商叫起来。“我连二位下榻的旅馆都不得而知！”

“我跟你说过，我们借住在朋友家，”拉夫尔斯说；他虽受挫，垂头丧气，却并不动怒。“不过你说得对，先生！哦，对得很，而要你去冒堂吉诃德的险，我是天下最后一个肯的人。我正想法子找出路呢。是，先生，我正想法子。”

“但愿您想得出来，先生，”珠宝商动了真情。“又不是没见过二位钱的成色。可有些规矩，我立过誓要遵守；我这不是给自己做的买卖；何况——您说明早就要动身去巴黎！”

“九点的车，”拉夫尔斯沉吟道；“帕里斯的珠宝铺子，我听过的故事海了去了。可这么说人不地道；你别放在心上。我正想法子找出路呢。是，先生！”

===Original===
He was smoking cigarettes out of a twenty-five box; the tradesman and I had cigars. Raffles sat frowning with a pregnant eye, and it was only too clear to me that his plans had miscarried. I could not help thinking, however, that they deserved to do so, if he had counted upon buying credit for all but £400 by a single payment of some ten percent. That again seemed unworthy of Raffles, and I, for my part, still sat prepared to spring any moment at our visitor’s throat.
“We could mail you the money from Parrus,” drawled Raffles at length. “But how should we know you’d hold up your end of the string, and mail us the same articles we’ve selected tonight?”
The visitor stiffened in his chair. The name of his firm should be sufficient guarantee for that.
“I guess I’m no better acquainted with their name than they are with mine,” remarked Raffles, laughing. “See here, though! I got a scheme. You pack ’em in this!”

===Chinese===
他抽着一听二十五支装里的香烟；珠宝商与我叼着雪茄。拉夫尔斯皱眉坐镇，一眼睛的主意；而他的计划已然失手，这层我再清楚不过。可我仍不免想：他若是指望单付一成上下的头款，便赊下 £400 之外的全数货款，那这计划也活该失手。这又不似拉夫尔斯的手笔；而我这一边，仍旧端坐不动，随时预备朝来客的咽喉扑去。

“钱可以从帕里斯寄给你，”拉夫尔斯终于慢吞吞地说。“可我们怎么知道，你抓得住绳子的另一头，把我们今晚选中的原物寄还我们？”

来客在椅子里挺直了腰。他商号的名号，理应是足够的担保。

“估摸着我跟那名号不熟，正如它跟我不熟，”拉夫尔斯说着，笑了笑。“不过听着！我有个主意。你把它们装进这个！”

===Original===
He turned the cigarettes out of the tin box, while the jeweller and I joined wondering eyes.
“Pack ’em in this,” repeated Raffles, “the three things we want, and never mind the boxes; you can pack ’em in cotton-wool. Then we’ll ring for string and sealing wax, seal up the lot right here, and you can take ’em away in your grip. Within three days we’ll have our remittance, and mail you the money, and you’ll mail us this darned box with my seal unbroken! It’s no use you lookin’ so sick, Mr. Jooler; you won’t trust us any, and yet we’re goin’ to trust you some. Ring the bell, Ezra, and we’ll see if they’ve gotten any sealing-wax and string.”

===Chinese===
他把马口铁盒里的香烟倒了出来，珠宝商和我交换着惊奇的眼光。

“装进这个，”拉夫尔斯重复道，“咱们要的那三样，原盒就免了；你拿棉绒裹上便是。然后咱们摇铃要绳子和火漆，就在此地当场封缄，你把它们揣进提包带走。不出三天，咱们的汇款便到：钱寄你，你再把这个要命的匣子寄还我们，我的封漆完好无损！你哭丧着脸没有用，珠宝商先生；你是一点也不肯信我们，我们倒要信你几分。按铃，埃兹拉，瞧瞧他们有没有火漆和绳子。”

===Original===
They had; and the thing was done. The tradesman did not like it; the precaution was absolutely unnecessary; but since he was taking all his goods away with him, the sold with the unsold, his sentimental objections soon fell to the ground. He packed necklet, ring, and star, with his own hands, in cotton-wool; and the cigarette-box held them so easily that at the last moment, when the box was closed, and the string ready, Raffles very nearly added a diamond bee-brooch at £51 10s. This temptation, however, he ultimately overcame, to the other’s chagrin. The cigarette-box was tied up, and the string sealed, oddly enough, with the diamond of the ring that had been bought and paid for.
“I’ll chance you having another ring in the store the dead spit of mine,” laughed Raffles, as he relinquished the box, and it disappeared into the tradesman’s bag. “And now, Mr. Robinson, I hope you’ll appreciate my true hospitality in not offering you anything to drink while business was in progress. That’s Château Margaux, sir, and I should judge it’s what you’d call an eighteen-carat article.”

===Chinese===
有；事情便这样办成了。店老板不情愿；这道防备纯属多余；可既然他要携走的是全部货物，卖出的与未售的同行，他那点情感上的异议，很快便偃旗息鼓。他亲手以棉绒裹了项链、戒指与星芒；香烟盒装下它们绰绰有余，以至于临到末尾，盒子已阖、绳子已备，拉夫尔斯险些又添上一枚 £51 10s. 的钻石蜜蜂胸针。这诱惑终归叫他克住了，直教对方懊丧。香烟盒捆好，绳上打了火漆，说来也妙，封缄用的，恰是那枚早已银货两讫的戒指上的钻石。

“我就赌你们铺子里另有一枚，跟我这枚一个模子，”拉夫尔斯笑着交出盒子，看它没入店家的提包。“那么，鲁滨逊先生，生意谈笑之间，我滴酒不奉，这一番真正的地主之谊，还望阁下心领。那是玛歌，先生；依我判断，正是您行里所谓十八开足金的好货。”

===Original===
In the cab which we took to the vicinity of the flat, I was instantly snubbed for asking questions which the driver might easily overhear, and took the repulse just a little to heart. I could make neither head nor tail of Raffles’s dealings with the man from Regent Street, and was naturally inquisitive as to the meaning of it all. But I held my tongue until we had regained the flat in the cautious manner of our exit, and even there until Raffles rallied me with a hand on either shoulder and an old smile upon his face.
“You rabbit!” said he. “Why couldn’t you wait till we got home?”
“Why couldn’t you tell me what you were going to do?” I retorted as of yore.
“Because your dear old phiz is still worth its weight in innocence, and because you never could act for nuts! You looked as puzzled as the other poor devil; but you wouldn’t if you had known what my game really was.”

===Chinese===
我们雇车回到公寓左近，我刚一开口便挨了抢白——车夫说不定句句入耳——这记抢白，叫我心里微觉怏怏。拉夫尔斯同摄政街来人的这番周旋，我全然摸不着首尾，自然急欲知晓其中就里。可我一直缄口，直到我们照出去时那样步步为营地回到公寓；到了那里我仍不出声，直到拉夫尔斯双手各搭我一肩、脸上挂着旧日的笑，把我逗引开来。

“你这只兔子！”他说。“怎么就熬不到进家门？”

“怎么就不能先告诉我，你要干什么？”我照老样子回敬。

“因为你那张亲爱的老脸，至今还值它自身重量的清白；也因为演戏这门手艺，你从来一窍不通！你瞧上去同另外那个可怜虫一样满头雾水；可你若知道我的把戏究竟是什么，就决不会那副模样了。”

===Original===
“And pray what was it?”
“That,” said Raffles, and he smacked the cigarette-box down upon the mantelpiece. It was not tied. It was not sealed. It flew open from the force of the impact. And the diamond ring that cost £95, the necklet for £200, and my flaming star at another £100, all three lay safe and snug in the jeweller’s own cotton-wool!
“Duplicate boxes!” I cried.
“Duplicate boxes, my brainy Bunny. One was already packed and weighted, and in my pocket. I don’t know whether you noticed me weighing the three things together in my hand? I know that neither of you saw me change the boxes, for I did it when I was nearest buying the bee-brooch at the end, and you were too puzzled, and the other Johnny too keen. It was the cheapest shot in the game; the dear ones were sending old Theobald to Southampton on a fool’s errand yesterday afternoon, and showing one’s own nose down Regent Street in broad daylight while he was gone; but some things are worth paying for, and certain risks one must always take. Nice boxes, aren’t they? I only wished they contained a better cigarette; but a notorious brand was essential; a box of Sullivans would have brought me to life tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“那么敢问，究竟是什么把戏？”

“就是这个，”拉夫尔斯说罢，把香烟盒啪地掼在壁炉架上。它没有捆。它没有封。掼的力道叫它应声而开。那枚 £95 的钻戒、那条 £200 的项链，连同我那枚又添 £100 的火焰星芒——三样都在珠宝商自家的棉绒里，安安稳稳，妥妥帖帖！

“调包盒！”我喊道。

“调包盒，我足智多谋的邦尼。另一只早已装好配重，揣在我口袋里。我把三件东西合在手里掂过分量，你留意到没有，我不知道；我只知道你们俩谁也没瞧见我换盒子，因为那是我临了最想买蜜蜂胸针的时候动的手，那时你太纳闷，那位老兄又太来劲。这是全场最便宜的一枪；贵的那几枪是：昨天下午打发老西奥博尔德上南安普敦（Southampton）跑一趟瞎腿，趁他去后，光天化日之下，把自己的鼻子亮到摄政街上走一遭；可有些东西值得付账，有些风险也永远不得不冒。盒子不错吧？我只盼里头装的烟更好些；可非用赫赫有名的牌子不可：一盒萨利文，明天就能教我还阳。”

===Original===
“But they oughtn’t to open it tomorrow.”
“Nor will they, as a matter of fact. Meanwhile, Bunny, I may call upon you to dispose of the boodle.”
“I’m on for any mortal thing!”
My voice rang true, I swear, but it was the way of Raffles to take the evidence of as many senses as possible. I felt the cold steel of his eyes through mine and through my brain. But what he saw seemed to satisfy him no less than what he heard, for his hand found my hand, and pressed it with a fervor foreign to the man.

===Chinese===
“可他们明天总不至于开匣吧。”

“事实上也不开。这期间，邦尼，这批货怎么出手，说不定要借重于你。”

“天大的事我也干！”

我敢说，我的嗓音字字掷地有声；可拉夫尔斯的做派，向来是能请多少感官作证，便请多少。我感到他眼中那冷冷的钢铁，透过我的眼睛，直贯我的头脑。而他眼见的，似乎与耳闻的一般叫他满意，因为他的手找到了我的手，紧握了一下，那份热忱，本不是他这人的性情里所有的。

===Original===
“I know you are, and I knew you would be. Only remember, Bunny, it’s my turn next to pay the shot!”
You shall hear how he paid it when the time came.

===Chinese===
“我知道你干，也知道你必干。只是记住，邦尼：下一回，轮到我来付账！”

到得付账之日他如何付法，诸位自会听闻。

## A Jubilee Present / 禧年贺礼

===Original===
The Room of Gold, in the British Museum, is probably well enough known to the inquiring alien and the travelled American. A true Londoner, however, I myself had never heard of it until Raffles casually proposed a raid.
“The older I grow, Bunny, the less I think of your so-called precious stones. When did they ever bring in half their market value in £ s. d.? There was the first little crib we ever cracked together﻿—you with your innocent eyes shut. A thousand pounds that stuff was worth; but how many hundreds did it actually fetch. The Ardagh emeralds weren’t much better; old Lady Melrose’s necklace was far worse; but that little lot the other night has about finished me. A cool hundred for goods priced well over four; and £35 to come off for bait, since we only got a tenner for the ring I bought and paid for like an ass. I’ll be shot if I ever touch a diamond again! Not if it was the Koh-i-noor; those few whacking stones are too well known, and to cut them up is to decrease their value by arithmetical retrogression. Besides, that brings you up against the Fence once more, and I’m done with the beggars for good and all. You talk about your editors and publishers, you literary swine. Barabbas was neither a robber nor a publisher, but a six-barred, barbed-wired, spike-topped Fence. What we really want is an Incorporated Society of Thieves, with some public-spirited old forger to run it for us on business lines.”

===Chinese===
大英博物馆（British Museum）中的黄金陈列室（Room of Gold），在好寻根究底的外国访客和游历甚广的美国人那里，想必早已声名在外。然而我，一个地道的伦敦人，此前竟从未听说过它——直到拉夫尔斯（Raffles）随口提议，去劫它一票。

“邦尼（Bunny），我年岁越大，就越瞧不上你们那些所谓的宝石。它们几时按 £ s. d. 实兑现钱，抵得上过市价的一半？咱们头一回合伙撬的那间小破屋——你还睁着一双清白眼睛装睡来着——那批货值一千镑，实际到手不过几百镑。阿达（Ardagh）家的绿宝石好不了多少；梅尔罗斯老夫人（old Lady Melrose）的项链更是等而下之；至于前几晚那一小批货，简直叫我从此断了念头。定价远超四百的货色，净得一个干干净净的一百镑；还得刨掉 £35 的诱饵本钱——因为我像头蠢驴似的自己掏钱买下的那枚戒指，转手只当了十镑。再叫我碰一颗钻石，我就不得好死！它是光山（Koh-i-noor）也不碰；那么几块唬人的大石头名气太响，把它们切开，身价便按算术级数一路倒退。再说，那又得去同窝主（Fence）打交道，这路叫花子我这辈子算是敬谢不敏。你们这帮文坛猪猡，还成天念叨你们的编辑和出版商。巴拉巴（Barabbas）既非强盗，亦非出版商，而是一个上着六道栅栏、挂满铁丝蒺藜、顶着尖刺的窝主。咱们真正需要的，是一家注册立案的盗贼股份有限公司，由某位急公好义的老伪造家替咱们按商务章程经营。”

===Original===
Raffles uttered these blasphemies under his breath, not, I am afraid, out of any respect for my one redeeming profession, but because we were taking a midnight airing on the roof, after a whole day of June in the little flat below. The stars shone overhead, the lights of London underneath, and between the lips of Raffles a cigarette of the old and only brand. I had sent in secret for a box of the best; the boon had arrived that night; and the foregoing speech was the first result. I could afford to ignore the insolent asides, however, where the apparent contention was so manifestly unsound.
“And how are you going to get rid of your gold?” said I, pertinently.
“Nothing easier, my dear rabbit.”
“Is your Room of Gold a roomful of sovereigns?”

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯压着嗓子大发这套亵渎之论，恐怕并非出于对我那唯一见得人的职业的敬意，而是因为我们在楼下小公寓里闷了一整个六月天之后，正趁着午夜到屋顶上透气。头顶星斗满天，脚下伦敦灯火，拉夫尔斯唇间则叼着一支老牌子的、也是唯一正宗的香烟。我此前悄悄订了一盒上品；这份恩惠当夜刚刚寄到；上面这番宏论便是头一桩成果。不过，他那套主张既然如此明显站不住脚，那些无礼的夹枪带棒，我尽可置之不理。

“那么，你打算怎么弄走你的金子呢？”我这一问，倒还切题。

“再容易不过了，我亲爱的小兔子。”

“你那黄金陈列室，难道是满满一屋子金镑？”

===Original===
Raffles laughed softly at my scorn.
“No, Bunny, it’s principally in the shape of archaic ornaments, whose value, I admit, is largely extrinsic. But gold is gold, from Phoenicia to Klondike, and if we cleared the room we should eventually do very well.”
“How?”
“I should melt it down into a nugget, and bring it home from the U.S.A. tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯听我出语讥诮，轻轻笑了。

“不是，邦尼，那里头主要是些古时的首饰，我承认，其价值大半是附着在外的东西。可金子终归是金子，从腓尼基（Phoenicia）到克朗代克（Klondike）皆然；倘若能把那间屋子清个干净，我们到头来必定进账可观。”

“怎么个进账法？”

“我会把它熔成一大块天然金块，明天就当作从美国（U.S.A.）带回来的，运进家门。”

===Original===
“And then?”
“Make them pay up in hard cash across the counter of the Bank of England. And you *can* make them.”
That I knew, and so said nothing for a time, remaining a hostile though a silent critic, while we paced the cool black leads with our bare feet, softly as cats.

===Chinese===
“然后呢？”

“然后让英格兰银行（Bank of England）在柜台上一分不少地付我硬通货。这种事，你*就是*能让他们照办。”

这个我知道，于是有一阵子没作声，只当一个心怀不满却缄口无言的批评者；我们光着脚板，在凉丝丝的黑色铅皮屋顶上踱步，轻软得像两只猫。

===Original===
“And how do you propose to get enough away,” at length I asked, “to make it worth while?”
“Ah, there you have it,” said Raffles. “I only propose to reconnoitre the ground, to see what we can see. We might find some hiding-place for a night; that, I am afraid, would be our only chance.”
“Have you ever been there before?”
“Not since they got the one good, portable piece which I believe that they exhibit now. It’s a long time since I read of it﻿—I can’t remember where﻿—but I know they have got a gold cup of sorts worth several thousands. A number of the immorally rich clubbed together and presented it to the nation; and two of the richly immoral intend to snaffle it for themselves. At any rate we might go and have a look at it, Bunny, don’t you think?”

===Chinese===
“那你打算怎么弄走足够的分量，”我终于问道，“好教这趟买卖值得一做？”

“啊，问到点子上了，”拉夫尔斯说，“我眼下只提议去踩一遍盘子，看看能看出什么名堂。兴许能寻上个能藏一夜的处所；恐怕，那便是我们唯一的机会了。”

“你从前去过那儿吗？”

“自打他们收进那件唯一像样、又便于携带的藏品——我想如今就在展出——之后，就没再去过。我读到它的消息已是许久以前，在哪儿读的也记不起了，但我知道他们弄到了一只金杯，好歹也值几千镑。一群为富不仁者凑份子把它献给了国家；而两位不仁为富之徒，则打算把它私自昧下。无论如何，咱们总可以去开开眼界，邦尼，你说是不是？”

===Original===
Think! I seized his arm.
“When? When? When?” I asked, like a quick-firing gun.
“The sooner the better, while old Theobald’s away on his honeymoon.”

===Chinese===
还用想！我一把抓住他的胳膊。

“什么时候？什么时候？什么时候？”我连声追问，活像一挺速射炮。

“越快越好，趁老西奥博尔德（Theobald）还在外头度蜜月。”

===Original===
Our medico had married the week before, nor was any fellow-practitioner taking his work﻿—at least not that considerable branch of it which consisted of Raffles﻿—during his brief absence from town. There were reasons, delightfully obvious to us, why such a plan would have been highly unwise in Dr. Theobald. I, however, was sending him daily screeds, and both matutinal and nocturnal telegrams, the composition of which afforded Raffles not a little enjoyment.
“Well, then, when﻿—when?” I began to repeat.
“Tomorrow, if you like.”
“Only to look?”

===Chinese===
我们这位大夫一周前刚娶了亲；他离城小住的这些日子，也没有同行替他接管业务——至少没接管拉夫尔斯这一门可观的分支。为什么西奥博尔德医生（Dr. Theobald）做此安排会大大失策，个中缘由，我们俩心照不宣，乐不可支。而我呢，正每天给他寄去大函长信，外加晨昏各一的电报，起草这些文字给了拉夫尔斯莫大的乐趣。

“那么好，究竟什么时候——什么时候？”我又念叨起来。

“明天，只要你愿意。”

“只去看看？”

===Original===
The limitation was my one regret.
“We must do so, Bunny, before we leap.”
“Very well,” I sighed. “But tomorrow it is!”
And the morrow it really was.

===Chinese===
这一条限制，是我唯一的遗憾。

“邦尼，咱们总得先看清了，再往下跳。”

“很好，”我叹了口气，“可明天就是明天！”

而第二天，果然说到做到。

===Original===
I saw the porter that night, and, I still think, bought his absolute allegiance for the second coin of the realm. My story, however, invented by Raffles, was sufficiently specious in itself. That sick gentleman, Mr. Maturin (as I had to remember to call him), was really, or apparently, sickening for fresh air. Dr. Theobald would allow him none; he was pestering me for just one day in the country while the glorious weather lasted. I was myself convinced that no possible harm could come of the experiment. Would the porter help me in so innocent and meritorious an intrigue? The man hesitated. I produced my half-sovereign. The man was lost. And at half-past eight next morning﻿—before the heat of the day﻿—Raffles and I drove to Kew Gardens in a hired landau which was to call for us at midday and wait until we came. The porter had assisted me to carry my invalid downstairs, in a carrying-chair hired (like the landau) from Harrod’s Stores for the occasion.
It was little after nine when we crawled together into the gardens; by half-past my invalid had had enough, and out he tottered on my arm; a cab, a message to our coachman, a timely train to Baker Street, another cab, and we were at the British Museum﻿—brisk pedestrians now﻿—not very many minutes after the opening hour of 10 a.m.

===Chinese===
当晚我见了门房；至今我仍相信，我用王国币制里的第二大钱，买下了他绝对的忠心。不过，我那套由拉夫尔斯杜撰的说辞，本身也足够冠冕堂皇。那位病弱的绅士马图林先生（Mr. Maturin）——我得记住这么称呼他——真的，或者说看上去真的，害着一场思慕新鲜空气的病。西奥博尔德医生一律不准；趁着天气大好，他正缠磨我，只求去乡下待上一天。我自己也深信，这一尝试断无半点害处。门房可愿助我玉成这桩无害而有益的密谋？那人犹豫了。我亮出我的半沙弗林金币。那人沦陷了。于是次日早晨八点半——趁一天的暑气未盛——拉夫尔斯和我乘一辆雇来的四轮马车驶往丘园（Kew Gardens），马车约定正午来接，接到之前悉听尊便。门房还帮我把我那位病人抬下了楼，抬在一顶轿椅里——为这场合从哈罗德百货商店（Harrod's Stores）租来的，马车也是那儿雇的。

九点刚过，我们一同蹭进园子；到九点半，我的病人已经受用够了，扶着我的胳膊摇摇晃晃走了出来；一辆马车，一句捎给我们车夫的话，一班赶得正巧、开往贝克街（Baker Street）的火车，再换一辆马车，我们便到了大英博物馆——此刻已是脚步轻快的行人——距离 10 a.m. 的开馆时刻，没过去几分钟。

===Original===
It was one of those glowing days which will not be forgotten by many who were in town at the time. The Diamond Jubilee was upon us, and Queen’s weather had already set in. Raffles, indeed, declared it was as hot as Italy and Australia put together; and certainly the short summer nights gave the channels of wood and asphalt and the continents of brick and mortar but little time to cool. At the British Museum the pigeons were crooning among the shadows of the grimy colonnade, and the stalwart janitors looked less stalwart than usual, as though their medals were too heavy for them. I recognized some habitual Readers going to their labor underneath the dome; of mere visitors we seemed among the first.
“That’s the room,” said Raffles, who had bought the twopenny guide, as we studied it openly on the nearest bench; “number 43, upstairs and sharp round to the right. Come on, Bunny!”
And he led the way in silence, but with a long methodical stride which I could not understand until we came to the corridor leading to the Room of Gold, when he turned to me for a moment.

===Chinese===
那是那种容光焕发、许多当时身在城中者终生难忘的日子。钻禧庆典（Diamond Jubilee）已迫在眼前，“女王的好天气”也早已登场。拉夫尔斯甚至宣称，这天热得赛过意大利（Italy）加澳大利亚（Australia）之和；而短短的夏夜，的确也没给那些木头与沥青的沟渠、砖石灰浆的大陆，留下多少冷却的工夫。在大英博物馆，鸽子在熏黑廊柱的暗影里咕咕呢喃，那些魁梧的门卫瞧上去不及往日魁梧，仿佛勋章重得他们扛不动了。我认出几位惯常的读者（Readers），正要去大圆顶底下各司其职；论纯粹的游客，我们似乎名列头班。

“就是那间屋子，”拉夫尔斯说；他买下了那本两便士的参观指南，我们正大大方方坐在最近的长凳上研读。“43 号，上楼，再一个急转弯到右手。走吧，邦尼！”

他一言不发地在头里带路，步子却迈得悠长而有章法，我一时不明就里，直到我们走上通往黄金陈列室的走廊，他才回头看了我片刻。

===Original===
“A hundred and thirty-nine yards from this to the open street,” said Raffles, “not counting the stairs. I suppose we *could* do it in twenty seconds, but if we did we should have to jump the gates. No, you must remember to loaf out at slow march, Bunny, whether you like it or not.”
“But you talked about a hiding-place for a night?”
“Quite so﻿—for all night. We should have to get back, go on lying low, and saunter out with the crowd next day﻿—after doing the whole show thoroughly.”
“What! With gold in our pockets﻿—”

===Chinese===
“从这儿到敞亮的街上，一共一百三十九码，”拉夫尔斯说，“楼梯还不算在内。我寻思咱们*倒*能在二十秒里冲完，可真要那么干，就得连大门一起翻过去了。不行，你得记住，出门时要慢条斯理、迈着正步溜达出去，邦尼，随你乐不乐意。”

“可你先前说的，不是找地方藏一夜吗？”

“正是——藏整整一夜。到时候咱们还得摸回来，接着蛰伏，第二天再随着人群闲逛而出——前提是把整个展览从头到尾看个透。”

“什么！兜里揣着金子——”

===Original===
“And gold in our boots, and gold up the sleeves and legs of our suits! You leave that to me, Bunny, and wait till you’ve tried two pairs of trousers sewn together at the foot! This is only a preliminary reconnoitre. And here we are.”
It is none of my business to describe the so-called Room of Gold, with which I, for one, was not a little disappointed. The glass cases, which both fill and line it, may contain unique examples of the goldsmith’s art in times and places of which one heard quite enough in the course of one’s classical education; but, from a professional point of view, I would as lief have the ransacking of a single window in the West End as the pick of all those spoils of Etruria and of ancient Greece. The gold may not be so soft as it appears, but it certainly looks as though you could bite off the business ends of the spoons, and stop your own teeth in doing so. Nor should I care to be seen wearing one of the rings; but the greatest fraud of all (from the aforesaid standpoint) is assuredly that very cup of which Raffles had spoken. Moreover, he felt this himself.

===Chinese===
“靴筒里也塞金子，衣服的袖筒裤筒里全塞金子！这个你交给我，邦尼，等你试过裤脚缝死、两条裤子连成一气的滋味，再来发表高见！眼下不过是初步踩盘。喏，到了。”

描绘这间所谓黄金陈列室，并非我的分内之事；单说我本人，就对它不无失望。那些既摆满中央又镶满四壁的玻璃柜里，或许盛着金匠工艺独一无二的典范，其时代与产地，一个人在古典教育的过程中早已听得够多；但以行家的眼光看，与其任我在伊特鲁里亚（Etruria）和古希腊的全部战利品里挑三拣四，我宁可得西区（West End）一扇橱窗的洗劫权。那金子未必如看上去那般软，可瞧那样子，你确实像能一口咬下汤匙使事的那一头，顺带把自个儿的牙也硌坏在里头。那些戒指，我也无意让人瞧见自己戴上一枚；而最大的一件挂羊头卖狗肉的东西（循上述行家立场），千真万确正是拉夫尔斯说过的那只杯子。何况，他自己也有同感。

===Original===
“Why, it’s as thin as paper,” said he, “and enamelled like a middle-aged lady of quality! But, by Jove, it’s one of the most beautiful things I ever saw in my life, Bunny. I should like to have it for its own sake, by all my gods!”
The thing had a little square case of plate-glass all to itself at one end of the room. It may have been the thing of beauty that Raffles affected to consider it, but I for my part was in no mood to look at it in that light. Underneath were the names of the plutocrats who had subscribed for this national gewgaw, and I fell to wondering where their £8,000 came in, while Raffles devoured his twopenny guidebook as greedily as a schoolgirl with a zeal for culture.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，薄得跟纸似的，”他说，“珐琅彩倒像一位中年贵妇！可是天哪，邦尼，它是我平生所见最美的东西之一。单冲它本身，我就想要它——凭我信奉的诸神起誓！”

那件东西独占着一具小小的方形平板玻璃柜，摆在展厅一端。它或许真是拉夫尔斯假意推崇的那种“美的事物”，至于我，可没心思从那个角度看它。柜子底下标着一批凑钱购置这件国家小玩意的财阀的大名，我正自纳闷，他们那 £8,000 究竟花在了什么地方，拉夫尔斯却已捧起那本两便士的指南狼吞虎咽，贪馋得像个醉心文化的女学生。

===Original===
“Those are scenes from the martyrdom of St. Agnes,” said he﻿ ﻿… “ ‘translucent on relief﻿ ﻿… one of the finest specimens of its kind.’ I should think it was! Bunny, you Philistine, why can’t you admire the thing for its own sake? It would be worth having only to live up to! There never was such rich enamelling on such thin gold; and what a good scheme to hang the lid up over it, so that you can see how thin it is. I wonder if we could lift it, Bunny, by hook or crook?”
“You’d better try, sir,” said a dry voice at his elbow.

===Chinese===
“那些是圣艾格尼丝（St. Agnes）殉难图景，”他说……“‘浮雕之上，晶莹剔透……同类器物中的登峰造极之作。’可不是嘛！邦尼，你这俗物，你怎么就不能单单为它本身而欣赏它？光是配得上它，就值得把它弄到手！这么薄的金子上，从不曾有过如此浓丽的珐琅；把杯盖高高悬吊在它上方，好教人看清它有多薄，又是多妙的主意。我在想啊，咱们能不能想个法子，把它请出去，邦尼？”

“先生，您最好动手试试。”他肘边响起一个干巴巴的声音。

===Original===
The madman seemed to think we had the room to ourselves. I knew better, but, like another madman, had let him ramble on unchecked. And here was a stolid constable confronting us, in the short tunic that they wear in summer, his whistle on its chain, but no truncheon at his side. Heavens! how I see him now: a man of medium size, with a broad, good-humored, perspiring face, and a limp moustache. He looked sternly at Raffles, and Raffles looked merrily at him.
“Going to run me in, officer?” said he. “That *would* be a joke﻿—my hat!”

===Chinese===
这疯子满以为这间屋子归我们两个独享。我心里有数，却也像另一个疯子似的，任由他信口开河，不加拦阻。这不，一名木呆呆的警察正堵在我们跟前，穿着他们夏季的短制服，哨子连着链子挂在胸前，腰边却不见警棍。天哪！至今我一闭眼还能看见他：中等身材，一张宽厚、和气、汗涔涔的脸，一撇疲沓的小胡子。他板着脸瞪拉夫尔斯，拉夫尔斯却笑吟吟地望他。

“要把我抓走吗，警官？”他说，“那可*就*成笑话了——我的老天！”

===Original===
“I didn’t say as I was, sir,” replied the policeman. “But that’s queer talk for a gentleman like you, sir, in the British Museum!” And he wagged his helmet at my invalid, who had taken his airing in frock-coat and top-hat, the more readily to assume his present part.
“What!” cried Raffles, “simply saying to my friend that I’d like to lift the gold cup? Why, so I should, officer, so I should! I don’t mind who hears me say so. It’s one of the most beautiful things I ever saw in all my life.”
The constable’s face had already relaxed, and now a grin peeped under the limp moustache. “I daresay there’s many as feels like that, sir,” said he.
“Exactly; and I say what I feel, that’s all,” said Raffles airily. “But seriously, officer, is a valuable thing like this quite safe in a case like that?”

===Chinese===
“俺没说要抓您，先生，”警察答道，“可像您这样一位先生，在大英博物馆里说这种话，也未免太离奇了！”说着，他朝我的病人摆了摆头盔。病人此番出游，穿长礼服，戴大礼帽，正是为了眼下的角色装扮起来更顺当。

“什么！”拉夫尔斯嚷道，“我不过是对我的朋友说，我想把那只金杯‘顺’走？咳，我是真想啊，警官，真想！这话谁听见去，我都无所谓。它是我这辈子见过的最美的物件之一。”

警察的脸色早已松了下来，这时一抹笑意又从那撇疲沓的胡子底下探出头来。“俺敢说，跟您一个心思的人多着呢，先生。”他说。

“正是；我心里怎么想，嘴上就怎么说，仅此而已，”拉夫尔斯轻飘飘地说，“不过，说正经的，警官，这么贵重的东西，搁在这么个柜子里，当真万无一失吗？”

===Original===
“Safe enough as long as I’m here,” replied the other, between grim jest and stout earnest. Raffles studied his face; he was still watching Raffles; and I kept an eye on them both without putting in my word.
“You appear to be single-handed,” observed Raffles. “Is that wise?”
The note of anxiety was capitally caught; it was at once personal and public-spirited, that of the enthusiastic savant, afraid for a national treasure which few appreciated as he did himself. And, to be sure, the three of us now had this treasury to ourselves; one or two others had been there when we entered; but now they were gone.

===Chinese===
“只要俺在这儿，就够安全的，”对方答道，半是冷面的打趣，半是结结实实的认真。拉夫尔斯端详他的脸；他仍旧盯着拉夫尔斯；我把他们两个一并照看在眼里，一言不发。

“您看来是孤身一人哪，”拉夫尔斯品评道，“这么做，明智吗？”

这份忧心忡忡的调子拿捏得妙到毫巅：既关乎私人安危，又忧国忧民，活脱脱一位热忱的饱学之士，为一件国宝提心吊胆——而能像他这样赏识此宝的人，本也没有几个。的确，此刻这间宝库已归我们三个独享；我们进来时另有一两位游客，如今也都散尽了。

===Original===
“I’m not single-handed,” said the officer, comfortably. “See that seat by the door? One of the attendants sits there all day long.”
“Then where is he now?”
“Talking to another attendant just outside. If you listen you’ll hear them for yourself.”
We listened, and we did hear them, but not just outside. In my own mind I even questioned whether they were in the corridor through which we had come; to me it sounded as though they were just outside the corridor.

===Chinese===
“俺不是孤身一人，”警官答得从容，“瞧见门边那把座椅没有？有位管理员成天坐在那儿。”

“那他这会儿人在哪儿？”

“就在外头跟另一位管理员说话呢。您竖起耳朵，自己就听得见。”

我们侧耳去听，也确乎听见了，只是并非近在门外。我心里甚至犯嘀咕，他们是否还在我们穿过来的那条走廊里；依我听来，他们分明在走廊那一头的外面。

===Original===
“You mean the fellow with the billiard-cue who was here when we came in?” pursued Raffles.
“That wasn’t a billiard-cue! It was a pointer,” the intelligent officer explained.
“It ought to be a javelin,” said Raffles, nervously. “It ought to be a poleaxe! The public treasure ought to be better guarded than this. I shall write to the *Times* about it﻿—you see if I don’t!”
All at once, yet somehow not so suddenly as to excite suspicion, Raffles had become the elderly busybody with nerves; why, I could not for the life of me imagine; and the policeman seemed equally at sea.

===Chinese===
“您是说那个拿台球杆的家伙？我们进来时他还在里头来着？”拉夫尔斯追问。

“那可不是台球杆！那是根指示棒，”这位头脑灵光的警官解释道。

“那玩意儿就该换成长矛，”拉夫尔斯焦躁地说，“就该换成战斧！公共财宝的守卫，断不该如此稀松。我要写信给《泰晤士报》（Times）——你瞧我写不写！”

说变就变，却又变不至于惹人起疑，拉夫尔斯一转眼成了个神经质的上了年纪的好事之徒；缘何如此，我绞尽脑汁也想不明白；那位警察看上去也同样一头雾水。

===Original===
“Lor’ bless you, sir,” said he, “I’m all right; don’t you bother your head about *me*.”
“But you haven’t even got a truncheon!”
“Not likely to want one either. You see, sir, it’s early as yet; in a few minutes these here rooms will fill up; and there’s safety in numbers, as they say.”
“Oh, it will fill up soon, will it?”

===Chinese===
“老天保佑您，先生，”他说，“俺没事；您就别为*俺*操那份心啦。”

“可您连根警棍都没带！”

“带了也多半用不着。您瞧，先生，眼下还早；过不了几分钟，这几间屋子就该上人了；俗话说得好，人多保险嘛。”

“哦，快上人了，是吗？”

===Original===
“Any minute now, sir.”
“Ah!”
“It isn’t often empty as long as this, sir. It’s the Jubilee, I suppose.”
“Meanwhile, what if my friend and I had been professional thieves? Why, we could have overpowered you in an instant, my good fellow!”

===Chinese===
“随时的事，先生。”

“啊！”

“这屋子难得空这么久，先生。俺寻思，是赶上了钻禧庆典。”

“可要是趁这工夫，我和我的朋友偏是惯偷呢？嗐，我们转眼就能把你制住，我的好朋友！”

===Original===
“That you couldn’t; leastways, not without bringing the whole place about your ears.”
“Well, I shall write to the *Times*, all the same. I’m a connoisseur in all this sort of thing, and I won’t have unnecessary risks run with the nation’s property. You said there was an attendant just outside, but he sounds to me as though he were at the other end of the corridor. I shall write today!”
For an instant we all three listened; and Raffles was right. Then I saw two things in one glance. Raffles had stepped a few inches backward, and stood poised upon the ball of each foot, his arms half raised, a light in his eyes. And another kind of light was breaking over the crass features of our friend the constable.

===Chinese===
“那你们办不到；至少，不闹得满馆的人都围上来，办不到。”

“哼，无论如何我都要写信给《泰晤士报》。这类东西我是行家，国家的财产蒙受不必要的风险，我绝不容许。你方才说，有位管理员就在门外；可依我听来，他简直在走廊的另一头。我今天就写！”

有那么一瞬，我们三个都侧耳倾听；拉夫尔斯说得没错。紧接着，我一眼望见两个景象。拉夫尔斯已向后挪了几英寸，踮在前脚掌上站定，双臂半抬，眼里燃着光。而另一种光，也正从我们的朋友、那位警察的憨直脸膛上渐渐亮起。

===Original===
“Then shall I tell you what *I’ll* do?” he cried, with a sudden clutch at the whistle-chain on his chest. The whistle flew out, but it never reached his lips. There were a couple of sharp smacks, like double barrels discharged all but simultaneously, and the man reeled against me so that I could not help catching him as he fell.
“Well done, Bunny! I’ve knocked him out﻿—I’ve knocked him out! Run you to the door and see if the attendants have heard anything, and take them on if they have.”
Mechanically I did as I was told. There was no time for thought, still less for remonstrance or reproach, though my surprise must have been even more complete than that of the constable before Raffles knocked the sense out of him. Even in my utter bewilderment, however, the instinctive caution of the real criminal did not desert me. I ran to the door, but I sauntered through it, to plant myself before a Pompeiian fresco in the corridor; and there were the two attendants still gossiping outside the further door; nor did they hear the dull crash which I heard even as I watched them out of the corner of each eye.

===Chinese===
“那俺告诉你们，*俺*要干什么？”他喝道，猛地一把抓向胸前的哨链。哨子弹了出来，却终究没能挨着他的嘴唇。两声又快又脆的脆响，活像双管猎枪几乎同时齐发；那人晃了两晃，直朝我倒来，我不由不伸手接住他下坠的身子。

“干得好，邦尼！我把他打晕了——我把他打晕了！你快到门口去，看看管理员们听见动静没有；要是听见了，就由你去缠住他们。”

我机械地照办了。既无暇思考，更无暇规劝或责难，尽管我的惊愕，只怕比那位警察挨拉夫尔斯那两下、被打掉知觉之前还要彻底。然而，即便在浑然错愕之中，真正罪犯那本能的谨慎也未曾弃我而去。我朝门口跑去，人却是溜溜达达踱过去的，随即在走廊里一幅庞贝（Pompeii）壁画前站定；那两位管理员仍在较远的一扇门外闲磕牙；那一声闷响，我用两只眼角瞟着他们的当儿都听见了，他们却充耳不闻。

===Original===
It was hot weather, as I have said, but the perspiration on my body seemed already to have turned into a skin of ice. Then I caught the faint reflection of my own face in the casing of the fresco, and it frightened me into some semblance of myself as Raffles joined me with his hands in his pockets. But my fear and indignation were redoubled at the sight of him, when a single glance convinced me that his pockets were as empty as his hands, and his mad outrage the most wanton and reckless of his whole career.
“Ah, very interesting, very interesting, but nothing to what they have in the museum at Naples or in Pompeii itself. You must go there some day, Bunny. I’ve a good mind to take you myself. Meanwhile﻿—slow march! The beggar hasn’t moved an eyelid. We may swing for him if you show indecent haste!”

===Chinese===
如前所述，那天天热，可我身上的汗，这会儿竟像结成了一层冰壳。随后，我从壁画玻璃罩上照见自己淡淡的面影，那副尊容吓得我多少找回几分本来面目；恰在此时，拉夫尔斯双手插兜，踱到我身边。可我一见他，恐惧和义愤反而倍增——只一眼我就认定：他的口袋和他两手一样空空如也；而这桩疯狂的暴行，是他整个作案生涯里最没来由、最不留退路的一桩。

“啊，很有意思，很有意思，可比那不勒斯（Naples）的博物馆、比庞贝城本地的藏品，可就小巫见大巫了。哪天你务必去一趟，邦尼。我真有心亲自带你去。眼下呢——慢步走！那家伙眼皮都没动一下。你要是赶路赶得失了体统，咱们俩没准得为他去荡秋千！”

===Original===
“We!” I whispered. “We!”
And my knees knocked together as we came up to the chatting attendants. But Raffles must needs interrupt them to ask the way to the Prehistoric Saloon.
“At the top of the stairs.”
“Thank you. Then we’ll work round that way to the Egyptian part.”

===Chinese===
“咱们！”我低声道。“咱们！”

走到那两位还在闲聊的管理员跟前时，我的两个膝盖直打碰。偏偏拉夫尔斯还要打断他们，开口打听史前展厅（Prehistoric Saloon）的路。

“楼梯顶上就是。”

“多谢。那我们就打那条道绕到埃及（Egyptian）那一部分去。”

===Original===
And we left them resuming their providential chat.
“I believe you’re mad,” I said bitterly as we went.
“I believe I was,” admitted Raffles; “but I’m not now, and I’ll see you through. A hundred and thirty-nine yards, wasn’t it? Then it can’t be more than a hundred and twenty now﻿—not as much. Steady, Bunny, for God’s sake. It’s *slow* march﻿—for our lives.”
There was this much management. The rest was our colossal luck. A hansom was being paid off at the foot of the steps outside, and in we jumped, Raffles shouting “Charing Cross!” for all Bloomsbury to hear.

===Chinese===
我们丢下他们，任他们继续那天意安排的闲谈。

“我看你是疯了，”我一边走，一边恨恨地说。

“我信我方才确是疯了，”拉夫尔斯承认道，“不过现在不疯了，我会保你全身而退。一共一百三十九码，对吧？那么眼下剩下的，顶多一百二十码——还不到呢。稳住，邦尼，千万看在上帝分上。是*慢*步走——这可性命攸关。”

谋算到此为止。其余的，全仗我们泼天的运气。门外台阶脚下恰有一辆双轮马车正在付账，我们跳将上去，拉夫尔斯高喊一声“查令十字（Charing Cross）！”，恨不能让整个布鲁姆斯伯里（Bloomsbury）都听见。

===Original===
We had turned into Bloomsbury Street without exchanging a syllable when he struck the trapdoor with his fist.
“Where the devil are you driving us?”
“Charing Cross, sir.”
“I said King’s Cross! Round you spin, and drive like blazes, or we miss our train! There’s one to York at 10:35,” added Raffles as the trapdoor slammed; “we’ll book there, Bunny, and then we’ll slope through the subway to the Metropolitan, and so to ground via Baker Street and Earl’s Court.”

===Chinese===
车拐进布鲁姆斯伯里街，我们彼此还不曾交换过一个音节，他已一拳砸响了车厢顶盖。

“你这是要把我们拉到哪儿去，见鬼？”

“查令十字，先生。”

“我说的是国王十字（King's Cross）！掉头，玩命地赶，不然就误了火车！10:35 有一班去约克（York）的车，”顶盖哐当合上时，拉夫尔斯又补了一句，“咱们就在那儿订票，邦尼，然后从地下通道溜之大吉，转上大都会线（Metropolitan），再取道贝克街和厄尔斯考特（Earl's Court），遁回洞去。”

===Original===
And actually in half an hour he was seated once more in the hired carrying chair, while the porter and I staggered upstairs with my decrepit charge, for whose shattered strength even one hour in Kew Gardens had proved too much! Then, and not until then, when we had got rid of the porter and were alone at last, did I tell Raffles, in the most nervous English at my command, frankly and exactly what I thought of him and of his latest deed. Once started, moreover, I spoke as I have seldom spoken to living man; and Raffles, of all men, stood my abuse without a murmur; or rather he sat it out, too astounded even to take off his hat, though I thought his eyebrows would have lifted it from his head.
“But it always was your infernal way,” I was savagely concluding. “You make one plan, and yet you tell me another﻿—”

===Chinese===
而区区半小时之后，他竟又坐回了那顶租来的轿椅，由我和门房抬着我那位衰朽的病人，一步一步往楼上挪——就凭他那副垮掉的身子骨，连丘园里的一个钟头都消受不起！直到那时，直到打发走门房、终于只剩我们两人，我才用我平生最紧张的英语，把我对他本人和他这桩最新杰作的想法，坦白而精确地一吐为快。而且话匣既开，我那通抢白，我对活人说的话里罕有其匹；而拉夫尔斯，在所有人里偏偏是他，竟一声不吭地受了；或者说，他坐着挨完了这一顿，惊得连帽子都忘了摘——虽然我看他那两条眉毛，险些把帽子从头顶上掀起来。

“可你这该死的做派从来如此，”我正狠狠地收尾，“你打的是一个主意，说给我听的却是另一个——”

===Original===
“Not today, Bunny, I swear!”
“You mean to tell me you really did start with the bare idea of finding a place to hide in for a night?”
“Of course I did.”
“It was to be the mere reconnoitre you pretended?”

===Chinese===
“今天可不是，邦尼，我发誓！”

“你是想告诉我，你出发时当真只揣着一个光秃秃的念头——寻个地方藏一夜？”

“当然是当真。”

“也当真只是你口口声声的踩盘子？”

===Original===
“There was no pretence about it, Bunny.”
“Then why on earth go and do what you did?”
