“The reason would be obvious to anyone but you,” said Raffles, still with no unkindly scorn. “It was the temptation of a minute﻿—the final impulse of the fraction of a second, when Roberto saw that I was tempted, and let me see that he saw it. It’s not a thing I care to do, and I shan’t be happy till the papers tell me the poor devil is alive. But a knockout shot was the only chance for us then.”
“Why? You don’t get run in for being tempted, nor yet for showing that you are!”

===Chinese===
“这里头没有半点虚言，邦尼。”

“那你究竟为什么要多此一举，跑去干那种事？”

“这缘由，换了谁都一目了然，独独除了你，”拉夫尔斯说，语带讥诮，却并无恶意。“那是片刻之间的诱惑——是几分之一秒里的最后一念：罗伯托（Roberto）看出我动了心，又故意让我看出他看出来了。这种事我本不愿干，报纸一日不告诉我那可怜虫还活着，我便一日不得安生。可在当时，一记打晕他的拳，是我们唯一的生机。”

“为什么？动了心不犯法，让人瞧出你动了心，也不犯法！”

===Original===
“But I should have deserved running in if I hadn’t yielded to such a temptation as that, Bunny. It was a chance in a hundred thousand! We might go there every day of our lives, and never again be the only outsiders in the room, with the billiard-marking Johnnie practically out of earshot at one and the same time. It was a gift from the gods; not to have taken it would have been flying in the face of Providence.”
“But you didn’t take it,” said I. “You went and left it behind.”
I wish I had had a Kodak for the little smile with which Raffles shook his head, for it was one that he kept for those great moments of which our vocation is not devoid. All this time he had been wearing his hat, tilted a little over eyebrows no longer raised. And now at last I knew where the gold cup was.

===Chinese===
“可面对那样一个诱惑还不伸手，那才真该被抓进去，邦尼。那是十万分之一的机会！咱们哪怕天天去，这辈子也休想再碰上：屋里只有咱们两个外人，而那个记分员似的小子偏偏又听不真切——两样凑在同一时刻。那是诸神所赐；不收下它，便是公然忤逆天意。”

“可你并没收下，”我说，“你倒好，转身把它撂在了原地。”

拉夫尔斯摇头时脸上那一丝浅笑，我真希望手边有架柯达（Kodak）把它拍下来——那样的笑，他是专为咱们这行并非全然无缘的伟大时刻留着的。这半天，他头上始终戴着帽子，微微压向那两条不再高挑的眉毛。直到这时，我才终于明白，金杯到底在哪儿了。

===Original===
It stood for days upon his chimneypiece, this costly trophy whose ancient history and final fate filled newspaper columns even in these days of Jubilee, and for which the flower of Scotland Yard was said to be seeking high and low. Our constable, we learnt, had been stunned only, and, from the moment that I brought him an evening paper with the news, Raffles’s spirits rose to a height inconsistent with his equable temperament, and as unusual in him as the sudden impulse upon which he had acted with such effect. The cup itself appealed to me no more than it had done before. Exquisite it might be, handsome it was, but so light in the hand that the mere gold of it would scarcely have poured three figures out of melting-pot. And what said Raffles but that he would never melt it at all!
“Taking it was an offence against the laws of the land, Bunny. That is nothing. But destroying it would be a crime against God and Art, and may I be spitted on the vane of St. Mary Abbot’s if I commit it!”

===Chinese===
此后好几天，它便立在他的壁炉台上。这件昂贵的战利品，其古老身世与最终下落，即便在这钻禧之年，也占满了报纸的专栏；据说苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的精英正为它上天入地地搜寻。我们后来得知，咱们那位警察不过是昏了过去；而自我把载着这条消息的晚报带回那一刻起，拉夫尔斯的兴致便涨到一个高度，高得与他素来平稳的性情全不相称——正如他心血来潮、一击奏效的那桩冲动一样反常。那只杯子本身，对我的吸引力仍不比先前多一分。精致或许精致，体面也真体面，可掂在手里轻飘飘的，光凭那点金子，从熔锅里倒出来，怕是连个三位数也倒不满。而拉夫尔斯说什么？他说，他压根儿永远不熔它！

“拿走它，干犯的是这个国家的法律，邦尼。那不算什么。可毁掉它，便是对上帝与艺术犯罪；我若胆敢犯下，就让我串在圣玛丽阿博茨教堂（St. Mary Abbot's）的风向鸡上，活活烤死！”

===Original===
Talk such as this was unanswerable; indeed, the whole affair had passed the pale of useful comment; and the one course left to a practical person was to shrug his shoulders and enjoy the joke. This was not a little enhanced by the newspaper reports, which described Raffles as a handsome youth, and his unwilling accomplice as an older man of blackguardly appearance and low type.
“Hits us both off rather neatly, Bunny,” said he. “But what none of them do justice to is my dear cup. Look at it; only look at it, man! Was ever anything so rich and yet so chaste? St. Agnes must have had a pretty bad time, but it would be almost worth it to go down to posterity in such enamel upon such gold. And then the history of the thing. Do you realize that it’s five hundred years old and has belonged to Henry the Eighth and to Elizabeth among others? Bunny, when you have me cremated, you can put my ashes in yonder cup, and lay us in the deep-delved earth together!”

===Chinese===
这等高论，无从驳起；说实在的，整个事件早越出了有益评议的界线；一个务实之人剩下的唯一出路，便是耸耸肩膀，把笑话当笑话享受。报纸的报道又给这笑话添色不少：它们把拉夫尔斯描绘成一个英俊青年，而把他那名不情愿的同伙，写成一位相貌流氓、气质卑下的年长男子。

“把咱们俩都勾勒得挺传神，邦尼，”他说，“可惜谁也没给我的宝贝杯子以公道。瞧瞧它；就看一眼，老兄！世上可曾有过这般浓丽而又这般素雅的东西？圣艾格尼丝当年必定受了大罪；可若能以这样的珐琅、这样的黄金流传后世，那罪几乎也算受得值。再说这东西的来历。你可意识到，它已经五百岁，先后归亨利八世（Henry the Eighth）和伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）等人所有？邦尼，等你把我火化了，就把我的骨灰装进那边那只杯子，把咱们俩一同葬入深掘的地土！”

===Original===
“And meanwhile?”
“It is the joy of my heart, the light of my life, the delight of mine eye.”
“And suppose other eyes catch sight of it?”
“They never must; they never shall.”

===Chinese===
“那么眼前呢？”

“它是我心中的欢愉，我生命的光华，我眼目的赏心乐事。”

“可万一叫别的眼睛瞧见了呢？”

“绝不能叫它们瞧见；也绝不会叫它们瞧见。”

===Original===
Raffles would have been too absurd had he not been thoroughly alive to his own absurdity; there was nevertheless an underlying sincerity in his appreciation of any and every form of beauty, which all his nonsense could not conceal. And his infatuation for the cup was, as he declared, a very pure passion, since the circumstances debarred him from the chief joy of the average collector, that of showing his treasure to his friends. At last, however, and at the height of his craze, Raffles and reason seemed to come together again as suddenly as they had parted company in the Room of Gold.
“Bunny,” he cried, flinging his newspaper across the room, “I’ve got an idea after your own heart. I know where I can place it after all!”

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯若非对自己的荒唐了然于胸，这副做派未免就太可笑了；然而，他对任何形态、一切形态之美的赏爱，底下自有一份真诚，任他怎样痴话连篇也遮掩不住。他对那只杯子的痴迷，正如他自己宣称的，是一段极纯粹的感情——因为处境剥夺了他身为普通收藏家的头号乐事：把珍藏拿给朋友观赏。然而，就在这痴劲登峰造极之际，拉夫尔斯和理性仿佛又骤然重归于好，恰如他们当初在黄金陈列室里突然分道扬镳。

“邦尼！”他嚷道，一把将报纸撂过房间，“我想到一个正合你心意的点子。我知道，到底能把它安置出去了！”

===Original===
“Do you mean the cup?”
“I do.”
“Then I congratulate you.”
“Thanks.”

===Chinese===
“你是说那只杯子？”

“正是。”

“那我恭喜你。”

“多谢。”

===Original===
“Upon the recovery of your senses.”
“Thanks galore. But you’ve been confoundedly unsympathetic about this thing, Bunny, and I don’t think I shall tell you my scheme till I’ve carried it out.”
“Quite time enough,” said I.
“It will mean your letting me loose for an hour or two under cloud of this very night. Tomorrow’s Sunday, the Jubilee’s on Tuesday, and old Theobald’s coming back for it.”

===Chinese===
“恭喜你神志清白如初。”

“多谢之至。不过这件事上，你冷血得可恨，邦尼；我想，在我动手之前，暂且不把我的计划告诉你。”

“等办完了再讲，正合适。”我说。

“这就得请你放我出去，趁今夜的夜色，一两个钟头。明天是礼拜天，礼拜二是钻禧庆典，老西奥博尔德也要赶回来过节。”

===Original===
“It doesn’t much matter whether he’s back or not if you go late enough.”
“I mustn’t be late. They don’t keep open. No, it’s no use your asking any questions. Go out and buy me a big box of Huntley & Palmer’s biscuits; any sort you like, only they must be theirs, and absolutely the biggest box they sell.”
“My dear man!”
“No questions, Bunny; you do your part and I’ll do mine.”

===Chinese===
“只要你出去得够晚，他回不回来，都无关紧要。”

“我可不能晚。人家夜里不开门。不行，你问也是白问。出去给我买一大盒亨特利-帕尔默（Huntley & Palmer）饼干；品种随你挑，只消认准他家的，而且务必是他们卖的最大的那一盒。”

“我的老兄！”

“别问，邦尼；你办你的差，我办我的。”

===Original===
Subtlety and success were in his face. It was enough for me, and I had done his extraordinary bidding within a quarter of an hour. In another minute Raffles had opened the box and tumbled all the biscuits into the nearest chair.
“Now newspapers!”
I fetched a pile. He bid the cup of gold a ridiculous farewell, wrapped it up in newspaper after newspaper, and finally packed it in the empty biscuit-box.
“Now some brown paper. I don’t want to be taken for the grocer’s young man.”

===Chinese===
他脸上写足了的，是诡巧，是胜算。有这个，对我也就够了；不出一刻钟，我已办妥他这道离奇的差事。再过一分钟，拉夫尔斯已拆开盒盖，把满盒饼干尽数倒进最近的一把椅子里。

“现在，拿报纸来！”

我抱来一摞。他对金杯道了一声荒唐的珍重，用报纸裹了一层又一层，末了把它装进那只空饼干盒。

“再来些牛皮纸。我可不想被人错当成杂货铺的小伙计。”

===Original===
A neat enough parcel it made, when the string had been tied and the ends cut close; what was more difficult was to wrap up Raffles himself in such a way that even the porter should not recognize him if they came face to face at the corner. And the sun was still up. But Raffles would go, and when he did I should not have known him myself.
He may have been an hour away. It was barely dusk when he returned, and my first question referred to our dangerous ally, the porter. Raffles had passed him unsuspected in going, but had managed to avoid him altogether on the return journey, which he had completed by way of the other entrance and the roof. I breathed again.

===Chinese===
绳子系紧、绳头剪齐之后，那也真算一包齐整的包裹了；更难的是把拉夫尔斯本人也“包裹”起来，包裹到哪怕与门房在街角撞个对脸，也认他不出。何况日头还悬在天上。可拉夫尔斯非去不可；而当他动身出门时，连我自己也险些认他不得。

他去了约莫一个钟头。回来时天刚擦黑，我的头一个问题，便直指我们那位危险的盟友——门房。拉夫尔斯去时打他眼皮底下安然而过，未曾露馅；回程则干脆设法整个避开了他，从另一处入口翻屋顶回了家。我这才重新喘匀了气。

===Original===
“And what have you done with the cup?”
“Placed it!”
“How much for? How much for?”
“Let me think. I had a couple of cabs, and the postage was a tanner, with another twopence for registration. Yes, it cost me exactly five-and-eight.”

===Chinese===
“那么杯子呢，你把它怎么处置了？”

“出手了！”

“卖了多少钱？多少钱？”

“让我算算。来回两趟马车，邮资一个六便士，挂号又添两便士。没错，统共花了我五先令八便士，分毫不差。”

===Original===
“*It* cost *you*! But what did you *get* for it, Raffles?”
“Nothing, my boy.”
“Nothing!”
“Not a crimson cent.”

===Chinese===
“*它*花的是*你的*钱！可你从它身上*捞*回了多少，拉夫尔斯？”

“分文没有，我的小伙。”

“分文没有！”

“一个大子儿也没有。”

===Original===
“I am not surprised. I never thought it had a market value. I told you so in the beginning,” I said, irritably. “But what on earth have you done with the thing?”
“Sent it to the Queen.”
“You haven’t!”
Rogue is a word with various meanings, and Raffles had been one sort of rogue ever since I had known him; but now, for once, he was the innocent variety, a great gray-haired child, running over with merriment and mischief.

===Chinese===
“我毫不奇怪。我从来就没指望它有什么行情。起初我就这么说过，”我恼火地说，“可你到底把那东西弄哪儿去了？”

“寄给女王了。”

“你不会吧！”

“无赖”这个词，义项繁多。自打我结识拉夫尔斯，他一直是其中的一种；可这一回，破天荒头一遭，他属于清白无辜的那一种——一个大个子的、头发花白的孩子，满心满腔，装不下的快活与顽皮。

===Original===
“Well, I’ve sent it to Sir Arthur Bigge, to present to her Majesty, with the loyal respects of the thief, if that will do for you,” said Raffles. “I thought they might take too much stock of me at the G.P.O. if I addressed it to the Sovereign herself. Yes, I drove over to St. Martin’s-le-Grand with it, and I registered the box into the bargain. Do a thing properly if you do it at all.”
“But why on earth,” I groaned, “do such a thing at all?”

===Chinese===
“是这样：我把它寄给了阿瑟·比奇爵士（Sir Arthur Bigge），请他转呈女王陛下，随附窃贼的一片忠忱——这样向你交差，可还满意？”拉夫尔斯说，“我寻思，倘若直接写明寄给君主本人，只怕邮政总局（G.P.O.）那边对我太过留心。不错，我是抱着它坐车去的圣马丁勒格朗（St. Martin's-le-Grand），还顺手给盒子挂了号。凡做一件事，要么不做，做就要做到家。”

“可你究竟何苦，”我呻吟道，“要干这种事？”

===Original===
“My dear Bunny, we have been reigned over for sixty years by infinitely the finest monarch the world has ever seen. The world is taking the present opportunity of signifying the fact for all it is worth. Every nation is laying of its best at her royal feet; every class in the community is doing its little level﻿—except ours. All I have done is to remove one reproach from our fraternity.”
At this I came round, was infected with his spirit, called him the sportsman he always was and would be, and shook his daredevil hand in mine; but, at the same time, I still had my qualms.

===Chinese===
“亲爱的邦尼，统治了我们六十年的，是这世上从未有过、也远远无可比拟的贤明君主。举世正趁此良机，倾其所能地彰显这一事实。万国都把自己最好的东西，捧到她的御足之下；社会上每一个阶层，也都在尽其绵薄——唯独咱们这一行除外。我所做的，不过是替咱们的行会，抹掉这一桩耻辱。”

听到这儿，我回心转意了，染上了他的兴致，称他是一条堂堂正正的好汉——过去是，往后也终究是——还把他的亡命之手攥进我手里，摇了又摇；可与此同时，我心里仍不免七上八下。

===Original===
“Supposing they trace it to us?” said I.
“There’s not much to catch hold of in a biscuit-box by Huntley & Palmer,” replied Raffles; “that was why I sent you for one. And I didn’t write a word upon a sheet of paper which could possibly be traced. I simply printed two or three on a virginal postcard﻿—another halfpenny to the bad﻿—which might have been bought at any post-office in the kingdom. No, old chap, the G.P.O. was the one real danger; there was one detective I spotted for myself; and the sight of him has left me with a thirst. Whisky and Sullivans for two, Bunny, if you please.”

===Chinese===
“万一他们循线追到咱们头上呢？”我说。

“亨特利-帕尔默的饼干盒上，抓不着多少把柄，”拉夫尔斯答道，“所以我才打发你去买它。信纸上，我也一个字没写，免得留下追得出的笔迹。我只在一张白璧无瑕的明信片上，印了两三个字——又贴进去半便士——那样的明信片，王国境内哪一家邮局都买得到。不，老伙计，邮政总局才是唯一真正的险处；那儿有个探子，是我自己认出来的；瞧了他那一眼，害我至今口渴。威士忌两杯，萨利文（Sullivans）香烟两支，有劳了，邦尼。”

===Original===
Raffles was soon clinking his glass against mine.
“The Queen,” said he. “God bless her!”

===Chinese===
不多时，拉夫尔斯的酒杯已与我的叮然相碰。

“敬女王，”他说，“愿上帝保佑她！”

## The Fate of Faustina / 福斯蒂娜的命运

===Original===
“Mar﻿—ga﻿—rì,
					

					e perzo a Salvatore!
					

					Mar﻿—ga﻿—rì,
					

					Ma l’ommo è cacciatore!
					

					Mar﻿—ga﻿—rì,
					

					Nun ce aje corpa tu!
					

					Chello ch’ è fatto, è fatto, un ne parlammo cchieù!”

A piano-organ was pouring the metallic music through our open windows, while a voice of brass brayed the words, which I have since obtained, and print above for identification by such as know their Italy better than I. They will not thank me for reminding them of a tune so lately epidemic in that land of aloes and blue skies; but at least it is unlikely to run in their heads as the ribald accompaniment to a tragedy; and it does in mine.

===Chinese===
“Mar—ga—rì,

e perzo a Salvatore!

Mar—ga—rì,

Ma l’ommo è cacciatore!

Mar—ga—rì,

Nun ce aje corpa tu!

Chello ch’ è fatto, è fatto, un ne parlammo cchieù!”（那不勒斯歌谣，大意为：玛加丽塔，失去了萨尔瓦托雷！玛加丽塔，男人天生是猎手！玛加丽塔，这怪不得你！木已成舟，木已成舟，咱们再也休提！）

一架手摇风琴正从我们敞开的窗口灌进那金属般的乐声，还有一副黄铜嗓子嘶吼着歌词。这歌词是我后来才弄到手的，特照录于上，好让比我更熟悉意大利的人辨认一番。他们不会领我这份情——提醒他们一支新近才在那芦荟与蓝天的国度里闹过时疫的曲子；不过这曲子总不至于也在他们脑子里，成了一出悲剧的谑浪伴奏；而在我的脑子里，偏偏就是。

===Original===
It was in the early heat of August, and the hour that of the lawful and necessary siesta for such as turn night into day. I was therefore shutting my window in a rage, and wondering whether I should not do the same for Raffles, when he appeared in the silk pajamas to which the chronic solicitude of Dr. Theobald confined him from morning to night.

“Don’t do that, Bunny,” said he. “I rather like that thing, and want to listen. What sort of fellows are they to look at, by the way?”

I put my head out to see, it being a primary rule of our quaint establishment that Raffles must never show himself at any of the windows. I remember now how hot the sill was to my elbows, as I leant upon it and looked down, in order to satisfy a curiosity in which I could see no point.

===Chinese===
时值八月乍起的热浪，正是把黑夜当白昼过的人合法而必需的午睡时辰。于是我正怒气冲冲地关窗，还盘算着要不要把拉夫尔斯（Raffles）的窗子也照样关上，他却来了，穿着一身丝绸睡衣——西奥博尔德医生（Dr. Theobald）那经年不渝的悬切关怀，把他从清早到深夜都圈在这身行头里。

“别关，邦尼（Bunny），”他说，“我倒挺喜欢这玩意儿，想听一听。对了，那两个家伙看上去是何等人物？”

我探出头去看——我们这处古怪居所的头号规矩，便是拉夫尔斯绝不能在任何一扇窗口露脸。此刻我还记得，我伏在窗台上往下望时，窗台烫得我两肘发烧；我就是要满足这么一桩连我自己也看不出名堂的好奇心。

===Original===
“Dirty-looking beggars,” said I over my shoulder: “dark as dark; blue chins, oleaginous curls, and earrings; ragged as they make them, but nothing picturesque in their rags.”

“Neapolitans all over,” murmured Raffles behind me; “and that’s a characteristic touch, the one fellow singing while the other grinds; they always have that out there.”

“He’s rather a fine chap, the singer,” said I, as the song ended. “My hat, what teeth! He’s looking up here, and grinning all round his head; shall I chuck him anything?”

===Chinese===
“邋里邋遢的叫花子，”我回头说道，“黑得没治了；青下巴，油鬈发，还戴耳环；衣衫烂得登峰造极，偏又烂得毫无画意。”

“彻头彻尾的那不勒斯（Naples）人，”拉夫尔斯在我身后低语，“还有一处点睛的细节：一个唱，一个摇琴；他们那边向来如此。”

“唱歌的那位倒是条漂亮汉子，”一曲终了我说道。“乖乖，好一口牙！他正抬头往这儿瞧呢，咧着嘴满脸堆笑；我要不要赏他点什么？”

===Original===
“Well, I have no reason to love the Neapolitans; but it takes me back﻿—it takes me back! Yes, here you are, one each.”

It was a couple of half-crowns that Raffles put into my hand, but I had thrown them into the street for pennies before I saw what they were. Thereupon I left the Italians bowing to the mud, as well they might, and I turned to protest against such wanton waste. But Raffles was walking up and down, his head bent, his eyes troubled; and his one excuse disarmed remonstrance.

“They took me back,” he repeated. “My God, how they took me back!”

===Chinese===
“唉，我没理由偏爱那不勒斯人；可这曲子把我带回去了——把我带回去了！来，拿着，一人一枚。”

拉夫尔斯塞进我手心的是两枚半克朗，我却在看清之前便把它们当便士扔到了街上。于是那两个意大利人只顾对着泥地叩谢——也理当如此——我转身抗议这般恣意的挥霍。可拉夫尔斯正来回踱步，头垂着，眼神惶乱；他那一句话，便叫人再也数落不下去。

“它们把我带回去了，”他又说了一遍。“天哪，它们把我带得多远啊！”

===Original===
Suddenly he stopped in his stride.

“You don’t understand, Bunny, old chap; but if you like you shall. I always meant to tell you some day, but never felt worked up to it before, and it’s not the kind of thing one talks about for talking’s sake. It isn’t a nursery story, Bunny, and there isn’t a laugh in it from start to finish; on the contrary, you have often asked me what turned my hair gray, and now you are going to hear.”

This was promising, but Raffles’s manner was something more. It was unique in my memory of the man. His fine face softened and set hard by turns. I never knew it so hard. I never knew it so soft. And the same might be said of his voice, now tender as any woman’s, now flying to the other extreme of equally unwonted ferocity. But this was toward the end of his tale; the beginning he treated characteristically enough, though I could have wished for a less cavalier account of the island of Elba, where, upon his own showing, he had met with much humanity.

===Chinese===
他突然收住脚步。

“你是不会懂的，邦尼，老伙计；不过你若想懂，就让你懂。我一直打算哪天讲给你听，只是从前总攒不起这个劲头，再说这也不是那种为聊天而聊天的事。这不是哄孩子睡觉的故事，邦尼，从头到尾没有一个笑点；恰恰相反，你常问我，是什么把我的头发熬白的——现在你就要听到了。”

这话头引人入胜，可拉夫尔斯的神态还不止于此。在我对这人的记忆里，那是独一无二的一回。他那张清俊的脸时而软化，时而绷得铁硬。我从没见它那么硬过。也从没见它那么软过。他的嗓音也可以这么说，一时温柔得赛过任何女子，一时又飞向另一个极端，凶悍得同样少见。不过那是到故事末尾才有的情形；开头的部分他讲得倒一如既往地从容，只是他对厄尔巴岛（Elba）的交代未免太轻慢了些——照他自己的说法，他在那岛上颇受过些礼遇。

===Original===
“Deadly, my dear Bunny, is not the word for that glorified snag, or for the mollusks, its inhabitants. But they started by wounding my vanity, so perhaps I am prejudiced, after all. I sprung myself upon them as a shipwrecked sailor﻿—a sole survivor﻿—stripped in the sea and landed without a stitch﻿—yet they took no more interest in me than you do in Italian organ-grinders. They were decent enough. I didn’t have to pick and steal for a square meal and a pair of trousers; it would have been more exciting if I had. But what a place! Napoleon couldn’t stand it, you remember, but he held on longer than I did. I put in a few weeks in their infernal mines, simply to pick up a smattering of Italian; then got across to the mainland in a little wooden timber-tramp; and ungratefully glad I was to leave Elba blazing in just such another sunset as the one you won’t forget.

“The tramp was bound for Naples, but first it touched at Baiae, where I carefully deserted in the night. There are too many English in Naples itself, though I thought it would make a first happy hunting-ground when I knew the language better and had altered myself a bit more. Meanwhile I got a billet of several sorts on one of the loveliest spots that ever I struck on all my travels. The place was a vineyard, but it overhung the sea, and I got taken on as tame sailorman and emergency bottle-washer. The wages were the noble figure of a lira and a half, which is just over a bob, a day, but there were lashings of sound wine for one and all, and better wine to bathe in. And for eight whole months, my boy, I was an absolutely honest man. The luxury of it, Bunny! I out-heroded Herod, wouldn’t touch a grape, and went in the most delicious danger of being knifed for my principles by the thieving crew I had joined.

===Chinese===
“死气沉沉，亲爱的邦尼，还配不上形容那块被吹上天的礁石，也配不上形容它的住户——那些软体动物。可他们一上来就伤了我的虚荣心，所以说不定我到底存了偏见。我把自己突然送到他们面前，扮的是遭难的水手——唯一的幸存者——在海里剥得精光，上岸时不剩一根纱——可他们对我的兴趣，还不及你对意大利摇琴匠的兴致。他们够体面。我不必靠摸扒偷窃去换一顿饱饭一条裤子；若真要那么干，倒还更刺激些。可那是怎样一个地方啊！连拿破仑都受不了，你总记得吧，可他还比我熬得久。我在他们那该死的矿洞里消磨了几个星期，纯粹为拾掇一点意大利语的皮毛；随后搭上一条运木料的小木壳货船渡到了本土；离开厄尔巴岛时，它正燃烧在那样一场落日里——同你忘不掉的那一场不差分毫——而我负义地欢喜着。”

“那条货船是开往那不勒斯的，却先在巴亚（Baiae）靠了一站，我便趁夜色从容地开了小差。那不勒斯城里英国人太多，不过我当时想，等语言学得更地道些、把自己改装得更彻底些，那倒是头一等的好猎场。这期间，我在平生游历所遇最美妙的一处地方，谋得了一个身兼数职的差事。那地方是一座葡萄园，却高悬在海上；我受雇当了驯顺的水手兼应急的洗瓶工。工钱是每天一里拉半的巨款——折合刚过一个先令——但人人都有大瓮好酒痛饮，还有更好的酒可以拿来泡澡。就这样足足八个月，小伙子，我是个不折不扣的诚实人。那份阔气啊，邦尼！我比希律王还希律王，一颗葡萄也不肯碰，日日夜夜冒着天下最美味的危险：随时会被我混迹其中的那伙窃贼，为着我的原则赏我一刀。”

===Original===
“It was the kind of place where every prospect pleases﻿—and all the rest of it﻿—especially all the rest. But may I see it in my dreams till I die﻿—as it was in the beginning﻿—before anything began to happen. It was a wedge of rock sticking out into the bay, thatched with vines, and with the rummiest old house on the very edge of all, a devil of a height above the sea: you might have sat at the windows and dropped your Sullivan-ends plumb into blue water a hundred and fifty feet below.

“From the garden behind the house﻿—such a garden, Bunny﻿—oleanders and mimosa, myrtles, rosemarys and red tangles of fiery, untamed flowers﻿—in a corner of this garden was the top of a subterranean stair down to the sea; at least there were nearly two hundred steps tunnelled through the solid rock; then an iron gate, and another eighty steps in the open air; and last of all a cave fit for pirates, a-penny-plain-and-twopence-colored. This cave gave upon the sweetest little thing in coves, all deep blue water and honest rocks; and here I looked after the vineyard shipping, a potbellied tub with a brown sail, and a sort of dingy. The tub took the wine to Naples, and the dingy was the tub’s tender.

===Chinese===
“那是这么一种地方——‘四望莫不悦目’——连同下半句也一样——尤其是下半句。（化自海伯赞美诗：‘四望景色皆悦目，唯人是卑劣货。’）但愿我到死都能在梦里再见它——照它最初的样子——在出事之前的样子。那是一角楔子似的岩石探进海湾，岩上苫满葡萄藤，最外缘上蹲着一栋顶古怪的老宅，高悬在海面上老高：你坐在窗口，把抽剩的萨利文（Sullivan）牌香烟头径直一丢，它便笔直落进一百五十英尺底下的碧水里。

“从宅子背后的园子——好一个园子，邦尼——夹竹桃与含羞草，香桃木与迷迭香，还有一蓬蓬火红泼辣、野性难驯的花——就在园子一角，有一道入地石梯的顶口，直通到海；至少将近两百级台阶凿穿整块山岩；接着一道铁门，再在露天里走八十级；最后是一个顶配海盗的洞窟，就是童剧布景上‘一便士素色、两便士彩色’的那一路货色。洞口开向小湾里最甜美的一个小家伙，尽是深蓝的水和老实的礁石；我就在这儿照管葡萄园的航运：一条撑褐色帆的大肚皮驳船，外加一叶小艇。驳船把酒运去那不勒斯，小艇便是驳船的接驳船。

===Original===
“The house above was said to be on the identical site of a suburban retreat of the admirable Tiberius; there was the old sinner’s private theatre with the tiers cut clean to this day, the well where he used to fatten his lampreys on his slaves, and a ruined temple of those ripping old Roman bricks, shallow as dominoes and ruddier than the cherry. I never was much of an antiquary, but I could have become one there if I’d had nothing else to do; but I had lots. When I wasn’t busy with the boats I had to trim the vines, or gather the grapes, or even help make the wine itself in a cool, dark, musty vault underneath the temple, that I can see and smell as I jaw. And can’t I hear it and feel it too! Squish, squash, bubble; squash, squish, guggle; and your feet as though you had been wading through slaughter to a throne. Yes, Bunny, you mightn’t think it, but this good right foot, that never was on the wrong side of the crease when the ball left my hand, has also been known to

‘crush the lees of pleasure
					

					From sanguine grapes of pain.’ ”

===Chinese===
“崖顶那座宅子，据说正坐落在可敬的提比略（Tiberius）一处郊外行宫的原址上；老罪人的私人剧场至今看台还凿截得干干净净，还有那口井——他从前惯在井里拿奴隶喂养他的八目鳗——另有一座倾颓的神庙，砌的是那种妙极了的古罗马砖，薄得像多米诺骨牌，红得赛过樱桃。我从来算不得什么考古家，可在那儿若是没有旁的事，我满能成得了一个；偏偏旁的事多得很。不忙船务的时候，我得整藤、摘葡萄，甚至到神庙底下一座阴凉、幽暗、发潮的酒窖里亲手帮着酿酒——那座窖，我这会儿一面絮叨还一面看得见、闻得着。何况还听得见、摸得着呢！滋唧，咕嚓，冒泡；咕嚓，滋唧，汩汩；两只脚底下，仿佛方才蹚过一路杀伐去登王座。是啊，邦尼，你怕是不信：我这只好右脚——每回球出手从不站错投球线的这一只——居然也挣下过这样的名声：

‘从血红的痛苦葡萄里，
踩出欢愉的酒渣。’”

===Original===
He made a sudden pause, as though he had stumbled on the truth in jest. His face filled with lines. We were sitting in the room that had been bare when first I saw it; there were basket-chairs and a table in it now, all meant ostensibly for me; and hence Raffles would slip to his bed, with schoolboy relish, at every tinkle of the bell. This afternoon we felt fairly safe, for Theobald had called in the morning, and Mrs. Theobald still took up much of his time. Through the open window we could hear the piano-organ and “Mar﻿—gar﻿—rì” a few hundred yards further on. I fancied Raffles was listening to it while he paused. He shook his head abstractedly when I handed him the cigarettes; and his tone hereafter was never just what it had been.

“I don’t know, Bunny, whether you’re a believer in transmigration of souls. I have often thought it easier to believe than lots of other things, and I have been pretty near believing in it myself since I had my being on that villa of Tiberius. The brute who had it in my day, if he isn’t still running it with a whole skin, was or is as cold-blooded a blackguard as the worst of the emperors, but I have often thought he had a lot in common with Tiberius. He had the great high sensual Roman nose, eyes that were sinks of iniquity in themselves, and that swelled with fatness, like the rest of him, so that he wheezed if he walked a yard; otherwise rather a fine beast to look at, with a huge gray moustache, like a flying gull, and the most courteous manners even to his men; but one of the worst, Bunny, one of the worst that ever was. It was said that the vineyard was only his hobby; if so, he did his best to make his hobby pay. He used to come out from Naples for the weekends﻿—in the tub when it wasn’t too rough for his nerves﻿—and he didn’t always come alone. His very name sounded unhealthy﻿—Corbucci. I suppose I ought to add that he was a Count, though Counts are two-a-penny in Naples, and in season all the year round.

===Chinese===
他蓦地顿住，仿佛玩笑间绊出了真话。他脸上布满了纹路。我们坐的这间屋子，我初见时空空如也，如今添了几把藤椅一张桌子，明面上全是为我置办的；于是每逢门铃一响，拉夫尔斯便揣着学童的欢劲儿溜回床上去。这天下午我们自觉相当安全，因为西奥博尔德上午来诊视过了，而西奥博尔德太太还占着他不少工夫。透过敞开的窗子，还听得见几百码外那架手摇风琴和《玛—加—丽》。我想，拉夫尔斯停顿的当口正听着它。我递烟给他，他心不在焉地摇了摇头；从此往下，他的语气再不复先前的模样。

“我不知道你信不信灵魂转世，邦尼。我常想，这比许多别的事情都容易信些；自从我在提比略那座别墅里活过一场，我自己也差一点儿信了。我那些日子占据它的那头畜生——倘若他至今还能囫囵带着一身人皮经营它的话——从前是、如今也还是个跟最坏的皇帝一样冷血的恶棍；可我常想，他与提比略颇有几分相通。他生着一只罗马式高挺多欲的鼻子，一双眼睛本身便是罪恶的渊薮，又同他周身一样虚胖浮肿，走上码把路便要喘；除此之外倒是一头相当气派的畜生，一部灰白大胡子像一只掠飞的海鸥，对自己的仆役也礼数周全；但他是最坏的货色之一，邦尼，空前最坏的货色之一。据说葡萄园不过是他的癖好；若果真如此，他倒是竭尽全力叫这癖好生利。他惯于从那不勒斯出来度周末——风浪不太惊他神经的时候，便乘那条胖驳船来——而且不总是独来独往。连他的名字听着都不健康——科尔布奇（Corbucci）。我想我还该补一句：他是个伯爵，虽然在那不勒斯，伯爵一便士能买俩，而且终年应季。”

===Original===
“He had a little English, and liked to air it upon me, much to my disgust; if I could not hope to conceal my nationality as yet, I at least did not want to have it advertised; and the swine had English friends. When he heard that I was bathing in November, when the bay is still as warm as new milk, he would shake his wicked old head and say, ‘You are very audashuss﻿—you are very audashuss!’ and put on no end of side before his Italians. By God, he had pitched upon the right word unawares, and I let him know it in the end!

“But that bathing, Bunny; it was absolutely the best I ever had anywhere. I said just now the water was like wine; in my own mind I used to call it blue champagne, and was rather annoyed that I had no one to admire the phrase. Otherwise I assure you that I missed my own particular kind very little indeed, though I often wished that *you* were there, old chap; particularly when I went for my lonesome swim; first thing in the morning, when the Bay was all rose-leaves, and last thing at night, when your body caught phosphorescent fire! Ah, yes, it was a good enough life for a change; a perfect paradise to lie low in; another Eden until﻿ ﻿…

“My poor Eve!”

===Chinese===
“他会一点儿英语，偏爱在我面前卖弄，腻烦死我；我的国籍既然一时还瞒不住，我至少不愿叫人拿它四处招摇；偏偏这猪猡又交结英国朋友。听说我十一月里还下海洗澡——那时节海湾还温得像新挤的奶——他便摇他那颗老不正经的脑袋说：‘You are very audashuss—you are very audashuss!’（你好大胆——你好大胆！）在意大利人跟前大摆其架子。老天在上，他无意间偏偏挑中了那个再对不过的字眼，到头来，我是让他自己知道这一点的！”

“可说回那洗澡，邦尼；那绝对是我天南地北享受过的头等好水。我方才说那水像酒；我心里向来管它叫蓝香槟，只是恼人得很，身边没有一个人可供我炫耀这句妙语。除此之外，我向你担保，我几乎不怎么惦念我自己那一行的乐趣，虽然我常盼着在场的是你，老伙计；尤其在我独自去游那一趟的时候；清早头一件事，海湾满铺玫瑰花瓣；夜里末一件事，你周身沾起磷火！啊，是的，换一种活法，那日子够美了；一座销声匿迹的十全十美的乐园；又一座伊甸园，直到……

“我可怜的夏娃！”

===Original===
And he fetched a sigh that took away his words; then his jaws snapped together, and his eyes spoke terribly while he conquered his emotion. I pen the last word advisedly. I fancy it is one which I have never used before in writing of A. J. Raffles, for I cannot at the moment recall any other occasion upon which its use would have been justified. On resuming, however, he was not only calm, but cold; and this flying for safety to the other extreme is the single instance of self-distrust which the present Achates can record to the credit of his impious Aeneas.

“I called the girl Eve,” said he. “Her real name was Faustina, and she was one of a vast family who hung out in a hovel on the inland border of the vineyard. And Aphrodite rising from the sea was less wonderful and not more beautiful than Aphrodite emerging from that hole!

===Chinese===
他发出一声长叹，叹尽了口中的言语；随后牙关一咬，眼睛可怕地说着话，一面制服自己的激动。这末一个词，我是斟酌过才落笔的。我想，在写A. J. 拉夫尔斯的文字里，我还不曾用过它，因为此刻我想不起还有别的场合，用上它能算名正言顺。然而再开口时，他不但是平静，简直是冰冷；这种一步飞逃到另一极端以求安全的事，是眼前的这位阿卡特斯（Achates）所能记录在案的唯一一桩自我动摇——也算给他那位渎神的埃涅阿斯（Aeneas）增光了。

“我管那姑娘叫夏娃，”他说。“她的真名叫福斯蒂娜（Faustina），她是一个人丁兴旺的大家族里的一分子，那一家子窝在葡萄园靠内陆边上一间破屋里。阿佛洛狄忒（Aphrodite）从海里升起，也不见得比阿佛洛狄忒从那个窟窿里钻出来更神奇、更美丽！”

===Original===
“It was the most exquisite face I ever saw or shall see in this life. Absolutely perfect features; a skin that reminded you of old gold, so delicate was its bronze; magnificent hair, not black but nearly; and such eyes and teeth as would have made the fortune of a face without another point. I tell you, Bunny, London would go mad about a girl like that. But I don’t believe there’s such another in the world. And there she was wasting her sweetness upon that lovely but desolate little corner of it! Well, she did not waste it upon me. I would have married her, and lived happily ever after in such a hovel as her people’s﻿—with her. Only to look at her﻿—only to look at her for the rest of my days﻿—I could have lain low and remained dead even to you! And that’s all I’m going to tell you about that, Bunny; cursed be he who tells more! Yet don’t run away with the idea that this poor Faustina was the only woman I ever cared about. I don’t believe in all that ‘only’ rot; nevertheless I tell you that she was the one being who ever entirely satisfied my sense of beauty; and I honestly believe I could have chucked the world and been true to Faustina for that alone.

“We met sometimes in the little temple I told you about, sometimes among the vines; now by honest accident, now by flagrant design; and found a ready-made rendezvous, romantic as one could wish, in the cave down all those subterranean steps. Then the sea would call us﻿—my blue champagne﻿—my sparkling cobalt﻿—and there was the dingy ready to our hand. Oh, those nights! I never knew which I liked best, the moonlit ones when you sculled through silver and could see for miles, or the dark nights when the fishermen’s torches stood for the sea, and a red zigzag in the sky for old Vesuvius. We were happy. I don’t mind owning it. We seemed not to have a care between us. My mates took no interest in my affairs, and Faustina’s family did not appear to bother about her. The Count was in Naples five nights of the seven; the other two we sighed apart.

===Chinese===
“那是我今生得见、也再见不到的一张最精致的脸。五官绝对无瑕；那皮肤叫人想起老金子，青铜色细腻到极处；一头华发，算不得纯黑，也相去不远；那样一双眼睛、一口牙齿，单凭其一便足够成就一张别无长处的脸。我告诉你，邦尼，伦敦准会为这样一个姑娘发疯。可我不信世上还有第二个。而她就在那儿，把她的美好虚掷于那可爱却荒凉的一隅！好在，她没有虚掷在我身上。我本可以娶了她，从此在她族人那样的破屋里幸福终老——同她一起。只消看着她——只消余生能看着她——我便甘心蛰伏隐没，连你也当我死了！这件事我对你讲的就到这儿，邦尼；再多说的，叫那人遭天罚！不过你可别顺势以为，可怜的福斯蒂娜是我唯一动过心的女人。我不信那一套‘唯一’的昏话；然而我告诉你：她是唯一一个全然餍足了我的美感的生灵；而且我真心相信，单凭这一点，我就能抛开整个世界，对福斯蒂娜守节到底。”

“我们有时在我讲过的那座小庙里相会，有时在葡萄藤间；一时是光明正大的不期而遇，一时是明目张胆的刻意安排；沿着那一路入地石梯到底的洞窟，更是现成的幽会之所，要多浪漫有多浪漫。随后海便召唤我们——我的蓝香槟——我那泛泡的钴蓝——小艇就在手边。啊，那些夜晚！我始终说不清更爱哪一样：是有月亮的夜，一桨划破白银，望得出去好几里；还是漆黑的夜，渔人的火炬权当是海，天上一道红色的锯齿火光权当是老维苏威（Vesuvius）。我们是快活的。我不怕承认。我们俩之间仿佛了无牵挂。我的伙伴们对我的私事毫无兴趣，福斯蒂娜家里似乎也不来管她。伯爵一星期七夜倒有五夜在那不勒斯；余下两夜，我们两地相思，各自嗟叹。”

===Original===
“At first it was the oldest story in literature﻿—Eden *plus* Eve. The place had been a heaven on earth before, but now it was heaven itself. So for a little; then one night, a Monday night, Faustina burst out crying in the boat; and sobbed her story as we drifted without mishap by the mercy of the Lord. And that was almost as old a story as the other.

“She was engaged﻿—what! Had I never heard of it? Did I mean to upset the boat? What was her engagement beside our love? ‘*Niente, niente*,’ crooned Faustina, sighing yet smiling through her tears. No, but what did matter was that the man had threatened to stab her to the heart﻿—and would do it as soon as look at her﻿—that I knew.

“I knew it merely from my knowledge of the Neapolitans, for I had no idea who the man might be. I knew it, and yet I took this detail better than the fact of the engagement, though now I began to laugh at both. As if I was going to let her marry anybody else! As if a hair of her lovely head should be touched while I lived to protect her! I had a great mind to row away to blazes with her that very night, and never go near the vineyard again, or let her either. But we had not a lira between us at the time, and only the rags in which we sat barefoot in the boat. Besides, I had to know the name of the animal who had threatened a woman, and such a woman as this.

===Chinese===
“起初，那是文学里最古老的故事——伊甸园外加一个夏娃。这地方本已是人间天上，如今索性成了天上本身。这样过了些时日；随后有一夜，一个星期一夜里，福斯蒂娜在小艇中放声哭了；我们随波漂荡，托主的慈悲不曾出事，她抽抽噎噎说出了她的故事。而那个故事，几乎同前一个一样古老。”

“她订了亲——什么！我竟从没听说过？我想不想把艇弄翻？她的婚约比起我们的爱情算得了什么？‘Niente, niente’（没什么，没什么），福斯蒂娜柔声哼着，含着泪叹息，又含着泪微笑。不，真正要紧的是：那男人威胁过要一刀捅进她的心口——而且见她一面就真下得去手——这一点我清楚。”

“我清楚，仅仅是凭我对那不勒斯人的了解，因为那男人可能是谁，我毫无概念。我清楚了，而且这个细节，我受用得竟比订亲的事实还平静——尽管如今回想，我对两者都付之一笑。好像我会让她嫁给旁人似的！好像有我活着护她一日，她那可爱的头顶会教人碰一根毫毛似的！那晚我真想干脆带她划到天涯海角去，从此不再走近葡萄园一步，也不让她回去。可当时我们俩凑不出一里拉，艇里只有赤脚坐着的一身破衣。再说，我总得知道那头威胁女人——威胁这样一个女人——的畜生姓甚名谁。”

===Original===
“For a long time she refused to tell me, with splendid obduracy; but I was as determined as she; so at last she made conditions. I was not to go and get put in prison for sticking a knife into him﻿—he wasn’t worth it﻿—and I did promise not to stab him in the back. Faustina seemed quite satisfied, though a little puzzled by my manner, having herself the racial tolerance for cold steel; and next moment she had taken away my breath. ‘It is Stefano,’ she whispered, and hung her head.

“And well she might, poor thing! Stefano, of all creatures on God’s earth﻿—for her!

“Bunny, he was a miserable little undersized wretch﻿—ill-favored﻿—servile﻿—surly﻿—and second only to his master in bestial cunning and hypocrisy. His face was enough for me; that was what I read in it, and I don’t often make mistakes. He was Corbucci’s own confidential body-servant, and that alone was enough to damn him in decent eyes: always came out first on the Saturday with the *spese*, to have all ready for his master and current mistress, and stayed behind on the Monday to clear and lock up. Stefano! That worm! I could well understand *his* threatening a woman with a knife; what beat me was how any woman could ever have listened to him; above all, that Faustina should be the one! It passed my comprehension. But I questioned her as gently as I could; and her explanation was largely the threadbare one you would expect. Her parents were so poor. They were so many in family. Some of them begged﻿—would I promise never to tell? Then some of them stole﻿—sometimes﻿—and all knew the pains of actual want. She looked after the cows, but there were only two of them, and brought the milk to the vineyard and elsewhere; but that was not employment for more than one; and there were countless sisters waiting to take her place. Then he was so rich, Stefano.

===Chinese===
“有好一阵子，她以一种堂皇的倔强拒绝告诉我；可我同她一样铁了心；于是末了她提出了条件。我不许去为了捅他一刀蹲大狱——他不配——我也当真答应了不从背后捅他。福斯蒂娜似乎大为满意，只是对我的态度有点儿纳闷，因为她自家生就那民族对白刃的宽容；下一刻，她便叫我屏住了呼吸。‘是斯特凡诺（Stefano），’她悄声道，垂下了头。”

“她也理当垂头，可怜的人！天底下芸芸众生，偏偏是斯特凡诺——给她配的！”

“邦尼，他是个可怜兮兮的矮个子货色——面目猥琐——奴颜婢膝——阴郁乖戾——而在禽兽般的狡诈与虚伪上，仅次于他的主人。他那副嘴脸于我就足够了；我在那上面读出的便是这些，而我很少看走眼。他是科尔布奇贴身的亲随，光这一条，在正经人眼里便够定他的罪：每逢星期六总是先带着spese（采买钱）赶过来，替主人和现任情妇打点一切，星期一又殿后收拾上锁。斯特凡诺！那条虫豸！他拿刀子吓唬女人，我完全能够理解；让我想不通的是，女人怎么会肯听他半句；尤其想不到，福斯蒂娜竟是那一个！这超出了我的理解力。可我尽量温言软语地追问；她的解释，大体不出你料得到的那套旧话。她爹娘穷极了。家中人口又多。有几个讨饭——我能担保永不外传吗？又有几个偷东西——偶尔为之——全家都尝过真正匮乏的痛楚。她放牛，可牛只两头，她把牛奶送到葡萄园和别处；可这活计养不起两个人；何况还有数不清的姐妹等着顶她的缺。再说他是那么有钱，斯特凡诺。”

===Original===
“ ‘Rich!’ I echoed. ‘Stefano?’

“ ‘*Si, Arturo mio.*’

“Yes, I played the game on that vineyard, Bunny, even to going my own first name.

“ ‘And how comes he to be rich?’ I asked, suspiciously.

===Chinese===
“‘有钱！’我应声道。‘斯特凡诺？’”

“‘Si, Arturo mio.’（是的，我的阿图罗。）”

“是的，在那座葡萄园上我是规规矩矩来的，邦尼，甚至连自己的教名——阿图罗（Arturo）——都用上了。”

“‘那他怎么会发财的？’我满腹狐疑地问。”

===Original===
“She did not know; but he had given her such beautiful jewels; the family had lived on them for months, she pretending an avocat had taken charge of them for her against her marriage. But I cared nothing about all that.

“ ‘Jewels! Stefano!’ I could only mutter.

“ ‘Perhaps the Count has paid for some of them. He is very kind.’

“ ‘To you, is he?’

===Chinese===
她不知道；不过他送过她一些顶漂亮的珠宝；一家人靠它们过了好几个月，她对外只推说有一位律师（avocat）替她收管着，留待她成婚之用。这些我一概不放在心上。

“‘珠宝！斯特凡诺！’我只喃喃得出声。”

“‘也许有几件是伯爵出钱置办的。他心肠好得很。’”

“‘对你好，是么？’”

===Original===
“ ‘Oh, yes, very kind.’

“ ‘And you would live in his house afterwards?’

“ ‘Not now, *mia cara*﻿—not now!’

“ ‘No, by God you don’t!’ said I in English. ‘But you would have done so, eh?’

===Chinese===
“‘哦，是的，好得很。’”

“‘往后你就要住进他的宅子？’”

“‘现在不去啦，mia cara（我亲爱的）——现在不去啦！’”

“‘不成，老天在上，你去不成！’我用英语说道。‘可你原本是打算去的，嗯？’”

===Original===
“ ‘Of course. That was arranged. The Count is really very kind.’

“ ‘Do you see anything of him when he comes here?’

“Yes, he had sometimes brought her little presents, sweetmeats, ribbons, and the like; but the offering had always been made through this toad of a Stefano. Knowing the men, I now knew all. But Faustina, she had the pure and simple heart, and the white soul, by the God who made it, and for all her kindness to a tattered scapegrace who made love to her in broken Italian between the ripples and the stars. She was not to know what I was, remember; and beside Corbucci and his henchman I was the Archangel Gabriel come down to earth.

“Well, as I lay awake that night, two more lines of Swinburne came into my head, and came to stay:

===Chinese===
“‘当然。那是讲定了的。伯爵实在心肠好。’”

“‘他到这儿来的时候，你们常见面么？’”

是的，他有时给她捎些小礼物，糖果啦、缎带啦这一类；可这些贡品一概经斯特凡诺那只癞蛤蟆的手转呈。我既深知这两个男人，此刻便全明白了。可是福斯蒂娜，她有颗纯朴的心，一个清白的灵魂——凭造它的上帝起誓——尽管她那般善待一个褴褛的无赖，在涟漪与星光之间用磕磕绊绊的意大利语向她求爱。她不该知道我是什么人，记住；站在科尔布奇和他的走卒旁边，我就是降临尘世的大天使加百列（Archangel Gabriel）。

“呃，那晚我睡不着躺在床上，斯温伯恩（Swinburne）又有两行诗飘进我脑子，而且就此扎了根：

===Original===
“God said ‘Let him who wins her take
					

					And keep Faustine.’

“On that couplet I slept at last, and it was my text and watchword when I awoke in the morning. I forget how well you know your Swinburne, Bunny; but don’t you run away with the idea that there was anything else in common between his Faustine and mine. For the last time let me tell you that poor Faustina was the whitest and the best I ever knew.

===Chinese===
上帝说：‘赢了她的人，娶她，
守着浮士婷（Faustine）。’

“我终究枕着这副对句睡着了，清晨醒来，它又成了我的经文与口号。我忘了你对斯温伯恩熟到几分，邦尼；可你千万别自作主张地以为，他那位浮士婷同我这一位还有别的什么相干。让我最后说一遍：可怜的福斯蒂娜，是我平生所知最清白、最好的人。”

===Original===
“Well, I was strung up for trouble when the next Saturday came, and I’ll tell you what I had done. I had broken the pledge and burgled Corbucci’s villa in my best manner during his absence in Naples. Not that it gave me the slightest trouble; but no human being could have told that I had been in, when I came out. And I had stolen nothing, mark you, but only borrowed a revolver from a drawer in the Count’s desk, with one or two trifling accessories; for by this time I had the measure of these damned Neapolitans. They are spry enough with a knife, but you show them the business end of a shooting-iron, and they’ll streak like rabbits for the nearest hole. But the revolver wasn’t for my own use. It was for Faustina, and I taught her how to use it in the cave down there by the sea, shooting at candles stuck upon the rock. The noise in the cave was something frightful, but high up above it couldn’t be heard at all, as we proved to each other’s satisfaction pretty early in the proceedings. So now Faustina was armed with munitions of self-defence; and I knew enough of her character to entertain no doubt as to their spirited use upon occasion. Between the two of us, in fact, our friend Stefano seemed tolerably certain of a warm weekend.

“But the Saturday brought word that the Count was not coming this week, being in Rome on business, and unable to return in time; so for a whole Sunday we were promised peace; and made bold plans accordingly. There was no further merit in hushing this thing up. ‘Let him who wins her take and keep Faustine.’ Yes, but let him win her openly, or lose her and be damned to him! So on the Sunday I was going to have it out with her people﻿—with the Count and Stefano as soon as they showed their noses. I had no inducement, remember, ever to return to surreptitious life within a cab-fare of Wormwood Scrubbs. Faustina and the Bay of Naples were quite good enough for me. And the prehistoric man in me rather exulted in the idea of fighting for my desire.

===Chinese===
“呃，到了下一个星期六，我已绷紧了弦专候祸事，还要告诉你我干了些什么。我破了戒，趁科尔布奇人在那不勒斯，拿出看家的本事光顾了他的别墅。倒不是说我费了什么气力；而是我出来的时候，任谁也看不出有人进去过。而且我什么都没偷，你记着，只是从伯爵书桌的抽屉里‘借’了一支左轮手枪，外加一两件不碍事的小配件；因为到这时，我对这班该死的那不勒斯人的斤两已摸得烂熟。玩刀子他们灵光得很，可你把火器的正脸朝他们一亮，他们便兔子似的直窜最近的窟窿。但那支左轮不是给我自己用的。是给福斯蒂娜的，我在下头海边的洞窟里教她怎么使，拿插在岩石上的蜡烛当靶子。洞里的枪声骇人得很，可上头高处一点儿也听不见——这事我们一上手便彼此验证妥当，双方都满意。于是福斯蒂娜如今有了自卫的军火；她的性子我也摸得够透，毫不怀疑事到临头她会放得响亮。说白了，凭我们两个之力，我们的朋友斯特凡诺那个周末多半要过得热辣辣的。”

“可星期六传来话说，伯爵这一周不来了，他在罗马办事，赶不回来；于是整整一个星期天，我们得了太平的许诺；便顺水推舟定下大胆的计划。这件事再遮遮掩掩，也没什么可夸奖的了。‘赢了她的人，娶她守着她。’是，可要赢就得光明正大地赢，否则便输掉，让他见鬼去！所以星期天我打算同她家里人把话说清——伯爵和斯特凡诺一露头就说清。我没有任何动机——记住——再回到那隐秘营生里去，住到离沃姆伍德·斯克拉布斯（Wormwood Scrubbs）监狱只消一趟马车钱的地方。福斯蒂娜和那不勒斯湾于我尽够了。我体内那个原始人，一想到要为自己的所欲而战，简直得意扬扬。”

===Original===
“On the Saturday, however, we were able to meet for the last time as heretofore﻿—just once more in secret﻿—down there in the cave﻿—as soon as might be after dark. Neither of us minded if we were kept for hours; each knew in the end that the other would come; and there was a charm of its own even in waiting with such knowledge. But that night I did lose patience: not in the cave, but up above, where first on one pretext and then on another the *direttore* kept me going until I smelt a rat. He was not given to exacting overtime, this *direttore*, whose only fault was his servile subjection to our common boss. It seemed pretty obvious, therefore, that he was acting upon some secret instructions from Corbucci himself, and, the moment I suspected this, I asked him to his face if it was not the case. And it was; he admitted it with many shrugs, being a conveniently weak person, whom one felt almost ashamed of bullying as the occasion demanded.

“The fact was, however, that the Count had sent for him on finding he had to go to Rome, and had said he was very sorry to go just then, as among other things he intended to speak to me about Faustina. Stefano had told him all about his row with her, and moreover that it was on my account, which Faustina had never told me, though I had guessed as much for myself. Well, the Count was going to take his jackal’s part for all he was worth, which was just exactly what I had expected him to do. He intended going for me on his return, but meanwhile I was not to make hay in his absence, and so this tool of a *direttore* had orders to keep me at it night and day. I undertook not to give the poor beast away, but at the same time told him I had not the faintest intention of doing another stroke of work that night.

===Chinese===
“不过星期六那天，我们还能照旧见最后一面——只此最后一面，偷偷地——在下头那洞窟里——天一擦黑就去。被耽搁几个钟头，谁也不在乎；彼此都知道对方终归会来；就连揣着这份笃定干等，也自有一种妙趣。可那晚我到底失了耐心：不是在洞里，而是在上头，那位direttore（总管）先借这个由头、再借那个由头把我支使得团团转，直到我嗅出耗子味。这位direttore本不是个克扣加班的，他唯一的毛病，是奴颜婢膝地受着我们共同老板的辖制。是以事情相当明显，他奉的是科尔布奇本人的什么密令；我一起疑，当面便问他是不是这么回事。正是如此；他耸了半天肩膀认了——他是个恰好合用的软骨头，情势所需、不得不欺负他的时候，人几乎要为此臊得慌。”

“其实呢，伯爵发觉自己非去罗马不可，便把总管唤了去，说偏偏这时节动身很抱歉，因为诸多事里，他还打算同我谈谈福斯蒂娜。斯特凡诺已把同她吵翻的事一五一十告诉了他，而且还说是为了我——这一层福斯蒂娜从未对我讲过，尽管我自己也猜了个八九不离十。于是伯爵打算全力偏袒他的豺狼，而这恰恰正中我的预料。他打算回来后再来收拾我，但在此期间不许我趁他不在翻天晒草，于是总管这件工具便领了令，日夜摁着我干活。我答应不把这可怜虫供出去，但同时也告诉他，当天夜里再要我动一根指头干活，我是连半点意思也没有的。”

===Original===
“It was very dark, and I remember knocking my head against the oranges as I ran up the long, shallow steps which ended the journey between the *direttore*’s lodge and the villa itself. But at the back of the villa was the garden I spoke about, and also a bare chunk of the cliff where it was bored by that subterranean stair. So I saw the stars close overhead, and the fishermen’s torches far below, the coastwise lights and the crimson hieroglyph that spelt Vesuvius, before I plunged into the darkness of the shaft. And that was the last time I appreciated the unique and peaceful charm of this outlandish spot.

“The stair was in two long flights, with an air-hole or two at the top of the upper one, but not another pinprick till you came to the iron gate at the bottom of the lower. As you may read of an infinitely lighter place, in a finer work of fiction than you are ever likely to write, Bunny, it was ‘gloomy at noon, dark as midnight at dusk, and black as the ninth plague of Egypt at midnight.’ I won’t swear to my quotation, but I will to those stairs. They were as black that night as the inside of the safest safe in the strongest strongroom in the Chancery Lane Deposit. Yet I had not got far down them with my bare feet before I heard somebody else coming up in boots. You may imagine what a turn that gave me! It could not be Faustina, who went barefoot three seasons of the four, and yet there was Faustina waiting for me down below. What a fright she must have had! And all at once my own blood ran cold: for the man sang like a kettle as he plodded up and up. It was, it must be, the short-winded Count himself, whom we all supposed to be in Rome!

===Chinese===
“天黑得紧，我记得一路跑上总管住所与别墅之间那道长长的浅阶时，脑袋撞在了橙子上。但别墅背后便是我说过的那座园子，还有一块光秃秃的崖面，入地石梯正打那儿凿穿下去。于是我望见头顶迫近的星子，底下远远的渔火，沿岸的灯盏，还有拼出‘维苏威’的那道深红象形文字——然后便一头扎进竖井似的黑暗。那是我最后一次领略这化外之地独一无二、太平安谧的韵致。”

“石梯分作两长段，上段的顶上有那么一两个透气孔，往下直到下段底脚的铁门，再没有针眼大的一点亮。正如你在一处亮堂得多的地方、在一部比你此生可能写得出的都好的小说里读到的，邦尼——‘正午里晦暝，黄昏黑如子夜，午夜则黑似埃及第九灾’。引文我不敢担保，那道石梯我敢。那晚它们黑得像大法官巷（Chancery Lane）保管库里最坚固的库房中最保险的那只保险箱的内部。可我赤着脚没下几级，便听见另有一个人穿着靴子往上走。你可想见这一惊非同小可！那不会是福斯蒂娜，她四季里倒有三季赤脚，可偏偏是福斯蒂娜在下面等我。她受的惊吓该有多大啊！倏忽之间，我自己的血也凉了：因为那人一步一步往上蹭，一面像个水壶似的唱。是他，只能是他，那个喘不匀气的伯爵本人，我们全都以为他人在罗马！”

===Original===
“Higher he came and nearer, nearer, slowly yet hurriedly, now stopping to cough and gasp, now taking a few steps by elephantine assault. I should have enjoyed the situation if it had not been for poor Faustina in the cave; as it was I was filled with nameless fears. But I could not resist giving that grampus Corbucci one bad moment on account. A crazy handrail ran up one wall, so I carefully flattened myself against the other, and he passed within six inches of me, puffing and wheezing like a brass band. I let him go a few steps higher, and then I let him have it with both lungs.

“ ‘*Buona sera, eccellenza, signorì!*’ I roared after him. And a scream came down in answer﻿—such a scream! A dozen different terrors were in it; and the wheezing had stopped, with the old scoundrel’s heart.

“ ‘*Chi sta la?*’ he squeaked at last, gibbering and whimpering like a whipped monkey, so that I could not bear to miss his face, and got a match all ready to strike.

“ ‘*Arturo, signorì.*’

===Chinese===
“他越上越高，越来越近，越来越近，又慢又急，一时停下咳嗽喘气，一时又靠大象式的冲锋挪上几级。若不是洞里还搁着一个可怜的福斯蒂娜，这局面本够我受用的；眼下的我，却满是无名的恐惧。可我还是忍不住，要替她记上一笔，送那条老鲸豚科尔布奇难受一阵。一道晃晃悠悠的扶手沿着一面墙通上去，我便把自己小心地贴平在另一面墙上；他离我不到六英寸擦身而过，喘着，哮着，活像一支铜管乐队。我放他又上了几级，然后鼓起两叶肺，冲他全力放了一嗓子。”

“‘Buona sera, eccellenza, signorì!’（晚安，大人，老爷！）我在他背后吼道。一声尖叫应声而下——好一声尖叫！里头装着十来种不同的恐怖；喘息也停了，连同那老恶徒的心跳。”

“‘Chi sta la?’（谁在那儿？）他终于尖声叫出，像挨了鞭子的猴子似的语无伦次、呜呜咽咽，害得我不忍错过他的尊容，摸出一根火柴，随时预备擦亮。”

“‘Arturo, signorì.’（阿图罗，老爷。）”

===Original===
“He didn’t repeat my name, nor did he damn me in heaps. He did nothing but wheeze for a good minute, and when he spoke it was with insinuating civility, in his best English.

“ ‘Come nearer, Arturo. You are in the lower regions down there. I want to speak with you.’

“ ‘No, thanks. I’m in a hurry,’ I said, and dropped that match back into my pocket. He might be armed, and I was not.

“ ‘So you are in a ’urry!’ and he wheezed amusement. ‘And you thought I was still in Rome, no doubt; and so I was until this afternoon, when I caught train at the eleventh moment, and then another train from Naples to Pozzuoli. I have been rowed here now by a fisherman of Pozzuoli. I had not time to stop anywhere in Naples, but only to drive from station to station. So I am without Stefano, Arturo, I am without Stefano.’

===Chinese===
他没有重念我的名字，也没有成堆地咒骂我。足足一分钟，他什么也没做，只顾喘；等他开口，已是曲意逢迎的客套，搬出了他最体面的英语。

“‘过来些，阿图罗。你在下头，可算到了阴曹地府。我有话同你讲。’”

“‘不了，谢谢。我赶时间。’我说着，把那根火柴丢回口袋。他身上或许有家伙，我可没有。”

“‘这么说你赶时间哪！’他喘息着表示有趣。‘你准以为我还在罗马；今天下午之前我确实在，随后我在最后关头赶上了火车，又从那不勒斯换车到了波佐利（Pozzuoli）。这会儿是一个波佐利的渔夫把我划过来的。那不勒斯我哪儿也来不及停，只来得及从一个车站赶到另一个车站。所以我身边没有斯特凡诺，阿图罗，我没有斯特凡诺。’”

===Original===
“His sly voice sounded preternaturally sly in the absolute darkness, but even through that impenetrable veil I knew it for a sham. I had laid hold of the handrail. It shook violently in my hand; he also was holding it where he stood. And these suppressed tremors, or rather their detection in this way, struck a strange chill to my heart, just as I was beginning to pluck it up.

“ ‘It is lucky for Stefano,’ said I, grim as death.

“ ‘Ah, but you must not be too ’ard on ’im,’ remonstrated the Count. ‘You have stole his girl, he speak with me about it, and I wish to speak with you. It is very audashuss, Arturo, very audashuss! Perhaps you are even going to meet her now, eh?’ ”

===Chinese===
绝对的黑暗里，他那奸猾的嗓音透出反常的奸猾；可哪怕隔着这层穿不透的帷幕，我也听得出那是装的。我攥住了扶手。它在我手里剧烈地抖；他在他站的地方也攥着它。这一阵阵强压下去的战栗——不如说，这样被我察觉的战栗——给我心头凭空一凛，正赶上我才开始壮胆的当口。

“‘这是斯特凡诺的运气。’我说，面沉如死。”

“‘啊，可你不能对他太狠哪，’伯爵劝道。‘你偷了他的姑娘，他跟我讲了这事，我要同你讲一讲。这可是非常audashuss，阿图罗，非常audashuss（大胆）！兴许你这会儿正是去会她的，嗯？’”

===Original===
I told him straight that I was.

“ ‘Then there is no ’urry, for she is not there.’

“ ‘You didn’t see her in the cave?’ I cried, too delighted at the thought to keep it to myself.

“ ‘I had no such fortune,’ the old devil said.

===Chinese===
我直截了当告诉他，正是。

“‘那就不用赶了，因为她不在那儿。’”

“‘您在洞里没见到她？’我叫道，这个念头让我乐得没能独享。”

“‘我没这份福气。’老魔鬼说。”

===Original===
“ ‘She is there, all the same.’

“ ‘I only wish I ’ad known.’

“ ‘And I’ve kept her long enough!’

“In fact I threw this over my shoulder as I turned and went running down.

===Chinese===
“‘可她在那儿，错不了。’”

“‘我巴不得早知道呢。’”

“‘反正我已让她等得够久啦！’”

这话其实是甩过肩头去的，我一边转身，一边跑着下去了。

===Original===
“ ‘I ’ope you will find her!’ his malicious voice came croaking after me. ‘I ’ope you will﻿—I ’ope so.’

“And find her I did.”

Raffles had been on his feet some time, unable to sit still or to stand, moving excitedly about the room. But now he stood still enough, his elbows on the cast-iron mantelpiece, his head between his hands.

===Chinese===
“‘我盼你找得着她！’他那恶毒的嗓音在我背后聒噪着追来。‘我盼你找着——我真心盼着。’”

“而我果然找着了她。”

拉夫尔斯已经站了有一阵子，坐也坐不定，站也站不安，只在屋里激动地转来转去。可这会儿他站得纹丝不动，两肘支在铸铁的炉台上，脑袋埋在两手之间。

===Original===
“Dead?” I whispered.

And he nodded to the wall.

“There was not a sound in the cave. There was no answer to my voice. Then I went in, and my foot touched hers, and it was colder than the rock﻿ ﻿… Bunny, they had stabbed her to the heart. She had fought them, and they had stabbed her to the heart!”

===Chinese===
“死了？”我嗫嚅道。

他冲着墙点了点头。

“洞里没有一点声息。我的唤声没有回应。随后我走了进去，我的脚碰到了她的脚，那脚比岩石还凉……邦尼，他们把她捅穿了心口。她同他们搏斗过，他们把她捅穿了心口！”

===Original===
“You say ‘they,’ ” I said gently, as he stood in heavy silence, his back still turned. “I thought Stefano had been left behind?”

Raffles was round in a flash, his face white-hot, his eyes dancing death.

“He was in the cave!” he shouted. “I saw him﻿—I spotted him﻿—it was broad twilight after those stairs﻿—and I went for him with my bare hands. Not fists, Bunny; not fists for a thing like that; I meant getting my fingers into his vile little heart and tearing it out by the roots. I was stark mad. But he had the revolver﻿—hers. He blazed it at arm’s length, and missed. And that steadied me. I had smashed his funny-bone against the rock before he could blaze again; the revolver fell with a rattle, but without going off; in an instant I had it tight, and the little swine at my mercy at last.”

===Chinese===
“你说‘他们’，”我轻声道，他还背身站在那里，沉默得沉重。“我记得斯特凡诺不是给留在后头了吗？”

拉夫尔斯霍地转过身来，脸白热如烧，眼里跳动着死亡。

“他就在洞里！”他吼道。“我看见了他——我认出了他——走过那道石梯，暮色已算得豁亮——我赤手空拳便朝他扑去。不是拳头，邦尼；干那种事不用拳头；我是要把手指头插进他那颗下流的小心脏，连根抠出来。我当时彻底疯了。可他手里有左轮——她的那支。他伸直胳膊放了一枪，没打中。这一下倒叫我定了神。趁他再放一枪之前，我已把他肘上的麻筋往岩石上磕了个正着；左轮哐啷落地，却没走火；眨眼间我已把它攥牢，那小猪猡终于落在我掌心里了。”

===Original===
“You didn’t show him any?”

“Mercy? With Faustina dead at my feet? I should have deserved none in the next world if I had shown him any in this! No, I just stood over him, with the revolver in both hands, feeling the chambers with my thumb; and as I stood he stabbed at me; but I stepped back to that one, and brought him down with a bullet in his guts.

“ ‘And I can spare you two or three more,’ I said, for my poor girl could not have fired a shot. ‘Take that one to hell with you﻿—and that﻿—and that!’

===Chinese===
“你没对他留情？”

“留情？福斯蒂娜死在我脚边？我在这一世若饶了他，来世便活该一个也得不到！不，我只管俯身立在他上头，双手端着左轮，拇指挨个摸着弹巢；我这么立着，他便朝我捅来；那一刀我退步让过，然后一颗子弹打穿他的肚肠，放倒了他。”

“‘剩下的两三发，我还奉送得起，’我说，因为我可怜的姑娘连一枪也不曾来得及放。‘带上这一发下地狱——还有这发——还有这发！’”

===Original===
“Then I started coughing and wheezing like the Count himself, for the place was full of smoke. When it cleared my man was very dead, and I tipped him into the sea, to defile that rather than Faustina’s cave. And then﻿—and then﻿—we were alone for the last time, she and I, in our own pet haunt; and I could scarcely see her, yet I would not strike a match, for I knew she would not have me see her as she was. I could say goodbye to her without that. I said it; and I left her like a man, and up the first open-air steps with my head in the air and the stars all sharp in the sky; then suddenly they swam, and back I went like a lunatic, to see if she was really dead, to bring her back to life﻿ ﻿… Bunny, I can’t tell you any more.”

“Not of the Count?” I murmured at last.

“Not even of the Count,” said Raffles, turning round with a sigh. “I left him pretty sorry for himself; but what was the good of that? I had taken blood for blood, and it was not Corbucci who had killed Faustina. No, the plan was his, but that was not part of the plan. They had found out about our meetings in the cave: nothing simpler than to have me kept hard at it overhead and to carry off Faustina by brute force in the boat. It was their only chance, for she had said more to Stefano than she had admitted to me, and more than I am going to repeat about myself. No persuasion would have induced her to listen to him again; so they tried force; and she drew Corbucci’s revolver on them, but they had taken her by surprise, and Stefano stabbed her before she could fire.”

===Chinese===
“随后我也咳嗽、气喘起来，活像伯爵本人，因为洞里满是硝烟。烟散了，我那位先生已经死透，我便把他从洞口翻进海里——宁可玷污大海，也不玷污福斯蒂娜的洞窟。然后——然后——她和我，最后一次单独待在我们俩心爱的老地方；我几乎看不清她，可我不肯擦一根火柴，因为我知道，她不愿叫人看见她那副模样。不擦火柴，我也一样能同她道别。我道了别；我像个男子汉那样离开了她，走上最初那几级露天台阶，头颅高昂，满天星斗锐利如针；忽然它们游成了一片，我便像个疯子似的奔回去，去看她是不是真的死了，去把她救活……邦尼，我再也讲不下去了。”

“伯爵的事呢？”我终于嗫嚅道。

“连伯爵的事也不讲了。”拉夫尔斯叹口气转过身来。“我留他在那儿自怨自艾；可那有什么用处？血债我已用血偿了，而杀死福斯蒂娜的并不是科尔布奇。不，主意是他出的，可那不在主意之内。他们探知了我们在洞中的相会：再没有比这更省事的了——叫上头的我埋头苦干，再用那条驳船凭蛮力把福斯蒂娜掳走。那是他们仅有的机会，因为她对斯特凡诺说过的话，比她向我承认的多，也比我不打算转述的、关于我自己的话多。任凭什么劝诱，也休想叫她再听他一回；于是他们动了武；她拔出科尔布奇的左轮对准他们，可他们攻了个出其不意，她还没来得及扣扳机，斯特凡诺便捅了她。”

===Original===
“But how do you know all that?” I asked Raffles, for his tale was going to pieces in the telling, and the tragic end of poor Faustina was no ending for me.

“Oh,” said he, “I had it from Corbucci at his own revolver’s point. He was waiting at his window, and I could have potted him at my ease where he stood against the light listening hard enough but not seeing a thing. So he asked whether it was Stefano, and I whispered, ‘*Si, signore*’; and then whether he had finished Arturo, and I brought the same shot off again. He had let me in before he knew who was finished and who was not.”

“And did you finish him?”

===Chinese===
“可这些你又是怎么知道的？”我问拉夫尔斯，因为他的故事讲到后来已支离破碎，而可怜的福斯蒂娜的惨死，于我并不是一个了结。”

“哦，”他说，“我是拿他自己的左轮抵着他，从科尔布奇嘴里问来的。他正在窗口守着，背光站在那儿，竖直了耳朵也听不出名堂、更看不见什么，我本可以从从容容赏他一枪。他问是不是斯特凡诺，我低声答了声‘Si, signore’（是，老爷）；又问阿图罗了结了没有，我把同样的答复又原样放了一枪。他还不知道谁了结了谁，就先把我放了进去。”

“那么你把他了结了？”

===Original===
“No; that was too good for Corbucci. But I bound and gagged him about as tight as man was ever gagged or bound, and I left him in his room with the shutters shut and the house locked up. The shutters of that old place were six inches thick, and the walls nearly six feet; that was on the Saturday night, and the Count wasn’t expected at the vineyard before the following Saturday. Meanwhile he was supposed to be in Rome. But the dead would doubtless be discovered next day, and I am afraid this would lead to his own discovery with the life still in him. I believe he figured on that himself, for he sat threatening me gamely till the last. You never saw such a sight as he was, with his head split in two by a ruler tied at the back of it, and his great moustache pushed up into his bulging eyes. But I locked him up in the dark without a qualm, and I wished and still wish him every torment of the damned.”

“And then?”

“The night was still young, and within ten miles there was the best of ports in a storm, and hundreds of holds for the humble stowaway to choose from. But I didn’t want to go further than Genoa, for by this time my Italian would wash, so I chose the old Norddeutscher Lloyd, and had an excellent voyage in one of the boats slung inboard over the bridge. That’s better than any hold, Bunny, and I did splendidly on oranges brought from the vineyard.”

===Chinese===
“没有；那对科尔布奇来说未免太便宜。可我把他堵了个严实、捆了个结实，大约人有史以来被堵被捆到过什么份上，他就是什么份上；我把他留在他自己房里，百叶窗关死，宅门上锁。那座老宅的百叶窗有六英寸厚，墙差不多六英尺；那是星期六夜里，而伯爵按说要到下一个星期六才会回葡萄园。这期间，他照理人该在罗马。可那具尸体第二天多半会被发现，只怕连带便要把他本人也发现——身上还剩着一条命呢。我相信他自己也打着这个算盘，因为他硬撑着威胁我，一直撑到最后一刻。你没见过他那副尊容：脑袋被绑在后脑的一根尺子勒成两半，大胡子直竖进他暴凸的双眼。可我把他在黑地里一锁了之，毫不动心；我从前愿、如今仍愿，叫他把下地狱者的万般酷刑受个遍。”

“后来呢？”

“夜还早着呢，方圆十英里之内，有的是风暴里上上选的港口，也有几百个货舱，供谦卑的偷渡客从容挑拣。可我不想走得比热那亚（Genoa）更远，因为到这时我的意大利语已经拿得出手，我便挑了老牌的北德意志劳埃德（Norddeutscher Lloyd），在桥楼上向内悬吊的一艘救生艇里，舒舒服服过了一路。这比什么货舱都强，邦尼，我靠着从葡萄园带来的橙子，日子过得阔绰极了。”

===Original===
“And at Genoa?”

“At Genoa I took to my wits once more, and have been living on nothing else ever since. But there I had to begin all over again, and at the very bottom of the ladder. I slept in the streets. I begged. I did all manner of terrible things, rather hoping for a bad end, but never coming to one. Then one day I saw a white-headed old chap looking at me through a shopwindow﻿—a window I had designs upon﻿—and when I stared at him he stared at me﻿—and we wore the same rags. So I had come to that! But one reflection makes many. I had not recognized myself; who on earth would recognize me? London called me﻿—and here I am. Italy had broken my heart﻿—and there it stays.”

Flippant as a schoolboy one moment, playful even in the bitterness of the next, and now no longer giving way to the feeling which had spoilt the climax of his tale, Raffles needed knowing as I alone knew him for a right appreciation of those last words. That they were no mere words I know full well. That, but for the tragedy of his Italian life, that life would have sufficed him for years, if not forever, I did and do still believe. But I alone see him as I saw him then, the lines upon his face, and the pain behind the lines; how they came to disappear, and what removed them, you will never guess. It was the one thing you would have expected to have the opposite effect, the thing indeed that had forced his confidence, the organ and the voice once more beneath our very windows:

===Chinese===
“到了热那亚呢？”

“到了热那亚，我重新吃上了脑力的饭，从此也再没吃过别的。可在那儿，我不得不从头来过，而且从梯子最底下那一级来起。我睡街头。我讨饭。各式各样可怕的勾当我都干过，与其说怕落个坏下场，不如说巴望着它，可它始终不来。后来有一天，我看见一个白头发的老家伙隔着橱窗瞅我——那扇橱窗，我正打着它的主意——我瞪他，他也瞪我——我们俩穿着同一样的破烂。原来我已落到这般田地！不过，一回照影，能照出百般念头。连我自己都没认出自己来；普天之下还有谁认得出我？伦敦召唤我——我便回来了。意大利伤了我的心——它便留在那儿。”

一时轻佻得像个学童，一时纵在苦涩里也还要耍笑，如今又不再纵容那股断送了故事高潮的情绪——要掂量得准他最后这几句话，就得像我独有的那样了解拉夫尔斯。这几句话绝非虚文，我是深知底里的。若不是他那意大利岁月里的惨剧，那样的日子本够他消受许多年，甚至一生的——这一点，我当时信，如今仍然信。可只有我一人见过他当时的样子：满脸的沟壑，和沟壑背后的痛楚；这些沟壑是怎么消失的、又是什么把它们抹平的，你永远猜不着。那正是你以为必定适得其反的那样东西，也正是逼出他这番倾诉的那样东西——那架风琴和那副嗓子，又一次来到我们的窗根底下：

===Original===
“Margarita de Parete,
					

					era a’ sarta d’ e’ signore;
					

					se pugneva sempe e ddete
					

					pe penzare a Salvatore!

“Mar﻿—ga﻿—rì,
					

					e perzo e Salvatore!
					

					Mar﻿—ga﻿—rì,
					

					Ma l’ommo è cacciatore!
					

					Mar﻿—ga﻿—rì,
					

					Nun ce aje corpa tu!
					

					Chello ch’ è fatto, è fatto, un ne parlammo cchieù!”

I simply stared at Raffles. Instead of deepening, his lines had vanished. He looked years younger, mischievous and merry and alert as I remembered him of old in the breathless crisis of some madcap escapade. He was holding up his finger; he was stealing to the window; he was peeping through the blind as though our side street were Scotland Yard itself; he was stealing back again, all revelry, excitement, and suspense.

===Chinese===
“Margarita de Parete,

era a’ sarta d’ e’ signore;

se pugneva sempe e ddete

pe penzare a Salvatore!

“Mar—ga—rì,

e perzo e Salvatore!

Mar—ga—rì,

Ma l’ommo è cacciatore!

Mar—ga—rì,

Nun ce aje corpa tu!

Chello ch’ è fatto, è fatto, un ne parlammo cchieù!”（歌为那不勒斯土语，大意为：玛加丽塔·达帕雷特，是老爷们的裁缝；她一针针刺破手指，总在想着萨尔瓦托雷！玛加丽塔，失去了萨尔瓦托雷！玛加丽塔，男人天生是猎手！玛加丽塔，这怪不得你！木已成舟便是了，咱们再也休提！）

我只是直勾勾地望着拉夫尔斯。他的皱纹非但没有加深，反倒无影无踪。他看上去年轻了好几岁，淘气、快活、机警，恰像记忆里旧日的他——在某桩胡作非为的历险那屏息的紧要关头。他竖起一根手指；他蹑手蹑脚挨向窗口；他隔着百叶帘往外窥探，仿佛我们这条小街就是苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）本尊；他又蹑手蹑脚踅了回来，浑身是欢腾、兴奋与悬心。

===Original===
“I half thought they were after me before,” said he. “That was why I made you look. I daren’t take a proper look myself, but what a jest if they were! What a jest!”

“Do you mean the police?” said I.

“The police! Bunny, do you know them and me so little that you can look me in the face and ask such a question? My boy, I’m dead to them﻿—off their books﻿—a good deal deader than being off the hooks! Why, if I went to Scotland Yard this minute, to give myself up, they’d chuck me out for a harmless lunatic. No, I fear an enemy nowadays, and I go in terror of the sometime friend, but I have the utmost confidence in the dear police.”

===Chinese===
“方才我就疑心他们是冲着我来的，”他说。“所以才叫你去瞧。我自己不敢正经看一眼——可他们要真是冲我来的，那多妙！那多妙！”

“你是说警察？”我道。

“警察！邦尼，你对他们、对我竟这么不了解，还能看着我的脸问出这种话？小伙子，我在他们那儿早是个死人了——销籍除名了——比两腿一蹬还死得透彻几分！哼，我这会儿若是跑到苏格兰场去投案，他们准把我当个无害的疯子轰出来。不，如今我怕的是敌人，惶恐的是昔日故交，至于亲爱的警察，我对他们抱有十二分的信心。”

===Original===
“Then whom do you mean?”

“The Camorra!”

I repeated the word with a different intonation. Not that I had never heard of that most powerful and sinister of secret societies; but I failed to see on what grounds Raffles should jump to the conclusion that these everyday organ-grinders belonged to it.

===Chinese===
“那你指的是谁？”

“克莫拉（Camorra）！”

我换了一种声调重复了这个词。倒不是我从没听说过那个势力最盛、最为阴森的秘密会党；我只是看不出，拉夫尔斯凭什么一跃而断定这两个平平常常的摇琴匠是它的门下。

===Original===
“It was one of Corbucci’s threats,” said he. “If I killed him the Camorra would certainly kill me; he kept on telling me so; it was like his cunning not to say that he would put them on my tracks whether or no.”

“He is probably a member himself!”

“Obviously, from what he said.”

===Chinese===
“那是科尔布奇的威胁之一，”他说。“我若杀了他，克莫拉必定杀我；他翻来覆去对我这样讲；他的狡猾就在这里——不肯说破：不论我杀不杀他，他都要把他们引上我的行踪。”

“他自己八成就是会党里的人！”

“明摆着，单凭他那几句话。”

===Original===
“But why on earth should you think that these fellows are?” I demanded, as that brazen voice came rasping through a second verse.

“I don’t think. It was only an idea. That thing is so thoroughly Neapolitan, and I never heard it on a London organ before. Then again, what should bring them back here?”

I peeped through the blind in my turn; and, to be sure, there was the fellow with the blue chin and the white teeth watching our windows, and ours only, as he bawled.

===Chinese===
“可你到底凭什么认为这两个家伙也是？”我诘问道，那副黄铜嗓子正刮擦着吼出第二段。

“我并不认为。不过是个念头。那曲子彻头彻尾是那不勒斯的，我从前在伦敦的琴上从未听过。再有，是什么把他们引回这儿来的呢？”

轮到我隔帘窥看；可不是么，正是那个青下巴、白牙齿的家伙，一面吼着，一面盯着我家的窗口——盯的唯独我家的窗口。

===Original===
“And why?” cried Raffles, his eyes dancing when I told him. “Why should they come sneaking back to *us*? Doesn’t that look suspicious, Bunny; doesn’t that promise a lark?”

“Not to me,” I said, having the smile for once. “How many people, should you imagine, toss them five shilling for as many minutes of their infernal row? You seem to forget that’s what you did an hour ago!”

Raffles had forgotten. His blank face confessed the fact. Then suddenly he burst outlaughing at himself.

===Chinese===
“还有，为什么？”我告诉他后，拉夫尔斯双眼放光地嚷道。“他们为什么偏偏溜回我们这儿来？这不可疑吗，邦尼；这不预示着一场好戏吗？”

“我看未必，”我说，难得由我占了回笑的上风。“你且估估，有几个人肯拿五个先令，买他们那五分钟的魔鬼吵闹？你好像忘了，一个钟头之前干这事的正是你自己！”

拉夫尔斯果然忘了。他一脸的茫然招认了这一事实。随即他忽然冲着他自己放声大笑起来。

===Original===
“Bunny,” said he, “you’ve no imagination, and I never knew I had so much! Of course you’re right. I only wish you were not, for there’s nothing I should enjoy more than taking on another Neapolitan or two. You see, I owe them something still! I didn’t settle in full. I owe them more than ever I shall pay them on this side Styx!”

He had hardened even as he spoke: the lines and the years had come again, and his eyes were flint and steel, with an honest grief behind the glitter.

===Chinese===
“邦尼，”他说，“你没有想象力，我可从不知道自己有这么多！当然，你是对的。我只巴不得你错了才好，因为再没有比再会一会一两个那不勒斯人更叫我痛快的事了。你瞧，我还欠着他们一笔账呢！我没有结清。欠下的，叫我在这斯提克斯河（Styx）此岸，一辈子也还不清！”

他说话的当口便又硬了起来：皱纹与岁月重新爬了回来，他的眼睛成了燧石与钢铁，闪光背后藏着一泓沉痛的哀伤。

## The Last Laugh / 最后一笑

===Original===
As I have had occasion to remark elsewhere, the pick of our exploits, from a frankly criminal point of view, are of least use for the comparatively pure purposes of these papers. They might be appreciated in a trade journal (if only that want could be supplied), by skilled manipulators of the jemmy and the large light bunch; but, as records of unbroken yet insignificant success, they would be found at once too trivial and too technical, if not sordid and unprofitable into the bargain. The latter epithets, and worse, have indeed already been applied, if not to Raffles and all his works, at least to mine upon Raffles, by more than one worthy wielder of a virtuous pen. I need not say how heartily I disagree with that truly pious opinion. So far from admitting a single word of it, I maintain it is the liveliest warning that I am giving to the world. Raffles was a genius, and he could not make it pay! Raffles had invention, resource, incomparable audacity, and a nerve in ten thousand. He was both strategian and tactician, and we all now know the difference between the two. Yet for months he had been hiding like a rat in a hole, unable to show even his altered face by night or day without risk, unless another risk were courted by three inches of conspicuous crepe. Then thus far our rewards had oftener than not been no reward at all. Altogether it was a very different story from the old festive, unsuspected, club and cricket days, with their *noctes ambrosianae* at the Albany.

And now, in addition to the eternal peril of recognition, there was yet another menace of which I knew nothing. I thought no more of our Neapolitan organ-grinders, though I did often think of the moving page that they had torn for me out of my friend’s strange life in Italy. Raffles never alluded to the subject again, and for my part I had entirely forgotten his wild ideas connecting the organ-grinders with the Camorra, and imagining them upon his own tracks. I heard no more of it, and thought as little, as I say. Then one night in the autumn﻿—I shrink from shocking the susceptible for nothing﻿—but there was a certain house in Palace Gardens, and when we got there Raffles would pass on. I could see no soul in sight, no glimmer in the windows. But Raffles had my arm, and on we went without talking about it. Sharp to the left on the Notting Hill side, sharper still up Silver Street, a little tacking west and south, a plunge across High Street, and presently we were home.

===Chinese===
正如我在别处有机会说过的：单以坦白的罪犯眼光来看，我们那些冒险中的精华之作，对这几篇文字较为纯正的宗旨而言，恰恰最派不上用场。它们或许能在某本行业期刊上博得赏识（但愿真能有这份刊物问世），供操弄撬棍与轻巧大串钥匙的行家里手品评；然而，作为一份从未失手却无关宏旨的成功记录，它们立刻就会显得既太琐碎又太专门，甚而至于兼有卑污与无利可图之弊。后面这两条评语，以及更难听的字眼，其实早已有人奉送——即便不是送给拉夫尔斯（Raffles）及其全部作为，至少也是送给我笔下关于拉夫尔斯的文字——出手的不止一位高擎道德之笔的君子。不必说，我对这一真正虔诚的高见何等由衷地不敢苟同。我非但不认其中一个字，反而坚称：我此刻给予世人的，乃是最鲜活的一记警世之钟。拉夫尔斯是天才，而天才尚且无利可图！拉夫尔斯有机变、有巧思、有无可比拟的大胆、有万中无一的胆魄。他兼为战略家与战术家，而如今我们都知道这两者有何区别了。然而一连数月，他却像洞中之鼠般东躲西藏，那张改头换面的脸无论昼夜都不敢露一露，除非甘冒另一种风险，拿三英寸扎眼的黑纱招摇。再者，我们此后的进项，往往干脆分文全无。总而言之，这同旧日那种佳节般无忧无虑的俱乐部与板球岁月，同在奥尔巴尼（Albany）的那些*琼浆夜话*（*noctes ambrosianae*），已是全然两回事了。

而眼下，除了被认出的万世不易之险，又添了一重我却一无所知的祸患。我们那两个那不勒斯（Naples）手摇风琴师，我不再多想；只是他们从我朋友在意大利的那段奇诡生涯里替我撕下的动人一页，我倒时常想起。拉夫尔斯此后再未触及这个话题，而就我而言，他那些把手摇风琴师与克莫拉（Camorra）牵到一处、幻想他们已盯上自己踪迹的奇想，我早忘得干干净净。正像我说的，我再没听人提起，也就不再去想。直到秋天的一夜——我素来不肯平白惊吓易受惊的诸君——那晚帕莱斯花园（Palace Gardens）有一座宅子，我们行至其处，拉夫尔斯却径直往前走。我四下望不见一个人影，窗内也不见一丝灯光。可拉夫尔斯挽住我的手臂，我们便一声不响地继续前行。到诺丁山（Notting Hill）一侧猛地向左一拐，进西尔弗街（Silver Street）更是急转直上，再向西向南略作迂回，一跃横过高街（High Street），不多时我们便到了家。

===Original===
“Pyjamas first,” said Raffles, with as much authority as though it mattered. It was a warm night, however, though September, and I did not mind until I came in clad as he commanded to find the autocrat himself still booted and capped. He was peeping through the blind, and the gas was still turned down. But he said that I could turn it up, as he helped himself to a cigarette and nothing with it.

“May I mix you one?” said I.

“No, thanks.”

“What’s the trouble?”

===Chinese===
“先换睡衣。”拉夫尔斯发话道，那口吻俨然此事干系重大。不过九月之夜毕竟温热，我并不在意，直到遵命换装出来，才发现这位独裁君王本人依旧靴帽齐全。他正从百叶窗帘缝里向外窥探，煤气灯也还压得低低的。但他说我可以把灯拧亮，一面自顾自取了一支香烟，此外什么也不要。

“我给你调一杯？”我说。

“不必，谢了。”

“出什么事了？”

===Original===
“We were followed.”

“Never!”

“You never saw it.”

“But *you* never looked round.”

===Chinese===
“我们被人盯上了。”

“不会吧！”

“你是没瞧见罢了。”

“可你*分明*也没回头看一眼啊。”

===Original===
“I have an eye at the back of each ear, Bunny.”

I helped myself and I fear with less moderation than might have been the case a minute before.

“So that was why﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我每只耳朵背后还各长着一只眼睛呢，邦尼（Bunny）。”

我也自斟了一杯，而且恐怕比一分钟前该有的分寸少了几分节制。

“原来方才那是为了——”

===Original===
“That was why,” said Raffles, nodding; but he did not smile, and I put down my glass untouched.

“They were following us then!”

“All up Palace Gardens.”

===Chinese===
“正是为了这个。”拉夫尔斯点头道；可他并没有笑，我便把自己那杯原封未动地放下了。

“这么说，他们方才真是在跟踪我们！”

“一路跟到帕莱斯花园顶头。”

===Original===
“I thought you wound about coming back over the hill.”

“Nevertheless, one of them’s in the street below at this moment.”

No, he was not fooling me. He was very grim. And he had not taken off a thing; perhaps he did not think it worth while.

===Chinese===
“我还以为你回程绕着山坡兜了圈子呢。”

“尽管如此，此刻他们中的一位就站在楼下街上。”

不，他不是在同我玩笑。他神色极为冷峻。而且他一样东西也没脱；或许是觉得不值当吧。

===Original===
“Plain clothes?” I sighed, following the sartorial train of thought, even to the loathly arrows that had decorated my person once already for a little aeon. Next time they would give me double. The skilly was in my stomach when I saw Raffles’s face.

“Who said it was the police, Bunny?” said he. “It’s the Italians. They’re only after me; they won’t hurt a hair of *your* head, let alone cropping it! Have a drink, and don’t mind me. I shall score them off before I’m done.”

===Chinese===
“便衣？”我叹了口气，顺着衣装的思路一路想下去，甚至想到那曾在我身上装点过一小段永恒的可憎箭纹（囚服标记）。下一回，他们就要给我来个加倍了。待我看清拉夫尔斯的脸色，牢里的稀粥已经在我胃里翻涌。

“谁说是警察了，邦尼？”他说，“是意大利人。他们只冲着我来；他们动不了你*脑袋上的*一根头发，更别说把你剃成囚头了！喝你的酒，别管我。不等我把事情办完，我定要赢他们一回。”

===Original===
“And I’ll help you!”

“No, old chap, you won’t. This is my own little show. I’ve known about it for weeks. I first tumbled to it the day those Neapolitans came back with their organs, though I didn’t seriously suspect things then; they never came again, those two, they had done their part. That’s the Camorra all over, from all accounts. The Count I told you about is pretty high up in it, by the way he spoke, but there will be grades and grades between him and the organ-grinders. I shouldn’t be surprised if he had every low-down Neapolitan ice-creamer in the town upon my tracks! The organization’s incredible. Then do you remember the superior foreigner who came to the door a few days afterwards? You said he had velvet eyes.”

===Chinese===
“那我来助你！”

“不行，老伙计，你帮不上。这是我个人的一台小戏。事情我已知晓好几个星期了。头一回起疑，是那两个那不勒斯人带着风琴回来那天，不过当时还没当真疑心；那两位再没有来过，他们那一班的岗已经站完了。据各方传闻，这十足是克莫拉的做派。我同你说起过的那位伯爵，听他口气，在里面地位相当之高，只是他与手摇风琴师之间还隔着一级又一级。倘若他把全城每一个下等的那不勒斯冰淇淋贩子都撒到我的踪迹上，我也不会吃惊！这组织之严密，令人难以置信。还有，你可记得几天之后登门的那位气派不凡的外国人？你说他有一双天鹅绒般的眼睛。”

===Original===
“I never connected him with those two!”

“Of course you didn’t, Bunny, so you threatened to kick the fellow downstairs, and only made them keener on the scent. It was too late to say anything when you told me. But the very next time I showed my nose outside I heard a camera click as I passed, and the fiend was a person with velvet eyes. Then there was a lull﻿—that happened weeks ago. They had sent me to Italy for identification by Count Corbucci.”

===Chinese===
“我压根没把他同那两位扯到一处！”

“你当然没有，邦尼，所以你还扬言要把那家伙踢下楼去，结果只是叫他们嗅得更起劲。等你告诉我时，再说什么也晚了。可我下一次把鼻子探出门外，走过时便听得照相机咔哒一响，那魔鬼正是那位天鹅绒眼睛的主儿。随后是一段沉寂——那是几个星期前的事。他们曾把我的影像送往意大利，交科尔布奇伯爵（Count Corbucci）验明正身。”

===Original===
“But this is all theory,” I exclaimed. “How on earth can you know?”

“I don’t know,” said Raffles, “but I should like to bet. Our friend the bloodhound is hanging about the corner near the pillar-box; look through my window, it’s dark in there, and tell me who he is.”

===Chinese===
“可这些全是凭空推想，”我失声道，“你究竟从何得知？”

“我并不知晓，”拉夫尔斯说，“但我愿意为此一赌。咱们那位猎犬朋友正在邮筒近旁的街角一带徘徊；从我这扇窗望出去——屋里黑，你来看看他是谁。”

===Original===
The man was too far away for me to swear to his face, but he wore a covert-coat of un-English length, and the lamp across the road played steadily on his boots; they were very yellow, and they made no noise when he took a turn. I strained my eyes, and all at once I remembered the thin-soled, low-heeled, splay yellow boots of the insidious foreigner, with the soft eyes and the brown-paper face, whom I had turned from the door as a palpable fraud. The ring at the bell was the first I had heard of him, there had been no warning step upon the stairs, and my suspicious eye had searched his feet for rubber soles.

“It’s the fellow,” I said, returning to Raffles, and I described his boots.

===Chinese===
那人离得太远，我无法对他的相貌打包票，但他穿一件长得不像英国尺寸的暗扣猎装大衣，街对面的路灯稳稳地照着他的靴子；那靴子黄得扎眼，他踱步转身时竟不发出一点声响。我眯细双眼，猛然记起那个阴险的外国人——柔软的眼睛、牛皮纸色的脸——那双薄底、矮跟、外撇的黄靴子；此人正是我当作明摆着的骗子轰出门去的那一个。当时门铃一响我才头回听说有他这么个人，楼梯上并无预警的脚步声，我那双起疑的眼睛还专门搜过他的鞋底有没有胶皮。

“就是那家伙。”我回到拉夫尔斯身边说，并把他那双靴子描述了一番。

===Original===
Raffles was delighted.

“Well done, Bunny; you’re coming on,” said he. “Now I wonder if he’s been over here all the time, or if they sent him over expressly? You did better than you think in spotting those boots, for they can only have been made in Italy, and that looks like the special envoy. But it’s no use speculating. I must find out.”

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯喜形于色。

“好样的，邦尼；你有长进了。”他说，“如今我倒纳闷，他是一直待在此地呢，还是他们特意专程遣来的？你认出那双靴子，功劳比你自以为的还大，因为它们只可能是意大利的出品，这一来倒像是一位特派专使了。不过瞎猜无益，我必须查个水落石出。”

===Original===
“How can you?”

“He won’t stay there all night.”

“Well?”

“When he gets tired of it I shall return the compliment and follow *him*.”

===Chinese===
“你怎么查？”

“他总不至于在那儿站一整夜。”

“然后呢？”

“等他站腻了，我便礼尚往来，反过来盯*他*的梢。”

===Original===
“Not alone,” said I, firmly.

“Well, we’ll see. We’ll see at once,” said Raffles, rising. “Out with the gas, Bunny, while I take a look. Thank you. Now wait a bit﻿ ﻿… yes! He’s chucked it; he’s off already; and so am I!”

But I slipped to our outer door, and held the passage.

===Chinese===
“不许你单独去。”我斩钉截铁地说。

“这个嘛，到时再说。现在就说。”拉夫尔斯起身道，“把灯关了，邦尼，我看一眼。谢谢。再等等……好！他撂挑子了；人已经走了；我也就动身！”

可我一闪身抢到大门跟前，堵住了过道。

===Original===
“I don’t let you go alone, you know.”

“You can’t come with me in pyjamas.”

“Now I see why you made me put them on!”

===Chinese===
“你知道，我不能让你独自去。”

“你总不能穿着睡衣跟我去吧。”

“这下我明白你为什么叫我换上睡衣了！”

===Original===
“Bunny, if you don’t shift I shall have to shift you. This is my very own private one-man show. But I’ll be back in an hour﻿—there!”

“You swear?”

“By all my gods.”

===Chinese===
“邦尼，你要是不自己挪窝，我就只好亲手挪你了。这是我纯属一人独享的私人戏码。不过我一小时准回来——就这样！”

“你起誓？”

“凭我所有的神明起誓。”

===Original===
I gave in. How could I help giving in? He did not look the man that he had been, but you never knew with Raffles, and I could not have him lay a hand on me. I let him go with a shrug and my blessing, then ran into his room to see the last of him from the window.

The creature in the coat and boots had reached the end of our little street, where he appeared to have hesitated, so that Raffles was just in time to see which way he turned. And Raffles was after him at an easy pace, and had himself almost reached the corner when my attention was distracted from the alert nonchalance of his gait. I was marvelling that it alone had not long ago betrayed him, for nothing about him was so unconsciously characteristic, when suddenly I realized that Raffles was not the only person in the little lonely street. Another pedestrian had entered from the other end, a man heavily built and clad, with an astrakhan collar to his coat on this warm night, and a black slouch hat that hid his features from my bird’s-eye view. His steps were the short and shuffling ones of a man advanced in years and in fatty degeneration, but of a sudden they stopped beneath my very eyes. I could have dropped a marble into the dinted crown of the black felt hat. Then, at the same moment, Raffles turned the corner without looking round, and the big man below raised both his hands and his face. Of the latter I saw only the huge white moustache, like a flying gull, as Raffles had described it; for at a glance I divined that this was his arch-enemy, the Count Corbucci himself.

===Chinese===
我让步了。我又怎能让不了步？他看上去已不是当年那条汉子，可拉夫尔斯这人你永远拿不准，况且我不能容他对我动手。我耸耸肩，祝了他一声福，放他出门，随即奔进他的房间，好从窗口送他最后一程。

那个大衣加黄靴的人已走到我们这条小街的尽头，在那里似乎迟疑了一下，拉夫尔斯正好赶上看见他拐向哪一边。拉夫尔斯便迈着从容的步子跟了上去，自己也几乎走到街角，我的注意力才从他步态间那份警觉的闲适里分了神。我正惊叹单是这步态竟没早早出卖他——他身上再没有比这更浑然不觉、更具个人印记的东西了——忽然省悟到，这条冷清的小街上并非只有拉夫尔斯一人。另有行人从另一头进来，是个身量与衣着都同样厚重的人，这样温热的夜晚，大衣上却缀着阿斯特拉罕羔皮领，一顶黑色垂檐软帽，遮住了他在我这鸟瞰视野里的面目。他的步子短促而拖沓，是上了年纪又添了肥胖退化之人的步子，可忽然间，那步子就在我眼皮底下停住了。我简直能往那顶黑毡帽瘪陷的帽顶里丢进一颗弹珠。也就在同一刻，拉夫尔斯头也不回地拐过街角，楼下那个大块头扬起了双手、抬起了脸。那张脸我只看见一把雪白的大胡子，宛如一只展翅的海鸥，正如拉夫尔斯描述过的那样；因为我一眼便料定，这正是他的死敌，科尔布奇伯爵本人。

===Original===
I did not stop to consider the subtleties of the system by which the real hunter lagged behind while his subordinate pointed the quarry like a sporting dog. I left the Count shuffling onward faster than before, and I jumped into some clothes as though the flats were on fire. If the Count was going to follow Raffles in his turn, then I would follow the Count in mine, and there would be a midnight procession of us through the town. But I found no sign of him in the empty street, and no sign in the Earl’s Court Road, that looked as empty for all its length, save for a natural enemy standing like a waxwork figure with a glimmer at his belt.

“Officer,” I gasped, “have you seen anything of an old gentleman with a big white mustache?”

===Chinese===
我没有停下来推敲这套章法的精微——真正的猎手缀在后面，由下属像猎犬似的指点猎物。我撇下步子比先前更急促的伯爵，一头扎进衣服堆里，仿佛整片公寓都着了火。倘若伯爵接下来要去跟踪拉夫尔斯，那么我便去跟踪伯爵，我们这一行人马就要在午夜列队穿城而过了。可空荡荡的街上不见他的踪影，厄尔斯考特路（Earl’s Court Road）也一眼望到头都是空的，只剩一个天敌似的角色立在路边，蜡像一般，腰间一点微光。

“警官，”我喘着气说，“您可曾见过一位留着大白胡子的老先生？”

===Original===
The unlicked cub of a common constable seemed to eye me the more suspiciously for the flattering form of my address.

“Took a hansom,” said he at length.

A hansom! Then he was not following the others on foot; there was no guessing his game. But something must be said or done.

“He’s a friend of mine,” I explained, “and I want to overtake him. Did you hear where he told the fellow to drive?”

===Chinese===
那个还没调教成人的巡警崽子，似乎正因为我这称呼奉承得体面，反而愈发狐疑地打量我。

“坐双轮马车走了。”他终于说。

双轮马车（hansom）！这么说，他并非徒步缀着那两位；他的算盘无从猜起。可总得说点什么、做点什么。

“他是我的一位朋友，”我解释道，“我想追上他。您可听见他吩咐车夫往哪里赶了？”

===Original===
A curt negative was the policeman’s reply to that; and if ever I take part in a night assault-at-arms, revolver versus baton, in the back kitchen, I know which member of the Metropolitan Police Force I should like for my opponent.

If there was no overtaking the Count, however, it should be a comparatively simple matter in the case of the couple on foot, and I wildly hailed the first hansom that crawled into my ken. I must tell Raffles who it was that I had seen; the Earl’s Court Road was long, and the time since he vanished in it but a few short minutes. I drove down the length of that useful thoroughfare, with an eye apiece on either pavement, sweeping each as with a brush, but never a Raffles came into the pan. Then I tried the Fulham Road, first to the west, then to the east, and in the end drove home to the flat as bold as brass.
