And I let him have his way, I will not say as usual, for I had even less than my normal power of resistance that night. That villainous uppercut! My head still sang and throbbed, as I seated myself on one of the aforesaid parapets, and buried it in my hot hands. Nor was the night one to dispel a headache; there was distinct thunder in the air. Thus I sat in a heap, and brooded over my misadventure, a pretty figure of a subordinate villain, until the step came for which I waited; and it never struck me that it came from the wrong direction.

“You have been quick,” said I, simply.

===Chinese===
“那咱们就不下去，”拉夫尔斯当机立断；“就在上头松快一会儿。不，邦尼，你待在原地别动！我去给你拿杯喝的、一把帆布躺椅，你缓过劲儿之前不许下来。”

我由他去了——我不肯说“照例”二字，因为那晚我连平日的抵抗力都没有。那记歹毒的上勾拳！我坐上上述的一道井沿，把那颗还在嗡嗡作痛的脑袋埋进滚烫的双手。这样的夜也断然化不开头疼；空中的雷声已然分明。我便这样蜷作一团坐着，闷想自己的倒霉遭遇，俨然一幅二流恶棍的体面小像，直到我等候的那阵脚步声响起；而它竟来自相反的方向，这一层我当时压根没想到。

“你回来得真快。”我平平地说。

===Original===
“Yes,” hissed a voice I recognized; “and you’ve got to be quicker still! Here, out with your wrists; no, one at a time; and if you utter a syllable you’re a dead man.”

It was Lord Ernest Belville; his close-cropped, iron-gray moustache gleamed through the darkness, drawn up over his set teeth. In his hand glittered a pair of handcuffs, and before I knew it one had snapped its jaws about my right wrist.

“Now come this way,” said Lord Ernest, showing me a revolver also, “and wait for your friend. And, recollect, a single syllable of warning will be your death!”

===Chinese===
“是吗，”一个我认得的嗓音嘶嘶作响；“那你还得更快些！来，手腕伸出来；不，一只一只来；你敢出半个音节，你就是个死人。”

来者是欧内斯特·贝尔维尔勋爵；黑暗里看得见他剪得极短的铁灰髭须，随着咬紧的牙关向上掀起。他手里闪着一副手铐，我还不知就里，一只铐环已咬住我的右腕。

“现在往这边来。”欧内斯特勋爵说着，又给我亮了一支左轮枪，“等着你那位朋友。记住，半声警告便是你的死期！”

===Original===
With that the ruffian led me to the very bridge I had just crossed at Raffles’s heels, and handcuffed me to the iron rail midway across the chasm. It no longer felt warm to my touch, but icy as the blood in all my veins.

So this highborn hypocrite had beaten us at our game and his, and Raffles had met his match at last! That was the most intolerable thought, that Raffles should be down in the flat on my account, and that I could not warn him of his impending fate; for how was it possible without making such an outcry as should bring the mansions about our ears? And there I shivered on that wretched plank, chained like Andromeda to the rock, with a black infinity above and below; and before my eyes, now grown familiar with the peculiar darkness, stood Lord Ernest Belville, waiting for Raffles to emerge with full hands and unsuspecting heart! Taken so horribly unawares, even Raffles must fall an easy prey to a desperado in resource and courage scarcely second to himself, but one whom he had fatally underrated from the beginning. Not that I paused to think how the thing had happened; my one concern was for what was to happen next.

And what did happen was worse than my worst foreboding, for first a light came flickering into the sort of companion-hatch at the head of the stairs, and finally Raffles﻿—in his shirtsleeves! He was not only carrying a candle to put the finishing touch to him as a target; he had dispensed with coat and waistcoat downstairs, and was at once full-handed and unarmed.

===Chinese===
说罢这恶徒把我领到我方才才尾随拉夫尔斯走过的那道小桥上，就深渊正中，把我铐在了铁栏杆上。它摸上去再不温热，而是冰冷，同我浑身的血一般。

如此说来，这位出身高贵的伪君子，在他的行当和我们的行当里都赢了咱们，拉夫尔斯到底遇上了对手！最叫人忍无可忍的念头是：拉夫尔斯正为了我待在楼下的公寓里，而我无法向他示警，让他知道悬在头顶的命运；因为除非大呼小叫、招得整座大厦涌到我们耳边，又如何示得了警？我便在那条倒霉的木板上哆嗦，像锁在礁石上的安德洛墨达（Andromeda），上下都是黑色的无穷；在我眼前——我的眼睛这时已看惯了这份独特的黑暗——站着欧内斯特·贝尔维尔勋爵，专候拉夫尔斯双手满满、心怀不疑地走出来！这样骇人的措手不及，即便是拉夫尔斯，也必定成为一头在机智与胆魄上仅逊于他本人的亡命徒的轻易猎物——而这人，他从一开始便致命地低估了。我倒不曾停下来细想事情如何至此；我一心只在接下来会发生什么。

而实际发生的，比我最坏的预感还坏：先是一点火光摇摇曳曳地进了楼梯口那座舱口似的格子间，随后便是拉夫尔斯——只穿着衬衫！他不但手里擎着一支蜡烛，把自己作靶子的成色又添了最后一笔；他在楼下索性连上装和马甲都脱了，此刻双手满满的，又全然赤手空拳。

===Original===
“Where are you, old chap?” he cried, softly, himself blinded by the light he carried; and he advanced a couple of steps towards Belville. “This isn’t you, is it?”

And Raffles stopped, his candle held on high, a folding chair under the other arm.

“No, I am not your friend,” replied Lord Ernest, easily; “but kindly remain standing exactly where you are, and don’t lower that candle an inch, unless you want your brains blown into the street.”

Raffles said never a word, but for a moment did as he was bid; and the unshaken flame of the candle was testimony alike to the stillness of the night and to the finest set of nerves in Europe. Then, to my horror, he coolly stooped, placing candle and chair on the leads, and his hands in his pockets, as though it were but a popgun that covered him.

===Chinese===
“你在哪儿，老伙计？”他轻声喊道，自己叫手中的光晃得看不见东西；他朝贝尔维尔的方向走了两步。“这不是你吧，是吗？”

拉夫尔斯站住了，蜡烛高举，另一只胳膊底下夹着一把折叠椅。

“不，我不是你的朋友。”欧内斯特勋爵从容答道；“不过劳驾，你站的地方一步也别动，那蜡烛一寸也别放低——除非你想让你的脑浆溅到街上去。”

拉夫尔斯一言不发，倒也照吩咐立了片刻；那支纹丝不晃的烛焰，既见证了夜的沉静，也见证了全欧洲最好的一副神经。随后，令我惊骇的是，他若无其事地弯下腰，把蜡烛和椅子搁在铅皮顶上，双手插进口袋，仿佛罩着他的是一杆玩具气枪。

===Original===
“Why didn’t you shoot?” he asked insolently as he rose. “Frightened of the noise? I should be, too, with an old-pattern machine like that. All very well for service in the field﻿—but on the housetops at dead of night!”

“I shall shoot, however,” replied Lord Ernest, as quietly in his turn, and with less insolence, “and chance the noise, unless you instantly restore my property. I am glad you don’t dispute the last word,” he continued after a slight pause. “There is no keener honor than that which subsists, or ought to subsist, among thieves; and I need hardly say that I soon spotted you as one of the fraternity. Not in the beginning, mind you! For the moment I did think you were one of these smart detectives jumped to life from some sixpenny magazine; but to preserve the illusion you ought to provide yourself with a worthier lieutenant. It was he who gave your show away,” chuckled the wretch, dropping for a moment the affected style of speech which seemed intended to enhance our humiliation; “smart detectives don’t go about with little innocents to assist them. You needn’t be anxious about him, by the way; it wasn’t necessary to pitch him into the street; he is to be seen though not heard, if you look in the right direction. Nor must you put all the blame upon your friend; it was not he, but you, who made so sure that I had got out by the window. You see, I was in my bathroom all the time﻿—with the door open.”

===Chinese===
“你怎么不开枪？”他直起身来，出言不逊。“怕响声？换了那杆老古董，我也会怕。在战场上凑合还使得——可在这深更半夜的房顶上！”

“不过我会开的。”欧内斯特勋爵答道，这回轮到他心平气和，火气也小了些，“响声嘛，听天由命——除非你立刻归还我的财物。我很高兴你没来争辩最后一个字，”他略停了停，接着说，“天底下再没有比贼与贼之间那份——或者说理应存在的——道义更精细的了；不消说，我很快便认出你是同行。起初倒没有，请注意！有一阵我真当你是哪本六便士杂志里跳出来的精明侦探；只是要维持这个幻觉，你该给自己配一位更像样的副手。坏就坏在那位副手身上。”那恶棍低声笑着，暂时卸下了那套似乎专为加重我们羞辱而设的拿腔作调，“精明侦探出门，可不带小天真当帮手。顺带说一句，你不必替他担心；犯不着把他扔到街上去；朝对的方向看，看得见人，听不见声。你也不可把罪名全推在你朋友身上；咬定我是从窗户出去的，不是他，是你自己。你瞧，我一直在我那浴室里——门还开着。”

===Original===
“The bathroom, eh?” Raffles echoed with professional interest. “And you followed us on foot across the park?”

“Of course.”

“And then in a cab?”

“And afterwards on foot once more.”

===Chinese===
“浴室，呃？”拉夫尔斯带着行家里手的兴味应道。“然后你就步行跟着我们穿过了公园？”

“当然。”

“再后来坐马车？”

“再后来又步行。”

===Original===
“The simplest skeleton would let you in down below.”

I saw the lower half of Lord Ernest’s face grinning in the light of the candle set between them on the ground.

“You follow every move,” said he; “there can be no doubt you are one of the fraternity; and I shouldn’t wonder if we had formed our style upon the same model. Ever know A. J. Raffles?”

===Chinese===
“最简易的一把万能钥匙，就能让你从楼下开门进来。”

我看见欧内斯特勋爵那张脸的下半截，在两人之间置于地上的烛光里咧着笑。

“着着都看得出。”他说；“毫无疑问，你是同道中人；咱们俩的路数若说出自同一个范本，我也不奇怪。认得 A. J. 拉夫尔斯（A. J. Raffles）吗？”

===Original===
The abrupt question took my breath away; but Raffles himself did not lose an instant over his answer.

“Intimately,” said he.

“That accounts for you, then,” laughed Lord Ernest, “as it does for me, though I never had the honor of the master’s acquaintance. Nor is it for me to say which is the worthier disciple. Perhaps, however, now that your friend is handcuffed in midair, and you yourself are at my mercy, you will concede me some little temporary advantage?”

And his face split in another grin from the cropped moustache downward, as I saw no longer by candlelight but by a flash of lightning which tore the sky in two before Raffles could reply.

===Chinese===
这劈头一问惊得我屏住了气；拉夫尔斯本人却一瞬也不耽搁他的回答。

“深交。”他说。

“这就说得通了。”欧内斯特勋爵笑道，“于你于我都是——虽然我始终无缘识荆那位大师。至于哪一位算更配的门徒，更轮不到我来置喙。不过，眼下你的朋友吊在半空，你自己也在我掌心里，你总肯承认，我暂时略占几分便宜了吧？”

他的脸又从剪短的髭须往下裂开一付笑容；此景我已不是借烛光看见，而是借一道闪电——它在拉夫尔斯还不及作答之前，把天空撕成了两半。

===Original===
“You have the bulge at present,” admitted Raffles; “but you have still to lay hands upon your, or our, ill-gotten goods. To shoot me is not necessarily to do so; to bring either one of us to a violent end is only to court a yet more violent and infinitely more disgraceful one for yourself. Family considerations alone should rule that risk out of your game. Now, an hour or two ago, when the exact opposite﻿—”

The remainder of Raffles’s speech was drowned from my ears by the belated crash of thunder which the lightning had foretold. So loud, however, was the crash when it came, that the storm was evidently approaching us at a high velocity; yet as the last echo rumbled away, I heard Raffles talking as though he had never stopped.

“You offered us a share,” he was saying; “unless you mean to murder us both in cold blood, it will be worth your while to repeat that offer. We should be dangerous enemies; you had far better make the best of us as friends.”

===Chinese===
“眼下你占着上风。”拉夫尔斯承认；“可你的——或者说我们的——不义之财，你还没到手呢。打死我并不等于拿到它；叫我二人任何一个横死，无非是给你自己招来一场更横、而且更不体面得无限倍的下场。单凭为家门着想，这一注风险也该从你的牌里剔出去。就说一两个钟头以前吧，那时情形恰恰相反——”

拉夫尔斯这番话的后半，被闪电预告过的那声迟到的雷霆从我耳边淹没了。然而那声雷既如此之响，风暴显然正高速向我们逼近；可当最后的回声隆隆散尽，我又听见拉夫尔斯谈锋如故，仿佛从未中断。

“你方才提过分成。”他正说着；“除非你打算把我们都冷血谋杀，重提一次那个提议，于你有百利而无一害。我们做敌人危险得很；你大可以把我们当朋友善加利用。”

===Original===
“Lead the way down to your flat,” said Lord Ernest, with a flourish of his service revolver, “and perhaps we may talk about it. It is for me to make the terms, I imagine, and in the first place I am not going to get wet to the skin up here.”

The rain was beginning in great drops, even as he spoke, and by a second flash of lightning I saw Raffles pointing to me.

“But what about my friend?” said he.

And then came the second peal.

===Chinese===
“头前带路，去你们的公寓。”欧内斯特勋爵说着，潇洒地一挥他那支军用左轮，“这事或许可以从长计议。我想，条件得由我来开；头一条，我可不想在这儿淋成落汤鸡。”

就在他说话的当口，豆大的雨点已然落下，借着第二道闪电，我看见拉夫尔斯指了指我。

“可我的朋友怎么办？”他说。

紧接着第二声雷炸响。

===Original===
“Oh, *he’s* all right,” the great brute replied; “do him good! You don’t catch me letting myself in for two to one!”

“You will find it equally difficult,” rejoined Raffles, “to induce me to leave my friend to the mercy of a night like this. He has not recovered from the blow you struck him in your own rooms. I am not such a fool as to blame you for that, but you are a worse sportsman than I take you for if you think of leaving him where he is. If he stays, however, so do I.”

And, just as it ceased, Raffles’s voice seemed distinctly nearer to me; but in the darkness and the rain, which was now as heavy as hail, I could see nothing clearly. The rain had already extinguished the candle. I heard an oath from Belville, a laugh from Raffles, and for a second that was all. Raffles was coming to me, and the other could not even see to fire; that was all I knew in the pitchy interval of invisible rain before the next crash and the next flash.

===Chinese===
“哦，*他*没事。”那大块头恶棍答道；“淋淋对他有益！你可别想叫我自讨苦头，弄成一挑二！”

“你同样休想，”拉夫尔斯回敬道，“叫我丢下朋友，任凭这样一夜摆布。他在你自己房里挨的那一拳还没缓过来。我没有蠢到为那件事怪你；可你若当真打算由他待在原地，那你这运动家就比我认定的更差劲。不过他若留下，我也留下。”

雷声方歇，拉夫尔斯的嗓音听来分明已离我更近；可在黑暗里，在如今密如冰雹的豪雨里，我什么也看不清。蜡烛早叫雨浇灭了。我听见贝尔维尔一声咒骂，拉夫尔斯一声轻笑，有一秒钟工夫，仅此而已。拉夫尔斯正朝我来，而那一位连瞄准开枪都看不清；在下一声霹雳、下一道闪光之前那一段墨黑的无形雨幕里，我知道的仅止于此。

===Original===
And then!

This time they came together, and not till my dying hour shall I forget the sight that the lightning lit and the thunder applauded. Raffles was on one of the parapets of the gulf that my footbridge spanned, and in the sudden illumination he stepped across it as one might across a garden path. The width was scarcely greater, but the depth! In the sudden flare I saw to the concrete bottom of the well, and it looked no larger than the hollow of my hand. Raffles was laughing in my ear; he had the iron railing fast; it was between us, but his foothold was as secure as mine. Lord Ernest Belville, on the contrary, was the fifth of a second late for the light, and half a foot short in his spring. Something struck our plank bridge so hard as to set it quivering like a harp-string; there was half a gasp and half a sob in midair beneath our feet; and then a sound far below that I prefer not to describe. I am not sure that I could hit upon the perfect simile; it is more than enough for me that I can hear it still. And with that sickening sound came the loudest clap of thunder yet, and a great white glare that showed us our enemy’s body far below, with one white hand spread like a starfish, but the head of him mercifully twisted underneath.

“It was all his own fault, Bunny. Poor devil! May he and all of us be forgiven; but pull yourself together for your own sake. Well, you can’t fall; stay where you are a minute.”

===Chinese===
紧接着——！

这一回它们一道来了；闪电照亮、雷霆喝彩的那幅景象，到我咽气之时也休想忘怀。拉夫尔斯立在我那座小木桥横跨的深渊的一道井沿上，骤然的亮光里，他一步跨了过去，如同跨过花园里的一条小径。宽度也确实相差无几，可是那深度！骤亮的火光里，我一眼望到了天井的混凝土底，它看上去不过我掌心的一握。拉夫尔斯在我耳边笑着；他已攥牢铁栏；栏杆隔在我们中间，而他的立足之地同我的一般稳当。欧内斯特·贝尔维尔勋爵恰恰相反，比那道光迟了五分之一秒，纵身一跃又短了半英尺。什么东西重重砸上我们的木板桥，震得它像琴弦般颤动；我们脚下的半空里传来半声喘息、半声呜咽；随后是极深处一声闷响，我不愿去描摹。我也没有把握找得出一个贴切的比喻；于我而言，我至今听得见它，就已绰绰有余。伴着那声令人作呕的闷响，到那时为止最响的一记霹雳当头炸开，一道巨大的白光，让我们看清了我们仇人的躯体躺在极深处，一只白手摊开如海星，而他的头，蒙上天垂怜，扭在了身子底下。

“这全怪他自己，邦尼。可怜的人！愿他，也愿我们大家，得到宽恕；不过为你自己着想，振作起来。好了，你掉不下去；在原地再待一分钟。”

===Original===
I remember the uproar of the elements while Raffles was gone; no other sound mingled with it; not the opening of a single window, not the uplifting of a single voice. Then came Raffles with soap and water, and the gyve was wheedled from one wrist, as you withdraw a ring for which the finger has grown too large. Of the rest, I only remember shivering till morning in a pitch-dark flat, whose invalid occupier was for once the nurse, and I his patient.

And that is the true ending of the episode in which we two set ourselves to catch one of our own kidney, albeit in another place I have shirked the whole truth. It is not a grateful task to show Raffles as completely at fault as he really was on that occasion; nor do I derive any subtle satisfaction from recounting my own twofold humiliation, or from having assisted never so indirectly in the death of a not uncongenial sinner. The truth, however, has after all a merit of its own, and the great kinsfolk of poor Lord Ernest have but little to lose by its divulgence. It would seem that they knew more of the real character of the apostle of Rational Drink than was known at Exeter Hall. The tragedy was indeed hushed up, as tragedies only are when they occur in such circles. But the rumor that did get abroad, as to the class of enterprise which the poor scamp was pursuing when he met his death, cannot be too soon exploded, since it breathed upon the fair fame of some of the most respectable flats in Kensington.

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯走开的那阵工夫，我记得的只有风雨雷电的怒号；再无别的声响与之相杂；没有一扇窗打开，没有一个人出声。随后拉夫尔斯带着肥皂和清水回来了，那道铁箍从手腕上哄了下来，像退下一枚指头已经长粗了的戒指。其余的事，我只记得在一套漆黑的公寓里哆嗦到天亮，那里面的病人这一回做了护士，而我做了他的病人。

这便是那桩公案真正的结局——我们两个处心积虑，要去捉一个同我们一个模子里的贼，虽说在另一处地方，我曾对全部真相有所回避。把拉夫尔斯写得像他那晚一样大出洋相，并不是一件令人愉快的差事；细数我自己两重的羞辱，或承认自己曾多么间接地插手了一位并不怎么讨人嫌的罪人之死，我也品不出什么微妙的快意。然而真相毕竟自有其价值，可怜的欧内斯特勋爵的那些显赫亲族，把它公之于众，损失也实在有限。看得出，他们对那位理性饮酒使徒的真面目，原本就比埃克塞特堂的听众知道得多。这场悲剧确实被捂了下来——悲剧也只有发生在那种圈子里才捂得下来。但确有一条谣言流了出去，说那可怜的流氓丧命之时正在经营的那类事业——这条谣言越早戳穿越好，因为它玷污了肯辛顿（Kensington）几家最体面公寓的清誉。

## An Old Flame / 旧情人

### I / 一

===Original===
The square shall be nameless, but if you drive due west from Piccadilly the cabman will eventually find it on his left, and he ought to thank you for two shillings. It is not a fashionable square, but there are few with a finer garden, while the studios on the south side lend distinction of another sort. The houses, however, are small and dingy, and about the last to attract the expert practitioner in search of a crib. Heaven knows it was with no such thought I trailed Raffles thither, one unlucky evening at the latter end of that same season, when Dr. Theobald had at last insisted upon the bath-chair which I had foreseen in the beginning. Trees whispered in the green garden aforesaid, and the cool, smooth lawns looked so inviting that I wondered whether some philanthropic resident could not be induced to lend us the key. But Raffles would not listen to the suggestion, when I stopped to make it, and what was worse, I found him looking wistfully at the little houses instead.

“Such balconies, Bunny! A leg up, and there you would be!”

I expressed a conviction that there would be nothing worth taking in the square, but took care to have him under way again as I spoke.

“I daresay you’re right,” sighed Raffles. “Rings and watches, I suppose, but it would be hard luck to take them from people who live in houses like these. I don’t know, though. Here’s one with an extra story. Stop, Bunny; if you don’t stop I’ll hold on to the railings! This is a good house; look at the knocker and the electric bell. They’ve had that put in. There’s some money here, my rabbit! I dare bet there’s a silver-table in the drawing-room; and the windows are wide open. Electric light, too, by Jove!”

===Chinese===
这广场的名字且按下不表；只是您若从皮卡迪利一路径直向西驶去，车夫终究会在左手边找到它，而他也该为两先令谢您一声。这不算一处时髦广场，可园子比它更精致的也没有几座；南侧那一排画室，又给它添了另一种体面。只是房子既小且暗，行家寻摸下手的宅子，最不会看中的大约就是这里。天知道，那个倒霉的傍晚我跟着拉夫尔斯到了那里——那时正当同一社交季的尾声，西奥博尔德医生终于坚持用上了轮椅，这东西我打一开始便已料到。前述绿园里树声絮絮，清凉平整的草坪看着那样诱人，我直纳闷能否说动哪位乐善好施的住户，把园门的钥匙借我们一用。可我停下提出这建议时，拉夫尔斯根本不听；更糟的是，我发现他正恋恋地打量着那些小房子。

“瞧这些阳台，邦尼！托你一把，人就在上头了！”

我表示深信这广场里没有值得一拿的东西，说这话时却留神让轮椅重新走动起来。

“我料你也说得对，”拉夫尔斯叹道。“无非戒指怀表之类；可住这种房子的人家，拿了他们的未免太狠。话又说回来，谁知呢。这一家还高出一层。停下，邦尼；你再不停，我可要抱住栏杆不撒手了！这是户好人家；瞧那门环，那电铃。电铃是特意装的。这儿有钱，我的兔子！我敢打赌客厅里就摆着银器桌；窗子还敞得大开。还是电灯呢，老天爷！”

===Original===
Since stop I must, I had done so on the other side of the road, in the shadow of the leafy palings, and as Raffles spoke the ground floor windows opposite had flown alight, showing as pretty a little dinner-table as one could wish to see, with a man at his wine at the far end, and the back of a lady in evening dress toward us. It was like a lantern-picture thrown upon a screen. There were only the pair of them, but the table was brilliant with silver and gay with flowers, and the maid waited with the indefinable air of a good servant. It certainly seemed a good house.

“She’s going to let down the blind!” whispered Raffles, in high excitement. “No, confound them, they’ve told her not to. Mark down her necklace, Bunny, and invoice his stud. What a brute he looks! But I like the table, and that’s her show. She has the taste; but he must have money. See the festive picture over the sideboard? Looks to me like a Jacques Saillard. But that silver-table would be good enough for me.”

“Get on,” said I. “You’re in a bath-chair.”

“But the whole square’s at dinner! We should have the ball at our feet. It wouldn’t take two twos!”

===Chinese===
既然非停不可，我早把轮椅停在了路对面绿叶婆娑的木栅阴影里；拉夫尔斯话音未落，对面底层的窗户倏然亮起，露出一桌再漂亮不过的小晚宴：远端一位男士自斟其酒，朝着我们的则是晚装女士的一段背影。恰似投在幕布上的一幅灯影画。席上只有他们两个，餐桌却银光熠熠、花影生辉，侍立的女仆带着好仆人那种说不出的气度。这瞧着确乎是户好人家。

“她要放窗帘了！”拉夫尔斯压着嗓子，兴奋已极。“不，见鬼，他们吩咐过她不许放。记下她的项链，邦尼，再给他领口的饰扣开张单子。瞧他那张脸，多么粗蠢！可我喜欢那张餐桌，那是她的排场。品味是她的；钱必定是他的。瞧见餐具柜上方那幅喜庆画没有？我看是雅克·萨亚尔（Jacques Saillard）的手笔。不过那张银器桌，于我也就尽够了。”

“走吧，”我说。“你还坐在轮椅里呢。”

“可满广场的人家都在用晚饭！机会就卧在咱们脚边。费不了片刻工夫！”

===Original===
“With those blinds up, and the cook in the kitchen underneath?”

He nodded, leaning forward in the chair, his hands upon the wraps about his legs.

“You must be mad,” said I, and got back to my handles with the word, but when I tugged the chair ran light.

“Keep an eye on the rug,” came in a whisper from the middle of the road; and there stood my invalid, his pale face in a quiver of pure mischief, yet set with his insane resolve. “I’m only going to see whether that woman has a silver-table﻿—”

===Chinese===
“窗帘就这样敞着，底下厨房里还坐着厨娘？”

他点点头，在椅子里俯身向前，双手按住腿上裹的毯子。

“你准是疯了，”我说罢回到车把后，可一拽，轮椅轻得出奇。

“看好毯子，”路中央传来一声耳语；但见我的病人已然立在那儿，苍白的脸因纯粹的恶作剧而颤动，却又凝着那股疯狂的决心。“我只想看看那女人有没有银器桌——”

===Original===
“We don’t want it﻿—”

“It won’t take a minute﻿—”

“It’s madness, madness﻿—”

“Then don’t you wait!”

===Chinese===
“咱们不要那个——”

“用不了一分钟——”

“这是发疯，发疯——”

“那你就别等！”

===Original===
It was like him to leave me with that, and this time I had taken him at his last word, had not my own given me an idea. Mad I had called him, and mad I could declare him upon oath if necessary. It was not as though the thing had happened far from home. They could learn all about us at the nearest mansions. I referred them to Dr. Theobald; this was a Mr. Maturin, one of his patients, and I was his keeper, and he had never given me the slip before. I heard myself making these explanations on the doorstep, and pointing to the deserted bath-chair as the proof, while the pretty parlor maid ran for the police. It would be a more serious matter for me than for my charge. I should lose my place. No, he had never done such a thing before, and I would answer for it that he never should again.

I saw myself conducting Raffles back to his chair, with a firm hand and a stern tongue. I heard him thanking me in whispers on the way home. It would be the first tight place I had ever got him out of, and I was quite anxious for him to get into it, so sure was I of every move. My whole position had altered in the few seconds that it took me to follow this illuminating train of ideas; it was now so strong that I could watch Raffles without much anxiety. And he was worth watching.

===Chinese===
丢下这么一句便扬长而去，正是他的做派；这一回我也真打算依他末一句话行事了——若不是我自己那句话给了我一个主意。我方才既骂他疯，必要时我也大可指天誓日说他疯。好在这事并非发生在离家遥远之地。左近的公寓里，随便什么人都能把咱们的底细打听个一清二楚。我可以把他们引见给西奥博尔德医生；这位是马图林先生，他的病人之一，我是病人的看护，他从没这样从我手里溜走过。我听见自己在人家门阶上如此这般地分说，指着那把空轮椅为凭，而漂亮的客厅女仆已跑去喊警察。这桩事于我可比我那被监护人严重得多。我会丢了饭碗。不，他从前从没干过这种事，我也敢担保他往后绝不会再犯。

我看见自己一手把着、一口训着，把拉夫尔斯押回他的轮椅。我听见他在归途上对我切切道谢。那将是我头一回把他从险境里捞出来；我简直巴不得他真陷进去——每一步我都有成算。顺着这串豁然开朗的念头想下去，不过几秒钟，我的整个处境已为之改观；如今这境地稳当得很，我尽可以不太慌张地看拉夫尔斯行事。而他，也确实值得一看。

===Original===
He had stepped boldly but softly to the front door, and there he was still waiting, ready to ring if the door opened or a face appeared in the area, and doubtless to pretend that he had rung already. But he had not to ring at all; and suddenly I saw his foot in the letter-box, his left hand on the lintel overhead. It was thrilling, even to a hardened accomplice with an explanation up his sleeve! A tight grip with that left hand of his, as he leant backward with all his weight upon those five fingers; a right arm stretched outward and upward to its last inch; and the base of the low, projecting balcony was safely caught.

I looked down and took breath. The maid was removing the crumbs in the lighted room, and the square was empty as before. What a blessing it was the end of the season! Many of the houses remained in darkness. I looked up again, and Raffles was drawing his left leg over the balcony railing. In another moment he had disappeared through one of the French windows which opened upon the balcony, and in yet another he had switched on the electric light within. This was bad enough, for now I, at least, could see everything he did; but the crowning folly was still to come. There was no point in it; the mad thing was done for my benefit, as I knew at once and he afterward confessed; but the lunatic reappeared on the balcony, bowing like a mountebank﻿—in his crepe mask!

===Chinese===
他早已大胆而又轻捷地跨到正门前，此刻仍在那儿候着：门若开了，或地下室的采光井里探出一张脸，他便随时按铃——而且十有八九要装出早已按过的模样。可他根本无须按铃；忽然间，我看见他一脚蹬进门底的投信口，左手攀住了头顶的门楣。这一幕真叫人屏息，哪怕看的人是我这袖中备好一套说辞的老练同伙！只见那只左手死死扣紧，全身重量后仰，全压在那五根手指上；右臂向外向上探到最后一寸；低低外凸的阳台底沿，就此稳稳落在他掌中。

我低下头，缓了口气。灯火通明的屋里，女仆正在收拾面包屑；广场上依旧空无一人。社交季到了尾声，真是天大的运气！好些宅子都还黑着。我再抬头，拉夫尔斯已把左腿跨过阳台栏杆。转眼间，他已从通阳台的一扇落地长窗里没了影；再一转眼，屋里的电灯已被他拧亮。这已经够糟了，因为至少他的一举一动全落在我眼里；可最登峰造极的荒唐还在后头。这半点必要也没有；这件疯事分明是专为我做的——我当场就明白，他后来也亲口认了；只见这疯子重新在阳台上现身，像卖大力丸的江湖客一般连连打拱——脸上还蒙着那副绉纱面具！

===Original===
I set off with the empty chair, but I came back. I could not desert old Raffles, even when I would, but must try to explain away his mask as well, if he had not the sense to take it off in time. It would be difficult, but burglaries are not usually committed from a bath-chair, and for the rest I put my faith in Dr. Theobald. Meanwhile Raffles had at least withdrawn from the balcony, and now I could only see his head as he peered into a cabinet at the other side of the room. It was like the opera of *Aïda*, in which two scenes are enacted simultaneously, one in the dungeon below, the other in the temple above. In the same fashion my attention now became divided between the picture of Raffles moving stealthily about the upper room, and that of the husband and wife at table underneath. And all at once, as the man replenished his glass with a shrug of the shoulders, the woman pushed back her chair and sailed to the door.

Raffles was standing before the fireplace upstairs. He had taken one of the framed photographs from the chimneypiece, and was scanning it at suicidal length through the eye-holes in the hideous mask which he still wore. He would need it after all. The lady had left the room below, opening and shutting the door for herself; the man was filling his glass once more. I would have shrieked my warning to Raffles, so fatally engrossed overhead, but at this moment (of all others) a constable (of all men) was marching sedately down our side of the square. There was nothing for it but to turn a melancholy eye upon the bath-chair, and to ask the constable the time. I was evidently to be kept there all night, I remarked, and only realized with the words that they disposed of my other explanations before they were uttered. It was a horrible moment for such a discovery. Fortunately the enemy was on the pavement, from which he could scarcely have seen more than the drawing-room ceiling, had he looked; but he was not many houses distant when a door opened and a woman gasped so that I heard both across the road. And never shall I forget the subsequent tableaux in the lighted room behind the low balcony and the French windows.

===Chinese===
我推着空轮椅拔脚便走，可又折了回来。老拉夫尔斯这里，我就是想弃也弃不下，还得连他的面具也一并想法圆谎——倘若他没那份急智及时摘掉它的话。这固然难，可谁家常有坐着轮椅来撬门的贼？至于其余，我把宝押在西奥博尔德医生身上。这工夫拉夫尔斯总算从阳台上退了进去，如今我只看得见他一颗头，正在屋子那头的橱柜前探看。这情景好比歌剧《阿依达》（*Aïda*），两幕戏同时上演：一幕在下面的地牢，一幕在上面的神殿。我的心神也就这般分作了两处：一处是楼上屋里蹑手蹑脚走动的拉夫尔斯，一处是楼下餐桌旁的那对夫妻。冷不防，那男子耸耸肩又斟满一杯，那女子便推开椅子，袅袅然向门口而去。

拉夫尔斯正站在楼上的壁炉前。他从壁炉台上取下一幅镶框相片，隔着脸上那副还没摘的可怕面具的眼孔，用一种足以送命的工夫细细端详。看来他到底还是用得着它。楼下那位太太已离了席，自己开门掩门而去；那男子又斟上一杯。我真想朝楼上那位浑然忘命的拉夫尔斯尖声示警，可偏偏在这千挑万选的一刻，一个千挑万选的人物——一名警察——正沿着我们这一侧的广场踱来，四平八稳。没奈何，只得朝那轮椅投去忧郁的一瞥，向警察请教时刻。我随口说自己这分明要在这儿守上一整夜了，话方出口才醒悟：这一句已把其余的托辞尽数作废，连说都无须再说了。在这样一个当口有此发现，真是苦不堪言。幸而那位对头走在人行道上，他纵然抬头，至多也只能望见那家的客厅天花板；可他还没走出几户人家远，一扇门开了，一个女人倒抽一口冷气——开门与抽气，两样声响我都隔着马路听得真切。至于随后在低矮阳台与落地长窗后那间灯火通明的屋里接连上演的一幕幕，我至死不忘。

===Original===
Raffles stood confronted by a dark and handsome woman whose profile, as I saw it first in the electric light, is cut like a cameo in my memory. It had the undeviating line of brow and nose, the short upper lip, the perfect chin, that are united in marble oftener than in the flesh; and like marble she stood, or rather like some beautiful pale bronze; for that was her coloring, and she lost none of it that I could see, neither trembled; but her bosom rose and fell, and that was all. So she stood without flinching before a masked ruffian, who, I felt, would be the first to appreciate her courage; to me it was so superb that I could think of it in this way even then, and marvel how Raffles himself could stand unabashed before so brave a figure. He had not to do so long. The woman scorned him, and he stood unmoved, a framed photograph still in his hand. Then, with a quick, determined movement she turned, not to the door or to the bell, but to the open window by which Raffles had entered; and this with that accursed policeman still in view. So far no word had passed between the pair. But at this point Raffles said something, I could not hear what, but at the sound of his voice the woman wheeled. And Raffles was looking humbly in her face, the crepe mask snatched from his own.

“Arthur!” she cried; and that might have been heard in the middle of the square garden.

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯对面，立着一位黑发的美人；她的侧影我初见于电灯之下，如今如浮雕宝石一般铭刻在我的记忆里。额与鼻连成一条决不游移的直线，短短的人中，无可挑剔的下颏——这几样线条在大理石上聚合，比在血肉之躯上更为常见；而她也果如大理石般伫立着，不如说像一尊美丽的淡色青铜像；那正是她的肤色，据我所见，这肤色一分未褪，她也不曾发抖；只有胸脯一起一伏，尽于此矣。她就这样毫无惧色地直面一个蒙面的匪徒；而我感觉，头一个懂得赏识这份勇气的，恰是匪徒本人；在我看来这勇气实在壮美，即便当场的我已能作如是想，且惊诧拉夫尔斯本人何以能在这等刚勇的人面前站得面无愧色。好在他无须久站。那女人满脸鄙夷地睨着他，他不动声色，手里仍握着那幅镶框相片。随后，她一个迅疾决绝的动作转过身去——不奔门，不奔铃，偏奔向拉夫尔斯进来的那扇敞开的窗；而那个可诅咒的警察还望得见。至此两人之间不曾交一语。可就在此刻拉夫尔斯开了口，说的什么我听不见，但一闻其声，那女人霍地回身。拉夫尔斯正谦卑地望着她的脸，绉纱面具已从他脸上扯了下来。

“亚瑟（Arthur）！”她叫出声来；这一声，怕是广场花园正中都听得见。

===Original===
Then they stood gazing at each other, neither unmoved any more, and while they stood the street-door opened and banged. It was her husband leaving the house, a fine figure of a man, but a dissipated face, and a step even now distinguished by the extreme caution which precedes unsteadiness. He broke the spell. His wife came to the balcony, then looked back into the room, and yet again along the road, and this time I saw her face. It was the face of one glancing indeed from Hyperion to a satyr. And then I saw the rings flash, as her hand fell gently upon Raffles’s arm.

They disappeared from that window. Their heads showed for an instant in the next. Then they dipped out of sight, and an inner ceiling flashed out under a new light; they had gone into the back drawing-room, beyond my ken. The maid came up with coffee, her mistress hastily met her at the door, and once more disappeared. The square was as quiet as ever. I remained some minutes where I was. Now and then I thought I heard their voices in the back drawing-room. I was seldom sure.

===Chinese===
于是两人伫立相望，谁也不再能无动于衷；正相望间，临街的大门开了，又砰地撞上。是她丈夫出门去了：好一条堂堂的汉子，却生一张纵酒过度的脸，连此刻的脚步，都带着打晃之前那种分外的小心。他破了这道魔咒。他妻子走到阳台上，先回望屋里，又沿街张望，这一回我可看清了她的脸。那是一张恰恰从日神许配利翁（Hyperion）瞥向羊怪萨提尔（satyr）的脸。接着我看见宝石戒指一闪——她的手，轻轻落在拉夫尔斯的臂上。

两人从那扇窗里消失了；人头在旁边一扇窗里晃得一晃，随即也没入不见，里间的天花板在一盏新灯下倏然亮起——他们进了后客厅，出了我的视界。女仆端咖啡上楼，女主人急急在门口把她截住，再次没了影。广场一如方才寂静。我在原地又立了几分钟。时而仿佛听见后客厅里他们的语声，却难得听真。

===Original===
My state of mind may be imagined by those readers who take an interest in my personal psychology. It does not amuse me to look back upon it. But at length I had the sense to put myself in Raffles’s place. He had been recognized at last, he had come to life. Only one person knew as yet, but that person was a woman, and a woman who had once been fond of him, if the human face could speak. Would she keep his secret? Would he tell her where he lived? It was terrible to think we were such neighbors, and with the thought that it was terrible came a little enlightenment as to what could still be done for the best. He would not tell her where he lived. I knew him too well for that. He would run for it when he could, and the bath-chair and I must not be there to give him away. I dragged the infernal vehicle round the nearer corner. Then I waited﻿—there could be no harm in that﻿—and at last he came.

He was walking briskly, so I was right, and he had not played the invalid to her; yet I heard him cry out with pleasure as he turned the corner, and he flung himself into the chair with a long-drawn sigh that did me good.

“Well done, Bunny﻿—well done! I am on my way to Earl’s Court, she’s capable of following me, but she won’t look for me in a bath-chair. Home, home, home, and not another word till we get there!”

===Chinese===
我当时的心境，凡对我的个人心理有兴趣的读者，不难想象。回首此事，于我并无乐趣。但末了我总算还有点头脑，能设身处地替拉夫尔斯一想。他到底还是被人认了出来，他死而复生了。眼下知情者只有一人；可这人是个女人，而且——倘若人脸能言——是个一度倾心于他的女人。她肯守他的秘密吗？他可会告诉她自己的住处？想到我们两家竟是这样近邻，实在可怕；而恰是这个可怕的念头，倒让我稍稍开了窍，明白眼下如何行事才算上策。他不会告诉她住处的。这一点我太了解他了。一得脱身他便会撒腿就走，轮椅和我千万不能留在原地坏事。我把这辆该死的车拖过就近的街角，然后等着——等着总归无妨——他终于来了。

他步履轻快——看来我料对了，他没在她面前充病人；转过街角时，我听见他欢然出声，随即一屁股跌进椅里，发出一声悠长的喟叹。这叹息，让我心里熨帖。

“干得好，邦尼——干得好！我这可是回厄尔斯考特去的路；她兴许会钉我，可她做梦也想不到到轮椅里找我。回家，回家，回家，到家之前再一个字也别提！”

===Original===
Capable of following him? She overtook us before we were past the studios on the south side of the square, the woman herself, in a hooded opera-cloak. But she never gave us a glance, and we saw her turn safely in the right direction for Earl’s Court, and the wrong one for our humble mansions. Raffles thanked his gods in a voice that trembled, and five minutes later we were in the flat. Then for once it was Raffles who filled the tumblers and found the cigarettes, and for once (and once only in all my knowledge of him) did he drain his glass at a draught.

“You didn’t see the balcony scene?” he asked at length; and they were his first words since the woman passed us on his track.

“Do you mean when she came in?”

===Chinese===
还兴许钉他？我们还没走过广场南侧那排画室，她本人已经追过了我们——一袭连兜帽的观剧斗篷。可她一眼也没朝我们看；我们眼睁睁望着她安然拐上通往厄尔斯考特的正路——也恰是背离咱们寒酸公寓的岔路。拉夫尔斯谢过他的列位神明，声音都发着颤；五分钟后，我们进了公寓。这一回，斟酒寻烟的竟是拉夫尔斯；也是我与他相识至今仅有的这一回，他端起杯来一饮而尽。

“阳台那一幕，你没瞧见吧？”他终于开口；这是自那女人循迹从我们身边走过之后，他的第一句话。

“你说她进屋那会儿？”

===Original===
“No, when I came down.”

“I didn’t.”

“I hope nobody else saw it,” said Raffles devoutly. “I don’t say that Romeo and Juliet were brother and sister to us. But you might have said so, Bunny!”

He was staring at the carpet with as wry a face as lover ever wore.

===Chinese===
“不，是我下来那会儿。”

“没瞧见。”

“但愿再没有第二个人瞧见，”拉夫尔斯虔诚地说。“我不敢说咱们那一幕，清白得能叫罗密欧与朱丽叶（Romeo and Juliet）成了兄妹；可你当时差一点也真可以这么说了，邦尼！”

他直瞪着地毯，一脸的苦相——恋爱中人再苦，也苦不过这一张脸。

===Original===
“An old flame?” said I, gently.

“A married woman,” he groaned.

“So I gathered.”

“But she always was one, Bunny,” said he, ruefully. “That’s the trouble. It makes all the difference in the world!”

===Chinese===
“旧情人？”我轻声问。

“一个有夫之妇，”他呻吟道。

“这我也瞧出来了。”

“可她当年就是，邦尼，”他苦着脸说。“麻烦正麻烦在这儿。这可是天壤之别！”

===Original===
I saw the difference, but said I did not see how it could make any now. He had eluded the lady, after all; had we not seen her off upon a scent as false as scent could be? There was occasion for redoubled caution in the future, but none for immediate anxiety. I quoted the bedside Theobald, but Raffles did not smile. His eyes had been downcast all this time, and now, when he raised them, I perceived that my comfort had been administered to deaf ears.

“Do you know who she is?” said he.

“Not from Eve.”

“Jacques Saillard,” he said, as though now I must know.

===Chinese===
这区别我看得分明，嘴上却说看不出如今还能有什么干系。他到底还是甩开了那位太太；咱们不是亲眼看着她循着一条假得无以复加的线索去了吗？往后加倍小心是应当的，眼下却犯不着发愁。我把病榻边那位西奥博尔德抬出来作保，拉夫尔斯却没有笑。他的眼睛一直低垂着；此刻抬起来，我才看出，我这套安慰全是喂了聋子的耳朵。

“你知道她是谁吗？”他说。

“一无所知。”

“雅克·萨亚尔，”他说，仿佛报出这名号，我这回总该知道了。

===Original===
But the name left me cold and stolid. I had heard it, but that was all. It was lamentable ignorance, I am aware, but I had specialized in Letters at the expense of Art.

“You must know her pictures,” said Raffles, patiently; “but I suppose you thought she was a man. They would appeal to you, Bunny; that festive piece over the sideboard was her work. Sometimes they risk her at the Academy, sometimes they fight shy. She has one of those studios in the same square; they used to live up near Lord’s.”

My mind was busy brightening a dim memory of nymphs reflected in woody pools. “Of course!” I exclaimed, and added something about “a clever woman.” Raffles rose at the phrase.

===Chinese===
可这名号并没有把我这块木头烘热。我听见过它，仅此而已。我知道这无知实在丢人，可我专攻的是文学，代价便是牺牲了艺术。

“她的画你一定见过，”拉夫尔斯耐心地说，“不过我猜你把她当成了男人。她的画正对你的胃口，邦尼；餐具柜上那幅喜庆之作就是她的手笔。有时皇家艺术院（the Academy）斗胆收她的画，有时又避之不及。她就住在同一广场的一间画室里；他们从前住在罗德（Lord's）板球场附近。”

我脑子里正忙着把一段模糊的旧忆擦亮：林木掩映的池水里，倒映着宁芙仙子。“当然！”我叫出声来，又添了一句什么“真是位才女”。一听这四个字，拉夫尔斯霍地站起。

===Original===
“A clever woman!” he echoed, scornfully; “if she were only that I should feel safe as houses. Clever women can’t forget their cleverness, they carry it as badly as a boy does his wine, and are about as dangerous. I don’t call Jacques Saillard clever outside her art, but neither do I call her a woman at all. She does man’s work over a man’s name, has the will of any ten men I ever knew, and I don’t mind telling you that I fear her more than any person on God’s earth. I broke with her once,” said Raffles, grimly, “but I know her. If I had been asked to name the one person in London by whom I was keenest *not* to be bowled out, I should have named Jacques Saillard.”

That he had never before named her to me was as characteristic as the reticence with which Raffles spoke of their past relations, and even of their conversation in the back drawing-room that evening.

===Chinese===
“才女！”他冷笑着重复；“她若只是个才女，我反倒稳如泰山。有才的女人忘不掉自己那份才，端着它就像毛头小子端酒，端得一样难看，也一样危险。在艺术之外，我不说雅克·萨亚尔聪明；可我也不把她当女人看。她顶着男人的名字，干男人的事业，意志抵得上我平生所识的随便哪十条汉子；我不妨对你直说：普天之下，我最怕的就是此人。我跟她绝过一回，”拉夫尔斯狠声说道，“可我摸她的底。倘有人要我指出伦敦城里我最*不*愿栽在谁手里，我指出的便是雅克·萨亚尔。”

他此前从未对我提过她的名姓——这跟他谈起两人旧日情缘、乃至那晚后客厅里那番谈话时的欲说还休一样，都是拉夫尔斯的本色。

===Original===
It was a question of principle with him, and one that I like to remember. “Never give a woman away, Bunny,” he used to say; and he said it again tonight, but with a heavy cloud upon him, as though his chivalry was sorely tried.

“That’s all right,” said I, “if you’re not going to be given away yourself.”

“That’s just it, Bunny! That’s just﻿—”

The words were out of him, it was too late to recall them. I had hit the nail upon the head.

===Chinese===
这于他是原则问题，也是我喜欢追忆的一条。“永远不出卖女人，邦尼，”他从前常这么说；今晚他又说了一遍，眉宇间却压着一片浓云，仿佛他的骑士风度正备受煎熬。

“那自然好，”我说，“只要被出卖的不是你本人。”

“糟就糟在这儿，邦尼！正是——”

话已出口，追悔莫及。我这一锤，正敲在钉头上。

===Original===
“So she threatened you,” I said, “did she?”

“I didn’t say so,” he replied, coldly.

“And she is mated with a clown!” I pursued.

“How she ever married him,” he admitted, “is a mystery to me.”

===Chinese===
“这么说，她威胁你了，”我说，“是不是？”

“我没这么说，”他冷冷答道。

“她还偏偏配了个粗胚！”我追问。

“她究竟怎么会嫁给他的，”他承认道，“于我也是个谜。”

===Original===
“It always is,” said I, the wise man for once, and rather enjoying the role.

“Southern blood?”

“Spanish.”

“She’ll be pestering you to run off with her, old chap,” said I.

===Chinese===
“向来都是个谜，”我说——这回总算当了一回智者，这角色我受用得很。

“南方血统？”

“西班牙。”

“她准会缠着你私奔的，老伙计，”我说。

===Original===
Raffles was pacing the room. He stopped in his stride for half a second. So she had begun pestering him already! It is wonderful how acute any fool can be in the affairs of his friend. But Raffles resumed his walk without a syllable, and I retreated to safer ground.

“So you sent her to Earl’s Court,” I mused aloud; and at last he smiled.

“You’ll be interested to hear, Bunny,” said he, “that I am now living in Seven Dials, and Bill Sikes couldn’t hold a farthing dip to me. Bless you, she had my old police record at her fingers’ ends, but it was fit to frame compared with the one I gave her. I had sunk as low as they dig. I divided my nights between the open parks and a thieves’ kitchen in Seven Dials. If I was decently dressed it was because I had stolen the suit down the Thames Valley beat the night before last. I was on my way back when first that sleepy square, and then her open window, proved too much for me. You should have heard me beg her to let me push on to the devil in my own way; there I spread myself, for I meant every word; but I swore the final stage would be a six-foot drop.”

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯正在屋里踱步。脚步顿了半秒钟。这么说，她已经缠上他了！天下的任何蠢材，在朋友的事情上能变得多敏锐，真是奇事。可拉夫尔斯一言不发，又踱了下去，我便退守到更稳当的地界。

“原来你把她支去了厄尔斯考特，”我自言自语；他终于笑了。

“你有兴致不妨听听，邦尼，”他说，“我如今是住在七晷区（Seven Dials）的，那份落魄相，比尔·赛克斯（Bill Sikes）给我举一支四分之一便士的小蜡烛都不配。老天爷，我的旧警局案底她背得滚瓜烂熟，可那案底比起我现编给她的这一份，简直干净得够装裱起来挂墙。我沉到了他们掘地所及的最深处。我的夜晚一半打发在露天的园子里，一半打发在七晷区的一间贼窝。我身上若还算体面，那是因为前天夜里我在泰晤士河谷（Thames Valley）沿线把这身行头偷到了手。我正往回走，先是那座昏昏欲睡的广场，接着是她那扇敞开的窗，终于叫我把持不住。你真该听听我怎么哀求她放我照自己的路子一路下地狱；那一节我着意铺陈了一番，因为字字句句出于真心；我还赌了咒：这趟行程的最后一程，必是六英尺深的一个坑。”

===Original===
“You did lay it on,” said I.

“It was necessary, and that had its effect. She let me go. But at the last moment she said she didn’t believe I was so black as I painted myself, and then there was the balcony scene you missed.”

So that was all. I could not help telling him that he had got out of it better than he deserved for ever getting in. Next moment I regretted the remark.

“If I have got out of it,” said Raffles, doubtfully. “We are dreadfully near neighbors, and I can’t move in a minute, with old Theobald taking a grave view of my case. I suppose I had better lie low, and thank the gods again for putting her off the scent for the time being.”

===Chinese===
“你这牛皮，吹得也真够圆的，”我说。

“非如此不可，而且果然奏了效。她放我走了。可在最后一刻，她说她不信我真把自己涂抹得那样黑；接着，便是你没瞧见的那幕阳台戏。”

原来如此，再无别的。我忍不住对他说：他这一身而退，比起他当初那桩无端招惹的祸事，下场好得实在过了分寸。话音方落，我便后悔了。

“我算是脱身了吗，”拉夫尔斯将信将疑。“咱们两家近得吓人；老西奥博尔德又把我的病看得郑重其事，我一时半刻挪不了窝。我看我还是蛰伏不动，再谢一回列位神明——总算暂时把她引出了嗅迹。”

===Original===
No doubt our conversation was carried beyond this point, but it certainly was not many minutes later, nor had we left the subject, when the electric bell thrilled us both to a sudden silence.

“The doctor?” I queried, hope fighting with my horror.

“It was a single ring.”

===Chinese===
我们的谈话无疑还往下进行了；但想必不出几分钟——话题也还没离开她——电铃蓦地一响，把我们两个齐齐震入了骤然的寂静。

“医生？”我问，一线希望正与满心惊惶相搏。

“只按了一声。”

===Original===
“The last post?”

“You know he knocks, and it’s long past his time.”

The electric bell rang again, but now as though it never would stop.

===Chinese===
“末班邮差？”

“你知道他向来是敲门的，何况早过了他来的时辰。”

电铃又响了，这一回像是再也休想住声。

===Original===
“You go, Bunny,” said Raffles, with decision. His eyes were sparkling. His smile was firm.

“What am I to say?”

“If it’s the lady let her in.”

It was the lady, still in her evening cloak, with her fine dark head half-hidden by the hood, and an engaging contempt of appearances upon her angry face. She was even handsomer than I had thought, and her beauty of a bolder type, but she was also angrier than I had anticipated when I came so readily to the door. The passage into which it opened was an exceedingly narrow one, as I have often said, but I never dreamt of barring this woman’s way, though not a word did she stoop to say to me. I was only too glad to flatten myself against the wall, as the rustling fury strode past me into the lighted room with the open door.

===Chinese===
“你去，邦尼，”拉夫尔斯当机立断。他双眼灼灼放光。他笑容笃定。

“我怎么说？”

“若是那位太太，请她进来。”

果然是那位太太，仍裹着晚宴斗篷，那颗俊美的乌发头颅半掩在兜帽里，恼怒的脸上带着一种引人好感的、对仪容的全然不屑。她比我方才忖度的还要美，美的路数也更大胆；可我如此爽快地开门相迎，换来的怒气也比预计的更盛。门内那条过道之窄，我不知说过多少回；但我做梦也没想拦这位太太的路，虽然她连屈尊对我施一句话都不肯。这团衣料窸窣的怒火大步自我身边擦过，径直闯进那扇敞着门的灯火通明的房间——我求之不得，只管把身子紧贴在墙上。

===Original===
“So this is your thieves’ kitchen!” she cried, in high-pitched scorn.

I was on the threshold myself, and Raffles glanced towards me with raised eyebrows.

“I have certainly had better quarters in my day,” said he, “but you need not call them absurd names before my man.”

“Then send your ‘man’ about his business,” said Jacques Saillard, with an unpleasant stress upon the word indicated.

===Chinese===
“原来这就是你的贼窝！”她尖声讥诮道。

我自己也正立在门槛上；拉夫尔斯朝我这边瞥来，双眉扬起。

“我当年确乎住过更好的居所，”他说，“可你也不必当着我仆人的面，给它们起这些难听的名目。”

“那就叫你的‘仆人’干他的正事去，”雅克·萨亚尔说，把那个字咬得极重，叫人听着不受用。

===Original===
But when the door was shut I heard Raffles assuring her that I knew nothing, that he was a real invalid overcome by a sudden mad temptation, and all he had told her of his life a lie to hide his whereabouts, but all he was telling her now she could prove for herself without leaving that building. It seemed, however, that she had proved it already by going first to the porter below stairs. Yet I do not think she cared one atom which story was the truth.

“So you thought I could pass you in your chair,” she said, “or ever in this world again, without hearing from my heart that it was you!”

===Chinese===
可等门一关上，我便听见拉夫尔斯向她连连保证：我什么都不知道；他是真病人，一时被疯狂的诱惑压倒了；先前对她讲的身世，全是为藏匿行踪编下的谎；而如今告诉她的一切，她不出这栋楼便能亲自查证。不过看来她早已查证过了——头一处便去了楼下的门房那里。然而依我看，两套说辞孰真孰假，她半点也不放在心上。

“原来你以为，”她说，“你坐在那轮椅里打我身边过——或者今生今世再有半次照面——我的心竟会听不出那是你！”

### II / 二

===Original===
“Bunny,” said Raffles, “I’m awfully sorry, old chap, but you’ve got to go.”

It was some weeks since the first untimely visitation of Jacques Saillard, but there had been many others at all hours of the day, while Raffles had been induced to pay at least one to her studio in the neighboring square. These intrusions he had endured at first with an air of humorous resignation which imposed upon me less than he imagined. The woman meant well, he said, after all, and could be trusted to keep his secret loyally. It was plain to me, however, that Raffles did not trust her, and that his pretence upon the point was a deliberate pose to conceal the extent to which she had him in her power. Otherwise there would have been little point in hiding anything from the one person in possession of the cardinal secret of his identity.

But Raffles thought it worth his while to hoodwink Jacques Saillard in the subsidiary matter of his health, in which Dr. Theobald lent him unwitting assistance, and, as we have seen, to impress upon her that I was actually his attendant, and as ignorant of his past as the doctor himself. “So you’re all right, Bunny,” he had assured me; “she thinks you knew nothing the other night. I told you she wasn’t a clever woman outside her work. But hasn’t she a will!” I told Raffles it was very considerate of him to keep me out of it, but that it seemed to me like tying up the bag when the cat had escaped. His reply was an admission that one must be on the defensive with such a woman and in such a case. Soon after this, Raffles, looking far from well, fell back upon his own last line of defence, namely, his bed; and now, as always in the end, I could see some sense in his subtleties, since it was comparatively easy for me to turn even Jacques Saillard from the door, with Dr. Theobald’s explicit injunctions, and with my own honesty unquestioned. So for a day we had peace once more. Then came letters, then the doctor again and again, and finally my dismissal in the incredible words which have necessitated these explanations.

===Chinese===
“邦尼，”拉夫尔斯说，“实在对不住，老伙计，可你只好走了。”

雅克·萨亚尔头一回不合时宜的大驾光临，已是几个星期前的事了；此后一天里无论什么时辰，她又驾临过许多回；拉夫尔斯也拗不过，至少回访过一趟她在相邻广场的画室。这些不速之扰，他起初都带着一副打趣认命的神气受了下去；只是这点神气的蒙蔽功效，远比他自以为的浅。他说，这女人到底存心不坏，守他秘密也尽可放心。然而我看得分明：拉夫尔斯并不信她；他在这上头的故作坦然，是存心摆出的一副姿态，为的是遮掩她已把他攥到了何种地步。若非如此，对唯一掌握着他身份头等机密的人再瞒任何事，便都近乎无谓了。

但拉夫尔斯觉得，在健康这桩次要事情上蒙一蒙雅克·萨亚尔还是值得一为之的——这方面西奥博尔德医生不知不觉帮了大忙；而且如前所见，他还让她牢牢记住：我确是他的看护，对他的过去跟医生本人一样一无所知。“所以你没事了，邦尼，”他向我打过包票，“她认定那晚你什么都不知道。我早说过，出了她的本行，她算不得聪明女人。可她那副意志，好家伙！”我对拉夫尔斯说，承他就此把我摘出去，想得真是周到；只是在我看来，这好比猫已跑了才去扎紧口袋。他的回答，等于承认对这样的女人、在这样的处境里，不能不严设守备。此后不久，拉夫尔斯气色很坏，便退守他自己最后一道防线——他的床；而每逢收场我总能看出他那些弯弯绕的所以然，这回也一样：有西奥博尔德医生的明令挡驾，加上我这人的诚实体面无人置疑，连雅克·萨亚尔，我也能不甚费力地拒之门外了。于是我们重又得了一天太平。接着是信件，接着医生一趟又一趟，末了便是我被打发走——所用的正是那几句令人难以置信的话，也就是逼出眼下这番交代的话。

===Original===
“Go?” I echoed. “Go where?”

“It’s that ass Theobald,” said Raffles. “He insists.”

“On my going altogether?”

He nodded.

===Chinese===
“走？”我重声道。“走去哪儿？”

“都怪那头蠢驴西奥博尔德，”拉夫尔斯说。“他非坚持不可。”

“要我整个儿地走？”

他点点头。

===Original===
“And you mean to let him have his way?”

I had no language for my mortification and disgust, though neither was as yet quite so great as my surprise. I had foreseen almost every conceivable consequence of the mad act which brought all this trouble to pass, but a voluntary division between Raffles and me had certainly never entered my calculations. Nor could I think that it had occurred to him before our egregious doctor’s last visit, this very morning. Raffles had looked irritated as he broke the news to me from his pillow, and now there was some sympathy in the way he sat up in bed, as though he felt the thing himself.

“I am obliged to give in to the fellow,” said he. “He’s saving me from my friend, and I’m bound to humor him. But I can tell you that we’ve been arguing about you for the last half hour, Bunny. It was no use; the idiot has had his knife in you from the first; and he wouldn’t see me through on any other conditions.”

===Chinese===
“那你打算由着他了？”

我的屈辱与愤懑，一时找不出言语形容；虽说这两样此刻都还及不上我的惊愕。那桩惹出这一切麻烦的荒唐事，凡想得到的后果，我几乎都预见过；唯独拉夫尔斯与我主动分手这一条，确实从未进入过我的算盘。我也想不出，在咱们那位“了不得的”医生今早末次登门之前，他何曾起过此念。拉夫尔斯倚在枕上向我透露这消息时，神色已见烦躁；此刻他从床上坐起身，举止里倒带了几分体恤，仿佛他自己也感同身受。

“我不得不向这家伙低头，”他说。“他这是救我于我朋友之手，我也只好哄着他。可我告诉你，邦尼，为你的事，我们足足争了半个钟头。没有用；那蠢材从头一天起就存心跟你过不去；不依这个条件，他便不肯把我看到头。”

===Original===
“So he is going to see you through, is he?”

“It tots up to that,” said Raffles, looking at me rather hard. “At all events he has come to my rescue for the time being, and it’s for me to manage the rest. You don’t know what it has been, Bunny, these last few weeks; and gallantry forbids that I should tell you even now. But would you rather elope against your will, or have your continued existence made known to the world in general and the police in particular? That is practically the problem which I have had to solve, and the temporary solution was to fall ill. As a matter of fact, I am ill; and now what do you think? I owe it to you to tell you, Bunny, though it goes against the grain. She would take me ‘to the dear, warm underworld, where the sun really shines,’ and she would ‘nurse me back to life and love!’ The artistic temperament is a fearsome thing, Bunny, in a woman with the devil’s own will!”

Raffles tore up the letter from which he had read these piquant extracts, and lay back on the pillows with the tired air of the veritable invalid which he seemed able to assume at will. But for once he did look as though bed was the best place for him; and I used the fact as an argument for my own retention in defiance of Dr. Theobald. The town was full of typhoid, I said, and certainly that autumnal scourge was in the air. Did he want me to leave him at the very moment when he might be sickening for a serious illness?

===Chinese===
“这么说，他要把你看到头了，嗯？”

“合计起来，便是这么回事，”拉夫尔斯说，目光相当逼视地落在我身上。“至少眼下，是他来救了我这一场；余下的戏，得由我自己来唱。这几个星期是什么光景，你不知道，邦尼；骑士道也不许我如今讲给你听。可我问你：你是情愿被人押着私奔，还是情愿让世人——尤其是警方——得知你至今活在世上？这差不多就是我必得求解的那道题；而临时的解法，便是生病。况且说实在的，我也真病了；现在你猜怎么着？这件事我亏你的情，不能不对你说了，邦尼，虽然说得我逆着性子。她要带我‘到那亲爱温暖的下界去，那里的太阳才真正灿烂’，还要‘把我调养回来，重获生命与爱’！艺术家气质是样可怕的东西，邦尼——装在一个女人身上，再配上魔鬼本尊的意志！”

拉夫尔斯把那封被他摘出这些妙文的信撕了，躺回枕上，一副真病人式的倦怠——这副神气他似乎随心可取。可这一回，他看上去倒真像卧床才是最好的去处；我便拿这一条做文章，公然违抗西奥博尔德医生，主张把我自己留下。我说，城里正闹着伤寒；那场秋令时疫，确实已在空气里酝酿。莫非他偏要在我可能正染大病的当口，叫我离了他？

===Original===
“You know I don’t, my good fellow,” said Raffles, wearily; “but Theobald does, and I can’t afford to go against him now. Not that I really care what happens to me now that that woman knows I’m in the land of the living; she’ll let it out, to a dead certainty, and at the best there’ll be a hue and cry, which is the very thing I have escaped all these years. Now, what I want you to do is to go and take some quiet place somewhere, and then let me know, so that I may have a port in the storm when it breaks.”

“Now you’re talking!” I cried, recovering my spirits. “I thought you meant to go and drop a fellow altogether!”

“Exactly the sort of thing you would think,” rejoined Raffles, with a contempt that was welcome enough after my late alarm. “No, my dear rabbit, what you’ve got to do is to make a new burrow for us both. Try down the Thames, in some quiet nook that a literary man would naturally select. I’ve often thought that more use might be made of a boat, while the family are at dinner, than there ever has been yet. If Raffles is to come to life, old chap, he shall go a-Raffling for all he’s worth! There’s something to be done with a bicycle, too. Try Ham Common or Roehampton, or some such sleepy hollow a trifle off the line; and say you’re expecting your brother from the colonies.”

===Chinese===
“你知道我不愿意，我的好伙计，”拉夫尔斯倦倦地说，“可西奥博尔德愿意，而我现在得罪不起他。倒不是我真把自己的死活放在心上——那女人既已知道我还活在人间，她准会走漏出去，十拿九稳；往最好处说，也是一片画影图形的追捕喧嚣，而那正是我这些年侥幸躲掉的东西。现在我要你做的，是出去寻一处清静地方住下，然后捎信给我，好叫风暴来时，我手边有个避风港。”

“这才像话！”我叫起来，精神为之一振。“我还当你要把老伙计整个儿甩了呢！”

“你也就想得出这种事，”拉夫尔斯应道，那份轻蔑，在我方才那场虚惊之后，反倒叫人安心。“不，亲爱的兔子，你要办的是给咱们俩掘个新窝。去泰晤士河（Thames）下游一带试试，找一处文人雅士自然挑中的清静角落。我常想，趁人家全家用饭的当口，一条船能派的用场，比到如今为止用过的可多得多。拉夫尔斯若是还要还阳，老伙计，那他就要拿出看家的本事，出去好好‘拉夫尔斯’一番！自行车这东西，也有文章可做。去汉姆公地（Ham Common）或者罗汉普顿（Roehampton），或者随便哪个离铁路线稍稍偏一点的僻静角落试试；就说你正盼着你的兄弟从殖民地来。”

===Original===
Into this arrangement I entered without the slightest hesitation, for we had funds enough to carry it out on a comfortable scale, and Raffles placed a sufficient share at my disposal for the nonce. Moreover, I for one was only too glad to seek fresh fields and pastures new﻿—a phrase which I determined to interpret literally in my choice of fresh surroundings. I was tired of our submerged life in the poky little flat, especially now that we had money enough for better things. I myself of late had dark dealings with the receivers, with the result that poor Lord Ernest Belville’s successes were now indeed ours. Subsequent complications had been the more galling on that account, while the wanton way in which they had been created was the most irritating reflection of all. But it had brought its own punishment upon Raffles, and I fancied the lesson would prove salutary when we again settled down.

“If ever we do, Bunny!” said he, as I took his hand and told him how I was already looking forward to the time.

===Chinese===
这番安排，我毫无迟疑地应承下来：我们手头的款项，尽够办得舒舒服服；拉夫尔斯也当场拨出足用的一份，归我随意支取。再说，单讲我自己，能去寻“新的田野、新的牧场”，实在是求之不得——这成语，我打定主意照字面领会，用它来挑选新鲜的去处。蜗居在狭小公寓里的潜伏日子，我过够了；何况如今我们的钱，已够过上好些的日子。我自己近来就一直与窝主们暗中有往来，结果是：可怜的欧内斯特·贝尔维尔勋爵（Lord Ernest Belville）名下那些得手的买卖，如今确实归到了我们名下。也因此，后来的种种纠葛愈发叫人咬牙；而这一切又是被人那样肆意地生出来的，想来想去，最叫人窝火。好在它已在拉夫尔斯身上自食其果，我想等我们再度安顿下来，这个教训必有益处。

“但愿真有那一天，邦尼！”我握住他的手，告诉他我已如何翘首盼着那一天时，他这样应道。

===Original===
“But of course we will!” I cried, concealing the resentment at leaving him which his tone and his appearance renewed in my breast.

“I’m not so sure of it,” he said, gloomily. “I’m in somebody’s clutches, and I’ve got to get out of them first.”

“I’ll sit tight until you do.”

“Well,” he said, “if you don’t see me in ten days you never will.”

===Chinese===
“那还用说，自然会有！”我喊道，一面掩住胸中重新涌起的离怨——他那语气、他那病容，又把它勾了上来。

“我可没那么大的把握，”他阴郁地说。“我如今落在什么人的爪子里，总得先挣脱出来。”

“你一日不脱身，我便一日守着不动。”

“那么，”他说，“十天里见不着我，你就永远见不着了。”

===Original===
“Only ten days?” I echoed. “That’s nothing at all.”

“A lot may happen in ten days,” replied Raffles, in the same depressing tone, so very depressing in him; and with that he held out his hand a second time, and dropped mine suddenly after as sudden a pressure for farewell.

I left the flat in considerable dejection after all, unable to decide whether Raffles was really ill, or only worried as I knew him to be. And at the foot of the stairs the author of my dismissal, that confounded Theobald, flung open his door and waylaid me.

===Chinese===
“才十天？”我应道。“这算得了什么。”

“十天里能出的事多了，”拉夫尔斯答道，还是那副叫人丧气的腔调——这腔调出在他身上，尤其叫人丧气；说罢，他第二次伸出手来，蓦地一握，握罢又蓦地撒开，就此作别。

末了我还是怀着好大一阵沮丧离开了公寓，断不定拉夫尔斯是真病了，还是只像我知道的那样在发愁。楼梯脚下，打发我走的那位始作俑者——可恶的西奥博尔德——敞开房门，半路把我截住了。

===Original===
“Are you going?” he demanded.

The traps in my hands proclaimed that I was, but I dropped them at his feet to have it out with him then and there.

“Yes,” I answered fiercely, “thanks to you!”

“Well, my good fellow,” he said, his full-blooded face lightening and softening at the same time, as though a load were off his mind, “it’s no pleasure to me to deprive any man of his billet, but you never were a nurse, and you know that as well as I do.”

===Chinese===
“要走啦？”他问。

我手里的行李袋早替我招认了；我却把它们往他脚边一撂，要当场同他见个分明。

“走，”我恶狠狠地答，“托福，都托你的福！”

“唉，我的好伙计，”他说，那张血色旺盛的脸霎时又开朗又柔和，仿佛心头卸下了一桩重担，“夺哪个人的饭碗，于我都不是乐事；可你压根儿不是当护士的料，这一点，你跟我一样心里有数。”

===Original===
I began to wonder what he meant, and how much he did know, and my speculations kept me silent. “But come in here a moment,” he continued, just as I decided that he knew nothing at all. And, leading me into his minute consulting-room, Dr. Theobald solemnly presented me with a sovereign by way of compensation, which I pocketed as solemnly, and with as much gratitude as if I had not fifty of them distributed over my person as it was. The good fellow had quite forgotten my social status, about which he himself had been so particular at our earliest interview; but he had never accustomed himself to treat me as a gentleman, and I do not suppose he had been improving his memory by the tall tumbler which I saw him poke behind a photograph as we entered.

“There’s one thing I should like to know before I go,” said I, turning suddenly on the doctor’s mat, “and that is whether Mr. Maturin is really ill or not!”

===Chinese===
我开始捉摸他这话是何用意、他又知道多少；这一揣度，倒叫我住了口。“不过，进来待一小会儿，”他又说——恰在此刻，我断定他其实一无所知。他把我让进他那间斗大的诊室；西奥博尔德医生郑重其事地捧出一枚金镑给我，权作补偿；我同样郑重其事地把它揣了兜里，感激之情一分不缺——仿佛我周身各口袋里这时没有散放着五十来枚似的。这位老好人早把我昔日的社会身份忘了个干净——头一回见面时，对这一点他自己可是锱铢必较的；况且他从来不曾习惯以绅士之礼待我；再说，进屋时我亲眼见他往一张相片后头塞了一只高脚杯，我想那玩意儿于他的记性，只怕也谈不上什么补益。

“走之前，还有一件事我想知道，”我说着，在医生门口的蹭鞋垫上蓦地转过身来，“那就是：马图林先生到底是不是真的病了！”

===Original===
I meant, of course, at the present moment, but Dr. Theobald braced himself like a recruit at the drill-sergeant’s voice.

“Of course he is,” he snapped﻿—“so ill as to need a nurse who can nurse, by way of a change.”

With that his door shut in my face, and I had to go my way, in the dark as to whether he had mistaken my meaning, and was telling me a lie, or not.

===Chinese===
我指的当然是眼下一刻；可西奥博尔德医生浑身一绷，活像新兵听见了操练军士的口令。

“当然是，”他厉声道——“病得非换一个会看护的看护不可了。”

说罢，门便冲着我的脸关上了；我只好走我的路，心里一团黑：他是听岔了我的意思呢，还是在对我说谎。

===Original===
But for my misgivings upon this point I might have extracted some very genuine enjoyment out of the next few days. I had decent clothes to my back, with money, as I say, in most of the pockets, and more freedom to spend it than was possible in the constant society of a man whose personal liberty depended on a universal supposition that he was dead. Raffles was as bold as ever, and I as fond of him, but whereas he would run any risk in a professional exploit, there were many innocent recreations still open to me which would have been sheer madness in him. He could not even watch a match, from the sixpenny seats, at Lord’s cricket-ground, where the Gentlemen were every year in a worse way without him. He never travelled by rail, and dining out was a risk only to be run with some ulterior object in view. In fact, much as it had changed, Raffles could no longer show his face with perfect impunity in any quarter or at any hour. Moreover, after the lesson he had now learnt, I foresaw increased caution on his part in this respect. But I myself was under no such perpetual disadvantage, and, while what was good enough for Raffles was quite good enough for me so long as we were together, I saw no harm in profiting by the present opportunity of “doing myself well.”

Such were my reflections on the way to Richmond in a hansom cab. Richmond had struck us both as the best centre of operations in search of the suburban retreat which Raffles wanted, and by road, in a well-appointed, well-selected hansom, was certainly the most agreeable way of getting there. In a week or ten days Raffles was to write to me at the Richmond post-office, but for at least a week I should be “on my own.” It was not an unpleasant sensation as I leant back in the comfortable hansom, and rather to one side, in order to have a good look at myself in the bevelled mirror that is almost as great an improvement in these vehicles as the rubber tires. Really I was not an ill-looking youth, if one may call one’s self such at the age of thirty. I could lay no claim either to the striking cast of countenance or to the peculiar charm of expression which made the face of Raffles like no other in the world. But this very distinction was in itself a danger, for its impression was indelible, whereas I might still have been mistaken for a hundred other young fellows at large in London. Incredible as it may appear to the moralists, I had sustained no external hallmark by my term of imprisonment, and I am vain enough to believe that the evil which I did had not a separate existence in my face. This afternoon, indeed, I was struck by the purity of my fresh complexion, and rather depressed by the general innocence of the visage which peered into mine from the little mirror. My straw-colored moustache, grown in the flat after a protracted holiday, again preserved the most disappointing dimensions, and was still invisible in certain lights without wax. So far from discerning the desperate criminal who has “done time” once, and deserved it over and over again, the superior but superficial observer might have imagined that he detected a certain element of folly in my face.

===Chinese===
若不是这一点上心存疑团，随后那几天，我本可以捞到些十分地道的享受。我身上有像样的衣裳，如我所说，大半口袋里都有钱，花起来也比往日自在——从前成天厮守着一个人，那人的身家性命全系于世人一概认定他已死这一桩上，可没这份自由。拉夫尔斯胆气一如既往，我眷恋他也一如既往；只是干本行时他什么险都肯冒，而许多无害的消遣，于我仍然敞开着门，于他却无异于纯粹的疯癫。他连坐在罗德板球场的六便士看台上看一场比赛都办不到——那座球场上，绅士队（the Gentlemen）一年不如一年，正为少了他。他从不坐火车；出门赴宴，更是非别有所图不肯一冒的险。总之，尽管世道已大不如前，拉夫尔斯再不能在任何地界、任何时辰毫无后患地露脸了。何况经过这一番教训，我预感他在这上头还会愈发谨慎。我自己却没这层与身相随的亏空；拉夫尔斯受用的排场，只要我们在一起，于我尽够受用；如今既有这个“让自家过得滋润些”的机会，我觉得趁一趁也无伤大雅。

乘着双轮马车往里士满（Richmond）去的一路，我心里转的便是这些。要寻拉夫尔斯要的那处郊外退居，里士满在我们两人看来都是最好的行动中枢；而走公路、坐一辆车马讲究、挑选得宜的双轮马车过去，无疑是诸般走法里最惬意的一种。一星期或十天之后，拉夫尔斯会写信到里士满邮局给我；但至少有一个星期，我要“自个儿当家”。往那舒适的车厢里一靠——还得稍稍偏侧着身子，好对着那面车边镜把自己端详个够——这滋味并不坏。这面镜子之于此类马车，几乎是堪与橡胶轮胎并列的一大改良。说实在的，我不算个难看的青年——三十岁的人还自称青年，但愿说得过去。拉夫尔斯那张世上独一无二的脸，一靠轮廊之夺目，二靠神情之别有魅力；这两样，我一样也认领不来。可正是这份独一无二，本身便是一桩危险：它给人的印象无可磨灭；而我呢，仍旧可能被错认作伦敦满街一百个闲逛青年里的随便哪一个。在道学家看来或许难以置信：蹲过一遭监狱，我竟不曾落下任何外在的烙印；我也虚荣得可以，竟至相信我作下的恶，并没有在我的脸上单独活出一张脸来。真的，那天下午，我为自家气色的鲜洁莹润吃了一惊，又为小镜里那张端详着我的脸那副通体无辜的神气，颇有些丧气。我那部麦秆色的小胡子，在公寓里歇了长假之后重新蓄起，尺寸照旧失望得很，不上蜡，在某些光线里依然看它不见。别说认出一个“蹲过号子”一遭、且十遭八遭都该蹲的亡命惯犯；那位高明而肤浅的观察者，说不定反倒会以为，在我脸上窥见了几分呆气。

===Original===
At all events it was not the face to shut the doors of a first-class hotel against me, without accidental evidence of a more explicit kind, and it was with no little satisfaction that I directed the man to drive to the Star and Garter. I also told him to go through Richmond Park, though he warned me that it would add considerably to the distance and his fare. It was autumn, and it struck me that the tints would be fine. And I had learnt from Raffles to appreciate such things, even amid the excitement of an audacious enterprise.

If I dwell upon my appreciation of this occasion it is because, like most pleasures, it was exceedingly short-lived. I was very comfortable at the Star and Garter, which was so empty that I had a room worthy of a prince, where I could enjoy the finest of all views (in patriotic opinion) every morning while I shaved. I walked many miles through the noble park, over the commons of Ham and Wimbledon, and one day as far as that of Esher, where I was forcibly reminded of a service we once rendered to a distinguished resident in this delightful locality. But it was on Ham Common, one of the places which Raffles had mentioned as specially desirable, that I actually found an almost ideal retreat. This was a cottage where I heard, on inquiry, that rooms were to be let in the summer. The landlady, a motherly body, of visible excellence, was surprised indeed at receiving an application for the winter months; but I have generally found that the title of “author,” claimed with an air, explains every little innocent irregularity of conduct or appearance, and even requires something of the kind to carry conviction to the lay intelligence. The present case was one in point, and when I said that I could only write in a room facing north, on mutton chops and milk, with a cold ham in the wardrobe in case of nocturnal inspiration, to which I was liable, my literary character was established beyond dispute. I secured the rooms, paid a month’s rent in advance at my own request, and moped in them dreadfully until the week was up and Raffles due any day. I explained that the inspiration would not come, and asked abruptly if the mutton was New Zealand.

===Chinese===
无论如何，单凭这张脸，头等酒店还不至于对我闭门不纳——除非再凑巧撞上更露骨的证据。于是我吩咐车夫赶往星嘉德酒店（Star and Garter），心里委实得意。我还让他打里士满公园里穿行，虽然他提醒我，路程和车费都要添上不少。正是秋天，我想那一林秋色必定可观。这类景致的情味，我已从拉夫尔斯处学得了领受，哪怕正当一桩胆大包天的买卖干得兴起之时，也照赏不误。

我之所以对这一段的受用絮叨不休，是因为它同大多数快乐一样，短得出奇。星嘉德酒店里空落落的，我住的那间房配得上亲王，每天早晨刮脸时都能领受（按爱国舆论的说法）天下第一的景致，好不安逸。我在那座气派的园子里走了许多英里，踏遍汉姆与温布尔登（Wimbledon）的公地，有一天一直走到埃舍尔（Esher）公地；在那儿，我不由得想起我们从前在这处妙境为某位显赫居民效过的一次劳。不过，真正让我寻到一处近乎理想的退居的，还是汉姆公地——拉夫尔斯点名属意的地段之一。那是一座村舍；一打听，说夏季有房间出租。房东太太是位慈母般的人物，一望便知贤德，听说竟有人申请租住过冬，着实吃了一惊；可我一向发现，“作家”这头衔，只要端着架子认领下来，行止或仪表上任何一点无伤大雅的古怪便都有了说法，甚至非得带上几分古怪，才能叫外行的头脑信服。眼下这桩便是一例：待我说，我写作只在一间朝北的屋里，只以羊排和牛奶糊口，衣柜里还得常备一只冷火腿，以防我随时会犯的夜间灵感——我这文学人格，便板上钉钉了。我定下房间，主动预付了一个月租金，随后在里头闷得要死，直熬到那一礼拜期满、拉夫尔斯随时会来。我解释说灵感迟迟不肯降临，又没头没脑地问了句：这羊肉可是新西兰（New Zealand）货？

===Original===
Thrice had I made fruitless inquiries at the Richmond post-office; but on the tenth day I was in and out almost every hour. Not a word was there for me up to the last post at night. Home I trudged to Ham with horrible forebodings, and back again to Richmond after breakfast next morning. Still there was nothing. I could bear it no more. At ten minutes to eleven I was climbing the station stairs at Earl’s Court.

It was a wretched morning there, a weeping mist shrouding the long, straight street, and clinging to one’s face in clammy caresses. I felt how much better it was down at Ham, as I turned into our side street, and saw the flats looming like mountains, the chimney-pots hidden in the mist. At our entrance stood a nebulous conveyance, that I took at first for a tradesman’s van; to my horror it proved to be a hearse; and all at once the white breath ceased upon my lips.

===Chinese===
在里士满邮局，我白打听了三回；到第十天，我几乎每个钟头都要进出一趟。直到夜里末班收信，也没有我半个字。我揣着一肚子凶兆，一步步蹭回汉姆；第二天早饭后，又一趟赶回里士满。依然杳无音信。我再也熬不住了。十一点差十分，我已经在攀厄尔斯考特车站的台阶。

那天早晨，那边天色愁惨：一片泪雾裹住又长又直的街，湿冷黏腻地贴上人的脸。拐进我们那条小街时，我只觉汉姆那边不知强出多少——眼前，公寓楼如群山般隐隐压来，烟囱管帽全没进了雾里。楼门口停着一辆影影绰绰的车，我起先只当是哪家铺子的送货马车；待看清竟是灵车，我唇边的白气霎时凝住了。

===Original===
I had looked up at our windows and the blinds were down!

I rushed within. The doctor’s door stood open. I neither knocked nor rang, but found him in his consulting-room with red eyes and a blotchy face. Otherwise he was in solemn black from head to heel.

“Who is dead?” I burst out. “Who is dead?”

===Chinese===
我抬头望我们的窗——窗帘全放了下来！

我冲进门去。医生的房门敞着。我不敲也不按铃，径直在诊室里寻着了他：红着眼睛，一脸斑驳。此外，他从头到脚，一身肃穆的黑。

“谁死了？”我脱口喊道。“谁死了？”

===Original===
The red eyes looked redder than ever as Dr. Theobald opened them at the unwarrantable sight of me; and he was terribly slow in answering. But in the end he did answer, and did not kick me out as he evidently had a mind.

“Mr. Maturin,” he said, and sighed like a beaten man.

I said nothing. It was no surprise to me. I had known it all these minutes. Nay, I had dreaded this from the first, had divined it at the last, though to the last also I had refused to entertain my own conviction. Raffles dead! A real invalid after all! Raffles dead, and on the point of burial!

===Chinese===
西奥博尔德医生一见我这全无资格露面的人影，睁大了那双红眼，红得愈发吓人；他答话答得慢极了。但末了他到底答了，也没有像我分明看出的那样，想把我一脚踹出去。

“马图林先生。”他说，叹得像个一败涂地的人。

我一言不发。这于我并非新闻。这几分钟里，我早知道了。不，我从头一天怕的便是这个，到末了也早已看破——只是直到末了，我仍不肯容自己把心里那点确信当真。拉夫尔斯死了！他到底真是个病人！拉夫尔斯死了，而且眼看就要下葬了！

===Original===
“What did he die of?” I asked, unconsciously drawing on that fund of grim self-control which the weakest of us seem to hold in reserve for real calamity.

“Typhoid,” he answered. “Kensington is full of it.”

“He was sickening for it when I left, and you knew it, and could get rid of me then!”

“My good fellow, I was obliged to have a more experienced nurse for that very reason.”

===Chinese===
“他死于什么病？”我问，不知不觉支取了那一宗冷硬的自制——我们这些最软弱的人，似乎都把它存着专备真正的大祸。

“伤寒，”他答。“肯辛顿（Kensington）一带闹满了这病。”

“我走的时候，他就在往这病里去了；你是知道的，正好赶那时把我打发掉！”

“我的好伙计，正是为这个，我才不得不换一位经验更老的看护。”

===Original===
The doctor’s tone was so conciliatory that I remembered in an instant what a humbug the man was, and became suddenly possessed with the vague conviction that he was imposing upon me now.

“Are you sure it was typhoid at all?” I cried fiercely to his face. “Are you sure it wasn’t suicide﻿—or murder?”

I confess that I can see little point in this speech as I write it down, but it was what I said in a burst of grief and of wild suspicion; nor was it without effect upon Dr. Theobald, who turned bright scarlet from his well-brushed hair to his immaculate collar.

“Do you want me to throw you out into the street?” he cried; and all at once I remembered that I had come to Raffles as a perfect stranger, and for his sake might as well preserve that character to the last.

===Chinese===
医生的语气如此赔着小心，我霎时记起这家伙原是个多么地道的大骗子，蓦地又生出个说不清的念头：他这会儿正在蒙我。

“你拿得准真是伤寒么？”我冲着他的脸厉声道。“你拿得准不是自尽——不是谋杀？”

我承认，这话落到纸面上，看不出什么道理；可当时我正是悲从中来、疑窦狂生，冲口说了出来；它在西奥博尔德医生身上也并非全无功效：他从梳得溜光的头发到一尘不染的领子，霎时涨得赤红。

“要我把你扔到街上去么？”他嚷道；我这才猛然想起：我到拉夫尔斯这里来时，本是个彻头彻尾的生人；为他的缘故，这角色索性演到底也罢。

===Original===
“I beg your pardon,” I said, brokenly. “He was so good to me﻿—I became so attached to him. You forget I am originally of his class.”

“I did forget it,” replied Theobald, looking relieved at my new tone, “and I beg *your* pardon for doing so. Hush! They are bringing him down. I must have a drink before we start, and you’d better join me.”

There was no pretence about his drink this time, and a pretty stiff one it was, but I fancy my own must have run it hard. In my case it cast a merciful haze over much of the next hour, which I can truthfully describe as one of the most painful of my whole existence. I can have known very little of what I was doing. I only remember finding myself in a hansom, suddenly wondering why it was going so slowly, and once more awaking to the truth. But it was to the truth itself more than to the liquor that I must have owed my dazed condition. My next recollection is of looking down into the open grave, in a sudden passionate anxiety to see the name for myself. It was not the name of my friend, of course, but it was the one under which he had passed for many months.

===Chinese===
“请您原谅，”我哽咽道。“他待我那么好——我跟他处出了感情。您忘了，我原就跟他是一路人。”

“我确实忘了，”西奥博尔德答道，见我改了口风，如释重负，“为此我请求*您*原谅。嘘！他们正把他抬下来。动身前我得喝一口，您也最好陪我一杯。”

这一回，他那杯酒可是半点不掺假，而且相当凶；不过我那杯，怕是与之旗鼓相当。就我而论，它把随后的一个钟头大半罩进了一层仁慈的迷雾——那一小时，我可以毫不夸张地算作平生最痛苦的一小时。当时我干了些什么，我知道的只怕极少。我只记得发现自己坐在一辆双轮马车里，忽然纳罕它为何走得这样慢，随即又一次醒悟到真相。可我这昏昏沉沉，多半该记在真相本身的账上，而非那杯酒的账上。再往下的记忆，是我俯望那敞开的墓穴，突然按捺不住地非要亲眼看看那个名字。那自然不是我朋友的名字，却是他顶了许多个月的那个。

===Original===
I was still stupefied by a sense of inconceivable loss, and had not raised my eyes from that which was slowly forcing me to realize what had happened, when there was a rustle at my elbow, and a shower of hothouse flowers passed before them, falling like huge snowflakes where my gaze had rested. I looked up, and at my side stood a majestic figure in deep mourning. The face was carefully veiled, but I was too close not to recognize the masterful beauty whom the world knew as Jacques Saillard. I had no sympathy with her; on the contrary, my blood boiled with the vague conviction that in some way she was responsible for this death. Yet she was the only woman present﻿—there were not a half a dozen of us altogether﻿—and her flowers were the only flowers.

The melancholy ceremony was over, and Jacques Saillard had departed in a funeral brougham, evidently hired for the occasion. I had watched her drive away, and the sight of my own cabman, making signs to me through the fog, had suddenly reminded me that I had bidden him to wait. I was the last to leave, and had turned my back upon the gravediggers, already at their final task, when a hand fell lightly but firmly upon my shoulder.

===Chinese===
我仍被那难以置信的损失弄得麻木，目光还没有离开那件正慢慢逼我认清现实的东西；肘边忽起一阵窸窣，一大捧温室鲜花自我眼前掠过，像大片大片的雪花，落在我方才注视的地方。我抬起头，身旁立着一身重孝的威严身影。那张脸用面纱遮得严严实实；可我离得太近，不会认不出那位举世称作雅克·萨亚尔的霸气美人。我对她毫无同情；恰恰相反，我血往上涌，只隐隐认定：这桩死亡，多少与她脱不了干系。然而在场的女人，只有她一个——我们拢共不到六个人——花，也只有她那一捧。

忧伤的仪式结束了，雅克·萨亚尔坐着一辆显然为这场面临时雇下的丧仪马车离去。我目送她走远；自家的车夫隔着雾向我做手势，才忽然提醒我吩咐过他等着。我最后一个离开，正要转身背向那些已在收尾的掘墓人，一只手轻轻却不容分说地落在我肩上。

===Original===
“I don’t want to make a scene in a cemetery,” said a voice, in a not unkindly, almost confidential whisper. “Will you get into your own cab and come quietly?”

“Who on earth are you?” I exclaimed.

I now remembered having seen the fellow hovering about during the funeral, and subconsciously taking him for the undertaker’s head man. He had certainly that appearance, and even now I could scarcely believe that he was anything else.

“My name won’t help you,” he said, pityingly. “But you will guess where I come from when I tell you I have a warrant for your arrest.”

===Chinese===
“我不想在墓园里闹出一场好戏，”一个声音说，口气不算凶狠，几乎像咬耳朵的知心话。“请你上你自己的车，安安静静地跟我走一趟，可好？”

“你究竟是什么人？”我失声道。

我这才想起，葬礼期间曾见这家伙在近旁转悠，下意识里把他当成了殡仪馆的领班。他生就的确是那副模样，乃至此刻，我仍难相信他还是别的什么人。

“我的名字，帮不上你什么忙，”他怜悯地说。“可我一告诉你，我手里有一张拘捕你的拘票，你便猜得着我从哪儿来了。”

===Original===
My sensations at this announcement may not be believed, but I solemnly declare that I have seldom experienced so fierce a satisfaction. Here was a new excitement in which to drown my grief; here was something to think about; and I should be spared the intolerable experience of a solitary return to the little place at Ham. It was as though I had lost a limb and someone had struck me so hard in the face that the greater agony was forgotten. I got into the hansom without a word, my captor following at my heels, and giving his own directions to the cabman before taking his seat. The word “station” was the only one I caught, and I wondered whether it was to be Bow Street again. My companion’s next words, however, or rather the tone in which he uttered them, destroyed my capacity for idle speculation.

“Mr. Maturin!” said he. “Mr. Maturin indeed!”

“Well,” said I, “what about him?”

===Chinese===
这句话在我身上激起的感受，说出来未必有人肯信；可我郑重声明：我平生很少尝过那样强烈的快意。有了这桩新刺激，正好把悲伤溺死在里头；有了可想的事；而且独自回汉姆那处小屋的难堪路途，也免了。这就好比断了一条胳膊，又冷不防挨人照脸上狠狠一拳，大痛顿时盖过了小痛。我一言不发上了车；捉我的人紧随脚跟，先向车夫交代了他自己的路线，方才落座。“警察局”是我唯一听清的字眼，我不禁揣度，这回是不是又要轮到弓街（Bow Street）。然而我这位同伴的下一句话——不如说他说这话的腔调——把我闲猜的本事毁了个干净。

“马图林先生！”他说。“亏他还马图林先生！”

“怎么，”我说，“他怎么了？”

===Original===
“Do you think we don’t know who he was?”

“Who was he?” I asked, defiantly.

“You ought to know,” said he. “You got locked up through him the other time, too. His favorite name was Raffles then.”

“It was his real name,” I said, indignantly. “And he has been dead for years.”

===Chinese===
“你以为我们不知道他是谁么？”

“他是谁？”我梗着脖子问。

“你该知道的，”他说。“上一回，你也为这个人蹲过班房。那时候他顶爱用的名字，就是拉夫尔斯。”

“那是他的真名，”我愤然道。“再说，他早已死了好几年了。”

===Original===
My captor simply chuckled.

“He’s at the bottom of the sea, I tell you!”

But I do not know why I should have told him with such spirit, for what could it matter to Raffles now? I did not think; instinct was still stronger than reason, and, fresh from his funeral, I had taken up the cudgels for my dead friend as though he were still alive. Next moment I saw this for myself, and my tears came nearer the surface than they had been yet; but the fellow at my side laughed outright.

===Chinese===
捉我的人只是嘿嘿一笑。

“告诉你，他早沉到海底去了！”

可我也不明白，自己为何偏要这样起劲地告诉他——如今这与拉夫尔斯还有什么相干？我没有想；本能仍强过理智；才从他的葬礼上下来，我已像他仍活着一般，替亡友挺身迎战了。下一刻，我自己也看破了这一点，眼泪险些夺眶而出；可身旁那家伙竟放声笑了。

===Original===
“Shall I tell you something else?” said he.

“As you like.”

“He’s not even at the bottom of that grave! He’s no more dead than you or I, and a sham burial is his latest piece of villainy!”

===Chinese===
“要不要再告诉你一件事？”他说。

“随你的便。”

“他连那座坟的底都没沾着！他跟你我一样，活得好好的；假葬礼，不过是他最新的那件恶行！”

===Original===
I doubt whether I could have spoken if I had tried. I did not try. I had no use for speech. I did not even ask him if he was sure, I was so sure myself. It was all as plain to me as riddles usually are when one has the answer. The doctor’s alarms, his unscrupulous venality, the simulated illness, my own dismissal, each fitted in its obvious place, and not even the last had power as yet to mar my joy in the one central fact to which all the rest were as tapers to the sun.

“He is alive!” I cried. “Nothing else matters﻿—he is alive!”

At last I did ask whether they had got him too; but thankful as I was for the greater knowledge, I confess that I did not much care what answer I received. Already I was figuring out how much we might each get, and how old we should be when we came out. But my companion tilted his hat to the back of his head, at the same time putting his face close to mine, and compelling my scrutiny. And my answer, as you have already guessed, was the face of Raffles himself, superbly disguised (but less superbly than his voice), and yet so thinly that I should have known him in a trice had I not been too miserable in the beginning to give him a second glance.

===Chinese===
只怕我想要开口，也开不了口。我没有试。我用不着说话。我甚至没有问他是否拿得稳——我自己已经稳到了十分。这一切于我豁然开朗，恰似谜语一旦有了答案。医生的惊慌、他那无所忌惮的贪财、那场装出来的病、我被扫地出门——件件各归其位，一望即知；就连最后这一件，一时也还无力败坏我心中那一桩唯一大事的欢喜——其余种种之于它，不过是细烛之于太阳。

“他活着！”我喊道。“别的都不要紧——他活着！”

到末了，我到底问了：他们是否连他也一并拿了？可尽管为那桩更大的真相感激不尽，我承认，得到什么回答，我都已不太在意。我已经在盘算：咱们一人能判多少，出来时该多大岁数了。不想我这位同伴随手把帽子往后脑一推，同时把脸凑近我，逼我细看。而我得到的回答——你们早已猜到——正是拉夫尔斯本人那张脸：化装得精妙绝伦（只是嗓音稍逊一筹），却又薄薄一层，一望即知；可惜起初我愁得太狠，没肯赏他第二眼。

===Original===
Jacques Saillard had made his life impossible, and this was the one escape. Raffles had bought the doctor for a thousand pounds, and the doctor had bought a “nurse” of his own kidney, on his own account; me, for some reason, he would not trust; he had insisted upon my dismissal as an essential preliminary to his part in the conspiracy. Here the details were half-humorous, half-gruesome, each in turn as Raffles told me the story. At one period he had been very daringly drugged indeed, and, in his own words, “as dead as a man need be”; but he had left strict instructions that nobody but the nurse and “my devoted physician” should “lay a finger on me” afterwards; and by virtue of this proviso a library of books (largely acquired for the occasion) had been impiously interred at Kensal Green. Raffles had definitely undertaken not to trust me with the secret, and, but for my untoward appearance at the funeral (which he had attended for his own final satisfaction), I was assured and am convinced that he would have kept his promise to the letter. In explaining this he gave me the one explanation I desired, and in another moment we turned into Praed Street, Paddington.

“And I thought you said Bow Street!” said I. “Are you coming straight down to Richmond with me?”

===Chinese===
雅克·萨亚尔已把他的日子搅得没法过，这便是唯一的脱身之计。拉夫尔斯出一千镑收买了医生，医生又自作主张，买通了一位跟他一路货色的“护士”；至于我，出于某种缘故他信不过；他非要先把我打发走，作为他入伙这桩密谋的必要前提。这里头的细节，经拉夫尔斯一一道来，半是好笑，半是骇人。有一阵子他着实被下药下得胆大包天，用他自己的话说，“死得一个男人所能死的那样透”；但他留有严令：事后除护士和“我那位忠诚的医生”外，任何人不得“碰我一根指头”；多亏这一条，一大堆书（多半是专为这场戏添置的）被大不敬地葬进了肯萨尔绿野公墓（Kensal Green）。拉夫尔斯明确承诺过，不把秘密托付给我；倘若不是我在葬礼上不巧露面（他到场原是为着亲眼看个最后的心安），我敢担保、至今也笃信——他本会把那句承诺履行到一字不差。讲清这一节时，他把我最想要的那一个解释给了我；说话间，我们已拐进帕丁顿（Paddington）的普拉德街（Praed Street）。

“我还当你说的是弓街呢！”我说。“这就直接随我下里士满去？”

===Original===
“I may as well,” said Raffles, “though I did mean to get my kit first, so as to start in fair and square as the long-lost brother from the bush. That’s why I hadn’t written. The function was a day later than I calculated. I was going to write tonight.”

“But what are we to do?” said I, hesitating when he had paid the cab. “I have been playing the colonies for all they are worth!”

“Oh, I’ve lost my luggage,” said he, “or a wave came into my cabin and spoilt every stitch, or I had nothing fit to bring ashore. We’ll settle that in the train.”

===Chinese===
“正好顺路，”拉夫尔斯说，“不过我原打算先取我的行装，好正正经经登场，做那位从殖民地丛莽间失散多年、刚刚归来的兄弟。所以一直没写信。仪式比我算的晚了一天；我本打算今晚就写。”

“可咱们怎么办呢？”车钱付讫，我踌躇着问。“殖民地这张牌，我已经打到山穷水尽了！”

“哦，我把行李丢了，”他说，“要么就是海浪打进船舱，泡坏了每一件行头，要么就是我压根没有一件像样的能带得上岸。这个，咱们上火车再把它编圆。”

## The Wrong House / 走错了门

===Original===
My brother Ralph, who now lived with me on the edge of Ham Common, had come home from Australia with a curious affection of the eyes, due to long exposure to the glare out there, and necessitating the use of clouded spectacles in the open air. He had not the rich complexion of the typical colonist, being indeed peculiarly pale, but it appeared that he had been confined to his berth for the greater part of the voyage, while his prematurely gray hair was sufficient proof that the rigors of bush life had at last undermined an originally tough constitution. Our landlady, who spoilt my brother from the first, was much concerned on his behalf, and wished to call in the local doctor; but Ralph said dreadful things about the profession, and quite frightened the good woman by arbitrarily forbidding her ever to let a doctor inside her door. I had to apologize to her for the painful prejudices and violent language of “these colonists,” but the old soul was easily mollified. She had fallen in love with my brother at first sight, and she never could do too much for him. It was owing to our landlady that I took to calling him Ralph, for the first time in our lives, on her beginning to speak of and to him as “Mr. Raffles.”
“This won’t do,” said he to me. “It’s a name that sticks.”
“It must be my fault! She must have heard it from me,” said I self-reproachfully.

===Chinese===
