我的哥哥拉尔夫（Ralph）如今同我一起住在汉姆公地（Ham Common）边上，他是从澳大利亚回来的，眼睛染上了一种古怪的毛病，缘于长年暴露在那边的强烈炫光之下，一到露天便须戴上磨砂眼镜。他并没有典型殖民地客的那种红润面色，实则反倒苍白得出奇，不过据称航行途中他大半时日都困守舱位，而他那过早花白的头发更足以证明：丛林生涯的严酷，终究摧垮了一副原本结实的筋骨。我们的房东太太打头一天起就把我哥哥宠得没边，为他的身子焦心得不行，一心要请本地医生；拉尔夫却把那个行当咒骂了个遍，还专横地下令禁止她日后放任何一个医生进门，把好心的妇人吓得够呛。我只得替“这些殖民地客”的痛心偏见与暴烈言辞向她赔不是，好在老人家一哄便软。她对我哥哥一见倾心，为他做什么都嫌不够。也正是拜这位房东太太所赐，我此生头一回改口唤他拉尔夫——只因她开口把他说成、也当面称他“拉夫尔斯先生（Mr. Raffles）。”

“这样可不行，”他对我说，“这个名字，一沾上就甩不掉。”

“准是我的不是！她准是听我说漏了嘴，”我自怨自艾道。

===Original===
“You must tell her it’s the short for Ralph.”
“But it’s longer.”
“It’s the short,” said he; “and you’ve got to tell her so.”
Henceforth I heard as much of “Mr. Ralph,” his likes and dislikes, what he would fancy and what he would not, and oh, what a dear gentleman he was, that I often remembered to say “Ralph, old chap,” myself.

===Chinese===
“你必须告诉她，‘拉夫尔斯’是‘拉尔夫’的昵称。”

“可它反倒更长。”

“它就是简称，”他说，“而且你非得这么告诉她不可。”

从此，我耳朵里灌满了“拉尔夫先生”的种种——他的好恶，他中意什么、不中意什么，还有哎呀，他是位多么可亲的绅士——以至于我自己也时常记得唤上一声“拉尔夫，老伙计”。

===Original===
It was an ideal cottage, as I said when I found it, and in it our delicate man became rapidly robust. Not that the air was also ideal, for, when it was not raining, we had the same faithful mist from November to March. But it was something to Ralph to get any air at all, other than night-air, and the bicycle did the rest. We taught ourselves, and may I never forget our earlier rides, through and through Richmond Park when the afternoons were shortest, upon the incomparable Ripley Road when we gave a day to it. Raffles rode a Beeston Humber, a Royal Sunbeam was good enough for me, but he insisted on our both having Dunlop tires.
“They seem the most popular brand. I had my eye on the road all the way from Ripley to Cobham, and there were more Dunlop marks than any other kind. Bless you, yes, they all leave their special tracks, and we don’t want ours to be extra special; the Dunlop’s like a rattlesnake, and the Palmer leaves telegraph-wires, but surely the serpent is more in our line.”

===Chinese===
那是一座理想的乡舍，我当初寻到它时便是这么说的；住进去以后，我们那位文弱先生很快便硬朗起来。倒不是说那儿的空气也称得上理想，因为只要不下雨，从十一月到三月，始终有同一场忠实的雾陪伴我们。不过对拉尔夫而言，除了夜气，能沾到任何空气总是好的，余下的事便交给自行车。我们是无师自通，但愿我永远别忘掉早先的那些骑行：趁着白昼最短的午后，横穿整个里士满公园（Richmond Park）；若肯豁出一整天，便去驰骋那条无与伦比的利普利路（Ripley Road）。拉夫尔斯骑一辆比斯顿·亨伯（Beeston Humber），我有一辆皇家阳光牌（Royal Sunbeam）足矣，但他坚持我们俩都得用邓禄普（Dunlop）车胎。

“这牌子似乎最普及。从利普利到科巴姆（Cobham）一路我都盯着路面，邓禄普的胎印比任何别家的都多。可不，各家车胎都留各家的印子，咱们可别让自己的格外特别；邓禄普的纹路像条响尾蛇，帕尔默（Palmer）压出来的是电报线，不过蛇总归更合咱们的路数。”

===Original===
That was the winter when there were so many burglaries in the Thames Valley from Richmond upward. It was said that the thieves used bicycles in every case, but what is not said? They were sometimes on foot to my knowledge, and we took a great interest in the series, or rather sequence of successful crimes. Raffles would often get his devoted old lady to read him the latest local accounts, while I was busy with my writing (much I wrote) in my own room. We even rode out by night ourselves, to see if we could not get on the tracks of the thieves, and never did we fail to find hot coffee on the hob for our return. We had indeed fallen upon our feet. Also, the misty nights might have been made for the thieves. But their success was not so consistent, and never so enormous as people said, especially the sufferers, who lost more valuables than they had ever been known to possess. Failure was often the caitiffs’ portion, and disaster once; owing, ironically enough, to that very mist which should have served them. But as I am going to tell the story with some particularity, and perhaps some gusto, you will see why who read.
The right house stood on high ground near the river, with quite a drive (in at one gate and out at the other) sweeping past the steps. Between the two gates was a half-moon of shrubs, to the left of the steps a conservatory, and to their right the walk leading to the tradesmen’s entrance and the back premises; here also was the pantry window, of which more anon. The right house was the residence of an opulent stockbroker who wore a heavy watch-chain and seemed fair game. There would have been two objections to it had I been the stockbroker. The house was one of a row, though a goodly row, and an army-crammer had established himself next door. There is a type of such institutions in the suburbs; the youths go about in knickerbockers, smoking pipes, except on Saturday nights, when they lead each other home from the last train. It was none of our business to spy upon these boys, but their manners and customs fell within the field of observation. And we did not choose the night upon which the whole row was likely to be kept awake.

===Chinese===
那个冬天，从里士满（Richmond）往上游的泰晤士河谷（Thames Valley）窃案频仍。据说窃贼案案都用自行车——可又有什么话没人说呢？据我所知，他们有时是步行的。我们对这一连串——不如说这一序列——连连得手的罪案大感兴趣。拉夫尔斯常请他那忠心耿耿的老太太把本地最新的案情报道念给他听，我则在自家房里忙我的笔墨（写得着实不少）。我们甚至夜里也骑车出去，看能不能摸上窃贼的辙迹，而每一趟归来，炉架上从未缺过一壶热咖啡。我们实在是一跤跌在了福地上。再者，那些雾蒙蒙的夜，简直像专为窃贼裁制。只是他们的得手远非那么整齐划一，也远没有人们——尤其失主们——嚷嚷的那么惊天动地：失主们报失的珍宝，比世人所知他们曾经拥有过的还要多。失手也是这伙恶徒常份的事，栽大跟头更有一次；说来讽刺，恰恰栽在那本该为他们效力的雾上。不过，我既打算把这故事细细讲来，兴许还讲得眉飞色舞，个中缘由，读下去自会明白。

那座对的宅子坐落在河畔高地上，屋前一条颇像样的车马道（从一道门进、另一道门出）逶迤掠过台阶。两道门之间是一弯新月形的灌木丛，台阶左侧一间暖房，右侧一条小径通往送货进口和后部诸屋；配膳室的窗户也在此间——此是后话。这座对的宅子，住着一位殷实的证券经纪人，戴着一条沉甸甸的表链，看着正是块合该下嘴的肥肉。然而倘若我是那位经纪人，对这宅子要提两条异议。它是排屋里的一座——虽说是一排气派的好宅子——而隔壁住定了一位开军校补习馆的先生。郊区自有这么一类学馆：少年们穿着灯笼裤（knickerbockers）叼着烟斗四处晃，唯独星期六夜里例外——那一晚他们彼此搀扶着从末班火车回家。窥探这些孩子与我等无干，但他们的风土人情，终究落在观察范围之内。而我们选的，并不是整排宅子多半要被吵醒的那一夜。

===Original===
The night that we did choose was as misty as even the Thames Valley is capable of making them. Raffles smeared vaseline upon the plated parts of his Beeston Humber before starting, and our dear landlady cosseted us both, and prayed we might see nothing of the nasty burglars, not denying as the reward would be very handy to them that got it, to say nothing of the honor and glory. We had promised her a liberal perquisite in the event of our success, but she must not give other cyclists our idea by mentioning it to a soul. It was about midnight when we cycled through Kingston to Surbiton, having trundled our machines across Ham Fields, mournful in the mist as those by Acheron, and so over Teddington Bridge.
I often wonder why the pantry window is the vulnerable point of nine houses out of ten. This house of ours was almost the tenth, for the window in question had bars of sorts, but not the right sort. The only bars that Raffles allowed to beat him were the kind that are let into the stone outside; those fixed within are merely screwed to the woodwork, and you can unscrew as many as necessary if you take the trouble and have the time. Barred windows are usually devoid of other fasteners worthy the name; this one was no exception to that foolish rule, and a push with the penknife did its business. I am giving householders some valuable hints, and perhaps deserving a good mark from the critics. These, in any case, are the points that I would see to, were I a rich stockbroker in a riverside suburb. In giving good advice, however, I should not have omitted to say that we had left our machines in the semicircular shrubbery in front, or that Raffles had most ingeniously fitted our lamps with dark slides, which enabled us to leave them burning.

===Chinese===
我们选定的那一夜，雾浓到了泰晤士河谷所能造出的极致。动身之前，拉夫尔斯给他的比斯顿·亨伯凡有镀件之处都抹上了凡士林，我们亲爱的房东太太把咱们俩都悉心娇养了一番，祷祝我们千万别撞见那些可恶的窃贼，却也并不否认，那笔赏金无论落到谁手里都顶合用——荣誉与光辉更不消说。我们许下她，一旦得手便奉上可观的一份外快，只是她千万不可对任何人提起，免得把我们的点子奉送给别的骑车人。约莫午夜时分，我们骑行穿过金斯顿（Kingston）前往苏比顿（Surbiton）——此前我们推着车横穿了汉姆田野（Ham Fields），雾中一片凄迷，直如阿克隆河（Acheron）畔的幽域——随后便过了特丁顿桥（Teddington Bridge）。

我常常纳闷，为什么十家宅子里倒有九家，最不禁一击的偏是配膳室的窗户。我们这一座几乎就是第十家：那扇窗固然装了铁栅，却不是对路的那种。拉夫尔斯唯一肯认输的铁栅，是嵌进外头石料里的那一种；安在里头的不过用螺钉拧在木框上，只要不怕费事、光阴充裕，要拧下几根便是几根。上了栅的窗子，通常便再没有一件配得上“锁”字的别的机关；这一扇也未能免俗，小折刀往前一顶，便完成了任务。我这可是在向各家户主进献金玉良言，说不定还该博评论家们一个好分数呢。无论如何，倘若我自己是河畔郊区一位阔绰的证券经纪人，要操心的正是这几点。不过，良言既出，还有一句不可漏说：我们的车就撂在屋前那半圆形的灌木丛里；再者，拉夫尔斯颇为巧妙地给我们的车灯配上了遮光片，好教车灯就这样燃着留在原处。

===Original===
It proved sufficient to unscrew the bars at the bottom only, and then to wrench them to either side. Neither of us had grown stout with advancing years, and in a few minutes we both had wormed through into the sink, and thence to the floor. It was not an absolutely noiseless process, but once in the pantry we were mice, and no longer blind mice. There was a gas-bracket, but we did not meddle with that. Raffles went armed these nights with a better light than gas; if it were not immoral, I might recommend a dark-lantern which was more or less his patent. It was that handy invention, the electric torch, fitted by Raffles with a dark hood to fulfil the functions of a slide. I had held it through the bars while he undid the screws, and now he held it to the keyhole, in which a key was turned upon the other side.
There was a pause for consideration, and in the pause we put on our masks. It was never known that these Thames Valley robberies were all committed by miscreants decked in the livery of crime, but that was because until this night we had never even shown our masks. It was a point upon which Raffles had insisted on all feasible occasions since his furtive return to the world. Tonight it twice nearly lost us everything﻿—but you shall hear.

===Chinese===
事实证明，只消把底下的栅条拧脱，再朝两侧扳开，便足够了。我们俩谁也没随年岁添膘，几分钟工夫，便都蠕动着钻过了洗涤槽，落到了地板上。这过程谈不上全然无声，可一进配膳室，我们便是老鼠，而且再不是瞎老鼠。屋里有座煤气灯壁托，我们未去招惹。那些夜晚拉夫尔斯随身带着比煤气更胜一筹的光源；倘若这不算伤风败俗，我倒愿举荐一种遮光提灯，那多多少少算是他的专利。正是那件趁手的发明——电筒，由拉夫尔斯加装了一只遮光罩，以尽遮光片之职。方才拧螺丝时是我隔着栅栏替他举着它，此刻他把它照上锁孔：钥匙就反锁在门的那一边。

我们停了片刻，权作斟酌；便在这停顿之中，我们戴上了面具。从来无人知晓，泰晤士河谷这些劫案尽是一伙身披罪犯罪衣的歹徒所为——那是因为直到今夜之前，我们连面具也不曾露过一回。自从拉夫尔斯悄没声地重返人世，但凡做得到的场合，他必坚持这一条。今夜它两度险些教我们输个精光——且容我细表。

===Original===
There is a forceps for turning keys from the wrong side of the door, but the implement is not so easy of manipulation as it might be. Raffles for one preferred a sharp knife and the corner of the panel. You go through the panel because that is thinnest, of course in the corner nearest the key, and you use a knife when you can, because it makes least noise. But it does take minutes, and even I can remember shifting the electric torch from one hand to the other before the aperture was large enough to receive the hand and wrist of Raffles.
He had at such times a motto of which I might have made earlier use, but the fact is that I have only once before described a downright burglary in which I assisted, and that without knowing it at the time. The most solemn student of these annals cannot affirm that he has cut through many doors in our company, since (what was to me) the maiden effort to which I allude. I, however, have cracked only too many a crib in conjunction with A. J. Raffles, and at the crucial moment he would whisper “Victory or Wormwood Scrubbs, Bunny!” or instead of Wormwood Scrubbs it might be Portland Bill. This time it was neither one nor the other, for with that very word “victory” upon his lips, they whitened and parted with the first taste of defeat.

===Chinese===
世上自有一种钳子，能从门的这一边把那一边的钥匙拧转过来，只是这器械使唤起来，远不像表面看来那般容易。就拿拉夫尔斯来说，他宁可要一把快刀加门板的一角。你从门板上开刀，因为它最薄，自然拣离钥匙最近的角落；能用刀便用刀，因为动静最小。可这着实要费上几分钟，连我都还记得，在豁口开大到容得下拉夫尔斯的手掌与手腕之前，我把电筒从这只手倒到了那只手。

每逢这种时刻，他有一句格言，我本该早些派上用场；只是实情在于，此前我只记述过一回自己亲身协助、不折不扣的入室盗窃，而且当时并不知情。本编年史最较真的研读者，也不敢断言曾随我们切割过许多扇门——自打我暗指的那桩于我为开山之作以来。然而，与 A. J. 拉夫尔斯（A. J. Raffles）合伙，我撬过的宅子实在太多，每到紧要关头，他总会咬耳朵：“不胜利，便进沃姆伍德·斯克拉布斯（Wormwood Scrubbs），邦尼！”有时不说沃姆伍德·斯克拉布斯，换成波特兰角（Portland Bill）。这一回两者皆非：因为“胜利”二字方才沾唇，那两片嘴唇便白了、张开了，头一回尝到了失败的滋味。

===Original===
“My hand’s held!” gasped Raffles, and the white of his eyes showed all round the iris, a rarer thing than you may think.
At the same moment I heard the shuffling feet and the low, excited young voices on the other side of the door, and a faint light shone round Raffles’s wrist.

===Chinese===
“我的手被攥住了！”拉夫尔斯倒抽一口气说，眼白从虹膜四周一齐露了出来——这情形可比您想的少见。

同一瞬间，我听见门那一边脚步拖沓，压得低低的年轻嗓音兴奋嘈嘈，一线微光正映着拉夫尔斯的手腕。

===Original===
“Well done, Beefy!”
“Hang on to him!”
“Good old Beefy!”
“Beefy’s got him!”

===Chinese===
“好样的，比菲（Beefy）！”

“揪住他别撒手！”

“好一个比菲！”

“比菲逮住他啦！”

===Original===
“So have I﻿—so have I!”
And Raffles caught my arm with his one free hand. “They’ve got me tight,” he whispered. “I’m done.”
“Blaze through the door,” I urged, and might have done it had I been armed. But I never was. It was Raffles who monopolized that risk.
“I can’t﻿—it’s the boys﻿—the wrong house!” he whispered. “Curse the fog﻿—it’s done me. But you get out, Bunny, while you can; never mind me; it’s my turn, old chap.”

===Chinese===
“我也搭了把手——我也搭了把手！”

拉夫尔斯腾出仅剩的那只手来抓住我的胳膊。“他们把我攥得死死的，”他低声道，“我完了。”

“朝门开枪，”我急切地怂恿；倘若我带了家伙，没准真会动手。可我从来不带。这份风险，向来由拉夫尔斯一人包圆。

“我不能——是那帮孩子——咱们走错了门！”他低声道，“该死的雾——是它坑了我。可你趁早出去，邦尼；别管我；这回轮到我了，老伙计。”

===Original===
His one hand tightened in affectionate farewell. I put the electric torch in it before I went, trembling in every inch, but without a word.
Get out! His turn! Yes, I would get out, but only to come in again, for it was my turn﻿—mine﻿—not his. Would Raffles leave me held by a hand through a hole in a door? What he would have done in my place was the thing for me to do now. I began by diving headfirst through the pantry window and coming to earth upon all fours. But even as I stood up, and brushed the gravel from the palms of my hands and the knees of my knickerbockers, I had no notion what to do next. And yet I was halfway to the front door before I remembered the vile crepe mask upon my face, and tore it off as the door flew open and my feet were on the steps.

===Chinese===
他那只手一紧，权作深情的诀别。临去之前，我把电筒塞进那只手里，浑身每一寸都在打颤，却一言未发。

出去！轮到他！是啊，我要出去——可只为再进来，因为轮到的是我——是我——不是他。拉夫尔斯岂会弃我于不顾，任我一只手被隔着门板上的窟窿攥住？换作我的境地他会怎么做，此刻我便该怎么做。我的头一着，便是头朝前扎出配膳室的窗，四肢着地落了地。可不等我站起身、掸净掌心和灯笼裤膝头的碎石子，我全然不知下一步当如何。然而不知不觉间，人已走到正门半途，方才记起脸上那块可恶的绉纱面具（crepe mask），一把扯下——正门应声洞开，我双脚已踏上门阶。

===Original===
“He’s into the next garden,” I cried to a bevy of pyjamas with bare feet and young faces at either end of them.
“Who? Who?” said they, giving way before me.
“Some fellow who came through one of your windows headfirst.”
“The other Johnny, the other Johnny,” the cherubs chorused.

===Chinese===
“他窜进隔壁花园了，”我朝一群睡衣嚷道——一水儿的赤脚，睡衣两端各顶着一张年轻的脸。

“谁？谁？”他们说着，在我面前让开道来。

“有个家伙，头朝前从你们家一扇窗户里栽出去了。”

“另一个家伙，另一个家伙，”这帮小天使齐声唱和。

===Original===
“Biking past﻿—saw the light﻿—why, what have you there?”
Of course it was Raffles’s hand that they had, but now I was in the hall among them. A red-faced barrel of a boy did all the holding, one hand round the wrist, the other palm to palm, and his knees braced up against the panel. Another was rendering ostentatious but ineffectual aid, and three or four others danced about in their pyjamas. After all, they were not more than four to one. I had raised my voice, so that Raffles might hear me and take heart, and now I raised it again. Yet to this day I cannot account for my inspiration, that proved nothing less.

===Chinese===
“骑车路过——瞧见灯光——咦，你们手里抓着的是什么？”

他们攥着的自然是拉夫尔斯的手，可此刻我已置身门厅，在他们中间。一个红脸膛、桶一般粗壮的少年独任其艰：一只手箍住手腕，另一只与他手掌相抵，双膝死死抵住门板。还有一个在卖力帮衬，实则全然无用，另有三四个穿着睡衣团团乱转。说到底，不过四对一。方才我提高了嗓门，为的是让拉夫尔斯听见、壮起胆来；此刻我又提了一回。然而直到今天，我也说不清我那阵灵感的来路——而它事后证明，绝非等闲。

===Original===
“Don’t talk so loud,” they were crying below their breath; “don’t wake ’em upstairs, this is our show.”
“Then I see you’ve got one of them,” said I, as desired. “Well, if you want the other you can have him, too. I believe he’s hurt himself.”
“After him, after him!” they exclaimed as one.
“But I think he got over the wall﻿—”

===Chinese===
“别嚷这么响，”他们压着嗓子直叫，“别吵醒楼上的人，这出好戏是咱们自个儿的。”

“这么说，你们已经到手一个了，”我依着他们的指望说道。“好哇，你们若还想要那一个，也一并奉送。我看他摔伤了自己。”

“追他去，追他去！”他们异口同声。

“不过我看他翻墙跑了——”

===Original===
“Come on, you chaps, come on!”
And there was a soft stampede to the hall door.
“Don’t all desert me, I say!” gasped the red-faced hero who held Raffles prisoner.
“We must have them both, Beefy!”

===Chinese===
“快上，伙计们，快上！”

于是一阵轻悄悄的蜂拥夺门，直奔门厅大门而去。

“喂喂，可别都把我撂在这儿！”揪住拉夫尔斯不放的红脸英雄喘道。

“两个都得拿下，比菲！”

===Original===
“That’s all very well﻿—”
“Look here,” I interposed, “I’ll stay by you. I’ve a friend outside, I’ll get him too.”
“Thanks awfully,” said the valiant Beefy.
The hall was empty now. My heart beat high.

===Chinese===
“话是这么说——”

“听我说，”我插进一句，“我留下来陪你。我有位朋友在外头，我去把他也叫来。”

“多谢多谢，”英勇的比菲说。

门厅里此刻空了。我的心跳得正高。

===Original===
“How did you hear them?” I inquired, my eye running over him.
“We were down having drinks﻿—game o’ Nap﻿—in there.”
Beefy jerked his great head toward an open door, and the tail of my eye caught the glint of glasses in the firelight, but the rest of it was otherwise engaged.

===Chinese===
“你们是怎么听见他们的？”我问道，一面把他上下打量。

“我们在楼下喝酒来着——打纳普牌（Nap）——就在那屋里。”

比菲把他那颗大脑袋朝一扇敞开的门一摆，我眼角余光扫见炉火里杯盏的微光，其余的眼神却另有公务在身。

===Original===
“Let me relieve you,” I said, trembling.
“No, I’m all right.”
“Then I must insist.”

===Chinese===
“让我来换换你的手，”我说，声音发颤。

“不必，我撑得住。”

“那我就只好强求了。”

===Original===
And before he could answer I had him round the neck with such a will that not a gurgle passed my fingers, for they were almost buried in his hot, smooth flesh. Oh, I am not proud of it; the act was as vile as act could be; but I was not going to see Raffles taken, my one desire was to be the saving of him, and I tremble even now to think to what lengths I might have gone for its fulfilment. As it was, I squeezed and tugged until one strong hand gave way after the other and came feeling round for me, but feebly because they had held on so long. And what do you suppose was happening at the same moment? The pinched white hand of Raffles, reddening with returning blood, and with a clot of blood upon the wrist, was craning upward and turning the key in the lock without a moment’s loss.
“Steady on, Bunny!”

===Chinese===
不等他答话，我已一把箍住他的脖子，下手之狠，竟教一丝咯咯声都没能从我指缝间漏出——我的手指几乎全埋进了他滚烫光滑的皮肉。哦，此事我并不引以为荣；这桩行径，卑鄙得无以复加；可我不能眼看拉夫尔斯被擒，我唯一的念头便是救下他，至今想起自己为遂此愿可能走到哪一步，我犹自不寒而栗。事实上，我又掐又拽，直到他那双有力的手先后松脱，摸索着回身来抓我——却软弱无力，因为它们攥得实在太久了。您道同一时刻正发生着什么？拉夫尔斯那只被掐得惨白的手，正随血液回流渐渐转红，腕上凝着一道血痂，探向锁孔，争分夺秒地拧动了那把钥匙。

“松着点儿，邦尼！”

===Original===
And I saw that Beefy’s ears were blue; but Raffles was feeling in his pockets as he spoke. “Now let him breathe,” said he, clapping his handkerchief over the poor youth’s mouth. An empty vial was in his other hand, and the first few stertorous breaths that the poor boy took were the end of him for the time being. Oh, but it was villainous, my part especially, for he must have been far gone to go the rest of the way so readily. I began by saying I was not proud of this deed, but its dastardly character has come home to me more than ever with the penance of writing it out. I see in myself, at least my then self, things that I never saw quite so clearly before. Yet let me be quite sure that I would not do the same again. I had not the smallest desire to throttle this innocent lad (nor did I), but only to extricate Raffles from the most hopeless position he was ever in; and after all it was better than a blow from behind. On the whole, I will not alter a word, nor whine about the thing any more.
We lifted the plucky fellow into Raffles’s place in the pantry, locked the door on him, and put the key through the panel. Now was the moment for thinking of ourselves, and again that infernal mask which Raffles swore by came near the undoing of us both. We had reached the steps when we were hailed by a voice, not from without but from within, and I had just time to tear the accursed thing from Raffles’s face before he turned.

===Chinese===
我看见比菲的耳朵已泛了青；拉夫尔斯一面说话，一面正在自己口袋里摸索。“现在让他喘口气吧，”他说罢，把手帕捂上那可怜少年的口鼻。他另一只手里握着一只空药瓶，那可怜的孩子头几声带着鼾音的喘息，便算暂时结果了他。哦，这实在歹毒，我那一份尤其歹毒：他必定早已昏沉透顶，才这么顺顺当当地走完了余下的路。我开篇便说过，此事我不以为荣；可借着把它一笔一画写出来的苦刑，这桩卑怯勾当的本相，比以往任何时候都更深地烙上了我的心头。我在自己身上——至少在当年的自己身上——看出了从前从未看得这般分明的东西。不过，容我先拿准了再说：同样的事，我断不会再来一遍。我丝毫没有掐毙这无辜少年的意思（也确实没有掐毙），一心只想把拉夫尔斯从他有生以来最绝望的困境里拔出来；况且说到底，这总比从背后给他一记闷棍强。通盘算来，我一个字也不改，这桩事，也不再哀声叹气地絮叨了。

我们把这个有种的家伙抬进配膳室，安顿在拉夫尔斯方才的位置上，反锁了门，又把钥匙从门板豁口塞了出去。此刻该想想我们自己了，而拉夫尔斯奉为至宝的那张劳什子面具，又一次险些把我们俩一齐断送。我们已走到台阶，忽听得一声呼问——不来自外头，而来自里头——我刚来得及赶在他转身之前，把那可诅咒的物件从他脸上扯了下来。

===Original===
A stout man with a blonde moustache was on the stairs, in his pyjamas like the boys.
“What are you doing here?” said he.
“There has been an attempt upon your house,” said I, still spokesman for the night, and still on the wings of inspiration.
“Your sons﻿—”

===Chinese===
一个蓄着淡黄唇髭的胖子立在楼梯上，和孩子们一样，也穿着睡衣。

“你们在这里做什么？”他说。

“有人对贵宅下了手，”我说——这一夜我仍是代言人，而且仍乘着灵感的翅膀。

“您的公子们——”

===Original===
“My pupils.”
“Indeed. Well, they heard it, drove off the thieves, and have given chase.”
“And where do you come in?” inquired the stout man, descending.
“We were bicycling past, and I actually saw one fellow come headfirst through your pantry window. I think he got over the wall.”

===Chinese===
“是我的学生。”

“原来如此。是这样：他们听见了动静，赶跑了窃贼，眼下追出去了。”

“那么你们二位又是怎么掺和进来的？”胖子一面下楼一面问道。

“我们骑车路过，我亲眼瞧见一个家伙头朝前从您家配膳室的窗户里栽出去。我看他翻墙跑了。”

===Original===
Here a breathless boy returned.
“Can’t see anything of him,” he gasped.
“It’s true, then,” remarked the crammer.
“Look at that door,” said I.

===Chinese===
正说着，一个上气不接下气的少年回来了。

“连他的影子也没见着，”他喘道。

“这么说，倒真有其事了，”补习班主任评了一句。

“看看那扇门吧，”我说。

===Original===
But unfortunately the breathless boy looked also, and now he was being joined by others equally short of wind.
“Where’s Beefy?” he screamed. “What on earth’s happened to Beefy?”
“My good boys,” exclaimed the crammer, “will one of you be kind enough to tell me what you’ve been doing, and what these gentlemen have been doing for you? Come in all, before you get your death. I see lights in the classroom, and more than lights. Can these be signs of a carouse?”
“A very innocent one, sir,” said a well setup youth with more moustache than I have yet.

===Chinese===
可不巧，那喘作一团的少年也看了，而且眼下又有几个同样气短的赶来会齐。

“比菲呢？”他尖声叫道，“比菲到底出了什么事？”

“我亲爱的孩子们，”补习班主任扬声道，“哪一位行个好，告诉我你们方才在做什么，这两位先生又替你们做了些什么？都进来吧，免得冻出人命来。我看见教室里有灯火，而且不止灯火。这些，莫非是纵酒作乐的迹象？”

“清白得很的一场作乐，先生，”一个身量匀称的少年答道，唇上的胡子比我迄今留的还浓。

===Original===
“Well, Olphert, boys will be boys. Suppose you tell me what happened, before we come to recriminations.”
The bad old proverb was my first warning. I caught two of the youths exchanging glances under raised eyebrows. Yet their stout, easygoing mentor had given me such a reassuring glance of sidelong humor, as between man of the world and man of the world, that it was difficult to suspect him of suspicion. I was nevertheless itching to be gone.

===Chinese===
“好吧，奥尔弗特（Olphert），孩子终归是孩子。趁咱们还没掰扯到互相问罪，你先把事情的经过给我讲讲。”

那句老掉牙的坏谚语，是我的第一记警钟。我瞥见两个少年眉梢高挑，互递眼色。然而他们那位心宽体胖的导师，方才朝我飞来一个斜睨的眼风——世故人对世故人的那种幽默——教人很难疑心他心怀疑心。话虽如此，我仍痒痒地只求脱身。

===Original===
Young Olphert told his story with engaging candor. It was true that they had come down for an hour’s Nap and cigarettes; well, and there was no denying that there was whiskey in the glasses. The boys were now all back in their classroom, I think entirely for the sake of warmth; but Raffles and I were in knickerbockers and Norfolk jackets, and very naturally remained without, while the army-crammer (who wore bedroom slippers) stood on the threshold, with an eye each way. The more I saw of the man the better I liked and the more I feared him. His chief annoyance thus far was that they had not called him when they heard the noise, that they had dreamt of leaving him out of the fun. But he seemed more hurt than angry about that.
“Well, sir,” concluded Olphert, “we left old Beefy Smith hanging on to his hand, and this gentleman with him, so perhaps he can tell us what happened next?”

===Chinese===
小奥尔弗特讲起经过来，那份坦率煞是可爱。他们确是为着打一个钟头的纳普牌、抽抽烟才下楼的；呃，杯子里有威士忌，这也没什么可抵赖的。孩子们此刻都已回了教室，我想纯粹是为了取暖；拉夫尔斯和我一身灯笼裤、诺福克外套（Norfolk jacket），自然而然留在外面，而那位军校补习班主任（足蹬卧室拖鞋）立在门槛上，一眼两头兼顾。这人我越看越喜欢，也越看越忌惮。他迄今最大的懊恼，是孩子们听见动静竟不来叫他，竟做梦想把这份热闹瞒过他。不过在这一点上，他与其说是恼怒，不如说是伤了心。

“就这样，先生，”奥尔弗特收了尾，“我们丢下老比菲·史密斯（Beefy Smith）死攥着那只手，还有这位先生陪着他，所以接下来的事，兴许他能给咱们讲一讲？”

===Original===
“I wish I could,” I cried with all their eyes upon me, for I had had time to think. “Some of you must have heard me say I’d fetch my friend in from the road?”
“Yes, I did,” piped an innocent from within.
“Well, and when I came back with him things were exactly as you see them now. Evidently the man’s strength was too much for the boy’s; but whether he ran upstairs or outside I know no more than you do.”

===Chinese===
“但愿我能啊，”我扬声道，众人的目光一齐落在我身上——好在我已得了盘算的工夫。“你们中间总有人听见我说过，要上大路把我的朋友叫回来吧？”

“听见了，我听见了，”屋里一个天真汉尖着嗓子应道。

“正是这样。我领他回来的时候，一切恰如你们眼下所见。分明是那人的膂力压过了这孩子的；至于他是跑上了楼还是跑出了门，我知道的不比你们多。”

===Original===
“It wasn’t like that boy to run either way,” said the crammer, cocking a clear blue eye on me.
“But if he gave chase!”
“It wasn’t like him even to let go.”
“I don’t believe Beefy ever would,” put in Olphert. “That’s why we gave him the billet.”

===Chinese===
“那孩子可不是拔腿便溜的性子——往哪边溜都不是，”补习班主任说着，把一只清澈的蓝眼睛朝我一挑。

“可要是他追出去了呢！”

“连撒手都不像他干的事。”

“我看比菲压根儿就不会撒手，”奥尔弗特插话道，“所以才把这差事派给了他。”

===Original===
“He may have followed him through the pantry window,” I suggested wildly.
“But the door’s shut,” put in a boy.
“I’ll have a look at it,” said the crammer.

===Chinese===
“说不定他是跟着那人，打配膳室窗户追出去了，”我情急生智道。

“可那扇门是关着的呀，”一个少年插嘴。

“我去瞧上一瞧，”补习班主任说。

===Original===
And the key no longer in the lock, and the insensible youth within! The key would be missed, the door kicked in; nay, with the man’s eye still upon me, I thought I could smell the chloroform.
I thought I could hear a moan, and prepared for either any moment. And how he did stare! I have detested blue eyes ever since, and blonde moustaches, and the whole stout easygoing type that is not such a fool as it looks. I had brazened it out with the boys, but the first grown man was too many for me, and the blood ran out of my heart as though there was no Raffles at my back. Indeed, I had forgotten him. I had so longed to put this thing through by myself! Even in my extremity it was almost a disappointment to me when his dear, cool voice fell like a delicious draught upon my ears. But its effect upon the others is more interesting to recall. Until now the crammer had the centre of the stage, but at this point Raffles usurped a place which was always his at will. People would wait for what he had to say, as these people waited now for the simplest and most natural thing in the world.

===Chinese===
而钥匙已不在锁上，不省人事的少年就在门内！钥匙一旦失踪，门便要被一脚踹开；非但如此——那人的眼睛还落在我身上，我却仿佛已嗅得出氯仿的气味。

我仿佛听得见一声呻吟，随时预备着两样中随便哪一样降临。他那双眼睛，瞪得可真叫一个狠！打那以后，我便厌恶蓝眼睛，厌恶淡黄唇髭，厌恶整个这一路心宽体胖的人物——他们才不像看上去那么蠢。对付孩子们，我凭一张厚脸皮硬撑了过去；可头一个成年汉子就教我招架不住，血从我心里退了出去，仿佛背后并没有一个拉夫尔斯。实实在在，我已把他忘了。我一心只盼凭一己之力闯过这一关！即便身陷绝境，当他那亲爱的、冷静的嗓音如一瓢清泉浇上我耳鼓时，我心里竟几乎掠过一阵失望。不过它对其余众人的效验，回想起来更有味道。直到此刻，戏台的中心一直是补习班主任的；可打这一刻起，拉夫尔斯篡据了一个向来任他予取予求的位置。人们总会候着他开口，正如眼下这些人，静候一件天下最简单、最自然不过的事。

===Original===
“One moment!” he had begun.
“Well?” said the crammer, relieving me of his eyes at last.
“I don’t want to lose any of the fun﻿—”
“Nor must you,” said the crammer, with emphasis.

===Chinese===
“且慢！”他便是这样起的头。

“有何见教？”补习班主任总算把眼睛从我身上卸了下来。

“这样的热闹，我不想错过——”

“你也确实不容错过，”补习班主任加重语气道。

===Original===
“But we’ve left our bikes outside, and mine’s a Beeston Humber,” continued Raffles. “If you don’t mind, we’ll bring ’em in before these fellows get away on them.”
And out he went without a look to see the effect of his words, I after him with a determined imitation of his self-control. But I would have given something to turn round. I believe that for one moment the shrewd instructor was taken in, but as I reached the steps I heard him asking his pupils whether any of them had seen any bicycles outside.

===Chinese===
“只是我们的自行车还撂在外头，我那辆是比斯顿·亨伯，”拉夫尔斯接着说，“诸位若不见怪，我们得赶在这伙家伙骑上它们溜走之前，先把车搬进来。”

说罢他便扬长而去，一眼也不回头看看这番话的效验，我随他出去，狠命仿效他的镇定。可若许我回头望上一眼，叫我出什么都肯。我相信有那么一瞬，这位精明的教书人真被瞒过了；然而待我走到台阶，已听见他在问学生们，有谁在外面见过什么自行车没有。

===Original===
That moment, however, made the difference. We were in the shrubbery, Raffles with his electric torch drawn and blazing, when we heard the kicking at the pantry door, and in the drive with our bicycles before man and boys poured pell-mell down the steps.
We rushed our machines to the nearer gate, for both were shut, and we got through and swung it home behind us in the nick of time. Even I could mount before they could reopen the gate, which Raffles held against them for half an instant with unnecessary gallantry. But he would see me in front of him, and so it fell to me to lead the way.

===Chinese===
然而正是那一瞬，分出了高下。我们刚进灌木丛——拉夫尔斯的电筒已然拔出、光焰灼灼——就听见配膳室那扇门被踹得咚咚作响；待到师生人等连滚带爬涌下台阶，我们已推着车站在车道上了。

我们推车直扑较近的那道门——两道门都闩着——挤将出去，千钧一发之际反手把门荡回原位。不等他们重新推开门，连我都已跨上了车；方才拉夫尔斯还抵住门撑了半息——那份骑士风度，实在多余。可他偏要我骑在他前头，于是领路的差事便落到了我头上。

===Original===
Now, I have said that it was a very misty night (hence the whole thing), and also that these houses were on a hill. But they were not nearly on the top of the hill, and I did what I firmly believe that almost everybody would have done in my place. Raffles, indeed, said he would have done it himself, but that was his generosity, and he was the one man who would not. What I did was to turn in the opposite direction to the other gate, where we might so easily have been cut off, and to pedal for my life﻿—uphill!
“My God!” I shouted when I found it out.
“Can you turn in your own length?” asked Raffles, following loyally.
“Not certain.”

===Chinese===
好了，我说过那晚雾重（祸根全在它身上），也说过这些宅子坐落在一座山丘上。可它们远不在丘顶，于是我干了一件事——我笃信几乎人人处在我那境地都会那么干。拉夫尔斯倒说，换了他也会这么办，可那是他的慷慨；天底下唯独他这一个人不会。我干的，便是背朝另一道门的方向拐去——奔那道门去，我们轻易便会被兜头截住——然后拼了命地蹬车——上坡！

“我的老天！”等我醒悟过来，不禁失声喊道。

“你在一车身之内掉得了头吗？”拉夫尔斯忠心耿耿地紧随着问。

“拿不准。”

===Original===
“Then stick to it. You couldn’t help it. But it’s the devil of a hill!”
“And here they come!”
“Let them,” said Raffles, and brandished his electric torch, our only light as yet.
A hill seems endless in the dark, for you cannot see the end, and with the patter of bare feet gaining on us, I thought this one could have no end at all. Of course the boys could charge up it quicker than we could pedal, but I even heard the voice of their stout instructor growing louder through the mist.

===Chinese===
“那就咬牙骑下去。这也怪不得你。可这坡真够魔鬼的！”

“他们来了！”

“来便来，”拉夫尔斯说着，挥了挥电筒——那还是我们仅有的一盏灯。

黑暗里的山丘仿佛没有尽头，因为你望不见尽头；耳听得赤脚的啪嗒声步步进逼，我直疑心这一座压根儿就没有尽头。孩子们冲上坡，自然比我们蹬车快，可我连他们那位胖导师的嗓门，也正穿过雾气，一阵响似一阵。

===Original===
“Oh, to think I’ve let you in for this!” I groaned, my head over the handlebars, every ounce of my weight first on one foot and then on the other. I glanced at Raffles, and in the white light of his torch he was doing it all with his ankles, exactly as though he had been riding in a Gymkhana.
“It’s the most sporting chase I was ever in,” said he.
“All my fault!”
“My dear Bunny, I wouldn’t have missed it for the world!”

===Chinese===
“唉，想不到竟把你也拖累到了这步田地！”我呻吟道，脑袋伏在车把上，全身的斤两先是压一只脚，再压另一只。我瞥了瞥拉夫尔斯，在他电筒的白光里，他全凭一双脚踝从容作功，活脱脱像在车技赛会（Gymkhana）上骑行。

“这是我此生混迹过的最有运动味儿的一场追逐，”他说。

“全怪我！”

“亲爱的邦尼，拿全世界来换，我也不肯错过它！”

===Original===
Nor would he forge ahead of me, though he could have done so in a moment, he who from his boyhood had done everything of the kind so much better than anybody else. No, he must ride a wheel’s length behind me, and now we could not only hear the boys running, but breathing also. And then of a sudden I saw Raffles on my right striking with his torch; a face flew out of the darkness to meet the thick glass bulb with the glowing wire enclosed; it was the face of the boy Olphert, with his enviable moustache, but it vanished with the crash of glass, and the naked wire thickened to the eye like a tuning-fork struck red-hot.
I saw no more of that. One of them had crept up on my side also; as I looked, hearing him pant, he was grabbing at my left handle, and I nearly sent Raffles into the hedge by the sharp turn I took to the right. His wheel’s length saved him. But my boy could run, was overhauling me again, seemed certain of me this time, when all at once the Sunbeam ran easily; every ounce of my weight with either foot once more, and I was over the crest of the hill, the gray road reeling out from under me as I felt for my brake. I looked back at Raffles. He had put up his feet. I screwed my head round still further, and there were the boys in their pyjamas, their hands upon their knees, like so many wicket-keepers, and a big man shaking his fist. There was a lamppost on the hilltop, and that was the last I saw.

===Chinese===
可他也断不肯赶超到我前头去，虽说一转眼他便办得到——这等人，自幼年起，凡属这路本领，样样远胜旁人。不，他偏要吊在我身后一个车轮远的地方；而此刻，我们不但听得见孩子们奔跑，连喘息也听得见了。蓦地，我看见拉夫尔斯在我右侧抡起了电筒；一张脸从黑暗里迎着那裹着发光灯丝的厚玻璃泡飞来；那是少年奥尔弗特的脸，带着那撇令人艳羡的胡子——可它随一声玻璃碎裂一同消散，裸露的灯丝在眼中胀粗，宛如一根烧红了的、被敲响的音叉。

后面的事我没有看见。我这一侧也摸上来一个；听见他喘息，我侧头一看，他正抓向我左手边的车把，我一个急右转，险些把拉夫尔斯送进树篱。他那一车轮的间距救了他。可我这孩子真能跑，又追了上来，这一回眼看十拿九稳了，偏在此时，阳光牌脚下忽然轻快起来；全身的斤两重新压上两脚，我已翻过丘顶，灰白的路面打我脚下一路翻卷着抽出去，我一面摸索着车闸。我回头看拉夫尔斯。他已把双脚收了起来。我把脑袋又拧过去一些，只见孩子们一身睡衣，双手撑膝，活脱一排守桩员（wicket-keeper），还有一个大块头在挥舞拳头。丘顶上立着一根灯柱，那是我见的最后一眼。

===Original===
We sailed down to the river, then on through Thames Ditton as far as Esher Station, when we turned sharp to the right, and from the dark stretch by Imber Court came to light in Molesey, and were soon pedalling like gentlemen of leisure through Bushey Park, our lights turned up, the broken torch put out and away. The big gates had long been shut, but you can manoeuvre a bicycle through the others. We had no further adventures on the way home, and our coffee was still warm upon the hob.
“But I think it’s an occasion for Sullivans,” said Raffles, who now kept them for such. “By all my gods, Bunny, it’s been the most sporting night we ever had in our lives! And do you know which was the most sporting part of it?”

===Chinese===
我们顺坡滑到河边，再穿过泰晤士迪顿（Thames Ditton）一路骑到埃舍尔车站（Esher Station），随即向右一个急转，借着因伯府（Imber Court）旁那段黑黢黢的路程藏住身形，行至莫尔西（Molesey）方见灯火，不多时便已俨然两位有闲绅士，悠然蹬着车穿过布希公园（Bushey Park）——车灯拧亮，砸坏的电筒熄掉收好。大门早就关死了，可侧门之间，总还容得下一辆自行车侧身而过。归途再无别的风波，炉架上我们的咖啡犹自温着。

“不过我想，今夜合当点上一支萨利文（Sullivans），”拉夫尔斯说——他如今专为这类时刻存着它们。“凭我信奉的诸神起誓，邦尼，这是咱们这辈子最够运动味儿的一夜！你可知道，其中最够味儿的是哪一段？”

===Original===
“That uphill ride?”
“I wasn’t thinking of it.”
“Turning your torch into a truncheon?”
“My dear Bunny! A gallant lad﻿—I hated hitting him.”

===Chinese===
“坡上那一段？”

“我没有想它。”

“把你的电筒抡成了警棍？”

“亲爱的邦尼！好一位勇士——我实在不忍下手。”

===Original===
“I know,” I said. “The way you got us out of the house!”
“No, Bunny,” said Raffles, blowing rings. “It came before that, you sinner, and you know it!”

===Chinese===
“我知道，”我说，“是你带咱们脱出那宅子的手段！”

“不对，邦尼，”拉夫尔斯说着，吹出一串烟圈，“还在那之前，你这罪人，你自己心里有数！”

===Original===
“You don’t mean anything I did?” said I, self-consciously, for I began to see that this was what he did mean. And now at latest it will also be seen why this story has been told with undue and inexcusable gusto; there is none other like it for me to tell; it is my one ewe-lamb in all these annals. But Raffles had a ruder name for it.
“It was the Apotheosis of the Bunny,” said he, but in a tone I never shall forget.

===Chinese===
“你总不会是指我干下的什么吧？”我说，自觉忸怩，因为我开始看出，他指的正是这个。事到如今，诸位也终归看得出，这故事为何讲得这般忘乎所以、这般不可宽恕地津津有味；似这般的故事，我再没有第二篇可讲；它是我全部编年史里独生的一只羔羊。只是拉夫尔斯给它起了个粗些的名目。

“那是邦尼的登神大典（Apotheosis），”他说——用的却是我永生难忘的一种语气。

===Original===
“I hardly knew what I was doing or saying,” I said. “The whole thing was a fluke.”
“Then,” said Raffles, “it was the kind of fluke I always trusted you to make when runs were wanted.”
And he held out his dear old hand.

===Chinese===
“我自己也简直不知道当时做了什么、说了什么，”我说。“整桩事情，全凭侥幸。”

“那么，”拉夫尔斯说，“这正是我素来信得过你打出的那种侥幸球——每逢需要得分的时候。”

说着，他伸出了那只亲爱的老手。

## The Knees of the Gods / 诸神的膝上

### I / 一

===Original===
“The worst of this war,” said Raffles, “is the way it puts a fellow off his work.”

It was, of course, the winter before last, and we had done nothing dreadful since the early autumn. Undoubtedly the war was the cause. Not that we were among the earlier victims of the fever. I took disgracefully little interest in the Negotiations, while the Ultimatum appealed to Raffles as a sporting flutter. Then we gave the whole thing till Christmas. We still missed the cricket in the papers. But one russet afternoon we were in Richmond, and a terrible type was shouting himself hoarse with “ ’Eavy British lorsses﻿—orful slorter o’ the Bo‑wers! Orful slorter! Orful slorter! ’Eavy British lorsses!” I thought the terrible type had invented it, but Raffles gave him more than he asked, and then I held the bicycle while he tried to pronounce Eland’s Laagte. We were never again without our sheaf of evening papers, and Raffles ordered three morning ones, and I gave up mine in spite of its literary page. We became strategists. We knew exactly what Buller was to do on landing, and, still better, what the other Generals should have done. Our map was the best that could be bought, with flags that deserved a better fate than standing still. Raffles woke me to hear “The Absentminded Beggar” on the morning it appeared; he was one of the first substantial subscribers to the fund. By this time our dear landlady was more excited than we. To our enthusiasm for Thomas she added a personal bitterness against the Wild Boars, as she persisted in calling them, each time as though it were the first. I could linger over our landlady’s attitude in the whole matter. That was her only joke about it, and the true humorist never smiled at it herself. But you had only to say a syllable for a venerable gentleman, declared by her to be at the bottom of it all, to hear what she could do to him if she caught him. She could put him in a cage and go on tour with him, and make him howl and dance for his food like a debased bear before a fresh audience every day. Yet a more kindhearted woman I have never known. The war did not uplift our landlady as it did her lodgers.

===Chinese===
“这场战争顶糟的地方，”拉夫尔斯说，“就在于它害得一个人没心思干自己的本行。”

那当然是前年冬天的事了；自初秋以来，我们还不曾干过一桩像样的凶事。毫无疑问，罪魁正是这场战争。倒不是说我们早早染上了那场热病。我对“谈判”的那点兴趣少得可耻，而那份“最后通牒”落到拉夫尔斯（Raffles）眼里，倒像一注有滋味的赌。随后我们索性把整桩事宽限到圣诞节再说。报上的板球消息没有了，我们仍旧惦记。可是在一个红褐色的午后，我们正在里士满，一个吓人的角色正喊哑了嗓子吆喝：“英军死伤惨重哎——布尔人惨遭屠杀喽！惨遭屠杀喽！惨遭屠杀喽！英军死伤惨重哎！”我还当是那角色自己编出来的，拉夫尔斯却给了他多过讨价的赏钱，随后我扶着自行车，听他费劲地念“伊兰兹拉赫特（Eland's Laagte）”。从此我们手里再没断过一沓沓晚报，拉夫尔斯订了三份晨报，我自己那份也戒了——尽管它还有个文学版。我们成了战略家。布勒（Buller）登陆后要做什么，我们一清二楚；更了然的，是其余各位将军本该做什么。我们的地图是买得到的最好的一张，上面插着小旗，这些旗子只立不动，实在屈才。《心不在焉的乞丐》见报的那个早晨，拉夫尔斯把我摇醒来听；他是那笔基金最早的几名慷慨认捐人之一。到这时，我们亲爱的房东太太比我们还兴奋。在我们对托马斯（Thomas，英国大兵的代称）的一片热忱之外，她又添了对“野猪”的一腔私恨——这是她认死了称呼布尔人（Boers）的名号（Boers 音谐 boars，“野猪”），每回叫出口，都像头一回想起这个俏皮似的。关于我们这位房东太太在整桩事上的态度，我本可以细细流连一番。“野猪”是她在这桩事上唯一的笑话，而真正的幽默家自己是从来不笑的。可你只要替一位可敬的老先生吐出半个音节——她一口咬定这老先生才是一切的祸根——你便能领教她逮住他要怎么发落。她能把他关进笼子，带着他四处巡回，叫他像一头堕落的熊，每天换一批新看客，为口粮又嚎又舞。然而再没有比她更心善的女人了。这场战争抬举了她的房客，却没有同样抬举她。

===Original===
But presently it ceased to have that precise effect upon us. Bad was being made worse and worse; and then came more than Englishmen could endure in that black week across which the names of three African villages are written forever in letters of blood. “All three pegs,” groaned Raffles on the last morning of the week; “neck-and-crop, neck-and-crop!” It was his first word of cricket since the beginning of the war.

We were both depressed. Old schoolfellows had fallen, and I know Raffles envied them; he spoke so wistfully of such an end. To cheer him up I proposed to break into one of the many more or less royal residences in our neighborhood; a tough crib was what he needed; but I will not trouble you with what he said to me. There was less crime in England that winter than for years past; there was none at all in Raffles. And yet there were those who could denounce the war!

===Chinese===
可是不久，这股抬举的效力在我们身上也到了头。坏局面正越变越坏；接着便来了那黑色的一周——英国人再也受不住的一周，三个非洲村寨的名字，从此用血写的字迹永远横贯其上。“三根桩子全倒了，”那个星期最后一天的早晨，拉夫尔斯呻吟道，“一败涂地，一败涂地！”这是开战以来他嘴里头一句板球话。

我们两个都灰心丧气。老同学接连阵亡，我知道拉夫尔斯是羡慕他们的；他谈起那样的结局时，神情那么怅惘。为了给他提神，我提议去撬我们左近某一处多少沾点王气的宅邸；他需要的正是一桩够硬的案子；至于他回了我什么话，就不劳诸位听了。那年冬天，英格兰的罪案比过去多少年都少；拉夫尔斯身上则一件也没有。可居然还有人骂得出这场战争！

===Original===
So we went on for a few of those dark days, Raffles very glum and grim, till one fine morning the Yeomanry idea put new heart into us all. It struck me at once as the glorious scheme it was to prove, but it did not hit me where it hit others. I was not a foxhunter, and the gentlemen of England would scarcely have owned me as one of them. The case of Raffles was in that respect still more hopeless (he who had even played for them at Lord’s), and he seemed to feel it. He would not speak to me all the morning; in the afternoon he went for a walk alone. It was another man who came home, flourishing a small bottle packed in white paper.

“Bunny,” said he, “I never did lift my elbow; it’s the one vice I never had. It has taken me all these years to find my tipple, Bunny; but here it is, my panacea, my elixir, my magic philtre!”

I thought he had been at it on the road, and asked him the name of the stuff.

“Look and see, Bunny.”

===Chinese===
就这样又熬了几天那样的暗日子，拉夫尔斯一直阴沉板滞，直到一个晴朗的早晨，义勇骑兵（Yeomanry）的构想给举国上下重新鼓起了劲。我一眼便看出，这是个日后必将证明辉煌的计划，可它击中我的地方，同击中旁人的不一样。我不是猎狐客，英格兰的绅士们几乎不会认我作同类。拉夫尔斯在这层上更没指望（他甚至在罗德板球场上替绅士队出过场），而他似乎也感觉到了。一整个上午他不肯同我说话；下午他独自出门散步。回家的已是另一个人，挥着一只用白纸包着的小瓶。

“邦尼（Bunny），”他说，“我这辈子从不举肘贪杯；那是我唯一不曾沾染的恶习。这么多年我才寻见我那口好，邦尼；瞧，就是它——我的万灵药，我的不老丹，我的魔幻灵液！”

我还当他半道上已经喝上了，便问那东西的名目。

“自己瞧去，邦尼。”

===Original===
And if it wasn’t a bottle of ladies’ hair-dye, warranted to change any shade into the once fashionable yellow within a given number of applications!

“What on earth,” said I, “are you going to do with this?”

“Dye for my country,” he cried, swelling. “Dulce et decorum est, Bunny, my boy!”

“Do you mean that you are going to the front?”

“If I can without coming to it.”

===Chinese===
竟是一瓶妇女用的染发剂，保票上写明：只消按限定的回数涂用，管保任何发色都染成当年风行一时的那种黄！

“你到底，”我说，“打算拿它做什么？”

“为祖国染发！”他挺起胸膛嚷道。“为国捐躯，其甘如饴（Dulce et decorum est），邦尼，我的孩子！”（按：原文 dye“染发”谐音 die“死”，双关“为国而死”。）

“你是说，你要上前线？”

“去得成、又不至于真凑上去的话。”

===Original===
I looked at him as he stood in the firelight, straight as a dart, spare but wiry, alert, laughing, flushed from his wintry walk; and as I looked, all the years that I had known him, and more besides, slipped from him in my eyes. I saw him captain of the eleven at school. I saw him running with the muddy ball on days like this, running round the other fifteen as a sheepdog round a flock of sheep. He had his cap on still, and but for the gray hairs underneath﻿—but here I lost him in a sudden mist. It was not sorrow at his going, for I did not mean to let him go alone. It was enthusiasm, admiration, affection, and also, I believe, a sudden regret that he had not always appealed to that part of my nature to which he was appealing now. It was a little thrill of penitence. Enough of it.

“I think it great of you,” I said, and at first that was all.

How he laughed at me! He had had his innings; there was no better way of getting out. He had scored off an African millionaire, the Players, a Queensland Legislator, the Camorra, the late Lord Ernest Belville, and again and again off Scotland Yard. What more could one man do in one lifetime? And at the worst it was the death to die: no bed, no doctor, no temperature﻿—and Raffles stopped himself.

===Chinese===
我望着他站在火光里，身姿挺拔如箭，精瘦而筋骨结实，机警、含笑、冬夜散步归来满面红光；我望着望着，认识他以来的那些岁月，连同更多的岁月，在我眼里一寸寸从他身上滑落了。我看见他做学校十一队队长。我看见他在这样的日子里抱着泥糊糊的球飞奔，像牧羊犬绕着羊群，绕过对方那十五个人。他头上还扣着那顶帽，若不是帽檐底下那几缕白发——可在这里，一阵突然升起的迷雾把他从我眼前藏了去。那不是伤别的愁绪，因为我本没打算让他独自走。那是热忱，是钦慕，是爱恋，还有——我相信——一种突然的悔意：他从前竟不曾唤起我天性的这一面，而如今他唤起了。那是一阵小小的忏悔的战栗。够了。

“我觉得你这了不起，”我说，起初只说了这一句。

他笑我笑得多厉害！他的击球轮次已经打完；退场，再没有比这更好的路数。他扳倒过一位非洲百万富翁、“职业选手”们、一位昆士兰立法委员、克莫拉、已故的欧内斯特·贝尔维尔勋爵，还一次又一次扳倒过苏格兰场。一个人一辈子还能做更多么？而往最坏里说，那也是该死的一种死法：没有病床，没有医生，没有体温——说到这里，拉夫尔斯把话头收住了。

===Original===
“No pinioning, no white cap,” he added, “if you like that better.”

“I don’t like any of it,” I cried, cordially; “you’ve simply got to come back.”

“To what?” he asked, a strange look on him. And I wondered﻿—for one instant﻿—whether my little thrill had gone through him. He was not a man of little thrills.

Then for a minute I was in misery. Of course I wanted to go too﻿—he shook my hand without a word﻿—but how could I? They would never have me, a branded jailbird, in the Imperial Yeomanry! Raffles burst out laughing; he had been looking very hard at me for about three seconds.

“You rabbit,” he cried, “even to think of it! We might as well offer ourselves to the Metropolitan Police Force. No, Bunny, we go out to the Cape on our own, and that’s where we enlist. One of these regiments of irregular horse is the thing for us; you spent part of your pretty penny on horseflesh, I believe, and you remember how I rode in the bush! We’re the very men for them, Bunny, and they won’t ask to see our birthmarks out there. I don’t think even my hoary locks would put them off, but it would be too conspicuous in the ranks.”

===Chinese===
“不捆胳膊，不扣白帽，”他补了一句，“你要是觉得这样听着受用些的话。”

“我一样也不喜欢，”我由衷地嚷道，“你只管得回来。”

“回到什么上去？”他问，脸上有一种异样的神情。有一瞬——只有一瞬——我疑心我那点小战栗是不是也传到了他身上。他可不是个只会打小战栗的人。

随后有那么一分钟，我陷在苦闷里。我自然也想同去——他一言不发地握了握我的手——可我怎么去得了？他们决不会收我，一个烙了印的牢犯，进帝国义勇骑兵队！拉夫尔斯放声大笑；他足足盯了我约莫三秒钟。

“你这兔子，”他嚷道，“连想都别想！我们还不如自荐去投首都警察部队。不行，邦尼，我们自己想法子到开普去，就在那儿入伍。眼下那些非正规骑兵团才是给我们预备的；我记得你那份可观的家私，有一部分花在了马匹上，你也记得我在丛林里骑马的样子！我们正是他们要的那种人，邦尼，而且在那边，他们不会要人出示我们的‘胎记’。我想我这把白发苍髯倒未必吓得退他们，只是搁在队列里太惹眼了。”

===Original===
Our landlady first wept on hearing our determination, and then longed to have the pulling of certain whiskers (with the tongs, and they should be red-hot); but from that day, and for as many as were left to us, the good soul made more of us than ever. Not that she was at all surprised; dear brave gentlemen who could look for burglars on their bicycles at dead of night, it was only what you might expect of them, bless their lion hearts. I wanted to wink at Raffles, but he would not catch my eye. He was a ginger-headed Raffles by the end of January, and it was extraordinary what a difference it made. His most elaborate disguises had not been more effectual than this simple expedient, and, with khaki to complete the subdual of his individuality, he had every hope of escaping recognition in the field. The man he dreaded was the officer he had known in old days; there were ever so many of him at the Front; and it was to minimize this risk that we went out second-class at the beginning of February.

It was a weeping day, a day in a shroud, cold as clay, yet for that very reason an ideal day upon which to leave England for the sunny Front. Yet my heart was heavy as I looked my last at her; it was heavy as the raw, thick air, until Raffles came and leant upon the rail at my side.

“I know what you are thinking, and you’ve got to stop,” said he. “It’s on the knees of the gods, Bunny, whether we do or we don’t, and thinking won’t make us see over their shoulders.”

===Chinese===
房东太太初听我们的决定先哭了，随后一心巴望去亲手拔掉某几撇大胡子（拿火钳拔，还得先烧得通红）；但从那一天起，直到余下的每一天，好心的老人家待我们比以往更亲厚。倒不是她有多惊讶；像这样深更半夜骑着自行车捉贼的亲爱的勇敢绅士，落得这般下场原是意料之中，愿上天保佑这颗颗狮子心。我想朝拉夫尔斯眨眼，他却不肯接我的眼色。到一月底，拉夫尔斯已经是一头姜黄发了，那改换之大实在出奇。他从前最讲究的化装，也不曾比这个简便的权宜之计更有效；再加上卡其军装替他把个性掩了个彻底，他满有希望在战场上逃过相识人的眼睛。他最怕的那种人，是他旧日认识的军官；前线满处都是这样的人；正是为了把这点风险降到最小，二月初我们才坐二等舱动身。

那是个垂泪的日子，裹着尸衣的一天，冷得像墓土，然而正因如此，才是撇下英格兰奔赴阳光前线的好日子。可我的心还是沉甸甸的，当我最后望她一眼；它沉得像那又生又厚的空气，直到拉夫尔斯走过来，靠着栏杆，立在我身旁。

“我知道你在想什么，你非停下不可，”他说。“成与不成，全悬在诸神的膝上，邦尼；光是想，是想不站到他们肩后去张望的。”

### II / 二

===Original===
Now I made as bad a soldier (except at heart) as Raffles made a good one, and I could not say a harder thing of myself. My ignorance of matters military was up to that time unfathomable, and is still profound. I was always a fool with horses, though I did not think so at one time, and I had never been any good with a gun. The average Tommy may be my intellectual inferior, but he must know some part of his work better than I ever knew any of mine. I never even learnt to be killed. I do not mean that I ever ran away. The South African Field Force might have been strengthened if I had.

The foregoing remarks do not express a pose affected out of superiority to the usual spirit of the conquering hero, for no man was keener on the war than I, before I went to it. But one can only write with gusto of events (like that little affair at Surbiton) in which one has acquitted oneself without discredit, and I cannot say that of my part in the war, of which I now loathe the thought for other reasons. The battlefield was no place for me, and neither was the camp. My ineptitude made me the butt of the looting, cursing, swashbuckling lot who formed the very irregular squadron which we joined; and it would have gone hard with me but for Raffles, who was soon the darling devil of them all, but never more loyally my friend. Your fireside fire-eater does not think of these things. He imagines all the fighting to be with the enemy. He will probably be horrified to hear that men can detest each other as cordially in khaki as in any other wear, and with a virulence seldom inspired by the bearded dead-shot in the opposite trench. To the fireside fire-eater, therefore (for you have seen me one myself), I dedicate the story of Corporal Connal, Captain Bellingham, the General, Raffles, and myself.

===Chinese===
说到当兵，我坏得同拉夫尔斯的好一样到家（心是例外），这话再没有更重的了。军中的事，我的无知那时深不见底，如今依然深厚。我一向是马上的笨蛋，虽然曾有一时自己不觉得；枪也从来打不好。寻常一名英国大兵，智力未必及我，但他对本行的把握，总比我对我那一行的任何部分都强。我连“怎么挨杀”都没学会。我不是说我逃跑过。我若逃了，南非野战军倒说不定还能添些实力。

上面这些话，并非故作姿态、以一副自居优越的架子，凌驾于凯旋英雄惯常的豪情之上；没上前线之前，论对这场战争的热心，没有人比得过我。可人只能津津乐道自己不曾丢脸的事（譬如苏比顿那一桩小插曲），至于我在战争里的那一角，我却不能这么说——如今另有缘故，一想起来就叫我憎恶。战场不是我这号人的地方，营盘也不是。我的无能，叫我成了那伙劫掠的、咒骂的、耀武扬威的家伙们的靶子，他们凑成我们投效的那支最不正规的骑队；要不是拉夫尔斯，我的日子会很不好过，他不久便成了他们人人宠爱的淘气魔头，却从来没有比那阵子更忠心做我的朋友。你们炉边的喊战客想不着这些。他以为仗全是同敌人打的。听说穿卡其的人彼此厌恶起来，可以同穿任何别的衣裳时一样狠，而那份恶毒，对面战壕里留大胡子的神枪手都难得激起，他多半要吓一跳。所以，谨以此篇——康纳尔下士（Connal）、贝林厄姆上尉（Bellingham）、将军、拉夫尔斯，与我——献给炉边的喊战客（因为诸位见过，我自己也当过一回）。

===Original===
I must be vague, for obvious reasons. The troop is fighting as I write; you will soon hear why I am not; but neither is Raffles, nor Corporal Connal. They are fighting as well as ever, those other hard-living, harder-dying sons of all soils; but I am not going to say where it was that we fought with them. I believe that no body of men of equal size has done half so much heroic work. But they had got themselves a bad name off the field, so to speak; and I am not going to make it worse by saddling them before the world with Raffles and myself, and that ruffian Connal.

The fellow was a mongrel type, a Glasgow Irishman by birth and upbringing, but he had been in South Africa for years, and he certainly knew the country very well. This circumstance, coupled with the fact that he was a very handy man with horses, as all colonists are, had procured him the first small step from the ranks which facilitates bullying if a man be a bully by nature, and is physically fitted to be a successful one. Connal was a hulking ruffian, and in me had ideal game. The brute was offensive to me from the hour I joined. The details are of no importance, but I stood up to him at first in words, and finally for a few seconds on my feet. Then I went down like an ox, and Raffles came out of his tent. Their fight lasted twenty minutes, and Raffles was marked, but the net result was dreadfully conventional, for the bully was a bully no more.

But I began gradually to suspect that he was something worse. All this time we were fighting every day, or so it seems when I look back. Never a great engagement, and yet never a day when we were wholly out of touch with the enemy. I had thus several opportunities of watching the other enemy under fire, and had almost convinced myself of the systematic harmlessness of his own shooting, when a more glaring incident occurred.

===Chinese===
我只能说得含糊，理由显而易见。我动笔之际，这支骑队还在作战；诸位不久会听到我为什么不在了；可拉夫尔斯不在，康纳尔下士也不在。那些别的汉子还在打，打得同从前一样好——那些五湖四海出身、活得艰难、死得更难的汉子；但我们同他们一起打过仗的地方，我不打算说。我相信，同等规模的队伍，没有一支立过他们一半的英雄业绩。可是，姑且这么说吧，他们在战场之外给自己挣了个坏名声；我不肯拿拉夫尔斯和我，还有那恶棍康纳尔，驮到他们名下、送到世人眼前，把这名声再糟蹋一分。

那家伙是个杂糅的种，籍贯和教养都是格拉斯哥的爱尔兰人，可他在南非洲混了好些年，对那地方确实熟得很。这层情形，加上他马上的功夫出众（殖民地客个个如此），给他挣来了脱离行伍的头一级小台阶；这一级台阶，若人天生是个恶霸、身子又是当恶霸的好料，最能助长威风。康纳尔是个五大三粗的恶棍，而我，正是他理想的猎物。打我入队那个时辰起，这畜生就处处冒犯我。细节无关紧要，总之起初我拿话顶他，末了站起身顶了他几秒钟。然后我像一头牛似的倒了下去，拉夫尔斯出了帐篷。两人的架打了二十分钟，拉夫尔斯挂了彩，可净结果俗套得可怕：恶霸不再是恶霸了。

可我渐渐起疑：他还不止是个恶霸。这段时候，我们天天在打——回望起来似乎如此。从没有一场大仗，却也从没有一天同敌人完全断了接触。于是我得了好些机会，在枪火底下观察那另一个敌人，几乎已说服自己：他放的枪自有其一贯的无害——这时节，一件更扎眼的事故发生了。

===Original===
One night three troops of our squadron were ordered to a certain point whither they had patrolled the previous week; but our own particular troop was to stay behind, and in charge of no other than the villainous corporal, both our officer and sergeant having gone into hospital with enteric. Our detention, however, was very temporary, and Connal would seem to have received the usual vague orders to proceed in the early morning to the place where the other three companies had camped. It appeared that we were to form an escort to two squadron-wagons containing kits, provisions, and ammunition.

Before daylight Connal had reported his departure to the commanding officer, and we passed the outposts at gray dawn. Now, though I was perhaps the least observant person in the troop, I was not the least wideawake where Corporal Connal was concerned, and it struck me at once that we were heading in the wrong direction. My reasons are not material, but as a matter of fact our last week’s patrol had pushed its khaki tentacles both east and west; and eastward they had met with resistance so determined as to compel them to retire; yet it was eastward that we were travelling now. I at once spurred alongside Raffles, as he rode, bronzed and bearded, with warworn wideawake over eyes grown keen as a hawk’s, and a cutty-pipe sticking straight out from his front teeth. I can see him now, so gaunt and grim and debonair, yet already with much of the nonsense gone out of him, though I thought he only smiled on my misgivings.

“Did he get the instructions, Bunny, or did we? Very well, then; give the devil a chance.”

There was nothing further to be said, but I felt more crushed than convinced; so we jogged along into broad daylight, until Raffles himself gave a whistle of surprise.

“A white flag, Bunny, by all my gods!”

===Chinese===
一夜，我们中队的另外三支小队奉命开往一处地点——上周他们巡逻去过那里；我们这一支偏奉命留守，交由的偏是那个恶棍下士，我们的军官和军士都因伤寒进了医院。不过这扣留非常短暂，看来康纳尔接到的仍是那种惯常的含混命令：翌晨开到另外三队扎营之处。看样子，我们是要给两辆中队的辎重车押队，车上装着行装、粮秣和弹药。

天亮之前，康纳尔已向指挥官报告启程，我们在灰蒙蒙的黎明越过了前哨。固然我或许是全队眼力最差的人，可一说到康纳尔下士，我却不曾有一刻不警醒；当下我心里便是一动：我们走的方向不对。理由无关紧要；事实是，上周的巡逻把卡其色的触须东西两向都伸了出去；向东那一向遇上了坚决的抵抗，不得不退回；而我们此刻偏偏朝东走。我立刻策马赶上去，同拉夫尔斯并辔。他骑在马上，面皮晒成古铜，须髯满脸，一顶战损的宽檐毡帽（wideawake）罩在鹰隼一般锐利的眼睛上，一杆短烟斗从前牙间直直戳出。他此刻那副样子我还看得见，又憔悴、又冷峻、又洒脱，虽然他身上那些不着边际的戏谑已褪去大半——当时我只当他对我的疑心不过一笑置之。

“命令是发给他的，还是发给我们的？好，那就给魔鬼一个机会。”

此外没什么可说的了，可我与其说是被说服，不如说是被压垮；于是我们缓缓前行，直到天光大亮，直到拉夫尔斯自己惊得打了一声呼哨。

“白旗，邦尼，凭我信奉的诸神！”

===Original===
I could not see it; he had the longest sight in all our squadron; but in a little the fluttering emblem, which had gained such a sinister significance in most of our eyes, was patent even to mine. A little longer, and the shaggy Boer was in our midst upon his shaggy pony, with a half-scared, half-incredulous look in his deep-set eyes. He was on his way to our lines with some missive, and had little enough to say to us, though frivolous and flippant questions were showered upon him from most saddles.

“Any Boers over there?” asked one, pointing in the direction in which we were still heading.

“Shut up!” interjected Raffles in crisp rebuke.

The Boer looked stolid but sinister.

“Any of our chaps?” added another.

The Boer rode on with an open grin.

===Chinese===
我看不见；全中队数他眼力最远；可不多时，那面飘摇的旗——它在我们多数人眼里已带上凶兆的意味——连我也看得明白了。再过一会儿，那毛发蓬乱的布尔人骑着同样毛发蓬乱的小马，已到了我们中间，深陷的眼睛里一半是怕，一半是不敢信。他是往我们防线送信去的，同我们没什么可说，尽管大半副鞍子上朝他泼来轻浮刻薄的问话。

“那边有布尔人没有？”一个指着我们仍朝前进的方向问。

“住口！”拉夫尔斯脆生生地喝住。

布尔人木然不动，目光却凶。

“我们的人有没有？”另一个又问。

布尔人咧着嘴，径自骑过去了。

===Original===
And the incredible conclusion of the matter was that we were actually within their lines in another hour; saw them as large as life within a mile and a half on either side of us; and must every man of us have been taken prisoner had not every man but Connal refused to go one inch further, and had not the Boers themselves obviously suspected some subtle ruse as the only conceivable explanation of so madcap a manoeuvre. They allowed us to retire without firing a shot; and retire you may be sure we did, the Kaffirs flogging their teams in a fury of fear, and our precious corporal sullen but defiant.

I have said this was the conclusion of the matter, and I blush to repeat that it practically was. Connal was indeed wheeled up before the colonel, but his instructions were not written instructions, and he lied his way out with equal hardihood and tact.

“You said ‘over there,’ sir,” he stoutly reiterated; and the vagueness with which such orders were undoubtedly given was the saving of him for the time being.

===Chinese===
这桩事的结果不可思议：一个钟头后，我们竟真的进了他们的防线之内；两旁一英里半光景，他们的人清清楚楚，同活生生立在眼前一样；若不是除了康纳尔人人拒绝再往前挪一寸，若不是布尔人自己也显然疑心，这场疯子般的机动只有一种可想象的解释——某种诡秘的计策——我们全队无一不被俘。他们未发一弹，放我们退去；退，我们自然退了，卡菲尔（Kaffir，旧时对南非黑人役夫的通称）车夫们在恐惧的狂怒中鞭打牲口，我们那位宝贝下士沉着脸，却毫不服软。

我说这是这桩事的结局，说来脸红，实际上也差不多就是。康纳尔确实被拉到上校面前，可他的命令不是书面命令；他撒谎撒得又硬气又圆滑，硬是脱了身。

“您说的是‘在那边’，长官，”他一口咬定地重申；而这类命令无疑向来给得含混，倒把他暂时保住了。

===Original===
I need not tell you how indignant I felt, for one.

“The fellow is a spy!” I said to Raffles, with no nursery oath, as we strolled within the lines that night.

He merely smiled in my face.

“And have you only just found it out, Bunny? I have known it almost ever since we joined; but this morning I did think we had him on toast.”

“It’s disgraceful that we had not,” cried I. “He ought to have been shot like a dog.”

“Not so loud, Bunny, though I quite agree; but I don’t regret what has happened as much as you do. Not that I am less bloodthirsty than you are in this case, but a good deal more so! Bunny, I’m mad-keen on bowling him out with my own unaided hand﻿—though I may ask you to take the wicket. Meanwhile, don’t wear all your animosity upon your sleeve; the fellow has friends who still believe in him; and there is no need for you to be more openly his enemy than you were before.”

===Chinese===
我有多愤慨，不必说给你们听，至少我如此。

“那家伙是奸细！”那天夜里，我们在防线内溜达时，我对拉夫尔斯说，没有夹半句孩子气的赌咒。

他只对着我的脸笑。

“你也才看出来么，邦尼？我们入队以来我几乎一直心里有数；可今天早上，我当真以为他已上了咱们的砧板。”

“没拿下他才是耻辱！”我嚷道。“早该像条狗似的把他毙了。”

“小声点，邦尼，虽然我完全同意；可方才的事，我不像你那么懊悔。这件事上我不是不如你嗜血，是比你还嗜血得多！邦尼，我痴心妄想要凭自己赤手空拳把他投杀出局——不过守桩的活儿，或许得请你来。眼前你且别把敌意全挂在袖子上；那家伙还有几个信他的朋友，你犯不着比从前更露骨地同他为敌。”

===Original===
Well, I can only vow that I did my best to follow this sound advice; but who but a Raffles can control his every look? It was never my forte, as you know, yet to this day I cannot conceive what I did to excite the treacherous corporal’s suspicions. He was clever enough, however, not to betray them, and lucky enough to turn the tables on us, as you shall hear.

===Chinese===
好吧，我只能赌咒说，我尽了力照这条好主意办；可除了拉夫尔斯，谁能管住自己的每一副眼神？这不是我的长项，你们知道；直到今天，我也想不出自己做了什么，惹动了那奸猾下士的疑心。好在他够聪明，没有把疑心露出来；又够走运，反将了我们一军——诸位往下听便知。

### III / 三

===Original===
Bloemfontein had fallen since our arrival, but there was plenty of fight in the Free Staters still, and I will not deny that it was these gentry who were showing us the sport for which our corps came in. Constant skirmishing was our portion, with now and then an action that you would know at least by name, did I feel free to mention them. But I do not, and indeed it is better so. I have not to describe the war even as I saw it, I am thankful to say, but only the martial story of us two and those others of whom you wot. Corporal Connal was the dangerous blackguard you have seen. Captain Bellingham is best known for his position in the batting averages a year or two ago, and for his subsequent failure to obtain a place in any of the five Test Matches. But I only think of him as the officer who recognized Raffles.

We had taken a village, making quite a little name for it and for ourselves, and in the village our division was reinforced by a fresh brigade of the Imperial troops. It was a day of rest, our first for weeks, but Raffles and I spent no small part of it in seeking high and low for a worthy means of quenching the kind of thirst which used to beset Yeomen and others who had left good cellars for the veldt. The old knack came back to us both, though I believe that I alone was conscious of it at the time; and we were leaving the house, splendidly supplied, when we almost ran into the arms of an infantry officer, with a scowl upon his red-hot face, and an eyeglass flaming at us in the sun.

“Peter Bellingham!” gasped Raffles under his breath, and then we saluted and tried to pass on, with the bottles ringing like church-bells under our khaki. But Captain Bellingham was a hard man.

===Chinese===
我们到了之后，布隆方丹（Bloemfontein）陷落了，可自由邦的战士们还有的是斗志，我也不想否认，正是这班老爷们，让我们这支部队见识了它此来所为何来的那种“游戏”。小接触不断是我们的日常；偶尔也有一场战役，我若敢提，诸位至少听过名字。但我不提，而且不提确实更好。说来谢天谢地，我无须描写这场战争，哪怕是我亲眼所见的样子；我要写的，只是我们两个的军旅故事，和诸位心知肚明的那几个人的。康纳尔下士，是诸位已见识过的那个危险的恶棍。贝林厄姆上尉最出名的，是他一两年前在击球率榜上的名次，以及随后未能跻身那五场对抗赛（Test Matches）的任何一场。可在我心里，他只是那个认出拉夫尔斯的军官。

我们攻占了一个村子，给村子也给我们自己挣了不小的名声；在这村子里，我们的师得了帝国军队一个生力旅的增援。那是个歇兵的日子，几个星期来的头一个，可拉夫尔斯和我把大半日光景花在了上穷下索，要找一样够格的东西来解那种渴——凡是撇下好酒窖跑到草原（veldt）上来的义勇骑兵之流，都被这种渴缠过身。那副老练的手艺回到我们两人身上，不过我相信当时只有我一个人心里有数；我们补给得富丽堂皇，正要出那座宅子，险些一头撞进一名步兵军官怀里——他通红的脸上锁着怒容，单片眼镜在太阳底下朝我们冒火。

“彼得·贝林厄姆！”拉夫尔斯压着嗓子倒吸一口气；随后我们行了个礼，想径直走过去，卡其军衣底下的瓶子叮当作响，像教堂的钟。可贝林厄姆上尉是个难缠的人。

===Original===
“What have you men been doin’?” drawled he.

“Nothing, sir,” we protested, like innocence with an injury.

“Lootin’ ’s forbidden,” said he. “You had better let me see those bottles.”

“We are done,” whispered Raffles, and straightway we made a sideboard of the stoop across which he had crept at so inopportune a moment. I had not the heart to raise my eyes again, yet it was many moments before the officer broke silence.

“Uam Var!” he murmured reverentially at last. “And Long John of Ben Nevis! The first drop that’s been discovered in the whole psalm-singing show! What lot do you two belong to?”

===Chinese===
“你们这伙人在干——什么？”他拖长调门问。

“没什么，长官，”我们分辩道，活像受了冤枉的无辜者。

“抢掠是明令禁止的，”他说。“你们最好把那些瓶子让我看看。”

“完了，”拉夫尔斯低声说，随即我们权把门廊台阶当了餐边柜——他偏偏挑了那么个不是时候的当口摸上台阶来。我再没有勇气抬眼，军官却又过了好一会儿才开口。

“乌阿姆·瓦尔（Uam Var）！”他终于肃然低叹。“还有本尼维斯的‘朗约翰（Long John）’！整场唱诗班大军里搜出来的头一滴好酒！你们两个是哪一队的人马？”

===Original===
I answered.

“I must have your names.”

In my agitation I gave my real one. Raffles had turned away, as though in heartbroken contemplation of our lost loot. I saw the officer studying his half-profile with an alarming face.

“What’s *your* name?” he rapped out at last.

But his strange, low voice said plainly that he knew, and Raffles faced him with the monosyllable of confession and assent. I did not count the seconds until the next word, but it was Captain Bellingham who uttered it at last.

===Chinese===
我答了。

“我得记下你们的名字。”

慌乱里，我把真名报了。拉夫尔斯早已转过身去，仿佛在心碎地凭吊我们得而复失的赃物。我看见那军官端详着他的半侧脸，脸色可怖。

“*你*叫什么名字？”他终于厉声喝问。

可那古怪的低嗓门分明在说：他知道。拉夫尔斯迎着他，吐出一个既认罪又认账的单音字。下一个字来之前隔了几秒，我没有数；可末了把它吐出来的，是贝林厄姆上尉。

===Original===
“I thought you were dead.”

“Now you see I am not.”

“But you are at your old games!”

“I am not,” cried Raffles, and his tone was new to me. I have seldom heard one more indignant. “Yes,” he continued, “this is loot, and the wrong ’un will out. That’s what you’re thinking, Peter﻿—I beg your pardon﻿—sir. But he isn’t let out in the field! We’re playing the game as much as you are, old﻿—sir.”

The plural number caused the captain to toss me a contemptuous look. “Is this the fellah who was taken when you swam for it?” he inquired, relapsing into his drawl. Raffles said I was, and with that took a passionate oath upon our absolute rectitude as volunteers. There could be no doubting him; but the officer’s eyes went back at the bottles on the stoop.

===Chinese===
“我还当你死了。”

“如今你瞧见我没有。”

“可你又在玩你那套老把戏！”

“我没有！”拉夫尔斯嚷道，那语气是我从未听过的；出离愤怒的语气，我少有听见。“不错，”他接着说，“这是战利品，可坏种总归要露馅。你心里正这么想，彼得——对不起——长官。可在战场上，坏种是不放出来的！我们同你一样守规矩，老——长官。”

那个复数叫上尉朝我甩来一瞥轻蔑。“就是你泅水逃命时被抓住的那个伙计？”他又拖起腔来问。拉夫尔斯说我就是，随即慷慨起誓，赌咒我们以志愿兵身份行事堂堂正正、绝无虚假。他这话没有可疑之处；可军官的眼睛又溜回台阶上的瓶子。

===Original===
“But look at those,” said he; and as he looked himself the light eye melted in his fiery face. “And I’ve got Sparklets in my tent,” he sighed. “You make it in a minute!”

Not a word from Raffles, and none, you may be sure, from me. Then suddenly Bellingham told me where his tent was, and, adding that our case was one for serious consideration, strode in its direction without another word until some sunlit paces separated us.

“You can bring that stuff with you,” he then flung over a shoulder-strap, “and I advise you to put it where you had it before.”

A trooper saluted him some yards further on, and looked evilly at us as we followed with our loot. It was Corporal Connal of ours, and the thought of him takes my mind off the certainly gallant captain who only that day had joined our division with the reinforcements. I could not stand the man myself. He added soda-water to our whiskey in his tent, and would only keep a couple of bottles when we came away. Softened by the spirit, to which disuse made us all a little sensitive, our officer was soon convinced of the honest part that we were playing for once, and for fifty minutes of the hour we spent with him he and Raffles talked cricket without a break. On parting they even shook hands; that was Long John in the captain’s head; but the snob never addressed a syllable to me.

===Chinese===
“可你瞧瞧这些，”他说；他自己看着看着，那张火辣脸上的浅色眼睛竟融化了。“我帐篷里还备着斯帕克莱特汽水机（Sparklets），”他叹口气。“兑上一杯，你们顶多一分钟！”

拉夫尔斯一言不发；我嘛，不用说你也能料到，同样一言不发。随后贝林厄姆忽然告诉我他的帐篷在哪儿，又补一句说，我们这案子值得认真考虑；他朝那方向大步走去，再没说一个字，直到阳光把我们隔开几步。

他把头也不回，话甩过肩来：“这好东西你们可以带上，不过我劝你们还是搁回原处去。”

再走几步，一名骑兵向他敬礼，在我们带着赃物跟上去时，恶狠狠地打量我们。那正是我们队的康纳尔下士。一想到他，我的心便从那位确乎英勇的上尉身上挪开了——那位上尉，就是当天随增援部队编入我们师的。我自己也受不了那家伙。他在帐篷里往我们的威士忌里兑苏打水，等我们告辞时，也只肯留下两瓶。酒精软化了这位军官——久违了杯中之物，我们都有些不胜酒力——他不久便信了：这一回，我们扮的可是诚实角色。同他相处的一个钟头里，有五十分钟，他和拉夫尔斯不间断地谈板球。临别他们竟还握了手——那是“朗约翰”在上尉脑袋里使的劲；可那位势利眼，自始至终没对我吐过一个音节。

===Original===
And now to the gallows-bird who was still corporal of our troop: it was not long before Raffles was to have his wish and the traitor’s wicket. We had resumed our advance, or rather our humble part in the great surrounding movement then taking place, and were under pretty heavy fire once more, when Connal was shot in the hand. It was a curious casualty in more than one respect, and nobody seems to have seen it happen. Though a flesh wound, it was a bloody one, and that may be why the surgeon did not at once detect those features which afterwards convinced him that the injury had been self-inflicted. It was the right hand, and until it healed the man could be of no further use in the firing line; nor was the case serious enough for admission to a crowded field-hospital; and Connal himself offered his services as custodian of a number of our horses which we were keeping out of harm’s way in a donga. They had come there in the following manner. That morning we had been heliographed to reinforce the C.M.R., only to find that the enemy had the range to a nicety when we reached the spot. There were trenches for us men, but no place of safety for our horses nearer than this long and narrow donga which ran from within our lines towards those of the Boers. So some of us galloped them thither, six-in-hand, amid the whine of shrapnel and the whistle of shot. I remember the man next me being killed by a shell with all his team, and the tangle of flying harness, torn horseflesh, and crimson khaki, that we left behind us on the veldt; also that a small red flag, ludicrously like those used to indicate a putting-green, marked the single sloping entrance to the otherwise precipitous donga, which I for one was duly thankful to reach alive.

===Chinese===
至于那个绞架上讨活路的惯鸟、仍在我们队里当下士的家伙：要不了多久，拉夫尔斯便要如愿以偿，取下叛徒的桩。我们恢复了推进——不如说，恢复了在这场其时正在展开的大包围运动里我们那份卑微的份——再度处在相当猛烈的火力之下，这时节，康纳尔的手上中了弹。这桩伤亡的稀奇处不止一端，而且似乎没人看见它发生。虽是皮肉伤，血却流得凶；军医或许因此没有立刻看出那些特征——正是这些特征，后来叫军医确信：伤是自己打的。伤在右手；治愈之前，此人在火线上再无用处；伤势又不够重，挤不进那拥挤的野战医院；康纳尔于是自己请缨，看守我们安置在一条干沟（donga）里避弹的一批马。这些马是这样到那儿的。那天早晨，我们奉日光信号电令去增援 C.M.R.，待赶到时才发现，敌人早把射程校得毫厘不爽。人有战壕可藏，马却没有比那条又长又窄的干沟更近的安全去处——那沟从我们防线之内一直通到布尔人的防线跟前。于是我们中间几个人把它们飞骑赶到那里，六匹一串，顶着榴霰弹的呼啸和子弹的尖哨。我记得身边那名骑士连同他的整串人马被一发炮弹打翻，记得我们丢在草原上的那团狼藉：乱飞的辔具、撕裂的马肉、染透的红卡其；也记得一面小红旗——滑稽得像标果岭的旗子——标出干沟那唯一的斜坡入口，沟壁此外皆陡如刀削；我总算活着到了沟里，实在谢天谢地。

===Original===
The same evening Connal, with a few other light casualties to assist him, took over the charge for which he had volunteered and for which he was so admirably fitted by his knowledge of horses and his general experience of the country; nevertheless, he managed to lose three or four fine chargers in the course of the first night; and, early in the second, Raffles shook me out of a heavy slumber in the trenches where we had been firing all day.

“I have found the spot, Bunny,” he whispered; “we ought to out him before the night is over.”

“Connal?”

Raffles nodded.

“You know what happened to some of his horses last night? Well, he let them go himself.”

“Never!”

===Chinese===
当晚，康纳尔带着另外几名轻伤号当帮手，接过了他自愿请领的那份差事——论相马之识，论对这国家的历练，他实在胜任得可敬；然而头一夜工夫，他硬是弄丢了两三匹上好的战马；到第二夜刚起头，拉夫尔斯在战壕里把我从沉睡中摇醒——我们整天都在那儿放枪。

“我找到地方了，邦尼，”他低声说，“趁夜没过完，就该把他击杀出局。”

“康纳尔？”

拉夫尔斯点头。

“昨晚他的马出了什么事，你听说了吧？哼，是他亲手放走的。”

“不会吧！”

===Original===
“I’m as certain of it,” said Raffles, “as though I’d seen him do it; and if he does it again I shall see him. I can even tell you how it happened. Connal insisted on having one end of the donga to himself, and of course his end is the one nearest the Boers. Well, then, he tells the other fellows to go to sleep at their end﻿—I have it direct from one of them﻿—and you bet they don’t need a second invitation. The rest I hope to see tonight.”

“It seems almost incredible,” said I.

“Not more so than the Light Horseman’s dodge of poisoning the troughs; that happened at Ladysmith before Christmas; and two kind friends did for that blackguard what you and I are going to do for this one, and a firing-party did the rest. Brutes! A mounted man’s worth a file on foot in this country, and well they know it. But this beauty goes one better than the poison; that was wilful waste; but I’ll eat my wideawake if our loss last night wasn’t the enemy’s double gain! What we’ve got to do, Bunny, is to catch him in the act. It may mean watching him all night, but was ever game so well worth the candle?”

One may say in passing that, at this particular point of contact, the enemy were in superior force, and for once in a mood as aggressive as our own. They were led with a dash, and handled with a skill, which did not always characterize their commanders at this stage of the war. Their position was very similar to ours, and indeed we were to spend the whole of next day in trying with an equal will to turn each other out. The result will scarcely be forgotten by those who recognize the occasion from these remarks. Meanwhile it was the eve of battle (most evenings were), and there was that villain with the horses in the donga, and here were we two upon his track.

===Chinese===
“我确定得如同亲眼看见他做的一样，”拉夫尔斯说，“他若再来一回，我就要亲眼看着他。我甚至能说给你那事是怎么出的。康纳尔非要在干沟独占一头不可，他那一头，自然正是离布尔人最近的一头。好，他就叫其余几个人在他们那头睡觉——这话我是从他们其中一位那儿直接听来的——你放心，这邀请不必说第二遍。剩下的，我今夜就等着看。”

“这几乎叫人难以相信。”我说。

“也难信不到哪儿去——那个轻骑兵毒水槽的把戏；那事儿圣诞前就出了，在莱迪史密斯（Ladysmith）；两位好心的朋友，把你我今夜要给这一个干的事，先给那恶棍干了一遍，行刑队干完了剩下的。畜生！在这个国家，马上的人一个抵得上步行的一列，他们心里明白得很。可这位妙人比下毒又高一着；下毒那还是故意糟蹋；可我敢吞下我的宽檐帽——昨晚我们折的马，准是敌人进账的双份！我们要做的，邦尼，是当场拿住他。这兴许要盯他一整夜，可天底下哪一局赌注，值得过这么一支蜡烛？”

顺带可以说一句：在这个特定的接火点上，敌人兵力占优，且破天荒摆出了与我们一样的攻势。他们受着敢冲敢打的统率，调度又见章法，而这在战争的这个阶段，并非他们将官的常色。他们的阵地同我们的极为相似；实际上，第二天一整天，我们就要以同样顽强的意志，各自想法子把对方撵出去。从这几句里认出是哪一仗的人，其结果大约不会忘怀。眼前，这是大战前夜（多数夜晚都是）；那恶棍带着马匹蹲在干沟里，我们两个正循着他的踪迹。

===Original===
Raffles’s plan was to reconnoitre the place, and then take up a position from which we could watch our man and pounce upon him if he gave us cause. The spot that we eventually chose and stealthily occupied was behind some bushes through which we could see down into the donga; there were the precious horses; and there sure enough was our wounded corporal, sitting smoking in his cloak, some glimmering thing in his lap.

“That’s his revolver, and it’s a Mauser,” whispered Raffles. “He shan’t have a chance of using it on us; either we must be on him before he knows we are anywhere near, or simply report. It’s easily proved once we are sure; but I should like to have the taking of him too.”

There was a setting moon. Shadows were sharp and black. The man smoked steadily, and the hungry horses did what I never saw horses do before; they stood and nibbled at each other’s tails. I was used to sleeping in the open, under the jewelled dome that seems so much vaster and grander in these wide spaces of the earth. I lay listening to the horses, and to the myriad small strange voices of the veldt, to which I cannot even now put a name, while Raffles watched. “One head is better than two,” he said, “when you don’t want it to be seen.” We were to take watch and watch about, however, and the other might sleep if he could; it was not my fault that I did nothing else; it was Raffles who could trust nobody but himself. Nor was there any time for recriminations when he did rouse me in the end.

===Chinese===
拉夫尔斯的计划是先侦察那地方，再择一位置伏定：既可监视那人，他若给了我们由头，还可猛扑上去。我们最终选中、又悄然伏定的位置，在一丛灌木之后，透过枝叶能望见干沟的深处；那些宝贝马就在那儿；果然，我们那位挂彩的下士也在，裹着斗篷坐着抽烟，膝上搁着一件微微闪光的玩意儿。

“那是他的左轮，还是把毛瑟，”拉夫尔斯低声说。“决不能给他对我们开火的机会；要么趁他浑然不觉我们已经贴近就扑上去，要么干脆上报。一旦确凿，取证不难；不过拿住他的手，我也想出一份。”

月亮正在西沉。影子又利又黑。那人烟抽个不停，饿马则做了我从未见马做过的事：站着彼此啃尾巴。我睡惯了露天，睡在缀满宝石的穹顶之下——在这大地广袤的空旷处，那穹顶显得格外辽阔壮丽。我躺着，听马，听草原上无数细小而陌生的声息——那些声息，我至今叫不出名字——拉夫尔斯放哨。“不想让人瞧见的时候，一颗脑袋总比两颗强，”他说。不过说好轮流值哨，另一个若睡得着就睡；我除了睡什么也没干，那可不能怪我；是拉夫尔斯谁也不肯信，只信他自己。到末了他把我摇醒时，也顾不上互相埋怨了。

===Original===
But a moment ago, as it seemed to me, I had been gazing upward at the stars and listening to the dear, minute sounds of peace; and in another the great gray slate was clean, and every bone of me set in plaster of Paris, and sniping beginning between pickets with the day. It was an occasional crack, not a constant crackle, but the whistle of a bullet as it passed us by, or a tiny transitory flame for the one bit of detail on a blue hillside, was an unpleasant warning that we two on ours were a target in ourselves. But Raffles paid no attention to their fire; he was pointing downward through the bushes to where Corporal Connal stood with his back to us, shooing a last charger out of the mouth of the donga towards the Boer trenches.

“That’s his third,” whispered Raffles, “but it’s the first I’ve seen distinctly, for he waited for the blind spot before the dawn. It’s enough to land him, I fancy, but we mustn’t lose time. Are you ready for a creep?”

I stretched myself, and said I was; but I devoutly wished it was not quite so early in the morning.

“Like cats, then, till he hears, and then into him for all we’re worth. He’s stowed his iron safe away, but he mustn’t have time even to feel for it. You take his left arm, Bunny, and hang on to that like a ferret, and I’ll do the rest. Ready? Then now!”

===Chinese===
在我仿佛方才还在仰观星斗，静听和平那细小可爱的声息；一转眼，那块巨大的灰色石板已拭得干干净净，我浑身的骨头都封进了熟石膏，哨兵之间的狙击已随天光打响。偶尔一声脆响，并非连绵的噼啪；可子弹掠过时的呼啸，或者青蓝山坡上一点即逝的微小火光——那山坡上唯一的细部——都在不愉快地警告：我们两人在我们这面山坡上，本身就是一个靶子。拉夫尔斯却没理会他们的枪火；他正透过灌木朝下指——康纳尔下士背对我们站在那儿，正轰赶最后一匹战马，把它从干沟口赶向布尔人的战壕。

“这是他的第三匹，”拉夫尔斯低声说，“却是我头一匹看得真切的，因为他专候黎明前那个看不见的时辰。拿这去治他，我想是够了，可我们不能耽搁。准备好爬了吗？”

我伸了伸懒腰，说准备好了；心里却极诚地巴望这早晨别来得这么早。

“那就像猫一样猫着，直到他察觉，然后拿出全部本事扑上去。他那把铁家伙已稳稳收好，可决不能容他来得及去摸一摸。你抓他的左臂，邦尼，像雪貂咬定猎物一样咬住不放，剩下的我来。预备了？那么——上！”

===Original===
And in less time than it would take to tell, we were over the lip of the donga and had fallen upon the fellow before he could turn his head; nevertheless, for a few instants he fought like a wild beast, striking, kicking, and swinging me off my feet as I obeyed my instructions to the letter, and stuck to his left like a leech. But he soon gave that up, panting and blaspheming, demanded explanations in his hybrid tongue that had half a brogue and half a burr. What were we doing? What had he done? Raffles at his back, with his right wrist twisted round and pinned into the small of it, soon told him that, and I think the words must have been the first intimation that he had as to who his assailants were.

“So it’s you two!” he cried, and a light broke over him. He was no longer trying to shake us off, and now he dropped his curses also, and stood chuckling to himself instead. “Well,” he went on, “you’re bloody liars both, but I know something else that you are, so you’d better let go.”

A coldness ran through me, and I never saw Raffles so taken aback. His grip must have relaxed for a fraction of time, for our captive broke out in a fresh and desperate struggle, but now we pinned him tighter than ever, and soon I saw him turning green and yellow with the pain.

“You’re breaking my wrist!” he yelled at last.

“Then stand still and tell us who we are.”

And he stood still and told us our real names. But Raffles insisted on hearing how he had found us out, and smiled as though he had known what was coming when it came. I was dumbfounded.

===Chinese===
说来话长、做来顷刻：我们已翻过干沟沿，那人还没来得及回头，我们已扑在他身上；然而有那么几瞬，他像野兽一样搏斗，拳打、脚踢，把我抡得双脚离地——而我遵旨唯谨，像条蚂蟥死死叮住他的左臂。可他很快撒了手，喘着粗气、骂着渎神的话，用他那半爱尔兰腔、半苏格兰卷舌的杂糅口音，讨起说法来。我们在干什么？他干了什么？拉夫尔斯在他背后，把他的右腕拧到背后、别进腰窝，很快让他明白了；我想，这几句话必定是他头一回得知：动手的是谁。

“原来是你们两个！”他嚷道，脸上豁然一亮。他不再挣甩我们了，连咒骂也撂下了，自顾自嘿嘿地笑。“好，”他接着说，“你们俩是天大的谎话精，可我还知道你们是什么，你们最好撒手。”

一股寒气掠过我全身；拉夫尔斯那副失措相，我前所未见。他的手必定松了一霎，因为我们的俘虏爆发出新一轮殊死的挣扎；可这回我们钳得更死，不多时，我便看见他疼得脸色青一阵黄一阵。

“你们拧断我的手腕啦！”他终于嚎起来。

“那就站定，说说我们是谁。”

他果真站定了，把我们的真名实姓说了出来。可拉夫尔斯定要问清他是怎么识破我们的；待答案来了，他微微一笑，仿佛早料到会是这样。我目瞪口呆。

===Original===
The accursed hound had followed us that evening to Captain Bellingham’s tent, and his undoubted cleverness in his own profession of spy had done the rest.

“And now you’d better let me go,” said the master of the situation, as I for one could not help regarding him.

“I’ll see you damned,” said Raffles, savagely.

“Then you’re damned and done for yourself, my cocky criminal. Raffles the burglar! Raffles the society thief! Not dead after all, but ’live and ’listed. Send him home and give him fourteen years, and won’t he like ’em, that’s all!”

“I shall have the pleasure of hearing you shot first,” retorted Raffles, through his teeth, “and that alone will make them bearable. Come on, Bunny, let’s drive the swine along and get it over.”

===Chinese===
该死的恶犬那晚一路跟我们到了贝林厄姆上尉的帐篷；剩下的事，全靠他那一行——奸细行当——里不可否认的聪明。

“现在你们最好放我走。”局面之主如此说道——我自己也情不自禁把他看作局面之主。

“我倒要先看你下地狱，”拉夫尔斯恶狠狠地说。

“那你自己也就下了地狱、完蛋大吉了，我那位神气活现的罪犯先生。窃贼拉夫尔斯！上流社会的贼拉夫尔斯！原来没死，还活着，还入了伍！把他送回国内，判他十四年——他坐起来才带劲呢，就等着瞧吧！”

“我会先荣幸地听着你挨枪子儿，”拉夫尔斯咬着牙回敬，“单凭这一件，那十四年就好受多了。来吧，邦尼，把那猪猡赶着走，早了早了。”

===Original===
And drive him we did, he cursing, cajoling, struggling, gloating, and blubbering by turns. But Raffles never wavered for an instant, though his face was tragic, and it went to my heart, where that look stays still. I remember at the time, though I never let my hold relax, there was a moment when I added my entreaties to those of our prisoner. Raffles did not even reply to me. But I was thinking of him, I swear. I was thinking of that gray set face that I never saw before or after.

“Your story will be tested,” said the commanding officer, when Connal had been marched to the guard-tent. “Is there any truth in his?”

“It is perfectly true, sir.”

“And the notorious Raffles has been alive all these years, and you are really he?”

“I am, sir.”

“And what are you doing at the front?”

===Chinese===
我们果真把他赶着走，他一路又咒又哄、又挣又得意、又抽抽搭搭地哭，轮番来。可拉夫尔斯一瞬也不曾动摇，尽管他的脸是悲怆的；那张脸扎进我心里，那副神情至今留在原处。我记得当时，尽管双手一刻未松，我曾有一个瞬间，把自己的恳求也加到俘虏的恳求里。拉夫尔斯连理也没理我。可我心里想的是他，我发誓。我想的是那张灰白的、绷紧的脸——此前此后，我都再没见过。

“你的话是要查证的，”康纳尔被押往卫兵帐篷之后，指挥官说道。“他的话有几分真？”

“千真万确，长官。”

“而那个声名狼藉的拉夫尔斯，这些年一直活着，你当真是他？”

“正是，长官。”

“那你在前线做什么？”

===Original===
Somehow I thought that Raffles was going to smile, but the grim set of his mouth never altered, neither was there any change in the ashy pallor which had come over him in the donga when Connal mouthed his name. It was only his eyes that lighted up at the last question.

“I am fighting, sir,” said he, as simply as any subaltern in the army.

The commanding officer inclined a grizzled head perceptibly, and no more. He was not one of any school, our General; he had his own ways, and we loved both him and them; and I believe that he loved the rough but gallant corps that bore his name. He once told us that he knew something about most of us, and there were things that Raffles had done of which he must have heard. But he only moved his grizzled head.

“Did you know he was going to give you away?” he asked at length, with a jerk of it toward the guard-tent.

===Chinese===
不知怎么，我以为拉夫尔斯要笑了，可他嘴角的紧绷纹丝未动；干沟里康纳尔念出他名字时爬上他面孔的那种死灰的苍白，也没有半分变化。只有眼睛，在最后一个问题上亮了起来。

“我在打仗，长官。”他说，语气平实得像军中随便哪个中尉。

指挥官那颗花白的头显而易见地点了点，再没有别的。我们的将军不属于任何流派；他有他自己的路数，我们既爱他这个人，也爱他的路数；而我相信，他也爱那支粗粝却骁勇的、以他名字命名的部队。他曾对我们说，我们大多数人的底细他知道一些；拉夫尔斯干过的那些事，他不可能没听说。可他只动了动那颗花白的头。

“你可知道他要把你们供出去？”他终于发问，头朝卫兵帐篷一甩。

===Original===
“Yes, sir.”

“But you thought it worth while, did you?”

“I thought it necessary, sir.”

The General paused, drumming on his table, making up his mind. Then his chin came up with the decision that we loved in him.

“I shall sift all this,” said he. “An officer’s name was mentioned, and I shall see him myself. Meanwhile you had better go on﻿—fighting.”

===Chinese===
“知道，长官。”

“可你觉得值得，是吗？”

“我觉得必须，长官。”

将军停了停，手指敲着桌子，打定主意。随后他扬起下巴，带着我们爱他的那份果决。

“这一切我要细细筛过，”他说。“方才提到一位军官的名字，我要亲自去见他。眼前，你们最好接着——打你们的仗去。”

### IV / 四

===Original===
